Revelation — Saints Holy Bible cross-references
405 completed source verses; 1040 retained pairs; 405 source units read; 0 deferred units.
First unfinished: none. Next new source review in this book: none. All original source units in this book have been read. Overall next new source: none.
Completed means an initial curated cross-reference pass: the complete supplied English source unit and each retained target passage were read, and each pair has a specific explanation. It does not mean exhaustive coverage or independent specialist approval.
Original Saints numbering is retained.

Revelation 1:1
A revelation of Jesus Christ, which God gave Him to show His slaves the things that must take place soon. He made it known by sending through His angel to His slave John,
  Revelation 22:6 — internal cross-reference: The book's closing repeats its opening formula almost word for word: God “sent His angel to show His slaves the things that must take place soon.” The two verses frame the whole revelation.
    Revelation 22:6 And he said unto me, “These words are faithful and true, and the Lord, the God of the spirits of the prophets, sent His angel to show His slaves the things that must take place soon.”
  Revelation 22:16 — internal cross-reference: Both verses describe the revelation as delivered through an angel: in 1:1 it is made known “by sending through His angel to His slave John,” and in 22:16 Jesus says, “I, Jesus, sent My angel to testify these things to you for the Churches.” Revelation 22:16 restates in Jesus’ own voice the chain of transmission set out in the opening verse.
    Revelation 22:16 “I, Jesus, sent My angel to testify these things to you for the Churches. I am the Root and the offspring of David, the bright morning star.”
  Daniel 2:28–2:29 — verbal allusion: Daniel speaks of a God in heaven who “disclosed” to the king “what must happen in the last days,” and Revelation opens with what God gave “to show His slaves the things that must take place.” Daniel’s disclosure comes through a dream to a king, whereas Revelation’s comes through an angel to John.
    Daniel 2:28 but there is a God in heaven who uncovers mysteries, who has disclosed to King Nebuchadnezzar what must happen in the last days. O king, live for ever! This is the dream and the vision of your head upon your bed:
    Daniel 2:29 You, O king, reclining upon your bed, saw everything that must happen in the last days, and the Revealer of mysteries disclosed to you what must happen.

Revelation 1:2
who testified to the word of God and the testimony of Jesus Christ, all that he saw.
  Revelation 1:9 — internal cross-reference: Both verses join “the word of God” and “the testimony of Jesus”: in 1:2 as what John testified to, and in 1:9 as the reason John came to be on Patmos.
    Revelation 1:9 I, John, your brother and fellow partner in the affliction and kingdom and endurance in Jesus, came to be on the island called Patmos because of the word of God and the testimony of Jesus.
  Revelation 6:9 — verbal echo: The martyrs under the altar were slain “because of the word of God and because of the testimony they held,” the same pairing that describes what John testified to. In Revelation 6:9 the testimony is that held by the martyrs rather than “the testimony of Jesus Christ” by name.
    Revelation 6:9 And when He opened the fifth seal, I saw beneath the altar the souls of those slaughtered because of the word of God and because of the testimony they held.

Revelation 1:3
Blessed is he that readeth, and they that hear the words of the prophecy and keep the things written therein, for the time is near.
  Revelation 22:6–22:7 — internal cross-reference: The opening and closing of the book both pronounce a blessing on the one who keeps “the words of the prophecy.” Revelation 22:7 joins this beatitude to the promise “I am coming quickly.”
    Revelation 22:6 And he said unto me, “These words are faithful and true, and the Lord, the God of the spirits of the prophets, sent His angel to show His slaves the things that must take place soon.”
    Revelation 22:7 “And behold, I am coming quickly. Blessed is the one who keepeth the words of the prophecy of this scroll.”
  Revelation 22:10 — internal cross-reference: Both verses speak of “the words of the prophecy” and close with the same reason, “for the time is near.” In Revelation 22:10 the point is that the scroll is not to be sealed.
    Revelation 22:10 And he saith unto me, “Do not seal the words of the prophecy of this scroll, for the time is near.
  Luke 11:28 — verbal echo: Both pronounce a blessing on those who hear and keep: “blessed are those who hear the word of God and keep it.” Luke speaks of the word of God generally, Revelation of the words of this prophecy.
    Luke 11:28 But He said, “Rather, blessed are those who hear the word of God and keep it!”

Revelation 1:4
John, to the seven Churches in Asia: grace to you and peace from the One who is and who was and who is coming, and from the seven spirits before His throne,
  Revelation 1:8 — same divine title: Both use the identical threefold title “the One who is and who was and who is coming.” Revelation 1:8 applies it to the Lord God, who says “I am the Alpha and the Omega,” and adds “the Almighty.”
    Revelation 1:8 “I am the Alpha and the Omega,” saith the Lord God, the One who is and who was and who is coming, the Almighty.
  Revelation 4:5 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 1:4 mentions “the seven spirits before His throne,” and Revelation 4:5 sees “seven lamps of fire” burning “before the throne, which are the seven spirits of God.” The later vision gives the image behind the greeting’s phrase.
    Revelation 4:5 And from the throne come out lightnings and voices and thunders; and seven lamps of fire burn before the throne, which are the seven spirits of God.
  Exodus 3:14 — divine name background: In the Greek wording followed here, God names Himself to Moses “the One Who Is,” the first element of Revelation’s title for God. Exodus has only this single designation; Revelation extends it with “who was and who is coming.”
    Exodus 3:14 And God said to Moses, “I am the One Who Is.” And He said, “This is what you shall say to the sons of Israel: ‘The One Who Is has sent me to you.’”

Revelation 1:5
and from Jesus Christ, the faithful witness, the firstborn of the dead and the ruler of the kings of the earth. To the One who loveth us and freed us from our sins in His blood—
  Psalms 88:28 — verbal allusion: The Psalm promises, “I will make him firstborn, high among the kings of the earth,” and Revelation calls Jesus “the firstborn of the dead and the ruler of the kings of the earth.” Revelation adds “of the dead,” applying the title to resurrection.
    Psalms 88:28 And I will make him firstborn, high among the kings of the earth.
  Colossians 1:18 — same title of Christ: Both call Christ “the firstborn from the dead” (Revelation: “of the dead”). Colossians links the title to His having “first place in all things,” Revelation to His rule over “the kings of the earth.”
    Colossians 1:18 And He is the head of the body, the Church; He is the beginning, the firstborn from the dead, so that He Himself might have first place in all things,
  1 John 1:7 — shared theme: Both speak of deliverance from sins by Jesus’ blood: “freed us from our sins in His blood” and “the blood of Jesus His Son cleanses us from every sin.” John’s letter frames this within walking in the light and fellowship.
    1 John 1:7 But if we walk in the light, as He is in the light, we have fellowship with one another, and the blood of Jesus His Son cleanses us from every sin.

Revelation 1:6
and made us a kingdom, priests to His God and Father—to Him be the glory and dominion for the ages of the ages. Amen.
  Revelation 5:10 — internal cross-reference: Both say that God’s people have been made “a kingdom” and “priests” to God (1:6 “made us a kingdom, priests”; 5:10 “Thou didst make them a kingdom and priests”). Revelation 5:10 says this in an address to “Thou” and adds that “they shall reign upon the earth.”
    Revelation 5:10 and Thou didst make them a kingdom and priests unto our God, and they shall reign upon the earth.”
  Exodus 19:6 — verbal allusion: God’s words for the sons of Israel, “you shall be to Me a royal priesthood and a holy nation,” stand behind Revelation’s “a kingdom, priests.” Exodus addresses the sons of Israel; Revelation applies the status to “us,” made a kingdom and priests to His God and Father.
    Exodus 19:6 And you shall be to Me a royal priesthood and a holy nation.’ These are the words which you shall speak to the sons of Israel.”
  1 Peter 4:11 — shared doxology: Both close with an ascription of “the glory and the dominion” forever, ending in “Amen.” Peter’s ascription follows “so that in all things God may be glorified through Jesus Christ,” while Revelation’s is addressed “to Him” who made us a kingdom, priests to His God and Father.
    1 Peter 4:11 If anyone speaks, let it be as words from God; if anyone serves, let it be with the strength God supplies, so that in all things God may be glorified through Jesus Christ, to whom belong the glory and the dominion forever and ever. Amen.

Revelation 1:7
Behold, He is coming with the clouds, and every eye will see Him, even those who pierced Him, and all the tribes of the earth will mourn over Him. Yea, amen.
  Daniel 7:13 — verbal allusion: Daniel sees “one like a son of man” coming “upon the clouds of heaven,” and Revelation announces, “He is coming with the clouds.” Daniel’s figure is seen in a night vision; Revelation applies the image to a coming seen by “every eye.”
    Daniel 7:13 I watched in the night vision, and there, upon the clouds of heaven, one like a son of man was coming. He was present as an Ancient of Days, and those standing by were present with him.
  Zechariah 12:10 — verbal allusion: Zechariah says that they “will look toward Me” and “mourn over him,” matching Revelation’s seeing and mourning “over Him.” The Greek-based wording here reads “because they danced in mockery,” so the word “pierced” in Revelation is not found in this Zechariah text.
    Zechariah 12:10 I will pour upon David’s house and Jerusalem’s residents a spirit of grace and compassion. They will look toward Me because they danced in mockery, and mourn over him as over a beloved one; they will suffer grief as over a firstborn.
  Matthew 24:30 — parallel saying: Both combine His coming on the clouds (Revelation “with the clouds”; Matthew “upon the clouds of heaven”) with His being seen and the statement that “all the tribes of the earth will mourn.” Matthew speaks of “the Son of Man” and “the sign” in heaven; Revelation adds “those who pierced Him.”
    Matthew 24:30 Then the sign of the Son of Man will appear in heaven, and then all the tribes of the earth will mourn; they will see the Son of Man coming upon the clouds of heaven with power and great glory.

Revelation 1:8
“I am the Alpha and the Omega,” saith the Lord God, the One who is and who was and who is coming, the Almighty.
  Revelation 22:13 — same divine title: Both use the self-designation “I am the Alpha and the Omega.” Revelation 22:13 expands it with “the first and the last, the beginning and the end.”
    Revelation 22:13 I am the Alpha and the Omega, the first and the last, the beginning and the end.”
  Revelation 4:8 — same divine title: Both join “Lord God” and “Almighty” with the threefold title of the One who is, was and is coming. Revelation 4:8 gives the order “who was and who is and who is coming” in the living creatures’ hymn.
    Revelation 4:8 And the four living creatures, each one of them having six wings, are full of eyes around and within; and they have no rest day and night, saying, “Holy, holy, holy, Lord God Almighty, the One who was and who is and who is coming.”
  Isaiah 44:6 — Old Testament background: God declares, “I am first, and I am after these things; apart from Me there is no God,” expressing in Isaiah what “the Alpha and the Omega” expresses with the first and last letters. Isaiah does not use the alphabet image.
    Isaiah 44:6 Thus says God, Israel’s King, who rescued him, God Sabaoth: “I am first, and I am after these things; apart from Me there is no God.

Revelation 1:9
I, John, your brother and fellow partner in the affliction and kingdom and endurance in Jesus, came to be on the island called Patmos because of the word of God and the testimony of Jesus.
  Revelation 1:2 — internal cross-reference: Both verses join “the word of God” and “the testimony of Jesus”: Revelation 1:2 describes them as what John testified to, and 1:9 gives the same pair as the reason he was on Patmos.
    Revelation 1:2 who testified to the word of God and the testimony of Jesus Christ, all that he saw.
  Acts 14:22 — shared theme: Both join affliction with the kingdom: John is a partner “in the affliction and kingdom,” and Acts teaches that “through many afflictions we must enter the kingdom of God.”
    Acts 14:22 strengthening the souls of the disciples, encouraging them to remain in the faith, and saying, “Through many afflictions we must enter the kingdom of God.”
  Revelation 19:10 — verbal echo: The angel calls himself John’s “fellow slave” and of his “brothers who hold the testimony of Jesus,” echoing John’s description of himself as “brother and fellow partner” because of “the testimony of Jesus.”
    Revelation 19:10 And I fell before his feet to worship him. And he saith unto me, “See that thou do it not! I am thy fellow slave and of thy brothers who hold the testimony of Jesus. Worship God, for the testimony of Jesus is the spirit of prophecy.”

Revelation 1:10
I came to be in the Spirit on the Lord’s day, and heard behind me a loud voice like a trumpet,
  Revelation 4:1–4:2 — parallel vision: Both describe a voice “like a trumpet” speaking to John and his coming to be “in the Spirit.” In Revelation 4:1–2 this leads to the vision of the heavenly throne.
    Revelation 4:1 After these things I saw, and behold, a door opened in heaven, and the first voice I heard like a trumpet speaking with me said, “Come up hither, and I will show thee what must take place after these things.”
    Revelation 4:2 immediately I came to be in the Spirit; and behold, a throne was set in heaven, and One sitting upon the throne.
  Revelation 17:3 — same visionary formula: John is “carried me away in the Spirit” to see the woman on the scarlet beast, using the same “in the Spirit” language that opens the first vision. Revelation 17:3 involves being carried to a place, which 1:10 does not mention.
    Revelation 17:3 And he carried me away in the Spirit into a wilderness. And I saw a woman sitting upon a scarlet beast, full of names of blasphemy, having seven heads and ten horns.
  Revelation 21:10 — same visionary formula: John is “carried me away in the Spirit” to see the holy city Jerusalem, the same phrase that introduces the first vision. As with 17:3, the later verse adds a transport to a location.
    Revelation 21:10 And he carried me away in the Spirit upon a great and high mountain, and showed me the holy city Jerusalem coming down out of heaven from God,

Revelation 1:11
saying, “What thou seest, write in a scroll and send to the seven Churches: to Ephesus and to Smyrna and to Pergamum and to Thyatira and to Sardis and to Philadelphia and to Laodicea.”
  Habakkuk 2:2 — prophetic commission to write: Both are divine commands to write down a vision: “What thou seest, write” and “Write a vision clearly.” Habakkuk names a tablet; Revelation names a scroll to be sent to seven Churches.
    Habakkuk 2:2 The Lord answered me: “Write a vision clearly on a boxwood tablet, so that the one reading these things may pursue them.
  Jeremiah 37:2 — prophetic commission to write: The Lord tells Jeremiah, “Write in a book all the words I delivered to you,” a close parallel to John’s command to “write in a scroll.” Jeremiah records spoken oracles, John what he sees.
    Jeremiah 37:2 Thus said the Lord, Israel’s God: Write in a book all the words I delivered to you as oracles,
  Revelation 1:4 — internal cross-reference: Both name the addressees as “the seven Churches,” 1:4 placing them “in Asia” and 1:11 listing the seven cities by name.
    Revelation 1:4 John, to the seven Churches in Asia: grace to you and peace from the One who is and who was and who is coming, and from the seven spirits before His throne,

Revelation 1:12
And I turned to see the voice that was speaking with me, and having turned, I saw seven golden lampstands,
  Zechariah 4:2 — shared image: Zechariah sees a golden lampstand with “seven lamps upon it,” the background for John’s golden lampstands. Zechariah sees one lampstand bearing seven lamps, John “seven golden lampstands.”
    Zechariah 4:2 and said to me, “What do you see?” I said, “I see a lampstand entirely of gold, with its lamp-bowl above it, seven lamps upon it, and seven pouring channels for the lamps above it.
  Revelation 1:20–2:1 — interpretation within the vision: Revelation 1:20 explains “the seven golden lampstands” as “seven Churches,” and 2:1 describes Christ as walking “in the midst of the seven golden lampstands.”
    Revelation 1:20 The mystery of the seven stars thou sawest upon My right hand, and the seven golden lampstands: the seven stars are angels of the seven Churches, and the seven lampstands are seven Churches.”
    Revelation 2:1 “To the angel of the Church in Ephesus write: These things saith the One who holdeth the seven stars in His right hand, who walketh in the midst of the seven golden lampstands:
  Revelation 1:13 — continuation of the vision: The next verse completes the scene John turned to see: “in the midst of the lampstands One like a son of man.”
    Revelation 1:13 and in the midst of the lampstands One like a son of man, clothed in a robe reaching to the feet and girded around the chest with a golden belt.

Revelation 1:13
and in the midst of the lampstands One like a son of man, clothed in a robe reaching to the feet and girded around the chest with a golden belt.
  Daniel 7:13 — same title: Both use the description “one like a son of man.” Daniel’s figure comes upon the clouds of heaven; Revelation’s stands among the lampstands.
    Daniel 7:13 I watched in the night vision, and there, upon the clouds of heaven, one like a son of man was coming. He was present as an Ancient of Days, and those standing by were present with him.
  Daniel 10:5–10:6 — shared image: Daniel sees “a man clothed in fine linen” with linen “wrapped around his waist,” comparable to the figure “clothed in a robe” and “girded around the chest with a golden belt.” Daniel places the girding at the waist and does not mention gold.
    Daniel 10:5 and I lifted my eyes and saw a man clothed in fine linen, with fine linen wrapped around his waist, and light coming from his middle.
    Daniel 10:6 His body was like a tarshish stone, his face like a flash of lightning, his eyes like flaming torches, his arms and feet like flashing bronze, and the sound of his speech like the sound of an uproar.
  Revelation 15:6 — verbal echo: The seven angels are “girded around their chests with golden belts,” the same attire described for the One like a son of man.
    Revelation 15:6 and the seven angels having the seven plagues came out of the temple, clothed in pure, bright linen and girded around their chests with golden belts.

Revelation 1:14
And His head and hair were white like white wool, like snow, and His eyes like a flame of fire,
  Daniel 7:9 — shared image: Daniel describes the Ancient of Days with a covering “like snow” and hair “like pure white wool,” with a throne “like a flame of fire.” Revelation applies white wool and snow to the head and hair and the flame of fire to the eyes.
    Daniel 7:9 I watched until thrones were set in place and an Ancient of Days was seated, wearing a covering like snow, with the hair of His head like pure white wool. The throne was like a flame of fire,
  Daniel 10:6 — shared image: Daniel’s heavenly man has “eyes like flaming torches,” comparable to “eyes like a flame of fire.”
    Daniel 10:6 His body was like a tarshish stone, his face like a flash of lightning, his eyes like flaming torches, his arms and feet like flashing bronze, and the sound of his speech like the sound of an uproar.
  Revelation 2:18 — internal cross-reference: The letter to Thyatira introduces the Son of God as having “His eyes like a flame of fire,” repeating this feature of the opening vision.
    Revelation 2:18 And to the angel of the Church in Thyatira write: These things saith the Son of God, who hath His eyes like a flame of fire, and His feet like burnished bronze:

Revelation 1:15
and His feet like burnished bronze, as refined in a furnace, and His voice like the sound of many waters.
  Revelation 2:18 — internal cross-reference: The letter to Thyatira repeats “His feet like burnished bronze” from the opening vision.
    Revelation 2:18 And to the angel of the Church in Thyatira write: These things saith the Son of God, who hath His eyes like a flame of fire, and His feet like burnished bronze:
  Daniel 10:6 — shared image: Daniel’s heavenly man has “arms and feet like flashing bronze” and speech like “the sound of an uproar,” paralleling the bronze feet and overwhelming voice. Daniel does not use the phrase “many waters.”
    Daniel 10:6 His body was like a tarshish stone, his face like a flash of lightning, his eyes like flaming torches, his arms and feet like flashing bronze, and the sound of his speech like the sound of an uproar.
  Psalms 92:4 — verbal echo: The Psalm speaks of “the voices of many waters” with the Lord wondrous on high, matching “the sound of many waters.” In the Psalm the waters are the sea, not the Lord’s voice itself.
    Psalms 92:4 From the voices of many waters, wondrous are the sea’s surges; wondrous on high is the Lord.

Revelation 1:16
And He held seven stars in His right hand, and from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out, and His appearance was like the sun shining in its strength.
  Revelation 2:12 — internal cross-reference: The letter to Pergamum introduces Christ as “the One who hath the sharp two-edged sword,” repeating this feature of the opening vision.
    Revelation 2:12 And to the angel of the Church in Pergamum write: These things saith the One who hath the sharp two-edged sword:
  Isaiah 49:2 — shared image: The speaker in Isaiah says that God “made my mouth like a sharp sword,” the image behind the sharp two-edged sword coming from His mouth in Revelation. In Isaiah the mouth is compared to a sword; in Revelation a sword comes out of it.
    Isaiah 49:2 and made my mouth like a sharp sword. In the shelter of His hand He hid me; He made me a chosen arrow and sheltered me in His quiver.
  Matthew 17:2 — shared image: At the transfiguration Jesus’ face “shone like the sun,” comparable to His appearance “like the sun shining in its strength.”
    Matthew 17:2 He was transfigured before them. His face shone like the sun, and His garments became white like the light.

Revelation 1:17
And when I saw Him, I fell at His feet as dead. And He placed His right hand upon me, saying, “Fear not. I am the first and the last,
  Isaiah 44:6 — divine self-designation: God says, “I am first, and I am after these things,” the background to “I am the first and the last.” Isaiah’s wording uses “after these things” rather than “the last.”
    Isaiah 44:6 Thus says God, Israel’s King, who rescued him, God Sabaoth: “I am first, and I am after these things; apart from Me there is no God.
  Revelation 2:8 — internal cross-reference: The letter to Smyrna introduces Christ as “the first and the last, who became dead and lived,” repeating the self-designation of 1:17 together with 1:18.
    Revelation 2:8 And to the angel of the Church in Smyrna write: These things saith the first and the last, who became dead and lived:
  Ezekiel 1:28 — parallel response to vision: Ezekiel, seeing the likeness of the Lord’s glory, falls upon his face and hears a voice speaking, as John falls “at His feet as dead” and is spoken to. Ezekiel is not touched by a hand in this verse.
    Ezekiel 1:28 Like the appearance of a bow when it is in the cloud on a rainy day, so was the arrangement of the brightness all around. This was the appearance of the likeness of the Lord’s glory. I saw it, and I fall upon my face, and I heard the voice of One speaking.

Revelation 1:18
and the living One. I became dead, and behold, I am living for the ages of the ages, and I have the keys of death and Hades.
  Romans 6:9 — shared theme: Both state that Christ died and now lives without death having power over Him: “no longer dies; death no longer rules over Him,” and “I became dead, and behold, I am living for the ages of the ages.”
    Romans 6:9 knowing that Christ, having been raised from the dead, no longer dies; death no longer rules over Him.
  Wisdom of Solomon 16:13 — shared image: Wisdom ascribes to God “authority over life and death” and the power to bring down to and up from “the gates of Hades,” comparable to Christ’s “keys of death and Hades.” Wisdom speaks of gates, Revelation of keys.
    Wisdom of Solomon 16:13 For Thou hast authority over life and death, and Thou bringest down to the gates of Hades and bringest up again;
  Revelation 20:14 — internal cross-reference: “Death and Hades” appear again as a pair when they are “thrown into the lake of fire,” showing the end of the powers whose keys Christ holds.
    Revelation 20:14 And Death and Hades were thrown into the lake of fire. This is the second death, the lake of fire.

Revelation 1:19
Therefore write the things thou sawest, and the things that are, and the things that are to take place after these.
  Revelation 1:11 — internal cross-reference: The command “write the things thou sawest” repeats the first commission in 1:11, “What thou seest, write in a scroll.” 1:19 adds the things that are and the things to take place after these.
    Revelation 1:11 saying, “What thou seest, write in a scroll and send to the seven Churches: to Ephesus and to Smyrna and to Pergamum and to Thyatira and to Sardis and to Philadelphia and to Laodicea.”

Revelation 1:20
The mystery of the seven stars thou sawest upon My right hand, and the seven golden lampstands: the seven stars are angels of the seven Churches, and the seven lampstands are seven Churches.”
  Revelation 2:1 — internal cross-reference: The first letter introduces Christ as holding “the seven stars in His right hand” and walking among “the seven golden lampstands,” the two symbols explained in 1:20.
    Revelation 2:1 “To the angel of the Church in Ephesus write: These things saith the One who holdeth the seven stars in His right hand, who walketh in the midst of the seven golden lampstands:
  Revelation 1:16 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 1:16 is the description being explained: “He held seven stars in His right hand.”
    Revelation 1:16 And He held seven stars in His right hand, and from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out, and His appearance was like the sun shining in its strength.
  Zechariah 4:2 — shared image: Zechariah’s golden lampstand with “seven lamps upon it” stands behind the seven golden lampstands. Zechariah has one lampstand, and the explanation as Churches is Revelation’s own.
    Zechariah 4:2 and said to me, “What do you see?” I said, “I see a lampstand entirely of gold, with its lamp-bowl above it, seven lamps upon it, and seven pouring channels for the lamps above it.

Revelation 2:1
“To the angel of the Church in Ephesus write: These things saith the One who holdeth the seven stars in His right hand, who walketh in the midst of the seven golden lampstands:
  Revelation 1:20 — internal cross-reference: Both mention the seven stars in the right hand and “the seven golden lampstands”; 1:20 explains them as angels of the Churches and the Churches themselves.
    Revelation 1:20 The mystery of the seven stars thou sawest upon My right hand, and the seven golden lampstands: the seven stars are angels of the seven Churches, and the seven lampstands are seven Churches.”
  Revelation 1:16 — internal cross-reference: The title in the letter to Ephesus repeats from the opening vision that He “held seven stars in His right hand.”
    Revelation 1:16 And He held seven stars in His right hand, and from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out, and His appearance was like the sun shining in its strength.

Revelation 2:2
‘I know thy works, and the labour and thine endurance, and that thou canst not bear evil people; and thou didst test those who call themselves apostles and are not, and didst find them false.
  2 Corinthians 11:13–11:15 — shared theme: Both concern people who claim to be apostles falsely: Paul speaks of “false apostles” disguising themselves “as apostles of Christ,” and Ephesus tested “those who call themselves apostles and are not.”
    2 Corinthians 11:13 For such people are false apostles, deceitful workers, disguising themselves as apostles of Christ.
    2 Corinthians 11:14 And no wonder, for Satan himself disguises himself as an angel of light.
    2 Corinthians 11:15 Therefore it is no surprise if his ministers also disguise themselves as ministers of righteousness; their end will be according to their works.
  1 John 4:1 — shared theme: Both commend testing claimants: “test the spirits to see whether they are from God,” and “thou didst test those who call themselves apostles.” John’s letter speaks of false prophets rather than false apostles.
    1 John 4:1 Beloved, do not believe every spirit, but test the spirits to see whether they are from God, because many false prophets have gone out into the world.
  Revelation 2:9 — parallel construction: The letter to Smyrna uses the same pattern of false self-description, “those who say that they themselves are Jews and are not,” as Ephesus’s “those who call themselves apostles and are not.”
    Revelation 2:9 ‘I know thine affliction and poverty—but thou art rich—and the slander from those who say that they themselves are Jews and are not, but are a synagogue of Satan.

Revelation 2:3
And thou hast endurance and didst bear up for My name’s sake, and hast not grown weary.
  John 15:21 — shared theme: Jesus foretells hostility “because of My name,” and the Ephesians are commended for bearing up “for My name’s sake.”
    John 15:21 But they will do all these things to you because of My name, because they do not know the One who sent Me.
  Revelation 3:10 — internal cross-reference: Philadelphia is commended for keeping “the word of My endurance,” the same quality of endurance tied to Christ for which Ephesus is praised.
    Revelation 3:10 Because thou didst keep the word of My endurance, I also will keep thee from the hour of trial that is about to come upon the whole inhabited world, to try those dwelling upon the earth.

Revelation 2:4
But I have this against thee: thou didst abandon thy first love.
  Jeremiah 2:2–2:5 — Old Testament background: The Lord remembers Israel’s early “love of your completion” and asks why their fathers “withdrew far from Me,” comparable to the charge “thou didst abandon thy first love.” Jeremiah addresses Israel, Revelation a Church.
    Jeremiah 2:2 And He said, Thus says the Lord: I remembered the mercy of your youth and the love of your completion, when you followed the Holy One of Israel, says the Lord.
    Jeremiah 2:3 Israel is holy. To the Lord he is the first of His produce. All who devour him will offend; calamities will come upon them, declares the Lord.
    Jeremiah 2:4 Hear the Lord’s word, house of Jacob and every clan of the house of Israel.
    Jeremiah 2:5 Thus says the Lord: What offense did your fathers find in Me, that they withdrew far from Me, followed futile things and became futile?
  Matthew 24:12–24:13 — shared theme: Jesus warns that “the love of many will grow cold,” a decline of love comparable to the abandoned first love. Matthew ties this to increasing lawlessness, which Revelation 2:4 does not mention.
    Matthew 24:12 Because lawlessness increases, the love of many will grow cold.
    Matthew 24:13 But the one who endures to the end will be saved.
  Revelation 2:20 — same formula: The letter to Thyatira uses the identical rebuke, “But I have this against thee.”
    Revelation 2:20 But I have this against thee: thou allowest the woman Jezebel, who calleth herself a prophetess, and she teacheth and deceiveth My slaves to commit sexual immorality and eat things sacrificed to idols.

Revelation 2:5
Therefore remember from whence thou hast fallen, and repent and do the first works. Otherwise I am coming to thee and will move thy lampstand from its place, unless thou repent.
  Revelation 2:16 — same formula: Both call for repentance with a conditional threat: “Therefore repent. Otherwise I am coming to thee.” At Pergamum the threat is war with the sword of His mouth; at Ephesus removal of the lampstand.
    Revelation 2:16 Therefore repent. Otherwise I am coming to thee quickly and will wage war against them with the sword of My mouth.
  Revelation 3:2–3:3 — parallel warning: Sardis is also told to “remember,” to “repent,” and that He will come upon them, matching the sequence remember, repent and coming in the warning to Ephesus.
    Revelation 3:2 Become watchful, and strengthen the things remaining that were about to die, for I have not found thy works fulfilled before My God.
    Revelation 3:3 Therefore remember how thou hast received and heard, and keep it and repent. Therefore if thou watch not, I will come like a thief, and thou shalt certainly not know at what hour I will come upon thee.
  Revelation 1:20 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 1:20 explains that “the seven lampstands are seven Churches,” giving the meaning of the threat to “move thy lampstand from its place.”
    Revelation 1:20 The mystery of the seven stars thou sawest upon My right hand, and the seven golden lampstands: the seven stars are angels of the seven Churches, and the seven lampstands are seven Churches.”

Revelation 2:6
But this thou hast: thou hatest the works of the Nicolaitans, which I also hate.
  Revelation 2:14–2:15 — internal cross-reference: The only other mention of the Nicolaitans is in the letter to Pergamum, which has “those who hold the teaching of the Nicolaitans.” Ephesus hates their works; Pergamum tolerates their teaching.
    Revelation 2:14 But I have a few things against thee: thou hast there those who hold the teaching of Balaam, who taught Balak to put a stumbling block before the sons of Israel, to eat things sacrificed to idols and to commit sexual immorality.
    Revelation 2:15 So thou also hast those who hold the teaching of the Nicolaitans in the same way.
  Psalms 138:21–138:22 — shared theme: The Psalmist hates “those who hate Thee,” sharing God’s hostility toward His enemies, as Ephesus hates works “which I also hate.” Revelation speaks of hating works, the Psalm of hating people.
    Psalms 138:21 Did I not hate those who hate Thee, Lord, and melt away over Thine enemies?
    Psalms 138:22 I hated them with complete hatred; they became enemies to me.

Revelation 2:7
Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. To the one who conquereth I will give to eat from the tree of life, which is in the Paradise of God.’
  Genesis 2:9 — Old Testament background: Genesis places “the tree of life in the midst of the garden,” the tree promised to the conqueror “in the Paradise of God.”
    Genesis 2:9 And God caused every tree that was beautiful to behold and good for food to spring up from the earth, with the tree of life in the midst of the garden, and the tree of the knowledge of good and evil.
  Revelation 22:14 — internal cross-reference: The book’s closing speaks of having “right to the tree of life,” fulfilling the promise to give the conqueror “to eat from the tree of life.” There the condition is washing robes rather than conquering.
    Revelation 22:14 Blessed are they who wash their robes, that they may have right to the tree of life and may enter through the gates into the city.
  Revelation 2:17 — same formula: The letter to Pergamum ends with the identical call to hear “what the Spirit saith to the Churches” and a promise beginning “To the one who conquereth I will give.”
    Revelation 2:17 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. To the one who conquereth I will give some of the hidden manna, and I will give him a white stone, and upon the stone a new name written, which no one knoweth except the one who receiveth it.’

Revelation 2:8
And to the angel of the Church in Smyrna write: These things saith the first and the last, who became dead and lived:
  Revelation 1:17–1:18 — internal cross-reference: The title of the letter to Smyrna repeats Christ’s words in the opening vision: “I am the first and the last” and “I became dead, and behold, I am living.”
    Revelation 1:17 And when I saw Him, I fell at His feet as dead. And He placed His right hand upon me, saying, “Fear not. I am the first and the last,
    Revelation 1:18 and the living One. I became dead, and behold, I am living for the ages of the ages, and I have the keys of death and Hades.
  Revelation 1:11 — internal cross-reference: Smyrna is the second of the seven Churches named in the commission to write to the Churches.
    Revelation 1:11 saying, “What thou seest, write in a scroll and send to the seven Churches: to Ephesus and to Smyrna and to Pergamum and to Thyatira and to Sardis and to Philadelphia and to Laodicea.”

Revelation 2:9
‘I know thine affliction and poverty—but thou art rich—and the slander from those who say that they themselves are Jews and are not, but are a synagogue of Satan.
  Revelation 3:9 — verbal parallel: Both speak of “the synagogue of Satan” and of “those who say that they themselves are Jews and are not.” Revelation 3:9 adds that they “lie” and will bow before the Church in Philadelphia.
    Revelation 3:9 Behold, I give some from the synagogue of Satan, those who say that they themselves are Jews and are not, but lie. Behold, I will make them come and bow before thy feet, and know that I loved thee.
  Revelation 3:17–3:18 — internal contrast: Smyrna is poor “but thou art rich,” whereas Laodicea says “I am rich” but is “poor.” The two letters reverse the same contrast of riches and poverty.
    Revelation 3:17 Because thou sayest, “I am rich and have become wealthy and need nothing,” and knowest not that thou art the wretched and pitiable and poor and blind and naked one,
    Revelation 3:18 I counsel thee to buy from Me gold refined by fire, that thou mayest become rich, and white garments, that thou mayest clothe thyself and the shame of thy nakedness may not be manifested, and eye salve to anoint thine eyes, that thou mayest see.
  James 2:5–2:6 — shared theme: James says God chose “those poor in the world to be rich in faith,” matching the paradox “poverty—but thou art rich.”
    James 2:5 Listen, my beloved brothers: has not God chosen those poor in the world to be rich in faith and heirs of the kingdom He promised to those who love Him?
    James 2:6 But you have dishonored the poor person. Is it not the rich who oppress you and drag you into courts?

Revelation 2:10
Fear none of the things thou art about to suffer. Behold, the devil is about to throw some of you into prison, that ye may be tested, and ye shall have affliction for ten days. Become faithful until death, and I will give thee the crown of life.
  James 1:12 — same promise: Both promise “the crown of life” to those who remain faithful under trial: James to the man who “endures a trial,” Revelation to those who are “tested” and become “faithful until death.”
    James 1:12 Blessed is the man who endures a trial, because, having been approved, he will receive the crown of life that He promised to those who love Him.

Revelation 2:11
Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. The one who conquereth shall certainly not be harmed by the second death.’
  Revelation 20:6 — internal cross-reference: Both state that the second death has no power over the faithful: “over these the second death hath no authority,” and “shall certainly not be harmed by the second death.”
    Revelation 20:6 Blessed and holy is the one who hath a share in the first resurrection; over these the second death hath no authority, but they will be priests of God and of Christ, and will reign with Him for a thousand years.
  Revelation 20:14 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 20:14 defines the second death as “the lake of fire,” explaining what the conqueror will not be harmed by.
    Revelation 20:14 And Death and Hades were thrown into the lake of fire. This is the second death, the lake of fire.
  Revelation 21:8 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 21:8 also identifies the burning lake as “the second death” and lists those whose share is in it, the contrast to the conqueror’s promise.
    Revelation 21:8 But as for the cowardly and unbelieving and abominable and murderers and sexually immoral and sorcerers and idolaters and all liars, their share is in the lake burning with fire and sulfur, which is the second death.”

Revelation 2:12
And to the angel of the Church in Pergamum write: These things saith the One who hath the sharp two-edged sword:
  Revelation 1:16 — internal cross-reference: The title repeats from the opening vision that “a sharp two-edged sword was coming out” of His mouth.
    Revelation 1:16 And He held seven stars in His right hand, and from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out, and His appearance was like the sun shining in its strength.
  Revelation 2:16 — internal cross-reference: Later in the same letter Christ threatens to “wage war against them with the sword of My mouth,” applying the title given in 2:12.
    Revelation 2:16 Therefore repent. Otherwise I am coming to thee quickly and will wage war against them with the sword of My mouth.
  Hebrews 4:12 — shared image: Hebrews compares the word of God to a “two-edged sword” that judges; Revelation presents Christ as having “the sharp two-edged sword.” Hebrews uses the sword as a comparison, not as a feature of a vision.
    Hebrews 4:12 For the word of God is living and active and sharper than any two-edged sword, penetrating as far as the division of soul and spirit, of joints and marrow, and able to judge the thoughts and intentions of the heart.

Revelation 2:13
‘I know where thou dwellest, where Satan’s throne is, and thou holdest fast My name and didst not deny My faith, even in the days of Antipas, My witness, My faithful one, who was killed among you, where Satan dwelleth.
  Revelation 3:8–3:9 — parallel commendation: Philadelphia is commended because it “didst not deny My name,” as Pergamum holds fast His name and “didst not deny My faith.”
    Revelation 3:8 ‘I know thy works. Behold, I have placed before thee an opened door, which no one can shut, because thou hast little strength and didst keep My word and didst not deny My name.
    Revelation 3:9 Behold, I give some from the synagogue of Satan, those who say that they themselves are Jews and are not, but lie. Behold, I will make them come and bow before thy feet, and know that I loved thee.
  Acts 22:20 — shared theme: Paul calls Stephen “Thy witness” at the time of his killing, as Antipas is “My witness, My faithful one, who was killed.”
    Acts 22:20 And when the blood of Stephen, Thy witness, was being poured out, I myself stood by, approving and guarding the garments of those who were killing him.’

Revelation 2:14
But I have a few things against thee: thou hast there those who hold the teaching of Balaam, who taught Balak to put a stumbling block before the sons of Israel, to eat things sacrificed to idols and to commit sexual immorality.
  Numbers 31:16 — Old Testament background: Numbers says that the women, “according to Balaam’s word,” caused the sons of Israel to turn away at Peor, the episode behind Balaam teaching Balak to put a stumbling block “before the sons of Israel.” Numbers does not name Balak in this verse.
    Numbers 31:16 For these women, according to Balaam’s word, caused the sons of Israel to turn away and disregard the Lord’s word because of Peor, and the plague came upon the Lord’s congregation.
  Revelation 2:20 — parallel charge: The charge against Thyatira names the same two sins, eating “things sacrificed to idols” and committing “sexual immorality,” attributed there to Jezebel rather than Balaam.
    Revelation 2:20 But I have this against thee: thou allowest the woman Jezebel, who calleth herself a prophetess, and she teacheth and deceiveth My slaves to commit sexual immorality and eat things sacrificed to idols.
  Acts 15:29 — shared prohibition: The Jerusalem letter requires abstaining from “things sacrificed to idols” and “sexual immorality,” the two practices condemned in the teaching of Balaam.
    Acts 15:29 to abstain from things sacrificed to idols, blood, things strangled, and sexual immorality. If you keep yourselves from these, you will do well. Farewell.”

Revelation 2:15
So thou also hast those who hold the teaching of the Nicolaitans in the same way.
  Revelation 2:6 — internal cross-reference: The only other mention of the Nicolaitans is in the letter to Ephesus, which hates “the works of the Nicolaitans.” Pergamum, by contrast, has those who hold their teaching.
    Revelation 2:6 But this thou hast: thou hatest the works of the Nicolaitans, which I also hate.

Revelation 2:16
Therefore repent. Otherwise I am coming to thee quickly and will wage war against them with the sword of My mouth.
  Revelation 1:16 — internal cross-reference: The “sword of My mouth” recalls the opening vision, in which “from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out.”
    Revelation 1:16 And He held seven stars in His right hand, and from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out, and His appearance was like the sun shining in its strength.
  Revelation 19:15 — internal cross-reference: In Revelation 19:15 “from His mouth cometh out a sharp sword, so that with it He may strike the nations,” the same image of judgment with the sword of His mouth threatened here. In 2:16 the threat concerns those in Pergamum; in 19:15 the sword strikes the nations.
    Revelation 19:15 And from His mouth cometh out a sharp sword, so that with it He may strike the nations; and He will shepherd them with an iron rod, and He treadeth the winepress of the wine of the fury of the wrath of God Almighty.
  Isaiah 11:4 — Old Testament background: The figure in Isaiah will “strike the earth with the word of his mouth,” and “with breath through his lips he will slay the ungodly,” the background for judgment by what comes from the mouth. Isaiah does not speak of a sword.
    Isaiah 11:4 but he will judge the cause of the lowly, and reprove the lowly of the earth; and he will strike the earth with the word of his mouth, and with breath through his lips he will slay the ungodly.

Revelation 2:17
Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. To the one who conquereth I will give some of the hidden manna, and I will give him a white stone, and upon the stone a new name written, which no one knoweth except the one who receiveth it.’
  Isaiah 62:2 — shared promise: Isaiah promises, “He will call you your new name, which the Lord will name,” comparable to the “new name written” given to the conqueror. Isaiah addresses a “you” whose righteousness the nations will see; Revelation an individual conqueror, with the name on a white stone.
    Isaiah 62:2 Nations will see your righteousness and kings your glory. He will call you your new name, which the Lord will name.
  Isaiah 65:15 — shared promise: Isaiah says, “Those serving Him will be called a new name,” paralleling the promise of a new name to the conqueror.
    Isaiah 65:15 For you will leave your name as a surfeit for My chosen ones, but the Lord will put you to death. Those serving Him will be called a new name,
  Revelation 3:12–3:13 — internal cross-reference: The promise to Philadelphia also includes a new name written on the conqueror: “I will write upon him … My new name.”
    Revelation 3:12 The one who conquereth, I will make him a pillar in the temple of My God, and he shall certainly go outside no more. And I will write upon him the name of My God and the name of the city of My God, the new Jerusalem, which cometh down out of heaven from My God, and My new name.
    Revelation 3:13 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.’

Revelation 2:18
And to the angel of the Church in Thyatira write: These things saith the Son of God, who hath His eyes like a flame of fire, and His feet like burnished bronze:
  Psalms 2:7 — Old Testament background: The Psalm’s decree, “You are My son,” lies behind the title “the Son of God” used in this letter. In the Psalm the Lord says this to the speaker; in Revelation the title introduces the One who speaks to Thyatira.
    Psalms 2:7 announcing the Lord’s decree: The Lord said to me, ‘You are My son; today I have begotten you.
  Revelation 1:11 — internal cross-reference: Thyatira is the fourth of the seven Churches named in the commission to write to the Churches.
    Revelation 1:11 saying, “What thou seest, write in a scroll and send to the seven Churches: to Ephesus and to Smyrna and to Pergamum and to Thyatira and to Sardis and to Philadelphia and to Laodicea.”

Revelation 2:19
‘I know thy works and love and faith and service and thine endurance, and thy last works are more than the first.
  Revelation 2:2–2:4 — internal contrast: Both letters begin “I know thy works” and commend “endurance,” but Ephesus has abandoned its “first love,” whereas Thyatira’s “last works are more than the first.”
    Revelation 2:2 ‘I know thy works, and the labour and thine endurance, and that thou canst not bear evil people; and thou didst test those who call themselves apostles and are not, and didst find them false.
    Revelation 2:3 And thou hast endurance and didst bear up for My name’s sake, and hast not grown weary.
    Revelation 2:4 But I have this against thee: thou didst abandon thy first love.
  2 Thessalonians 1:3 — shared theme: Paul gives thanks that the Thessalonians’ faith is growing and their love increasing, comparable to Thyatira’s love and faith with later works exceeding the first.
    2 Thessalonians 1:3 We ought always to give thanks to God for you, brothers, as is fitting, because your faith is growing abundantly and the love of each and every one of you for one another is increasing,

Revelation 2:20
But I have this against thee: thou allowest the woman Jezebel, who calleth herself a prophetess, and she teacheth and deceiveth My slaves to commit sexual immorality and eat things sacrificed to idols.
  1 Kings 16:31 — Old Testament background: In Kings “he took as wife Jezebel daughter of Ethbaal king of the Sidonians” and served Baal; her name is the one given to the false prophetess at Thyatira. Kings does not call Jezebel a prophetess.
    1 Kings 16:31 It was not enough for him to walk in the sins of Jeroboam son of Nebat: he took as wife Jezebel daughter of Ethbaal king of the Sidonians, and went and served Baal and bowed down to him.
  Revelation 2:14 — parallel charge: Both charges name the same two sins, “sexual immorality” and eating “things sacrificed to idols,” attributed in 2:14 to the teaching of Balaam.
    Revelation 2:14 But I have a few things against thee: thou hast there those who hold the teaching of Balaam, who taught Balak to put a stumbling block before the sons of Israel, to eat things sacrificed to idols and to commit sexual immorality.
  2 Kings 9:7 — Old Testament background: Kings speaks of “the blood of all the Lord’s slaves at Jezebel’s hand,” while Revelation says Jezebel “deceiveth My slaves.” In Kings she kills the Lord’s slaves; in Revelation she leads them astray.
    2 Kings 9:7 and you shall destroy the house of Ahab your master from before Me, and you shall avenge the blood of My slaves the prophets and the blood of all the Lord’s slaves at Jezebel’s hand

Revelation 2:21
And I gave her time to repent, and she doth not wish to repent of her sexual immorality.
  Revelation 9:20–9:21 — internal cross-reference: After the trumpet plagues the survivors “did not repent of … their sexual immorality,” the same refusal to repent of sexual immorality ascribed to Jezebel.
    Revelation 9:20 And the rest of the people, who were not killed by these plagues, did not even repent of the works of their hands, so as not to worship the demons and the idols of gold and silver and bronze and stone and wood, which can neither see nor hear nor walk.
    Revelation 9:21 And they did not repent of their murders or their sorcerous potions or their sexual immorality or their thefts.
  Wisdom of Solomon 12:10 — shared theme: Wisdom says that in judging gradually God “didst give room for repentance” to those who would not change, as Christ “gave her time to repent.”
    Wisdom of Solomon 12:10 but judging them little by little, Thou didst give room for repentance, not unaware that their origin was wicked, and their evil ingrained, and that their reasoning would never change.
  Romans 2:4–2:5 — shared theme: Paul speaks of God’s forbearance meant to lead to “repentance” and of an “unrepentant heart” that stores up wrath, comparable to time given and repentance refused.
    Romans 2:4 Or do you despise the wealth of His kindness and forbearance and patience, not knowing that God’s kindness leads you to repentance?
    Romans 2:5 But by your hardness and unrepentant heart, you store up wrath for yourself on the day of wrath and revelation of God’s righteous judgment,

Revelation 2:22
Behold, I am throwing her into a bed, and those committing adultery with her into great affliction, unless they repent of her works.
  Ezekiel 23:45–23:48 — Old Testament background: Ezekiel announces punishment “of an adulteress” and says “All women will be disciplined,” comparable to judgment on Jezebel and on those committing adultery with her. Ezekiel’s penalties are stoning and the sword rather than a bed and affliction.
    Ezekiel 23:45 Righteous men will punish them with the punishment of an adulteress and the punishment of bloodshed, for they are adulteresses, and blood is on their hands.
    Ezekiel 23:46 Thus says the Lord, the Lord: Bring a multitude against them and set turmoil and plunder among them,
    Ezekiel 23:47 and stone them with the multitude’s stones and pierce them with their swords. They will kill their sons and daughters and burn their houses.
    Ezekiel 23:48 I will turn impiety away from the land. All women will be disciplined and certainly will not act according to their impieties.
  Ezekiel 16:37–16:41 — Old Testament background: God gathers against the unfaithful one “all your lovers” and will exact “the retribution of an adulteress,” comparable to the judgment on Jezebel and those committing adultery with her. In Ezekiel the lovers are gathered as agents of the punishment, whereas in Revelation those committing adultery with her share in the affliction.
    Ezekiel 16:37 Therefore behold, I am gathering against you all your lovers with whom you mingled, all whom you loved together with all whom you hated. I will gather them against you from all around, reveal your evils to them, and they will see all your shame.
    Ezekiel 16:38 I will exact retribution upon you with the retribution of an adulteress and a woman shedding blood, and place you in blood of wrath and jealousy.
    Ezekiel 16:39 I will hand you into their hands. They will tear down your brothel, pull down your platform, strip your clothing from you, take your vessels of boasting, and leave you naked and exposed.
    Ezekiel 16:40 They will bring crowds against you, stone you with stones, and slaughter you with their swords.
    Ezekiel 16:41 They will burn your houses with fire and exact retributions upon you before many women. I will turn you away from your prostitution, and you certainly will give no more payments.
  Revelation 18:9 — shared image: The kings of the earth who “committed sexual immorality and lived in luxury with her” will “weep and mourn over her” when they see her burning; as in 2:22, those who shared a woman’s immorality are caught up in grief at her judgment. Revelation 18:9 does not name the woman and speaks of mourning rather than of affliction imposed on them.
    Revelation 18:9 And the kings of the earth who committed sexual immorality and lived in luxury with her will weep and mourn over her when they see the smoke of her burning,

Revelation 2:23
And I will kill her children with death, and all the Churches will know that I am the One who searcheth kidneys and hearts, and I will give to each of you according to your works.
  Jeremiah 17:10 — verbal allusion: The Lord says, “I, the Lord, examine hearts and test kidneys, to give each according to his ways,” combining both elements of Revelation 2:23: searching “kidneys and hearts” and giving “to each of you according to your works.”
    Jeremiah 17:10 I, the Lord, examine hearts and test kidneys, to give each according to his ways and the fruits of his practices.
  Psalms 7:10 — verbal allusion: The Psalm names God as the One “who tests hearts and kidneys,” the title Christ claims as “the One who searcheth kidneys and hearts.”
    Psalms 7:10 Let sinners’ wickedness come to an end, and Thou wilt direct the righteous one, God who tests hearts and kidneys.
  Matthew 16:27 — shared theme: The Son of Man “will repay each person according to his conduct,” matching “I will give to each of you according to your works.”
    Matthew 16:27 For the Son of Man is to come in the glory of His Father with His angels, and then He will repay each person according to his conduct.

Revelation 2:24
But to you, the rest in Thyatira, as many as do not have this teaching, who have not known the deep things of Satan, as they say, I say: I place no other burden upon you.
  Acts 15:28 — verbal echo: The Jerusalem letter says “to place no further burden upon you,” close to “I place no other burden upon you.” Acts attributes the decision to “the Holy Spirit and to us,” while in Revelation the speaker says, “I say.”
    Acts 15:28 For it seemed good to the Holy Spirit and to us to place no further burden upon you except these necessary things:
  1 Corinthians 2:10 — contrasting phrase: Paul speaks of “the depths of God” revealed through the Spirit, a counterpart to “the deep things of Satan” claimed by the false teachers.
    1 Corinthians 2:10 But God revealed them to us through the Spirit, for the Spirit searches everything, even the depths of God.

Revelation 2:25
Only hold fast what ye have until I come.
  Revelation 3:11 — same exhortation: Both pair the command to “hold fast what thou hast” (Thyatira: “what ye have”) with Christ’s coming: “I am coming quickly” and “until I come.”
    Revelation 3:11 I am coming quickly. Hold fast what thou hast, that no one may take thy crown.

Revelation 2:26
And to the one who conquereth and keepeth My works until the end, I will give authority over the nations,
  Psalms 2:8 — verbal allusion: The Psalm’s promise, “I will give you nations as your inheritance,” lies behind “I will give authority over the nations.” In the Psalm the promise is made to the one addressed as “you”; Revelation extends it to the conqueror.
    Psalms 2:8 Ask of Me, and I will give you nations as your inheritance, and the ends of the earth as your possession.
  Daniel 7:27 — shared theme: Daniel says “authority” and rule over kingdoms are given “to a holy people of the Most High,” comparable to authority over the nations given to the faithful conqueror.
    Daniel 7:27 He gave the kingdom, authority, their greatness, and rule over all the kingdoms under heaven to a holy people of the Most High, to reign with everlasting kingship; all authorities will be subject to him and obey him.
  Revelation 3:21 — internal cross-reference: The promise to Laodicea likewise gives the conqueror a share in Christ’s rule, “to sit with Me on My throne.”
    Revelation 3:21 The one who conquereth, I will give him to sit with Me on My throne, as I also conquered and sat with My Father on His throne.

Revelation 2:27
and he will shepherd them with an iron rod, as vessels of pottery are shattered,
  Psalms 2:8–2:9 — quotation: Revelation quotes Psalm 2:9: “You will shepherd them with an iron rod; you will crush them like a potter’s vessel.” Revelation applies to the conqueror the promise made in the Psalm to the one who is given “nations as your inheritance.”
    Psalms 2:8 Ask of Me, and I will give you nations as your inheritance, and the ends of the earth as your possession.
    Psalms 2:9 You will shepherd them with an iron rod; you will crush them like a potter’s vessel.’”
  Revelation 12:5 — internal cross-reference: The male child born to the woman is “to shepherd all the nations with an iron rod,” the same phrase used in 2:27 of the conqueror’s rule.
    Revelation 12:5 And she bore a son, a male, who is to shepherd all the nations with an iron rod; and her child was caught up to God and to His throne.
  Revelation 19:15 — internal cross-reference: In Revelation 19:15 the One from whose mouth comes a sharp sword “will shepherd them with an iron rod,” the same phrase used here of the conqueror’s rule.
    Revelation 19:15 And from His mouth cometh out a sharp sword, so that with it He may strike the nations; and He will shepherd them with an iron rod, and He treadeth the winepress of the wine of the fury of the wrath of God Almighty.

Revelation 2:28
as I also have received from My Father; and I will give him the morning star.
  Revelation 22:16 — internal cross-reference: Jesus calls Himself “the bright morning star,” which identifies the gift of “the morning star” promised to the conqueror.
    Revelation 22:16 “I, Jesus, sent My angel to testify these things to you for the Churches. I am the Root and the offspring of David, the bright morning star.”
  2 Peter 1:19 — shared image: Peter speaks of “the morning star” rising “in your hearts,” the same image of the morning star given to believers.
    2 Peter 1:19 And we have the prophetic word more firmly established, to which you do well to pay attention as to a lamp shining in a dark place, until day dawns and the morning star rises in your hearts,

Revelation 2:29
Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.’
  Revelation 2:7 — same formula: Both use the identical refrain, “Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.”
    Revelation 2:7 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. To the one who conquereth I will give to eat from the tree of life, which is in the Paradise of God.’

Revelation 3:1
And to the angel of the Church in Sardis write: These things saith the One who hath the seven spirits of God and the seven stars: ‘I know thy works, that thou hast a name that thou livest, and thou art dead.
  1 Timothy 5:6 — shared theme: Paul says that the self-indulgent woman “is dead while living,” paralleling the Church that has “a name that thou livest, and thou art dead.”
    1 Timothy 5:6 But the woman who lives in indulgence is dead while living.
  Revelation 1:4 — internal cross-reference: The greeting of the book mentions “the seven spirits before His throne,” which Christ is said to have in the title of the letter to Sardis.
    Revelation 1:4 John, to the seven Churches in Asia: grace to you and peace from the One who is and who was and who is coming, and from the seven spirits before His throne,
  Revelation 1:16 — internal cross-reference: In the opening vision “He held seven stars in His right hand,” the “seven stars” named in Christ’s title for Sardis.
    Revelation 1:16 And He held seven stars in His right hand, and from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out, and His appearance was like the sun shining in its strength.

Revelation 3:2
Become watchful, and strengthen the things remaining that were about to die, for I have not found thy works fulfilled before My God.
  Revelation 16:15 — shared warning: Both call for watchfulness: Sardis is told, “Become watchful,” and Revelation 16:15 says, “Blessed is the one who watcheth.” Revelation 16:15 grounds this in “I am coming like a thief” and adds keeping one’s garments, which the Sardis verse does not mention.
    Revelation 16:15 “Behold, I am coming like a thief. Blessed is the one who watcheth and keepeth his garments, so that he may not walk naked and they see his shame.”
  Mark 13:33–13:37 — shared warning: Jesus’ repeated command to “keep watch,” lest the master come and find them sleeping, parallels “Become watchful” addressed to a Church whose works were not found complete.
    Mark 13:33 Watch out; stay awake, for you do not know when the time is.
    Mark 13:34 It is like a man going abroad who left his house and gave his slaves authority, each his own work, and commanded the doorkeeper to keep watch.
    Mark 13:35 Therefore keep watch, for you do not know when the master of the house is coming: in the evening, at midnight, at cockcrow, or in the morning.
    Mark 13:36 Otherwise he may come suddenly and find you sleeping.
    Mark 13:37 What I say to you, I say to all: keep watch!”

Revelation 3:3
Therefore remember how thou hast received and heard, and keep it and repent. Therefore if thou watch not, I will come like a thief, and thou shalt certainly not know at what hour I will come upon thee.
  Revelation 16:15 — internal cross-reference: Both sayings have the Lord announcing that he will come “like a thief” and link this with watching. Revelation 16:15 adds a blessing on the one who keeps his garments, whereas the Sardis warning is conditional: “if thou watch not.”
    Revelation 16:15 “Behold, I am coming like a thief. Blessed is the one who watcheth and keepeth his garments, so that he may not walk naked and they see his shame.”
  Matthew 25:13 — verbal allusion: Both pair a call to watch with ignorance of the time of the coming: Sardis “shalt certainly not know at what hour,” and Matthew’s hearers “know neither the day nor the hour.”
    Matthew 25:13 Therefore keep watch, because you know neither the day nor the hour.
  1 Thessalonians 5:2 — shared image: Both use the image of coming “like a thief.” Paul applies it to “the day of the Lord,” while Revelation 3:3 has the speaker say “I will come like a thief” as a conditional warning.
    1 Thessalonians 5:2 For you yourselves know exactly that the day of the Lord comes in this way, like a thief in the night.

Revelation 3:4
But thou hast a few names in Sardis who did not defile their garments, and they shall walk with Me in white, because they are worthy.
  Revelation 3:5 — internal cross-reference: The next verse continues the promise: those who “walk with Me in white” correspond to the conqueror who will be “clothed in white garments.”
    Revelation 3:5 The one who conquereth will thus be clothed in white garments, and I will certainly not erase his name from the book of life, and I will confess his name before My Father and before His angels.
  Revelation 3:18 — shared image: Both connect white clothing with standing before the Lord: the few in Sardis “did not defile their garments” and “shall walk with Me in white,” and Laodicea is counselled to buy from Him “white garments” so that “the shame of thy nakedness may not be manifested.”
    Revelation 3:18 I counsel thee to buy from Me gold refined by fire, that thou mayest become rich, and white garments, that thou mayest clothe thyself and the shame of thy nakedness may not be manifested, and eye salve to anoint thine eyes, that thou mayest see.
  Jude 1:23 — shared image: Both use soiled clothing as an image of moral defilement: Sardis’ faithful “did not defile their garments,” and Jude speaks of “the tunic stained by the flesh.”
    Jude 1:23 save others, snatching them out of the fire; and have mercy on others with fear, hating even the tunic stained by the flesh.

Revelation 3:5
The one who conquereth will thus be clothed in white garments, and I will certainly not erase his name from the book of life, and I will confess his name before My Father and before His angels.
  Psalms 68:29 — verbal allusion: Both speak of names being erased from a book of life: the Psalm asks that the wicked “be erased from the book of the living,” while Revelation promises the conqueror, “I will certainly not erase his name from the book of life.”
    Psalms 68:29 Let them be erased from the book of the living, and let them not be written with the righteous.
  Matthew 10:32 — parallel saying: Both promise that Jesus will acknowledge or confess the faithful person “before My Father.” Matthew conditions this on acknowledging Jesus before people; Revelation on conquering.
    Matthew 10:32 Therefore everyone who acknowledges Me before people, I too will acknowledge before My Father in the heavens.
  Luke 12:8 — parallel saying: Both promise acknowledgement before the angels: Luke says the Son of Man will acknowledge “before the angels of God,” and Revelation “before His angels.”
    Luke 12:8 And I tell you, everyone who acknowledges Me before people, the Son of Man will also acknowledge before the angels of God.

Revelation 3:6
Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.’
  Revelation 2:7 — repeated refrain: Both verses contain the identical refrain “Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.” In Revelation 2:7 it is followed by a promise to the conqueror.
    Revelation 2:7 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. To the one who conquereth I will give to eat from the tree of life, which is in the Paradise of God.’

Revelation 3:7
And to the angel of the Church in Philadelphia write: These things saith the Holy One, the True One, the One who hath the key of David, who openeth and no one will shut, and shutteth and no one openeth:
  Revelation 6:10 — same divine titles: The speaker to Philadelphia is “the Holy One, the True One,” and the martyrs address God as “Master, holy and true.” Revelation 6:10 uses the pair as an address in prayer rather than as a self-description.
    Revelation 6:10 And they cried with a loud voice, saying, “How long, O Master, holy and true, before Thou dost judge and avenge our blood upon those dwelling on the earth?”
  Revelation 1:18 — same figure holding keys: Both describe Christ as holding a key or keys: “the key of David” here, and “the keys of death and Hades” in the inaugural vision.
    Revelation 1:18 and the living One. I became dead, and behold, I am living for the ages of the ages, and I have the keys of death and Hades.
  Isaiah 22:22 — Old Testament background: Both describe authority connected with David that cannot be opposed: Isaiah’s figure receives “David’s glory” and rules “with no one contradicting,” and Christ opens and “no one will shut.” The Saints (Greek) wording of Isaiah has “David’s glory” rather than a key and does not mention opening and shutting.
    Isaiah 22:22 I will give him David’s glory, and he will rule, with no one contradicting.

Revelation 3:8
‘I know thy works. Behold, I have placed before thee an opened door, which no one can shut, because thou hast little strength and didst keep My word and didst not deny My name.
  Revelation 3:7 — internal cross-reference: The “opened door, which no one can shut” applies the title of the previous verse, the One who “openeth and no one will shut.”
    Revelation 3:7 And to the angel of the Church in Philadelphia write: These things saith the Holy One, the True One, the One who hath the key of David, who openeth and no one will shut, and shutteth and no one openeth:
  Revelation 2:13 — parallel commendation: Both churches are commended for not denying under pressure: Philadelphia “didst not deny My name,” and Pergamum “holdest fast My name and didst not deny My faith.”
    Revelation 2:13 ‘I know where thou dwellest, where Satan’s throne is, and thou holdest fast My name and didst not deny My faith, even in the days of Antipas, My witness, My faithful one, who was killed among you, where Satan dwelleth.
  John 17:6 — verbal echo: Both commend those who have kept the word: Philadelphia “didst keep My word,” and Jesus says of the people the Father gave Him, “they have kept Thy word,” in the context of revealing “Thy name.”
    John 17:6 “I revealed Thy name to the people whom Thou gavest Me out of the world. They were Thine, and Thou gavest them to Me, and they have kept Thy word.

Revelation 3:9
Behold, I give some from the synagogue of Satan, those who say that they themselves are Jews and are not, but lie. Behold, I will make them come and bow before thy feet, and know that I loved thee.
  Revelation 2:9 — internal cross-reference: Both letters describe the same group in nearly the same words: “those who say that they themselves are Jews and are not” and “a synagogue of Satan.”
    Revelation 2:9 ‘I know thine affliction and poverty—but thou art rich—and the slander from those who say that they themselves are Jews and are not, but are a synagogue of Satan.
  Isaiah 49:23 — verbal allusion: Both describe outsiders who will “bow” at the feet of those addressed, followed by recognition: Isaiah’s “You will know that I am the Lord” and Revelation’s “know that I loved thee.” In Isaiah it is kings and ruling women who bow and “lick the dust of your feet.”
    Isaiah 49:23 and kings will be your foster fathers and ruling women your nurses. They will bow to you upon the earth’s surface and lick the dust of your feet. You will know that I am the Lord, and you will not be ashamed.
  Isaiah 43:4 — verbal allusion: Both have God declaring his love for his people with “I loved”: Isaiah “I loved you,” and Revelation, that opponents will “know that I loved thee.”
    Isaiah 43:4 Since you became honored before Me, you were glorified, and I loved you. I will give many people on your behalf and rulers for your head.

Revelation 3:10
Because thou didst keep the word of My endurance, I also will keep thee from the hour of trial that is about to come upon the whole inhabited world, to try those dwelling upon the earth.
  2 Peter 2:9 — parallel promise: Both speak of the Lord keeping or rescuing the faithful from trial: Peter says the Lord knows how “to rescue the godly from trial,” and Revelation promises, “I also will keep thee from the hour of trial.”
    2 Peter 2:9 then the Lord knows how to rescue the godly from trial, and to keep the unrighteous under punishment for the day of judgment,
  Revelation 14:12 — verbal echo: Both join endurance with keeping: Philadelphia “didst keep the word of My endurance,” and Revelation 14:12 names “the endurance of the saints, those keeping the commandments of God.”
    Revelation 14:12 Here is the endurance of the saints, those keeping the commandments of God and the faith of Jesus.
  Revelation 2:10 — parallel promise: Both letters speak of a coming testing that is “about to” happen, Smyrna being told that the devil is about to imprison some “that ye may be tested.” Smyrna is told to endure the testing, whereas Philadelphia is promised to be kept from the hour of trial.
    Revelation 2:10 Fear none of the things thou art about to suffer. Behold, the devil is about to throw some of you into prison, that ye may be tested, and ye shall have affliction for ten days. Become faithful until death, and I will give thee the crown of life.

Revelation 3:11
I am coming quickly. Hold fast what thou hast, that no one may take thy crown.
  Revelation 2:25 — parallel exhortation: Both exhortations are nearly identical in substance: “Hold fast what thou hast” here, and to Thyatira, “Only hold fast what ye have until I come.”
    Revelation 2:25 Only hold fast what ye have until I come.
  Revelation 22:12 — repeated promise: Both contain the words “I am coming quickly.” Revelation 22:12 adds that the reward is with him, while here the concern is that no one take the believer’s crown.
    Revelation 22:12 “Behold, I am coming quickly, and My reward is with Me, to repay each according to what his work is.
  Revelation 2:10 — shared image: Both speak of a crown held out to the faithful: Smyrna is promised “the crown of life” for faithfulness until death, and Philadelphia is warned that no one may take “thy crown.”
    Revelation 2:10 Fear none of the things thou art about to suffer. Behold, the devil is about to throw some of you into prison, that ye may be tested, and ye shall have affliction for ten days. Become faithful until death, and I will give thee the crown of life.

Revelation 3:12
The one who conquereth, I will make him a pillar in the temple of My God, and he shall certainly go outside no more. And I will write upon him the name of My God and the name of the city of My God, the new Jerusalem, which cometh down out of heaven from My God, and My new name.
  Revelation 21:2 — fulfilment of promise: Both describe “new Jerusalem” coming down out of heaven from God; Revelation 21:2 shows in vision the city whose name is promised here.
    Revelation 21:2 And I saw the holy city, new Jerusalem, coming down out of heaven from God, prepared as a bride adorned for her husband.
  Revelation 14:1 — shared image: Both describe the faithful bearing a divine name written on them: here “the name of My God,” and in Revelation 14:1 the Lamb’s name and “His Father’s name written upon their foreheads.”
    Revelation 14:1 And I saw, and behold, the Lamb standing upon Mount Zion, and with Him a hundred forty-four thousand having His name and His Father’s name written upon their foreheads.
  Revelation 2:17 — parallel promise: Both promises to the one who conquers involve “a new name” written. In Revelation 2:17 the new name is written on a white stone and known only to the receiver, whereas here it is called “My new name.”
    Revelation 2:17 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. To the one who conquereth I will give some of the hidden manna, and I will give him a white stone, and upon the stone a new name written, which no one knoweth except the one who receiveth it.’

Revelation 3:13
Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.’
  Revelation 2:7 — repeated refrain: Both verses contain the identical refrain “Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.” In Revelation 2:7 it is followed by a promise to the conqueror.
    Revelation 2:7 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. To the one who conquereth I will give to eat from the tree of life, which is in the Paradise of God.’

Revelation 3:14
And to the angel of the Church in Laodicea write: These things saith the Amen, the faithful and true witness, the beginning of God’s creation:
  Revelation 1:5 — same title of Christ: Both call Jesus Christ “the faithful witness”; Laodicea’s letter expands the title to “the faithful and true witness.”
    Revelation 1:5 and from Jesus Christ, the faithful witness, the firstborn of the dead and the ruler of the kings of the earth. To the One who loveth us and freed us from our sins in His blood—
  Revelation 19:11 — same title of Christ: Both apply the words “faithful” and “true” to Christ: here as “the faithful and true witness,” and in Revelation 19:11 the rider on the white horse “is called Faithful and True.”
