    Revelation 19:11 And I saw heaven opened, and behold, a white horse, and the One sitting upon it is called Faithful and True, and in righteousness He judgeth and wageth war.
  2 Corinthians 1:20 — shared title: Both connect the word “Amen” with Christ: he is called “the Amen” here, and Paul says that through him “is the ‘Amen’ to God.”
    2 Corinthians 1:20 For however many God’s promises are, in Him is the “Yes.” Therefore also through Him is the “Amen” to God, for His glory through us.

Revelation 3:15
‘I know thy works, that thou art neither cold nor hot. Would that thou wert cold or hot.
  Revelation 3:1 — internal formula parallel: Both letters open their verdict with “I know thy works, that thou…” and then expose a hidden condition: Sardis “thou art dead,” Laodicea “neither cold nor hot.”
    Revelation 3:1 And to the angel of the Church in Sardis write: These things saith the One who hath the seven spirits of God and the seven stars: ‘I know thy works, that thou hast a name that thou livest, and thou art dead.

Revelation 3:16
So because thou art lukewarm, and neither hot nor cold, I am about to vomit thee out of My mouth.
  Revelation 3:15 — internal cross-reference: 3:15 states the charge, “thou art neither cold nor hot,” and 3:16 draws the consequence for the one who is “lukewarm, and neither hot nor cold”: being vomited out of the Lord’s mouth.
    Revelation 3:15 ‘I know thy works, that thou art neither cold nor hot. Would that thou wert cold or hot.

Revelation 3:17
Because thou sayest, “I am rich and have become wealthy and need nothing,” and knowest not that thou art the wretched and pitiable and poor and blind and naked one,
  Hosea 12:9 — verbal allusion: Both quote a self-satisfied claim to wealth: Ephraim says, “Indeed I have become rich,” and Laodicea says, “I am rich and have become wealthy.” Hosea adds that his labours will not be found because of his injustices.
    Hosea 12:9 Ephraim said, ‘Indeed I have become rich; I have found refreshment for myself.’ All his labors will not be found for him because of the injustices in which he sinned.
  1 Corinthians 4:8–4:10 — verbal echo: Both expose complacent self-assessment of wealth: Paul ironically tells the Corinthians, “Already you have become rich!” and Laodicea says, “I am rich and have become wealthy and need nothing.”
    1 Corinthians 4:8 Already you have your fill! Already you have become rich! Without us you have become kings! And I wish indeed that you had become kings, so that we too might reign with you.
    1 Corinthians 4:9 For I think God has displayed us apostles last of all, as people condemned to death, because we have become a spectacle to the world, both to angels and to human beings.
    1 Corinthians 4:10 We are foolish for Christ’s sake, but you are wise in Christ. We are weak, but you are strong. You are honored, but we are dishonored.
  John 9:40–9:41 — shared image: Both expose people who think they see while being blind: the Pharisees say “We see,” and Laodicea “knowest not that thou art… blind.”
    John 9:40 Some of the Pharisees who were with Him heard these things and said to Him, “Are we also blind?”
    John 9:41 Jesus said to them, “If you were blind, you would not have sin. But now you say, ‘We see’; your sin remains.”

Revelation 3:18
I counsel thee to buy from Me gold refined by fire, that thou mayest become rich, and white garments, that thou mayest clothe thyself and the shame of thy nakedness may not be manifested, and eye salve to anoint thine eyes, that thou mayest see.
  Revelation 3:17 — internal cross-reference: The counsel answers the preceding diagnosis point by point: gold for the one who is “poor,” white garments for the “naked,” and eye salve for the “blind.”
    Revelation 3:17 Because thou sayest, “I am rich and have become wealthy and need nothing,” and knowest not that thou art the wretched and pitiable and poor and blind and naked one,
  Revelation 16:15 — shared image: Both link garments with avoiding the shame of nakedness: Laodicea is to clothe itself so that “the shame of thy nakedness may not be manifested,” and Revelation 16:15 blesses the one who keeps his garments “so that he may not walk naked and they see his shame.”
    Revelation 16:15 “Behold, I am coming like a thief. Blessed is the one who watcheth and keepeth his garments, so that he may not walk naked and they see his shame.”
  1 Peter 1:7 — shared image: Both use gold tested or “refined by fire” as an image of value. Peter compares tested faith to such gold, whereas Revelation offers the gold itself as a purchase from the Lord.
    1 Peter 1:7 so that the tested genuineness of your faith—more precious than gold that perishes, though tested by fire—may be found to result in praise and glory and honor at the revelation of Jesus Christ.

Revelation 3:19
As many as I love, I reprove and discipline. Therefore be zealous and repent.
  Proverbs 3:11–3:12 — verbal allusion: Both state that discipline comes from love: “As many as I love, I reprove and discipline,” and “the Lord disciplines the one He loves.” Proverbs also speaks of being “corrected by Him.”
    Proverbs 3:11 Son, do not make light of the Lord’s discipline or lose heart when corrected by Him,
    Proverbs 3:12 for the Lord disciplines the one He loves and scourges every son He receives.
  Job 5:17 — verbal echo: Both present the Lord’s reproof as something to accept: Job calls happy “the person whom the Lord reproved,” and Christ reproves those he loves.
    Job 5:17 Happy is the person whom the Lord reproved; do not refuse the Almighty’s admonition.
  Deuteronomy 8:5 — shared image: Both describe the Lord’s discipline as an expression of a close relationship: Deuteronomy compares it to how “a man chastens his son,” while Revelation grounds it in love.
    Deuteronomy 8:5 And you shall know in your heart that, as a man chastens his son, so shall the Lord your God chasten you.

Revelation 3:20
Behold, I stand at the door and knock. If anyone hear My voice and open the door, I will come in unto him and dine with him, and he with Me.
  Luke 12:36–12:37 — parallel image: Both picture someone who “knocks” and those inside who should “open” to him, followed by a shared meal: Luke’s master has his slaves “recline at table,” and Christ will “dine with him.”
    Luke 12:36 and be like people waiting for their own master to return from the wedding feast, so that when he comes and knocks, they may open to him at once.
    Luke 12:37 Blessed are those slaves whom the master finds keeping watch when he comes. Amen, I tell you: he will gird himself, have them recline at table, and come and serve them.
  Song of Songs 5:2–5:4 — shared image: Both describe a beloved voice and knocking at a door with an appeal to open: “he knocks upon the door! Open to me.” In the Song the speaker hesitates to open; Revelation leaves the response to “anyone.”
    Song of Songs 5:2 I sleep, but my heart keeps watch. The voice of my kinsman—he knocks upon the door! Open to me, my sister, my companion, my dove, my perfect one, for my head is filled with dew and my curls with the drops of night.
    Song of Songs 5:3 I took off my tunic; how could I put it on? I washed my feet; how could I soil them?
    Song of Songs 5:4 My kinsman extended his hand from the opening, and my belly was stirred for him.
  John 14:21–14:23 — parallel promise: Both promise that the Lord will come to the one who responds and be with him: “I will come in unto him and dine with him” and “We will come to him and make Our dwelling with him.”
    John 14:21 The one who has My commandments and keeps them is the one who loves Me. And the one who loves Me will be loved by My Father, and I will love him and reveal Myself to him.”
    John 14:22 Judas—not the Iscariot—says to Him, “Lord, what then has happened that Thou art going to reveal Thyself to us and not to the world?”
    John 14:23 Jesus answered and said to him, “If anyone loves Me, he will keep My word, and My Father will love him, and We will come to him and make Our dwelling with him.

Revelation 3:21
The one who conquereth, I will give him to sit with Me on My throne, as I also conquered and sat with My Father on His throne.
  Matthew 19:28 — parallel promise: Both promise that the faithful will sit on thrones alongside the Son: Matthew’s followers will “sit upon twelve thrones” when the Son of Man sits on “the throne of His glory.” Matthew specifies judging the twelve tribes; Revelation speaks of sharing Christ’s throne.
    Matthew 19:28 Jesus said to them, “Truly I tell you that you who have followed Me, in the regeneration, when the Son of Man sits upon the throne of His glory, shall also sit upon twelve thrones, judging the twelve tribes of Israel.
  Revelation 20:4 — fulfilment of promise: Revelation 20:4 shows faithful witnesses seated on “thrones” who “reigned with Christ,” corresponding to the promise that the conqueror will sit with Christ on his throne.
    Revelation 20:4 And I saw thrones, and they sat upon them, and judgment was given to them; and the souls of those beheaded because of the testimony of Jesus and because of the word of God, and those who did not worship the beast or its image and did not receive the mark upon their forehead and upon their hand. And they lived and reigned with Christ for a thousand years.
  John 16:33 — shared theme of Christ’s victory: Both ground the believer’s hope in Christ’s own victory: “as I also conquered” and “I have overcome the world.” The English wording differs (“conquered” / “overcome”).
    John 16:33 These things I have spoken to you so that in Me you may have peace. In the world you have affliction; but take courage: I have overcome the world.”

Revelation 3:22
Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.’”
  Revelation 2:7 — repeated refrain: Both verses contain the identical refrain “Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches,” which recurs in each of the seven letters. In Revelation 2:7 it is followed by the promise to the one who conquers.
    Revelation 2:7 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. To the one who conquereth I will give to eat from the tree of life, which is in the Paradise of God.’
  Revelation 3:6 — repeated refrain: Both verses contain the identical refrain “Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches,” here closing the letters to Sardis and Laodicea.
    Revelation 3:6 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.’
  Revelation 3:13 — repeated refrain: Both verses contain the identical refrain “Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches,” here closing the letters to Philadelphia and Laodicea.
    Revelation 3:13 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.’

Revelation 4:1
After these things I saw, and behold, a door opened in heaven, and the first voice I heard like a trumpet speaking with me said, “Come up hither, and I will show thee what must take place after these things.”
  Revelation 1:10 — internal cross-reference: “The first voice I heard like a trumpet” refers back to the “loud voice like a trumpet” John heard in Revelation 1:10.
    Revelation 1:10 I came to be in the Spirit on the Lord’s day, and heard behind me a loud voice like a trumpet,
  Revelation 1:19 — internal cross-reference: Both speak of things to take place “after these”: John was commanded to write them, and he is now invited up to be shown them.
    Revelation 1:19 Therefore write the things thou sawest, and the things that are, and the things that are to take place after these.
  Revelation 11:12 — verbal echo: Both contain a heavenly summons to ascend, “Come up hither” and “Come up here.” In Revelation 11:12 the two witnesses actually go up into heaven in a cloud.
    Revelation 11:12 And they heard a loud voice from heaven saying to them, “Come up here.” And they went up into heaven in the cloud, and their enemies watched them.

Revelation 4:2
immediately I came to be in the Spirit; and behold, a throne was set in heaven, and One sitting upon the throne.
  Isaiah 6:1 — parallel vision: Both prophets see the Lord “seated on a throne.” Isaiah locates the vision in the year of King Oziah’s death and describes the house full of his glory.
    Isaiah 6:1 In the year that King Oziah died, I saw the Lord seated on a throne, high and lifted up; and the house was full of His glory.
  1 Kings 22:19 — parallel vision: Both describe a vision of the Lord “sitting on His throne.” Micaiah also sees “all the army of heaven” standing around Him on His right and His left; Revelation 4:2 shows only the throne and the One sitting upon it.
    1 Kings 22:19 Micaiah said, “Not so—not I! Hear the Lord’s word—not so! I saw the Lord, the God of Israel, sitting on His throne, and all the army of heaven stood around Him, on His right and His left.
  Revelation 1:10 — repeated phrase: Both open a vision with “I came to be in the Spirit.” In Revelation 1:10 this leads to hearing a trumpet-like voice; here to seeing the heavenly throne.
    Revelation 1:10 I came to be in the Spirit on the Lord’s day, and heard behind me a loud voice like a trumpet,

Revelation 4:3
And the One sitting was in appearance like a jasper stone and a sardius, and a rainbow around the throne was in appearance like an emerald.
  Ezekiel 1:28 — shared image: Both surround the divine presence with a bow-like radiance: Ezekiel sees “the appearance of a bow when it is in the cloud” in the brightness all around “the likeness of the Lord’s glory,” and John sees “a rainbow around the throne.” Ezekiel does not compare it to an emerald.
    Ezekiel 1:28 Like the appearance of a bow when it is in the cloud on a rainy day, so was the arrangement of the brightness all around. This was the appearance of the likeness of the Lord’s glory. I saw it, and I fall upon my face, and I heard the voice of One speaking.
  Revelation 21:11 — shared image: Both describe divine radiance as like “a jasper stone”: the One on the throne here, and the city “having the glory of God” in Revelation 21:11.
    Revelation 21:11 having the glory of God. Her radiance was like a most precious stone, like a jasper stone shining as crystal,
  Revelation 21:19–21:20 — shared precious stones: The jasper, sardius and emerald named here all appear among the foundation stones of the new Jerusalem, where they adorn the city rather than describe God’s appearance.
    Revelation 21:19 The foundations of the wall of the city were adorned with every precious stone: the first foundation jasper, the second sapphire, the third chalcedony, the fourth emerald,
    Revelation 21:20 the fifth sardonyx, the sixth sardius, the seventh chrysolite, the eighth beryl, the ninth topaz, the tenth chrysoprase, the eleventh hyacinth, the twelfth amethyst.

Revelation 4:4
And around the throne were twenty-four thrones, and upon the thrones twenty-four elders sitting, clothed in white garments, and upon their heads golden crowns.
  Revelation 11:16 — internal cross-reference: Both mention “the twenty-four elders” sitting upon thrones before God; Revelation 11:16 shows them falling to worship.
    Revelation 11:16 And the twenty-four elders sitting upon their thrones before God fell upon their faces and worshipped God,
  Revelation 4:10 — internal cross-reference: The “golden crowns” of the elders introduced here are the crowns they “throw… before the throne” in worship in Revelation 4:10.
    Revelation 4:10 the twenty-four elders will fall before the One sitting upon the throne, and will worship the One living for the ages of the ages, and will throw their crowns before the throne, saying,
  Revelation 3:4–3:5 — shared image: The elders are “clothed in white garments,” the same clothing promised to the faithful conqueror in Revelation 3:5 (“clothed in white garments”).
    Revelation 3:4 But thou hast a few names in Sardis who did not defile their garments, and they shall walk with Me in white, because they are worthy.
    Revelation 3:5 The one who conquereth will thus be clothed in white garments, and I will certainly not erase his name from the book of life, and I will confess his name before My Father and before His angels.

Revelation 4:5
And from the throne come out lightnings and voices and thunders; and seven lamps of fire burn before the throne, which are the seven spirits of God.
  Exodus 19:16 — shared theophany imagery: Both describe God’s presence accompanied by thunder and lightning: at Sinai “thunder and lightning and a dark cloud,” and from the throne “lightnings and voices and thunders.”
    Exodus 19:16 Toward dawn on the third day there were thunder and lightning and a dark cloud over Mount Sinai. The trumpet sounded loudly, and all the people in the camp were terrified.
  Zechariah 4:2 — shared image: Both visions feature “seven lamps.” Zechariah’s lamps are on a golden lampstand, whereas John’s are lamps of fire burning before the throne and are identified as the seven spirits of God.
    Zechariah 4:2 and said to me, “What do you see?” I said, “I see a lampstand entirely of gold, with its lamp-bowl above it, seven lamps upon it, and seven pouring channels for the lamps above it.
  Revelation 1:4 — internal cross-reference: Both locate “the seven spirits” before God’s throne, the same figure named in John’s opening greeting.
    Revelation 1:4 John, to the seven Churches in Asia: grace to you and peace from the One who is and who was and who is coming, and from the seven spirits before His throne,

Revelation 4:6
And before the throne was something like a glassy sea, like crystal. And in the midst of the throne and around the throne were four living creatures, full of eyes in front and behind.
  Revelation 15:2 — internal cross-reference: Both describe “something like a glassy sea” before God. In Revelation 15:2 it is “mixed with fire” and the conquerors stand upon it.
    Revelation 15:2 And I saw something like a glassy sea mixed with fire, and those conquering the beast and its image and the number of its name standing upon the glassy sea, having lyres of God.
  Ezekiel 10:12 — shared image: Both describe heavenly beings “full of eyes” all around. In Ezekiel the eyes cover the cherubim’s backs, hands, wings and wheels; John’s living creatures have no wheels.
    Ezekiel 10:12 Their backs, their hands, their wings, and the wheels were full of eyes all around, upon their four wheels.

Revelation 4:7
And the first living creature was like a lion, and the second living creature like a calf, and the third living creature had a face like a human being’s, and the fourth living creature was like an eagle flying.
  Ezekiel 1:10 — shared image: Both describe four living beings associated with a lion, a calf, a human being and an eagle. In Ezekiel each creature has all four faces, whereas in Revelation each creature resembles one.
    Ezekiel 1:10 The likeness of their faces was a human face and a lion’s face on the right for all four, and a calf’s face on the left for all four, and an eagle’s face for all four.

Revelation 4:8
And the four living creatures, each one of them having six wings, are full of eyes around and within; and they have no rest day and night, saying, “Holy, holy, holy, Lord God Almighty, the One who was and who is and who is coming.”
  Revelation 1:8 — same divine title: Both name God as “Lord God” and “Almighty” and as “the One who is and who was and who is coming,” though the order of the tenses differs.
    Revelation 1:8 “I am the Alpha and the Omega,” saith the Lord God, the One who is and who was and who is coming, the Almighty.
  Revelation 11:17 — same divine title: Both address God as “Lord God Almighty” followed by the title “the One who is and who was” (here in the order “who was and who is”). Revelation 11:17 lacks “who is coming” and gives thanks because God has “taken Thy great power and reigned.”
    Revelation 11:17 saying, “We give Thee thanks, Lord God Almighty, the One who is and who was, because Thou hast taken Thy great power and reigned.

Revelation 4:9
And whenever the living creatures give glory and honour and thanksgiving to the One sitting upon the throne, the One living for the ages of the ages,
  Revelation 10:6 — same divine title: Both call God “the One living for the ages of the ages”; in Revelation 10:6 an angel swears by him as Creator.
    Revelation 10:6 and swore by the One living for the ages of the ages, who created heaven and the things in it, and the earth and the things in it, and the sea and the things in it, that there will be no more delay.
  Daniel 12:7 — shared divine title: Both describe God as the one who lives for ever: the one clothed in fine linen swears “by God who lives for ever,” and the living creatures honour “the One living for the ages of the ages.”
    Daniel 12:7 I heard the one clothed in fine linen above the river’s water say, “Until the time of completion.” He raised his right hand and his left to heaven and swore by God who lives for ever that in a time, times, and half a time would be the completion of the “hands of release” of a holy people, and all these things would be completed.
  Revelation 5:13–5:14 — internal cross-reference: Both describe glory and honour given “to the One sitting upon the throne,” and both use the phrase “the ages of the ages” (in 4:9 as a title of God, in 5:13 for the duration of the praise). In Revelation 5:13 the praise comes from every creature, is shared with the Lamb, and is answered by the living creatures’ “Amen.”
    Revelation 5:13 And every creature in heaven and upon the earth and beneath the earth and upon the sea, and all the things in them, I heard saying, “To the One sitting upon the throne and to the Lamb be the blessing and the honour and the glory and the dominion for the ages of the ages.”
    Revelation 5:14 And the four living creatures were saying, “Amen.” And the elders fell and worshipped.

Revelation 4:10
the twenty-four elders will fall before the One sitting upon the throne, and will worship the One living for the ages of the ages, and will throw their crowns before the throne, saying,
  Revelation 11:16 — internal cross-reference: Both show “the twenty-four elders” falling before God and worshipping; in Revelation 11:16 they are described as sitting upon their thrones before God.
    Revelation 11:16 And the twenty-four elders sitting upon their thrones before God fell upon their faces and worshipped God,
  Revelation 19:4 — internal cross-reference: Both show the twenty-four elders falling and worshipping God who sits upon the throne, and both introduce their spoken response.
    Revelation 19:4 And the twenty-four elders and the four living creatures fell and worshipped God, who sitteth upon the throne, saying, “Amen, Hallelujah!”
  Revelation 4:4 — internal cross-reference: The crowns the elders throw before the throne are the “golden crowns” on their heads described in Revelation 4:4.
    Revelation 4:4 And around the throne were twenty-four thrones, and upon the thrones twenty-four elders sitting, clothed in white garments, and upon their heads golden crowns.

Revelation 4:11
“Thou art worthy, our Lord and God, to receive the glory and the honour and the power, because Thou didst create all things, and because of Thy will they existed and were created.”
  Revelation 5:12 — parallel doxology: Both acclaim one who is “worthy… to receive” power, honour and glory. Here God is praised as Creator; in Revelation 5:12 the Lamb is praised as the one who was slaughtered.
    Revelation 5:12 saying with a loud voice, “Worthy is the Lamb who was slaughtered to receive the power and wealth and wisdom and strength and honour and glory and blessing.”
  Revelation 14:7 — shared theme of Creator worship: Both ground worship in God’s creating work: here “Thou didst create all things,” and the angel calls all to “worship the One who made heaven and the earth and sea.”
    Revelation 14:7 saying in a loud voice, “Fear God and give Him glory, because the hour of His judgment hath come, and worship the One who made heaven and the earth and sea and springs of waters.”
  Revelation 10:6 — shared theme of Creator: Both describe God as the creator of all: the angel swears by the One “who created heaven and the things in it, and the earth… and the sea.”
    Revelation 10:6 and swore by the One living for the ages of the ages, who created heaven and the things in it, and the earth and the things in it, and the sea and the things in it, that there will be no more delay.

Revelation 5:1
And I saw upon the right hand of the One sitting upon the throne a scroll written within and on the back, sealed with seven seals.
  Ezekiel 2:9–2:10 — shared image: Both describe a scroll held in a heavenly hand with writing on both sides: Ezekiel’s scroll had “writing on the back and the front,” John’s is “written within and on the back.” Ezekiel’s scroll is unrolled, whereas John’s is sealed.
    Ezekiel 2:9 I looked, and behold, a hand was stretched toward me, and in it was a scroll of a book;
    Ezekiel 2:10 and He unrolled it before me. It had writing on the back and the front, and upon it were written a lament, a song and woe.
  Isaiah 29:11 — shared image: Both describe a sealed book: Isaiah’s “sealed book,” which the reader “cannot read, for it is sealed,” and the scroll “sealed with seven seals.”
    Isaiah 29:11 All these sayings will be for you like the words of this sealed book. If they give it to a person who knows how to read, saying, “Read these,” he will say, “I cannot read, for it is sealed.”
  Revelation 5:7 — internal cross-reference: The scroll seen in the right hand of the One sitting upon the throne is taken from that hand by the Lamb in Revelation 5:7.
    Revelation 5:7 And He came and hath taken it from the right hand of the One sitting upon the throne.

Revelation 5:2
And I saw a mighty angel proclaiming in a loud voice, “Who is worthy to open the scroll and loose its seals?”
  Revelation 5:5 — internal cross-reference: The angel’s question about who is worthy to open the scroll and its seals is answered in Revelation 5:5: the Lion of Judah “conquered, to open the scroll and its seven seals.”
    Revelation 5:5 And one of the elders saith unto me, “Weep not. Behold, the Lion from the tribe of Judah, the Root of David, conquered, to open the scroll and its seven seals.”
  Isaiah 29:11–29:12 — shared image: Both concern a sealed book that must be opened: in Isaiah one says, “I cannot read, for it is sealed,” and the angel asks, “Who is worthy to open the scroll and loose its seals?”
    Isaiah 29:11 All these sayings will be for you like the words of this sealed book. If they give it to a person who knows how to read, saying, “Read these,” he will say, “I cannot read, for it is sealed.”
    Isaiah 29:12 This book will be given into the hands of a person who does not know how to read. Someone will say to him, “Read this,” and he will say, “I do not know how to read.”
  Revelation 10:1 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 10:1 introduces “another mighty angel,” recalling the mighty angel proclaiming here.
    Revelation 10:1 And I saw another mighty angel coming down from heaven, clothed with a cloud, and the rainbow upon his head, and his face like the sun, and his feet like pillars of fire.

Revelation 5:3
And no one in heaven or upon the earth or beneath the earth was able to open the scroll or look at it.
  Revelation 5:13 — internal cross-reference: Both use the three-part range “in heaven… upon the earth… beneath the earth.” Here no one in those realms can open the scroll; in Revelation 5:13 every creature in them praises the One on the throne and the Lamb.
    Revelation 5:13 And every creature in heaven and upon the earth and beneath the earth and upon the sea, and all the things in them, I heard saying, “To the One sitting upon the throne and to the Lamb be the blessing and the honour and the glory and the dominion for the ages of the ages.”

Revelation 5:4
And I wept much, because no one was found worthy to open the scroll or look at it.
  Daniel 12:8–12:9 — shared image: Both describe revealed matters that remain closed to the seer: Daniel is told that “the commands are covered and sealed,” and no one is found worthy to open John’s scroll. Daniel is told to go away, whereas John weeps.
    Daniel 12:8 I heard, but did not understand the time from him. I said, “My lord, what is the interpretation of this word, and what do these figures concern?”
    Daniel 12:9 He said to me, “Go away, Daniel, for the commands are covered and sealed until

Revelation 5:5
And one of the elders saith unto me, “Weep not. Behold, the Lion from the tribe of Judah, the Root of David, conquered, to open the scroll and its seven seals.”
  Revelation 22:16 — same title of Christ: Both use the title “Root… of David” for Jesus; in Revelation 22:16 Jesus calls himself “the Root and the offspring of David.”
    Revelation 22:16 “I, Jesus, sent My angel to testify these things to you for the Churches. I am the Root and the offspring of David, the bright morning star.”
  Isaiah 11:10 — verbal allusion: Both use the image of a root from David’s line: Isaiah speaks of “Jesse’s root” and one rising to rule nations, and Revelation of “the Root of David.”
    Isaiah 11:10 On that day there will be Jesse’s root and the one rising to rule nations; nations will hope in him, and his rest will be honor.
  Hebrews 7:14 — shared detail: Both identify Christ as coming from the tribe of Judah: Hebrews says our Lord “has sprung from Judah,” and Revelation calls him “the Lion from the tribe of Judah.”
    Hebrews 7:14 For it is clear that our Lord has sprung from Judah, a tribe about which Moses said nothing concerning priests.

Revelation 5:6
And I saw in the midst of the throne and the four living creatures, and in the midst of the elders, a Lamb standing as slaughtered, having seven horns and seven eyes, which are the seven spirits of God sent into all the earth.
  Zechariah 4:10 — verbal allusion: Both speak of seven eyes ranging over all the earth: Zechariah’s “seven are the Lord’s eyes, looking over all the earth,” and the Lamb’s “seven eyes… sent into all the earth.”
    Zechariah 4:10 For who treated the small days with contempt? They will rejoice and see the tin stone in Zerubbabel’s hand. These seven are the Lord’s eyes, looking over all the earth.”
  Zechariah 3:9 — shared image: Both speak of “seven eyes.” In Zechariah the eyes are on the stone set before Joshua; here they belong to the Lamb.
    Zechariah 3:9 For the stone I set before Joshua—on the one stone are seven eyes. Look, I am digging a pit, says the Lord Almighty, and I will feel out all the injustice of that land in one day.
  Revelation 13:8 — internal cross-reference: Both describe Christ as “the Lamb slaughtered,” a title Revelation 13:8 connects with the book of life.
    Revelation 13:8 And all those dwelling upon the earth will worship it, each whose name hath not been written in the book of life of the Lamb slaughtered from the foundation of the world.

Revelation 5:7
And He came and hath taken it from the right hand of the One sitting upon the throne.
  Revelation 5:1 — internal cross-reference: The Lamb takes the scroll “from the right hand of the One sitting upon the throne,” where John first saw it in Revelation 5:1.
    Revelation 5:1 And I saw upon the right hand of the One sitting upon the throne a scroll written within and on the back, sealed with seven seals.

Revelation 5:8
And when He took the scroll, the four living creatures and the twenty-four elders fell before the Lamb, each having a lyre and golden bowls full of incense, which are the prayers of the saints.
  Psalms 140:2 — shared image: Both liken prayer to incense: the Psalmist asks that his “prayer be directed as incense,” and the golden bowls of incense “are the prayers of the saints.”
    Psalms 140:2 Let my prayer be directed as incense before Thee, the lifting of my hands as an evening sacrifice.
  Revelation 8:3–8:4 — internal cross-reference: Both connect incense with “the prayers of the saints.” In Revelation 8:3–4 an angel with a golden censer offers the incense with the prayers upon the golden altar before the throne.
    Revelation 8:3 And another angel came and stood at the altar, having a golden censer, and much incense was given to him to offer with the prayers of all the saints upon the golden altar before the throne.
    Revelation 8:4 And the smoke of the incense went up with the prayers of the saints from the angel’s hand before God.

Revelation 5:9
And they sing a new song, saying, “Thou art worthy to take the scroll and open its seals, because Thou wast slaughtered and didst purchase for God in Thy blood people from every tribe and tongue and people and nation,
  Psalms 39:4 — verbal echo: Both speak of singing “a new song” to God. In the Psalm it is put into the singer’s mouth as “a hymn to our God”; here it is sung to the Lamb who was slaughtered.
    Psalms 39:4 He put a new song into my mouth, a hymn to our God. Many will see and fear and hope in the Lord.
  Acts 20:28 — shared theme of purchase by blood: Both speak of a people acquired by blood: the Lamb “didst purchase for God in Thy blood” people, and God’s Church was “acquired through His own blood.”
    Acts 20:28 Pay attention to yourselves and to all the flock, among whom the Holy Spirit has placed you as overseers, to shepherd the Church of God, which He acquired through His own blood.
  Revelation 14:6 — repeated formula: Both use the fourfold list of nation, tribe, tongue and people to describe the whole human range. In Revelation 14:6 they are recipients of the good news; here they are those purchased.
    Revelation 14:6 And I saw another angel flying in midheaven, having eternal good news to proclaim to those seated upon the earth and to every nation and tribe and tongue and people,

Revelation 5:10
and Thou didst make them a kingdom and priests unto our God, and they shall reign upon the earth.”
  Revelation 1:6 — internal cross-reference: Both state that Christ made his people “a kingdom” and “priests” to God, the second passage adding that “they shall reign upon the earth.”
    Revelation 1:6 and made us a kingdom, priests to His God and Father—to Him be the glory and dominion for the ages of the ages. Amen.
  Exodus 19:6 — fulfilment of promise: Both describe God’s people as royal and priestly: Israel is to be “a royal priesthood and a holy nation,” and the redeemed are made “a kingdom and priests unto our God.”
    Exodus 19:6 And you shall be to Me a royal priesthood and a holy nation.’ These are the words which you shall speak to the sons of Israel.”
  Revelation 20:6 — internal cross-reference: Both say the redeemed will be “priests” of God and will “reign.” Revelation 20:6 specifies a reign with Christ for a thousand years.
    Revelation 20:6 Blessed and holy is the one who hath a share in the first resurrection; over these the second death hath no authority, but they will be priests of God and of Christ, and will reign with Him for a thousand years.

Revelation 5:11
And I saw, and heard a voice of many angels around the throne and the living creatures and the elders; and their number was myriads of myriads and thousands of thousands,
  Daniel 7:10 — verbal allusion: Both count the heavenly attendants in the same paired form: Daniel’s “a thousand thousands… ten thousand times ten thousand,” and John’s “myriads of myriads and thousands of thousands.”
    Daniel 7:10 and a river of fire came out before Him. A thousand thousands attended Him, and ten thousand times ten thousand stood beside Him. A court sat, and books were opened.
  Revelation 7:11 — internal cross-reference: Both describe the angels gathered around the throne together with the elders and the four living creatures.
    Revelation 7:11 And all the angels were standing around the throne and the elders and the four living creatures, and fell upon their faces before the throne and worshipped God,
  Hebrews 12:22 — shared image: Both speak of “myriads of angels” in God’s heavenly presence; Hebrews places them in festive gathering at the heavenly Jerusalem.
    Hebrews 12:22 But you have approached Mount Zion and the city of the living God, heavenly Jerusalem, and myriads of angels in festive gathering,

Revelation 5:12
saying with a loud voice, “Worthy is the Lamb who was slaughtered to receive the power and wealth and wisdom and strength and honour and glory and blessing.”
  Revelation 4:11 — parallel doxology: Both acclaim one who is worthy “to receive” power, honour and glory. Revelation 4:11 addresses God as Creator; here the Lamb is praised as slaughtered.
    Revelation 4:11 “Thou art worthy, our Lord and God, to receive the glory and the honour and the power, because Thou didst create all things, and because of Thy will they existed and were created.”
  Revelation 7:12 — parallel doxology: Both list seven attributes of praise, sharing power, wisdom, strength, honour, glory and blessing. Revelation 7:12 has “thanksgiving” where this verse has “wealth,” and ascribes them to God.
    Revelation 7:12 saying, “Amen. The blessing and the glory and the wisdom and the thanksgiving and the honour and the power and the strength belong to our God for the ages of the ages. Amen.”
  Revelation 5:9 — internal cross-reference: Both declare the Lamb “worthy” because he was “slaughtered”; the new song of Revelation 5:9 is taken up here by the angels.
    Revelation 5:9 And they sing a new song, saying, “Thou art worthy to take the scroll and open its seals, because Thou wast slaughtered and didst purchase for God in Thy blood people from every tribe and tongue and people and nation,

Revelation 5:13
And every creature in heaven and upon the earth and beneath the earth and upon the sea, and all the things in them, I heard saying, “To the One sitting upon the throne and to the Lamb be the blessing and the honour and the glory and the dominion for the ages of the ages.”
  Philippians 2:10 — shared image: Both describe universal homage from those “in heaven and on earth and under the earth.” Philippians speaks of bending the knee at the name of Jesus; Revelation adds the sea.
    Philippians 2:10 so that at the name of Jesus every knee should bend, of those in heaven and on earth and under the earth,
  Revelation 7:9–7:10 — internal cross-reference: Both ascribe praise jointly “to the One sitting upon the throne” and “to the Lamb”; in Revelation 7:10 the praise is for salvation.
    Revelation 7:9 After these things I saw, and behold, a great multitude that no one could number, from every nation and tribes and peoples and tongues, standing before the throne and before the Lamb, clothed in white robes, and palm branches in their hands.
    Revelation 7:10 And they cry with a loud voice, saying, “Salvation belongeth to our God, the One sitting upon the throne, and to the Lamb.”
  Revelation 1:6 — verbal echo: Both doxologies ascribe “the glory and the dominion for the ages of the ages.” In Revelation 1:6 they are ascribed to a single “Him”; here to the One sitting upon the throne and to the Lamb.
    Revelation 1:6 and made us a kingdom, priests to His God and Father—to Him be the glory and dominion for the ages of the ages. Amen.

Revelation 5:14
And the four living creatures were saying, “Amen.” And the elders fell and worshipped.
  Revelation 19:4 — internal cross-reference: Both show the living creatures saying “Amen” and the elders falling to worship. In Revelation 19:4 the elders and the living creatures together fall, worship and say “Amen, Hallelujah!”
    Revelation 19:4 And the twenty-four elders and the four living creatures fell and worshipped God, who sitteth upon the throne, saying, “Amen, Hallelujah!”
  Revelation 4:9–4:11 — internal cross-reference: Both describe the living creatures giving praise and the elders falling to worship before the throne, the pattern established in Revelation 4:9–11.
    Revelation 4:9 And whenever the living creatures give glory and honour and thanksgiving to the One sitting upon the throne, the One living for the ages of the ages,
    Revelation 4:10 the twenty-four elders will fall before the One sitting upon the throne, and will worship the One living for the ages of the ages, and will throw their crowns before the throne, saying,
    Revelation 4:11 “Thou art worthy, our Lord and God, to receive the glory and the honour and the power, because Thou didst create all things, and because of Thy will they existed and were created.”

Revelation 6:1
And I saw when the Lamb opened one of the seven seals, and I heard one of the four living creatures saying as with a voice of thunder, “Come.”
  Revelation 5:1 — internal cross-reference: The Lamb now begins to open the “seven seals” of the scroll first seen in Revelation 5:1.
    Revelation 5:1 And I saw upon the right hand of the One sitting upon the throne a scroll written within and on the back, sealed with seven seals.
  Revelation 5:5–5:7 — internal cross-reference: The Lamb who was declared able to “open the scroll and its seven seals” and took the scroll in Revelation 5:5–7 now opens the first seal.
    Revelation 5:5 And one of the elders saith unto me, “Weep not. Behold, the Lion from the tribe of Judah, the Root of David, conquered, to open the scroll and its seven seals.”
    Revelation 5:6 And I saw in the midst of the throne and the four living creatures, and in the midst of the elders, a Lamb standing as slaughtered, having seven horns and seven eyes, which are the seven spirits of God sent into all the earth.
    Revelation 5:7 And He came and hath taken it from the right hand of the One sitting upon the throne.
  Revelation 6:3 — parallel sequence: Both describe a seal being opened followed by a living creature saying, “Come.” Revelation 6:3 continues the sequence with the second seal.
    Revelation 6:3 And when He opened the second seal, I heard the second living creature saying, “Come.”

Revelation 6:2
And I saw, and behold, a white horse, and the one sitting upon it had a bow, and a crown was given to him; and he went out conquering and to conquer.
  Zechariah 6:3–6:8 — shared image: Both visions include white horses that go out: in Zechariah “the white ones were going out,” and John’s rider on a white horse “went out conquering.” In Zechariah the horses draw chariots and are identified as the four winds of heaven.
    Zechariah 6:3 the third chariot white horses, and the fourth chariot dappled, gray horses.
    Zechariah 6:4 I answered and said to the angel speaking within me, “What are these, lord?”
    Zechariah 6:5 The angel speaking within me answered, “These are the four winds of heaven; they go out to stand beside the Lord of all the earth.
    Zechariah 6:6 The one with the black horses was going out toward the northern land; the white ones were going out behind them, and the dappled ones were going out toward the southern land,
    Zechariah 6:7 and the gray ones were going out and looking to go and travel around the earth.” He said, “Go and travel around the earth!” And they traveled around the earth.
    Zechariah 6:8 He cried out and said to me, “Look, those going out toward the northern land have brought My wrath to rest in the northern land.”
  Zechariah 1:8 — shared image: Both visions include a mounted rider and a white horse. In Zechariah the white horses are among several colours behind a man on a flame-red horse.
    Zechariah 1:8 I saw in the night: look, a man mounted on a flame-red horse! He stood between the two shaded mountains, and behind him were flame-red, gray, dappled and white horses.
  Revelation 19:11–19:12 — shared image: Both open with “behold, a white horse” and a rider associated with crowns and warfare. The texts do not by themselves establish that the two riders are the same; the rider of Revelation 19 is called Faithful and True.
    Revelation 19:11 And I saw heaven opened, and behold, a white horse, and the One sitting upon it is called Faithful and True, and in righteousness He judgeth and wageth war.
    Revelation 19:12 And His eyes are a flame of fire, and upon His head are many diadems, having a name written that no one knoweth except Himself.

Revelation 6:3
And when He opened the second seal, I heard the second living creature saying, “Come.”
  Revelation 6:1 — parallel sequence: Both describe a seal being opened followed by a living creature saying, “Come,” this verse continuing the sequence begun at the first seal.
    Revelation 6:1 And I saw when the Lamb opened one of the seven seals, and I heard one of the four living creatures saying as with a voice of thunder, “Come.”
  Revelation 4:7 — internal cross-reference: “The second living creature” speaking here is the one described in Revelation 4:7 as “like a calf.”
    Revelation 4:7 And the first living creature was like a lion, and the second living creature like a calf, and the third living creature had a face like a human being’s, and the fourth living creature was like an eagle flying.

Revelation 6:4
And another horse went out, fiery red, and the one sitting upon it was given to take peace from the earth, so that they would slaughter one another; and a great sword was given to him.
  Zechariah 6:2 — shared image: Both visions include red horses among horses of other colours: Zechariah’s first chariot “had flame-red horses,” and John sees “another horse” go out, “fiery red.”
    Zechariah 6:2 The first chariot had flame-red horses, and the second chariot black horses,
  Zechariah 1:8 — shared image: Both visions feature a rider on a red horse: Zechariah sees “a man mounted on a flame-red horse.” Zechariah’s rider is not given a sword or the power to take peace.
    Zechariah 1:8 I saw in the night: look, a man mounted on a flame-red horse! He stood between the two shaded mountains, and behind him were flame-red, gray, dappled and white horses.
  Matthew 10:34 — verbal echo: Both pair the removal of peace on the earth with a sword: Jesus did not come “to bring peace upon the earth… but a sword,” and the rider is given “to take peace from the earth” and “a great sword.” Jesus speaks of the purpose of his coming, not of mutual slaughter.
    Matthew 10:34 Do not suppose that I came to bring peace upon the earth. I did not come to bring peace, but a sword.

Revelation 6:5
And when He opened the third seal, I heard the third living creature saying, “Come.” And I saw, and behold, a black horse, and the one sitting upon it had a balance in his hand.
  Ezekiel 4:16 — shared image: Both use the weighing of food as a sign of scarcity: Jerusalem will “eat bread by weight and in need,” and the rider holds “a balance in his hand.”
    Ezekiel 4:16 He said to me, “Son of man, behold, I am breaking the support of bread in Jerusalem. They will eat bread by weight and in need, and drink water by measure and in devastation,
  Leviticus 26:26 — shared image: Both use rationing by weight as a sign of judgement: God threatens that bread will be given back “by weight” and they will not be filled. Revelation shows the balance without stating the threat.
    Leviticus 26:26 When I afflict you with a scarcity of bread, ten women shall bake your bread in one oven and give back your bread by weight. You shall eat and in no wise be filled.
  Zechariah 6:2 — shared image: Both visions include “black horses” among horses of several colours; in Zechariah they draw the second chariot.
    Zechariah 6:2 The first chariot had flame-red horses, and the second chariot black horses,

Revelation 6:6
And I heard something like a voice in the midst of the four living creatures saying, “A choenix of wheat for a denarius, and three choenices of barley for a denarius; and do not harm the oil and the wine.”
  Revelation 9:4 — parallel restriction: Both contain a command limiting the scope of a judgement by telling agents “not to harm” certain produce or vegetation: here “the oil and the wine,” in Revelation 9:4 “the grass of the earth or any green thing or any tree.” In Revelation 9:4 people without God’s seal are then targeted.
    Revelation 9:4 And they were told not to harm the grass of the earth or any green thing or any tree, but only the people who do not have the seal of God upon their foreheads.

Revelation 6:7
And when He opened the fourth seal, I heard the voice of the fourth living creature saying, “Come.”
  Revelation 6:3 — parallel seal formula: Both verses use the same pattern for the seal openings: when He opened a numbered seal, the matching numbered living creature is heard saying “Come.” Revelation 6:3 gives the second instance and 6:7 the fourth.
    Revelation 6:3 And when He opened the second seal, I heard the second living creature saying, “Come.”
  Revelation 4:7 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 4:7 introduces the “fourth living creature” who speaks at the fourth seal, describing it as “like an eagle flying.” The seal vision itself does not repeat this description.
    Revelation 4:7 And the first living creature was like a lion, and the second living creature like a calf, and the third living creature had a face like a human being’s, and the fourth living creature was like an eagle flying.

Revelation 6:8
And I saw, and behold, a pale green horse, and the one sitting upon it had the name Death, and Hades followed with him. And authority was given to them over a fourth of the earth, to kill with sword and with famine and with death and by the beasts of the earth.
  Ezekiel 5:12 — verbal allusion: Ezekiel divides the judgment by quarters, with portions consumed “by death,” “by famine” and falling “by the sword.” Revelation gives authority over “a fourth of the earth” to kill with sword, famine and death; Ezekiel's fourth portion is scattering rather than beasts.
    Ezekiel 5:12 A quarter of you will be consumed by death; a quarter of you will perish by famine within you; a quarter of you I will scatter to every wind; and a quarter of you will fall by the sword around you, and I will draw a sword after them.
  Ezekiel 5:15–5:17 — shared list of judgments: Ezekiel 5:17 names famine, “harmful wild animals,” death and the sword as judgments sent by the Lord. Revelation's rider kills “with sword and with famine and with death and by the beasts of the earth,” the same four agents.
    Ezekiel 5:15 you will be mourned and ravaged among the nations around you when I execute judgments against you in the vengeance of My wrath. I, the Lord, have spoken.
    Ezekiel 5:16 When I send My arrows of famine against them, they will bring exhaustion, and I will break your support of bread.
    Ezekiel 5:17 I will send famine and harmful wild animals against you, and I will punish you. Death and blood will pass over you, and I will bring a sword upon you all around. I, the Lord, have spoken.’”
  Revelation 20:13–20:14 — same personified figures: Both passages treat Death and Hades as a personified pair: here Death rides and “Hades followed with him,” while in Revelation 20:13–14 “Death and Hades” give up their dead and are thrown into the lake of fire.
    Revelation 20:13 And the sea gave the dead in it, and Death and Hades gave the dead in them, and they were judged, each according to their works.
    Revelation 20:14 And Death and Hades were thrown into the lake of fire. This is the second death, the lake of fire.

Revelation 6:9
And when He opened the fifth seal, I saw beneath the altar the souls of those slaughtered because of the word of God and because of the testimony they held.
  Revelation 20:4 — parallel vision: Both visions show “the souls” of those killed “because of the word of God” and because of testimony. Revelation 20:4 specifies they were beheaded for “the testimony of Jesus” and adds that they reign with Christ, whereas 6:9 places them “beneath the altar.”
    Revelation 20:4 And I saw thrones, and they sat upon them, and judgment was given to them; and the souls of those beheaded because of the testimony of Jesus and because of the word of God, and those who did not worship the beast or its image and did not receive the mark upon their forehead and upon their hand. And they lived and reigned with Christ for a thousand years.
  Revelation 1:9 — shared phrase: John is on Patmos “because of the word of God and the testimony of Jesus,” the same twofold cause given for the slaughter of the souls under the altar. John's case involves exile rather than death.
    Revelation 1:9 I, John, your brother and fellow partner in the affliction and kingdom and endurance in Jesus, came to be on the island called Patmos because of the word of God and the testimony of Jesus.

Revelation 6:10
And they cried with a loud voice, saying, “How long, O Master, holy and true, before Thou dost judge and avenge our blood upon those dwelling on the earth?”
  Revelation 19:2 — fulfilment of plea: The martyrs ask when God will “judge and avenge our blood”; Revelation 19:2 declares that He “judged the great prostitute” and “avenged the blood of His slaves.” The martyrs address the Master as “holy and true,” and 19:2 calls His judgments “true and righteous.”
    Revelation 19:2 because His judgments are true and righteous, because He judged the great prostitute who corrupted the earth by her sexual immorality, and avenged the blood of His slaves from her hand.”
  Psalms 78:10 — verbal allusion: The psalm asks that “vengeance for the poured-out blood of Thy servants be known among the nations.” The martyrs' cry likewise seeks vengeance for their blood, though it is addressed directly to the “Master, holy and true.”
    Psalms 78:10 lest the nations say, “Where is their God?” And let vengeance for the poured-out blood of Thy servants be known among the nations before our eyes.
  Zechariah 1:12 — shared lament form: Both appeal to God with the question “how long,” using a sovereign title (“Lord Almighty”; “O Master”). Zechariah’s angel asks about mercy for Jerusalem and Judah’s cities, whereas the martyrs ask for judgment and vengeance.
    Zechariah 1:12 The angel of the Lord answered, “Lord Almighty, how long wilt Thou refuse mercy to Jerusalem and Judah’s cities, which Thou hast overlooked this seventieth year?”

Revelation 6:11
And a white robe was given to each of them, and they were told to rest for a little time yet, until their fellow slaves and their brothers, who were about to be killed as they themselves had been, were also complete in number.
  Revelation 7:9 — shared image: The white robe given to each martyr reappears in the great multitude “clothed in white robes” before the throne in Revelation 7:9. The later vision does not state that this multitude consists only of martyrs.
    Revelation 7:9 After these things I saw, and behold, a great multitude that no one could number, from every nation and tribes and peoples and tongues, standing before the throne and before the Lamb, clothed in white robes, and palm branches in their hands.
  Daniel 12:13 — verbal parallel: Daniel is told to “go and rest,” for there are still days “until completion is fulfilled.” The martyrs are likewise told to rest “for a little time yet” until the number of their fellow slaves is complete.
    Daniel 12:13 But you, go and rest, for there are still days and hours until completion is fulfilled. You will rest and arise unto your glory at the completion of days.”
  Revelation 14:13 — shared theme of rest: Revelation 14:13 says the dead who die in the Lord “rest from their labours.” This connects with the instruction that the slain souls should “rest for a little time,” though 14:13 does not mention a waiting period.
    Revelation 14:13 And I heard a voice from heaven saying, “Write: Blessed are the dead who die in the Lord from now on.” “Yea,” saith the Spirit, “so that they may rest from their labours, for their works follow with them.”

Revelation 6:12
And I saw when He opened the sixth seal, and a great earthquake occurred, and the sun became black like a haircloth sack, and the whole moon became like blood,
  Isaiah 50:3 — shared image: Isaiah says God will “clothe heaven in darkness and make its covering like sackcloth.” Revelation describes the sun becoming “black like a haircloth sack.” Isaiah speaks of heaven generally, not the sun in particular.
    Isaiah 50:3 I will clothe heaven in darkness and make its covering like sackcloth.”
  Joel 2:10 — shared cosmic signs: Joel describes the earth in turmoil, heaven shaken, and “the sun and moon will grow dark.” The sixth seal joins an earthquake with a darkened sun and altered moon, though Joel does not say the moon becomes like blood.
    Joel 2:10 Before them the earth will be thrown into turmoil and heaven shaken; the sun and moon will grow dark, and the stars will sink their brightness.
  Matthew 24:29 — parallel prophecy: Jesus foretells that “the sun will be darkened, the moon will not give its brightness” and the heavens shaken. The sixth seal shows the same failure of sun and moon, adding the great earthquake and blood-like moon.
    Matthew 24:29 Immediately after the affliction of those days, the sun will be darkened, the moon will not give its brightness, the stars will fall from heaven, and the powers of the heavens will be shaken.

Revelation 6:13
and the stars of heaven fell to the earth, as a fig tree throweth off its unripe figs when shaken by a great wind.
  Isaiah 34:4 — verbal allusion: Isaiah 34:4 pictures the stars falling “like leaves falling from a fig tree.” Revelation likewise compares falling stars to a fig tree shedding its figs; Isaiah speaks of leaves, Revelation of unripe figs shaken by wind.
    Isaiah 34:4 Heaven will roll up like a scroll, and all the stars will fall like leaves from a vine, like leaves falling from a fig tree.
  Matthew 24:29 — parallel prophecy: Jesus foretells that “the stars will fall from heaven,” the same event shown here as “the stars of heaven fell to the earth.” Matthew does not use the fig-tree comparison.
    Matthew 24:29 Immediately after the affliction of those days, the sun will be darkened, the moon will not give its brightness, the stars will fall from heaven, and the powers of the heavens will be shaken.

Revelation 6:14
And heaven withdrew like a scroll being rolled up, and every mountain and island were moved from their places.
  Isaiah 34:4 — verbal allusion: Isaiah says “heaven will roll up like a scroll,” and Revelation says heaven withdrew “like a scroll being rolled up.” The image and comparison are the same in both texts.
    Isaiah 34:4 Heaven will roll up like a scroll, and all the stars will fall like leaves from a vine, like leaves falling from a fig tree.
  Revelation 16:20 — parallel judgment: At the seventh bowl “every island fled, and mountains were not found,” echoing the sixth seal's “every mountain and island were moved from their places.” The bowl text speaks of disappearance rather than displacement.
    Revelation 16:20 And every island fled, and mountains were not found.
  Revelation 20:11 — parallel vision: Before the great white throne “the earth and heaven fled, and no place was found for them,” comparable to heaven withdrawing and mountains and islands moved from their places. Revelation 20:11 is set at the final judgment.
    Revelation 20:11 And I saw a great white throne and the One sitting upon it, from whose face the earth and heaven fled, and no place was found for them.

Revelation 6:15
And the kings of the earth and the great ones and the commanders of thousands and the rich and the strong and every slave and free person hid themselves in the caves and among the rocks of the mountains.
  Isaiah 2:19 — verbal allusion: Isaiah speaks of carrying things “into caves, into clefts in the rocks” from the Lord’s terror when He rises to shatter the earth. Revelation shows the kings and people of every rank hiding themselves “in the caves and among the rocks of the mountains.”
    Isaiah 2:19 carrying them into caves, into clefts in the rocks and into holes in the earth, from the Lord’s terror and the glory of His strength, whenever He rises to shatter the earth.
  Isaiah 2:10 — verbal allusion: Isaiah 2:10 commands “enter the rocks and hide in the earth from the Lord's terror.” Revelation describes people of every rank who “hid themselves” among the rocks; Isaiah's verse is an imperative, Revelation's a narrative.
    Isaiah 2:10 And now enter the rocks and hide in the earth from the Lord’s terror and the glory of His strength, whenever He rises to shatter the earth.

Revelation 6:16
And they say to the mountains and the rocks, “Fall upon us and hide us from the face of the One sitting upon the throne and from the wrath of the Lamb,
  Hosea 10:8 — verbal allusion: Hosea has the people say to the mountains “Cover us!” and to the hills “Fall upon us!” Revelation adapts the cry as “Fall upon us and hide us,” addressed to the mountains and rocks, adding hiding from the face of the One on the throne and from the Lamb’s wrath.
    Hosea 10:8 The shrines of On, Israel’s sins, will be removed; thorns and thistles will grow on their altars. They will say to the mountains, ‘Cover us!’ and to the hills, ‘Fall upon us!’
  Luke 23:30 — verbal parallel: Jesus says people will begin “to say to the mountains, ‘Fall upon us,’ and to the hills, ‘Cover us.’” Revelation uses the same cry to the mountains, “Fall upon us.”
    Luke 23:30 Then they will begin to say to the mountains, ‘Fall upon us,’ and to the hills, ‘Cover us.’
  Revelation 20:11 — same divine figure: The people seek to hide “from the face of the One sitting upon the throne,” and Revelation 20:11 describes the One sitting on the throne “from whose face the earth and heaven fled.” Both stress flight from His face.
    Revelation 20:11 And I saw a great white throne and the One sitting upon it, from whose face the earth and heaven fled, and no place was found for them.

Revelation 6:17
because the great day of Their wrath hath come, and who is able to stand?”
  Malachi 3:2 — verbal allusion: Malachi asks “who will stand at his appearance?” concerning the day of the Lord's coming. Revelation closes with the same question, “who is able to stand?” on the great day of wrath.
    Malachi 3:2 And who will endure the day of his entrance? Or who will stand at his appearance? For he enters like a smelting fire and like launderers’ herb.
  Joel 2:11 — verbal allusion: Joel declares that “the Lord's day is great” and asks “who will be sufficient for it?” Revelation likewise speaks of “the great day” and asks who is able to stand.
    Joel 2:11 The Lord will give His voice before His force, because His camp is very large, because the works of His words are strong. For the Lord’s day is great, great and very manifest; who will be sufficient for it?
  Zephaniah 1:14–1:18 — shared title of the day: Zephaniah calls the Lord's “great day” a “day of wrath,” the combination used in “the great day of Their wrath.” Zephaniah's day falls on the earth's inhabitants; Revelation attributes the wrath to the One on the throne and the Lamb.
    Zephaniah 1:14 For the Lord’s great day is near, near and very swift. The sound of the Lord’s day is bitter and harsh; it is appointed mighty.
    Zephaniah 1:15 That day is a day of wrath, a day of affliction and constraint, a day of untimely ruin and destruction, a day of darkness and gloom, a day of cloud and mist,
    Zephaniah 1:16 a day of trumpet and shouting against fortified cities and high corners.
    Zephaniah 1:17 I will press people hard, and they will walk like the blind because they sinned against the Lord. He will pour out their blood like dust and their flesh like dung.
    Zephaniah 1:18 Their silver and gold will certainly not rescue them on the day of the Lord’s wrath. In the fire of His jealousy all the earth will be consumed, for He will bring completion and haste upon all the earth’s inhabitants.

Revelation 7:1
After this I saw four angels standing upon the four corners of the earth, holding the four winds of the earth, so that no wind might blow upon the earth or upon the sea or upon any tree.
  Revelation 7:3 — internal cross-reference: The restraint of the winds so that none might blow “upon the earth or upon the sea or upon any tree” is explained in 7:3: “Do not harm the earth or the sea or the trees” until the sealing is done.
    Revelation 7:3 saying, “Do not harm the earth or the sea or the trees until we seal the slaves of our God upon their foreheads.”
  Daniel 7:2 — shared image: Daniel sees “four winds of heaven falling upon the great sea,” and Revelation sees four angels holding “the four winds of the earth” back from the earth and sea. In Daniel the winds are released; here they are restrained.
    Daniel 7:2 “Upon my bed I was watching in dreams by night, and there were four winds of heaven falling upon the great sea.
  Matthew 24:31 — shared image: Jesus says His angels will gather the chosen “from the four winds,” and Revelation shows four angels holding “the four winds of the earth.” In Matthew the angels gather the chosen; here they hold back the winds.
    Matthew 24:31 He will send His angels with a great trumpet, and they will gather His chosen from the four winds, from one end of the heavens to the other.

Revelation 7:2
And I saw another angel coming up from the rising of the sun, having a seal of the living God, and he cried with a loud voice to the four angels who were given to harm the earth and the sea,
  Revelation 7:3–7:8 — internal cross-reference: The angel carrying “a seal of the living God” commands in 7:3 that harm wait until “we seal the slaves of our God upon their foreheads,” and 7:4–8 then numbers those sealed.
    Revelation 7:3 saying, “Do not harm the earth or the sea or the trees until we seal the slaves of our God upon their foreheads.”
    Revelation 7:4 And I heard the number of those sealed: a hundred forty-four thousand, sealed from every tribe of the sons of Israel.
    Revelation 7:5 From the tribe of Judah, twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Reuben, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Gad, twelve thousand;
    Revelation 7:6 from the tribe of Asher, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Naphtali, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Manasseh, twelve thousand;
    Revelation 7:7 from the tribe of Simeon, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Levi, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Issachar, twelve thousand;
    Revelation 7:8 from the tribe of Zebulun, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Joseph, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Benjamin, twelve thousand sealed.
  Revelation 9:4 — internal cross-reference: The locusts of Revelation 9:4 may harm only those “who do not have the seal of God upon their foreheads,” showing the protective purpose of the seal brought by this angel.
    Revelation 9:4 And they were told not to harm the grass of the earth or any green thing or any tree, but only the people who do not have the seal of God upon their foreheads.

Revelation 7:3
saying, “Do not harm the earth or the sea or the trees until we seal the slaves of our God upon their foreheads.”
  Ezekiel 9:4 — verbal allusion: In Ezekiel a man is told to “put the sign upon the foreheads” of those who grieve over the lawlessness in Jerusalem, as the angels here seal God’s slaves “upon their foreheads” before harm is done. Ezekiel speaks of a sign, Revelation of a seal.
    Ezekiel 9:4 and said to him, “Pass through the midst of Jerusalem and put the sign upon the foreheads of the men who sigh deeply and grieve over all the lawlessness being committed within her.”
  Revelation 9:4 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:4 restricts the locusts from harming grass or trees and directs them only at those without “the seal of God upon their foreheads,” the seal applied in 7:3.
    Revelation 9:4 And they were told not to harm the grass of the earth or any green thing or any tree, but only the people who do not have the seal of God upon their foreheads.
  Revelation 14:1 — internal cross-reference: The slaves of God sealed “upon their foreheads” here correspond to the hundred forty-four thousand with the Lamb on Mount Zion, who have His name and His Father’s name “written upon their foreheads.”
    Revelation 14:1 And I saw, and behold, the Lamb standing upon Mount Zion, and with Him a hundred forty-four thousand having His name and His Father’s name written upon their foreheads.

Revelation 7:4
And I heard the number of those sealed: a hundred forty-four thousand, sealed from every tribe of the sons of Israel.
  Revelation 14:1 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 14:1 shows the Lamb on Mount Zion with “a hundred forty-four thousand” bearing names on their foreheads, the same number as those sealed here. Chapter 14 does not repeat the tribal arrangement.
    Revelation 14:1 And I saw, and behold, the Lamb standing upon Mount Zion, and with Him a hundred forty-four thousand having His name and His Father’s name written upon their foreheads.
  Revelation 14:3 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 14:3 again names “the hundred forty-four thousand,” described as “purchased from the earth,” who alone can learn the new song. It shares the number but does not mention Israel's tribes.
    Revelation 14:3 And they sing something like a new song before the throne and before the four living creatures and the elders; and no one was able to learn the song except the hundred forty-four thousand purchased from the earth.

Revelation 7:5
From the tribe of Judah, twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Reuben, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Gad, twelve thousand;
  Exodus 1:2–1:4 — tribal list: Exodus lists the sons of Israel, including Reuben, Judah and Gad, the three tribes named in this verse. Revelation's order differs, placing Judah first.
    Exodus 1:2 Reuben, Simeon, Levi, and Judah;
    Exodus 1:3 Issachar, Zebulun, and Benjamin;
    Exodus 1:4 Dan and Naphtali, Gad and Asher.

Revelation 7:6
from the tribe of Asher, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Naphtali, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Manasseh, twelve thousand;
  Revelation 7:4 — internal cross-reference: Asher, Naphtali and Manasseh, each with “twelve thousand,” belong to the list whose total is announced in 7:4: “a hundred forty-four thousand, sealed from every tribe of the sons of Israel.”
    Revelation 7:4 And I heard the number of those sealed: a hundred forty-four thousand, sealed from every tribe of the sons of Israel.

Revelation 7:7
from the tribe of Simeon, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Levi, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Issachar, twelve thousand;
  Genesis 35:23 — tribal list: Genesis lists the sons of Leah as including Simeon, Levi and Issachar, the three tribes named consecutively in this verse. Genesis also names Reuben, Judah and Zebulun, who appear elsewhere in Revelation's list.
    Genesis 35:23 The sons of Leah: Reuben, Jacob’s firstborn, Simeon, Levi, Judah, Issachar, and Zebulun.

Revelation 7:8
from the tribe of Zebulun, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Joseph, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Benjamin, twelve thousand sealed.
  Revelation 7:4 — internal cross-reference: The last three tribes, each with “twelve thousand sealed,” close the list whose total is announced in 7:4: “a hundred forty-four thousand, sealed from every tribe of the sons of Israel.”
    Revelation 7:4 And I heard the number of those sealed: a hundred forty-four thousand, sealed from every tribe of the sons of Israel.

Revelation 7:9
After these things I saw, and behold, a great multitude that no one could number, from every nation and tribes and peoples and tongues, standing before the throne and before the Lamb, clothed in white robes, and palm branches in their hands.
  Revelation 5:9 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 5:9 says the Lamb purchased people “from every tribe and tongue and people and nation”; the great multitude is drawn “from every nation and tribes and peoples and tongues.” The fourfold formula is shared, in different order.
    Revelation 5:9 And they sing a new song, saying, “Thou art worthy to take the scroll and open its seals, because Thou wast slaughtered and didst purchase for God in Thy blood people from every tribe and tongue and people and nation,
  Revelation 6:11 — shared image: The martyrs under the altar are each given “a white robe,” and the great multitude stands “clothed in white robes.” Revelation 6:11 concerns a limited group awaiting completion; 7:9 a multitude no one could number.
    Revelation 6:11 And a white robe was given to each of them, and they were told to rest for a little time yet, until their fellow slaves and their brothers, who were about to be killed as they themselves had been, were also complete in number.
  John 12:13 — shared image: In John 12:13 the crowd takes “branches of the palm trees” to greet the King of Israel. The multitude likewise holds “palm branches” before the throne and the Lamb.
    John 12:13 took branches of the palm trees and went out to meet Him, and cried out, “Hosanna! Blessed is the One who comes in the name of the Lord, even the King of Israel!”

Revelation 7:10
And they cry with a loud voice, saying, “Salvation belongeth to our God, the One sitting upon the throne, and to the Lamb.”
  Psalms 3:9 — verbal allusion: The psalm declares “Salvation belongs to the Lord,” and the multitude cries “Salvation belongeth to our God.” Revelation adds the One sitting upon the throne and the Lamb as joint recipients.
    Psalms 3:9 Salvation belongs to the Lord, and Thy blessing is upon Thy people.
  Revelation 19:1 — parallel acclamation: A great multitude in heaven proclaims “The salvation and the glory and the power belong to our God,” with a loud voice, as the multitude does here. Revelation 19:1 adds glory and power and the call “Hallelujah.”
    Revelation 19:1 After these things I heard something like a loud voice of a great multitude in heaven, saying, “Hallelujah! The salvation and the glory and the power belong to our God,
  Revelation 5:13–5:14 — same paired recipients: Both acclamations are addressed jointly “to the One sitting upon the throne and to the Lamb.” Revelation 5:13 ascribes blessing, honour, glory and dominion rather than salvation.
    Revelation 5:13 And every creature in heaven and upon the earth and beneath the earth and upon the sea, and all the things in them, I heard saying, “To the One sitting upon the throne and to the Lamb be the blessing and the honour and the glory and the dominion for the ages of the ages.”
    Revelation 5:14 And the four living creatures were saying, “Amen.” And the elders fell and worshipped.

Revelation 7:11
And all the angels were standing around the throne and the elders and the four living creatures, and fell upon their faces before the throne and worshipped God,
  Revelation 5:11–5:13 — parallel heavenly scene: Revelation 5:11 shows “many angels around the throne and the living creatures and the elders,” the same arrangement given here. In chapter 5 the angels acclaim the Lamb with a loud voice; here they fall on their faces and worship.
    Revelation 5:11 And I saw, and heard a voice of many angels around the throne and the living creatures and the elders; and their number was myriads of myriads and thousands of thousands,
    Revelation 5:12 saying with a loud voice, “Worthy is the Lamb who was slaughtered to receive the power and wealth and wisdom and strength and honour and glory and blessing.”
    Revelation 5:13 And every creature in heaven and upon the earth and beneath the earth and upon the sea, and all the things in them, I heard saying, “To the One sitting upon the throne and to the Lamb be the blessing and the honour and the glory and the dominion for the ages of the ages.”
  Revelation 11:16 — same act of worship: The elders “fell upon their faces and worshipped God,” the identical action here ascribed to all the angels. Revelation 11:16 concerns the twenty-four elders only.
    Revelation 11:16 And the twenty-four elders sitting upon their thrones before God fell upon their faces and worshipped God,

Revelation 7:12
saying, “Amen. The blessing and the glory and the wisdom and the thanksgiving and the honour and the power and the strength belong to our God for the ages of the ages. Amen.”
  Revelation 5:12–5:14 — parallel doxology: Revelation 5:12 ascribes power, wisdom, strength, honour, glory and blessing to the Lamb, and 5:13–14 ends with “for the ages of the ages” and “Amen.” Chapter 7's sevenfold list replaces wealth with thanksgiving and is addressed to “our God.”
    Revelation 5:12 saying with a loud voice, “Worthy is the Lamb who was slaughtered to receive the power and wealth and wisdom and strength and honour and glory and blessing.”
    Revelation 5:13 And every creature in heaven and upon the earth and beneath the earth and upon the sea, and all the things in them, I heard saying, “To the One sitting upon the throne and to the Lamb be the blessing and the honour and the glory and the dominion for the ages of the ages.”
    Revelation 5:14 And the four living creatures were saying, “Amen.” And the elders fell and worshipped.
  Jude 1:25 — doxology form: Jude closes by ascribing glory, majesty, might and authority to God “into all the ages. Amen.” The shared elements are the ascription of attributes to God, the reference to the ages and the closing Amen; the lists of attributes differ largely.
    Jude 1:25 to the only God our Savior, through Jesus Christ our Lord, be glory, majesty, might, and authority before all time, and now, and into all the ages. Amen.

Revelation 7:13
And one of the elders answered, saying to me, “These who are clothed in the white robes, who are they, and from where did they come?”
  Revelation 7:9 — internal cross-reference: The elder's question concerns “these who are clothed in the white robes,” the great multitude seen in 7:9 “clothed in white robes” before the throne.
    Revelation 7:9 After these things I saw, and behold, a great multitude that no one could number, from every nation and tribes and peoples and tongues, standing before the throne and before the Lamb, clothed in white robes, and palm branches in their hands.
  Revelation 5:5 — same interpreting figure: In both scenes “one of the elders” speaks to John to explain what he sees. In 5:5 the elder comforts and announces the Lion of Judah; here he asks a question to prompt the explanation.
    Revelation 5:5 And one of the elders saith unto me, “Weep not. Behold, the Lion from the tribe of Judah, the Root of David, conquered, to open the scroll and its seven seals.”

Revelation 7:14
And I said to him, “My lord, thou knowest.” And he said unto me, “These are they who are coming out of the great tribulation, and they washed their robes and made them white in the blood of the Lamb.
  Revelation 22:14 — verbal parallel: Revelation 22:14 blesses “they who wash their robes,” echoing those who “washed their robes and made them white.” Chapter 22 links the washing to access to the tree of life rather than to the great tribulation.
    Revelation 22:14 Blessed are they who wash their robes, that they may have right to the tree of life and may enter through the gates into the city.
  Revelation 12:11 — same image: The faithful conquer “because of the blood of the Lamb,” as here their robes are made white “in the blood of the Lamb.” Revelation 12:11 adds the word of their testimony.
    Revelation 12:11 And they conquered him because of the blood of the Lamb and because of the word of their testimony, and they did not love their life until death.
