  Isaiah 1:18 — shared image: Isaiah promises that sins like scarlet will be whitened “like snow” and “like wool.” Revelation likewise speaks of robes made white, though by the blood of the Lamb rather than by direct divine pardon as in Isaiah.
    Isaiah 1:18 Come, let us reason together, says the Lord. Though your sins are like purple-red, I will whiten them like snow; though they are like scarlet, I will whiten them like wool.

Revelation 7:15
For this reason they are before the throne of God, and serve Him day and night in His temple, and the One sitting upon the throne will spread His tent over them.
  Revelation 21:3–21:4 — parallel promise: The promise that the One on the throne “will spread His tent over them” corresponds to the announcement “the tabernacle of God is with men, and He shall dwell with them.” Revelation 21:3 is set in the new Jerusalem.
    Revelation 21:3 And I heard a great voice from the throne, saying, “Behold, the tabernacle of God is with men, and He shall dwell with them, and they shall be His peoples, and God Himself shall be with them as their God,
    Revelation 21:4 and He shall wipe away every tear from their eyes, and death shall be no more; neither mourning nor crying nor pain shall be any more, for the former things passed away.”
  Revelation 22:3 — parallel promise: In the new city “the throne of God and of the Lamb” is present and “His slaves will serve Him,” echoing those before the throne who “serve Him day and night.”
    Revelation 22:3 And no accursed thing will be any more. And the throne of God and of the Lamb will be in it, and His slaves will serve Him,
  Isaiah 4:5–4:6 — shared image: Isaiah promises a covering over Mount Zion by day and night, “shelter and concealment” from heat and rain. Revelation pictures God spreading His tent over the redeemed; Isaiah speaks of cloud and shade rather than a tent.
    Isaiah 4:5 He will come, and it will be: a cloud will shade every place of Mount Zion and everything around her by day, and as of smoke and as of a light of fire burning by night; with all the glory it will be covered.
    Isaiah 4:6 It will be shade from heat, shelter and concealment from harshness and rain.

Revelation 7:16
They will hunger no more or thirst any more, nor will the sun fall upon them, nor any burning heat,
  Isaiah 49:10 — quotation: Isaiah says “they will neither hunger nor thirst, nor will scorching heat or the sun strike them,” which Revelation reproduces nearly word for word. Isaiah's next words about leading them to springs of water are echoed in Revelation 7:17.
    Isaiah 49:10 They will neither hunger nor thirst, nor will scorching heat or the sun strike them. Rather, the One showing them mercy will comfort them and lead them through springs of water.
  Psalms 120:6 — shared image: The psalm promises that “the sun will not scorch you,” the same protection from the sun given to those before the throne. The psalm also includes the moon by night.
    Psalms 120:6 By day the sun will not scorch you, nor the moon by night.

Revelation 7:17
because the Lamb in the midst of the throne will shepherd them and guide them to springs of waters of life; and God will wipe every tear from their eyes.”
  Isaiah 25:8 — verbal allusion: Isaiah says God “removed every tear from every face,” the image behind “God will wipe every tear from their eyes.” Isaiah connects it with death being swallowed.
    Isaiah 25:8 Death swallowed, having prevailed; and again God removed every tear from every face. He removed the people’s reproach from all the earth, for the Lord’s mouth has spoken.
  Revelation 21:4 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 21:4 repeats “He shall wipe away every tear from their eyes” in the new creation, adding that death, mourning, crying and pain will be no more.
    Revelation 21:4 and He shall wipe away every tear from their eyes, and death shall be no more; neither mourning nor crying nor pain shall be any more, for the former things passed away.”
  Revelation 21:6 — shared image: God promises to the thirsty “the spring of the water of life,” matching the Lamb's guiding them to “springs of waters of life.” In 21:6 the speaker is the Alpha and the Omega on the throne.
    Revelation 21:6 And He said unto me, “They have come to be. I am the Alpha and the Omega, the beginning and the end. To the one who thirsteth I will give freely from the spring of the water of life.

Revelation 8:1
And when He opened the seventh seal, there was silence in heaven for about half an hour.
  Revelation 5:1 — internal cross-reference: The seventh seal is the last of the “seven seals” on the scroll held by the One on the throne in Revelation 5:1; its opening completes that series.
    Revelation 5:1 And I saw upon the right hand of the One sitting upon the throne a scroll written within and on the back, sealed with seven seals.
  Revelation 6:12 — parallel seal formula: Revelation 6:12 opens the sixth seal with the same formula, “when He opened the … seal.” The sixth seal brings earthquake and cosmic upheaval, whereas the seventh brings silence.
    Revelation 6:12 And I saw when He opened the sixth seal, and a great earthquake occurred, and the sun became black like a haircloth sack, and the whole moon became like blood,

Revelation 8:2
And I saw the seven angels who stand before God, and seven trumpets were given to them.
  Luke 1:19 — shared title: Gabriel identifies himself as one “who stands before God,” the description given to the seven angels here. Luke names one angel; Revelation speaks of seven unnamed angels.
    Luke 1:19 The angel answered him, “I am Gabriel, who stands before God, and I was sent to speak to you and announce this good news to you.
  Revelation 11:15 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 11:15 records the sounding of the seventh angel, completing the series of trumpets given to the seven angels in 8:2.
    Revelation 11:15 And the seventh angel sounded, and loud voices arose in heaven, saying, “The kingdom of the world hath become the kingdom of our Lord and of His Christ, and He will reign for the ages of the ages.”

Revelation 8:3
And another angel came and stood at the altar, having a golden censer, and much incense was given to him to offer with the prayers of all the saints upon the golden altar before the throne.
  Revelation 5:8 — same image: Revelation 5:8 describes “golden bowls full of incense, which are the prayers of the saints,” and here incense is offered “with the prayers of all the saints.” In 5:8 the elders and living creatures hold the bowls; here an angel with a golden censer offers the incense.
    Revelation 5:8 And when He took the scroll, the four living creatures and the twenty-four elders fell before the Lamb, each having a lyre and golden bowls full of incense, which are the prayers of the saints.
  Psalms 140:2 — shared image: The psalmist asks, “Let my prayer be directed as incense before Thee.” Revelation pictures incense offered with the saints' prayers before the throne.
    Psalms 140:2 Let my prayer be directed as incense before Thee, the lifting of my hands as an evening sacrifice.
  Revelation 9:13 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:13 mentions a voice from the horns of “the golden altar before God,” the same altar on which the incense is offered here.
    Revelation 9:13 And the sixth angel sounded, and I heard one voice from the horns of the golden altar before God,

Revelation 8:4
And the smoke of the incense went up with the prayers of the saints from the angel’s hand before God.
  Psalms 140:2 — shared image: The psalm asks that prayer be directed “as incense before Thee,” and Revelation shows the smoke of incense rising “with the prayers of the saints … before God.”
    Psalms 140:2 Let my prayer be directed as incense before Thee, the lifting of my hands as an evening sacrifice.
  Revelation 8:3 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 8:3 describes the angel with the golden censer receiving incense to offer with the saints' prayers; 8:4 describes the smoke of that incense going up before God.
    Revelation 8:3 And another angel came and stood at the altar, having a golden censer, and much incense was given to him to offer with the prayers of all the saints upon the golden altar before the throne.

Revelation 8:5
And the angel took the censer and filled it from the fire of the altar, and threw it to the earth; and there were thunders and voices and lightnings and an earthquake.
  Ezekiel 10:2–10:7 — shared image: In Ezekiel the man in the robe is told to “fill your hands with burning coals” and “scatter them over the city.” The angel likewise fills the censer from the fire of the altar and throws it to the earth; Ezekiel's coals come from among the cherubim.
    Ezekiel 10:2 He said to the man wearing the robe, “Go in among the wheels beneath the cherubim, fill your hands with burning coals from among the cherubim, and scatter them over the city.” He went in before me.
    Ezekiel 10:3 The cherubim stood to the right of the house as the man entered, and the cloud filled the inner court.
    Ezekiel 10:4 The Lord’s glory departed from the cherubim to the forecourt of the house. The cloud filled the house, and the court filled with the radiance of the Lord’s glory.
    Ezekiel 10:5 The sound of the cherubim’s wings was heard as far as the outer court, like the voice of God Shaddai speaking.
    Ezekiel 10:6 When He commanded the man wearing the holy robe, saying, “Take fire from among the wheels, from among the cherubim,” he entered and stood beside the wheels,
    Ezekiel 10:7 and he stretched out his hand into the midst of the fire among the cherubim, took it and gave it into the hands of the man wearing the holy robe. He took it and went out.
  Leviticus 16:12 — shared cultic image: Leviticus prescribes a “firepan full of burning coals from the altar,” the equipment and source of fire used by the angel here. In Leviticus the coals are taken within the veil with incense, not thrown out in judgment.
    Leviticus 16:12 And he shall take the firepan full of burning coals from the altar opposite the Lord, fill his hands with finely compounded incense, and bring them within the veil,
  Revelation 11:19 — parallel theophany: Revelation 11:19 lists “lightnings and voices and thunders and an earthquake” when God's temple is opened, the same phenomena as here. Revelation 11:19 adds great hail.
    Revelation 11:19 And God’s temple in heaven was opened, and the ark of His covenant was seen in His temple; and there were lightnings and voices and thunders and an earthquake and great hail.

Revelation 8:6
And the seven angels who had the seven trumpets prepared themselves to sound them.
  Revelation 8:2 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 8:2 introduces the seven angels before God to whom “seven trumpets were given”; 8:6 shows the same angels preparing to sound them.
    Revelation 8:2 And I saw the seven angels who stand before God, and seven trumpets were given to them.

Revelation 8:7
And the first sounded, and there came hail and fire mixed with blood, and it was thrown to the earth; and a third of the earth was burned up, and a third of the trees was burned up, and all green grass was burned up.
  Psalms 104:32 — exodus plague background: The psalm recalls that God made Egypt's rains “hail, consuming fire in their land.” The first trumpet likewise brings hail and fire on the earth, adding blood and the burning of a third.
    Psalms 104:32 He made their rains hail, consuming fire in their land,
  Ezekiel 38:22 — shared judgment elements: Ezekiel's judgment on Gog includes “blood,” “hailstones” and raining fire, the three elements of the first trumpet. Ezekiel adds death, flooding rain and sulfur.
    Ezekiel 38:22 I will judge him with death, blood, flooding rain and hailstones. I will rain fire and sulfur upon him, all those with him and the many nations with him.
  Joel 3:3 — shared signs: Joel foretells wonders in heaven and on earth: “blood, fire and a vapor of smoke.” The first trumpet combines fire and blood thrown to the earth, but Joel does not mention hail.
    Joel 3:3 I will give wonders in heaven and upon the earth: blood, fire and a vapor of smoke.

Revelation 8:8
And the second angel sounded, and something like a great mountain burning with fire was thrown into the sea; and a third of the sea became blood,
  Jeremiah 28:25 — shared image: God threatens to roll the destroying mountain down from the rocks and make it “like a burned mountain.” Revelation sees “something like a great mountain burning with fire” thrown into the sea; Jeremiah’s oracle addresses a “ruined mountain that ruins the whole earth” and does not mention the sea.
    Jeremiah 28:25 I am against you, ruined mountain that ruins the whole earth. I will stretch My hand against you, roll you down from the rocks, and make you like a burned mountain.
  Exodus 7:17–7:21 — exodus plague background: In Exodus the river's water “turned to blood” and its fish died. The second trumpet turns a third of the sea into blood, extending the plague from river to sea and limiting it to a third.
    Exodus 7:17 Thus says the Lord: “By this you shall know that I am the Lord: behold, I strike with the staff in My hand upon the water in the river, and it shall be turned to blood.
    Exodus 7:18 The fish in the river will die, the river will stink, and the Egyptians will be unable to drink its water.”’”
    Exodus 7:19 And the Lord said to Moses, “Say to Aaron your brother, ‘Take your staff, and stretch out your hand over the waters of Egypt, over their rivers, and over their canals, and over their marshes, and over all their standing water, and it shall become blood.’” And there was blood throughout all the land of Egypt, both in wood and in stone.
    Exodus 7:20 Moses and Aaron did as the Lord commanded. He raised his staff and struck the river’s water before Pharaoh and his attendants, and all the water in the river turned to blood.
    Exodus 7:21 The fish in the river died, and the river stank. The Egyptians could not drink its water, and there was blood throughout the land of Egypt.

Revelation 8:9
and a third of the creatures in the sea, those having life, died, and a third of the ships were destroyed.
  Revelation 16:3 — parallel judgment: At the second bowl the sea becomes blood and “every living soul died, the things in the sea.” The second trumpet affects only a third of the sea creatures and also destroys ships.
    Revelation 16:3 And the second poured out his bowl into the sea, and it became blood like a dead person’s, and every living soul died, the things in the sea.
  Exodus 7:21 — exodus plague background: In Exodus “the fish in the river died” when the water became blood. The trumpet likewise brings death to living creatures in waters turned to blood, though in the sea and limited to a third.
    Exodus 7:21 The fish in the river died, and the river stank. The Egyptians could not drink its water, and there was blood throughout the land of Egypt.

Revelation 8:10
And the third angel sounded, and a great star fell from heaven, burning like a lamp, and fell upon a third of the rivers and upon the springs of the waters.
  Revelation 16:4 — parallel judgment: The third bowl is poured “into the rivers and the springs of the waters,” the same targets struck by the falling star at the third trumpet. The bowl turns them into blood; the trumpet affects a third of them.
    Revelation 16:4 And the third poured out his bowl into the rivers and the springs of the waters, and they became blood.
  Revelation 9:1 — internal cross-reference: At the fifth trumpet John sees “a star fallen from heaven to the earth,” another falling star in the same trumpet series. The fifth-trumpet star receives the key of the abyss.
    Revelation 9:1 And the fifth angel sounded, and I saw a star fallen from heaven to the earth, and the key of the shaft of the abyss was given to him.
  Isaiah 14:12 — shared image: Isaiah laments that the “dawn-bearer” fell “from heaven” and was shattered to earth. Revelation sees a great star falling from heaven; Isaiah's taunt is directed at a ruler and does not mention waters.
    Isaiah 14:12 How the dawn-bearer who rises in the morning fell from heaven! The one dispatching to all the nations was shattered to earth.

Revelation 8:11
And the name of the star is called Wormwood; and a third of the waters became wormwood, and many people died from the waters because they were made bitter.
  Jeremiah 23:15 — shared image: God threatens to “give them bitter water to drink” as judgment. Revelation describes waters made bitter so that many died; Jeremiah's judgment is on Jerusalem's prophets and does not name wormwood in this rendering.
    Jeremiah 23:15 Therefore thus says the Lord: Behold, I will feed them pain and give them bitter water to drink, because from Jerusalem’s prophets defilement went out into all the land.
  Jeremiah 9:14 — shared image: God says He will “make them drink gall-water” as punishment. Revelation's waters turned to wormwood and made bitter carry the same idea of judgment by poisoned water, though this rendering of Jeremiah says gall rather than wormwood.
    Jeremiah 9:14 therefore thus says the Lord, Israel’s God: Behold, I will feed them distresses and make them drink gall-water,

Revelation 8:12
And the fourth angel sounded, and a third of the sun and a third of the moon and a third of the stars were struck, so that a third of them might be darkened and the day might not shine for a third of it, and the night likewise.
  Isaiah 13:10 — shared cosmic signs: Isaiah foretells that heaven's stars “will not give light,” the sun will grow dark and “the moon will not give its light.” The fourth trumpet strikes sun, moon and stars so that they are darkened, limited to a third.
    Isaiah 13:10 For heaven’s stars, Orion and all heaven’s array will not give light; it will grow dark as the sun rises, and the moon will not give its light.
  Joel 2:10 — shared cosmic signs: Joel says “the sun and moon will grow dark, and the stars will sink their brightness,” the same three lights darkened at the fourth trumpet. Revelation limits the darkening to a third.
    Joel 2:10 Before them the earth will be thrown into turmoil and heaven shaken; the sun and moon will grow dark, and the stars will sink their brightness.
  Matthew 24:29 — parallel prophecy: Jesus foretells that the sun will be darkened, the moon will not give its brightness and the stars will fall. The fourth trumpet darkens sun, moon and stars partially; Matthew has the stars fall rather than dim.
    Matthew 24:29 Immediately after the affliction of those days, the sun will be darkened, the moon will not give its brightness, the stars will fall from heaven, and the powers of the heavens will be shaken.

Revelation 8:13
And I saw, and heard one eagle flying in midheaven saying with a loud voice, “Woe, woe, woe to those dwelling upon the earth, because of the remaining voices of the trumpet of the three angels who are about to sound!”
  Revelation 9:12 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:12 announces “The one woe hath passed” with two woes still to come, counting off the threefold “Woe, woe, woe” proclaimed here for the remaining trumpets.
    Revelation 9:12 The one woe hath passed. Behold, two woes are still coming after these things.
  Revelation 11:14 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 11:14 announces that the second woe has passed and “the third woe is coming quickly,” continuing the series of three woes announced by the eagle.
    Revelation 11:14 The second woe hath passed. Behold, the third woe is coming quickly.
  Revelation 14:6 — shared image: An angel is seen “flying in midheaven” to address those on the earth, as the eagle flies “in midheaven” crying to “those dwelling upon the earth.” The angel brings eternal good news, the eagle a message of woe.
    Revelation 14:6 And I saw another angel flying in midheaven, having eternal good news to proclaim to those seated upon the earth and to every nation and tribe and tongue and people,

Revelation 9:1
And the fifth angel sounded, and I saw a star fallen from heaven to the earth, and the key of the shaft of the abyss was given to him.
  Revelation 20:1 — same object: Revelation 20:1 shows an angel coming down from heaven “having the key of the abyss,” the same key given here to the fallen star. In chapter 20 the key is used to bind Satan.
    Revelation 20:1 And I saw an angel coming down from heaven, having the key of the abyss and a great chain upon his hand.
  Revelation 9:2 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:2 continues the scene: the one given the key “opened the shaft of the abyss,” releasing smoke that darkens the sun.
    Revelation 9:2 And he opened the shaft of the abyss, and smoke went up from the shaft like smoke of a great furnace, and the sun and the air were darkened from the smoke of the shaft.
  Revelation 8:10 — parallel image: At the third trumpet “a great star fell from heaven,” as at the fifth trumpet John sees “a star fallen from heaven.” The third-trumpet star falls on waters; this one receives the key of the abyss.
    Revelation 8:10 And the third angel sounded, and a great star fell from heaven, burning like a lamp, and fell upon a third of the rivers and upon the springs of the waters.

Revelation 9:2
And he opened the shaft of the abyss, and smoke went up from the shaft like smoke of a great furnace, and the sun and the air were darkened from the smoke of the shaft.
  Genesis 19:28 — shared image: Abraham sees a flame rising from the land of Sodom “as the vapour of a furnace.” Revelation describes smoke going up from the abyss “like smoke of a great furnace,” a similar comparison applied to a different source.
    Genesis 19:28 And he looked toward Sodom and Gomorrah, and toward the land of the surrounding country; and he saw, and behold, a flame rose from the earth as the vapour of a furnace.
  Joel 2:10 — shared cosmic sign: Joel describes the locust army's day with “the sun and moon will grow dark.” Here the smoke that releases the locusts darkens “the sun and the air”; Joel does not attribute the darkening to smoke.
    Joel 2:10 Before them the earth will be thrown into turmoil and heaven shaken; the sun and moon will grow dark, and the stars will sink their brightness.

Revelation 9:3
And from the smoke locusts went out to the earth, and authority was given to them as the scorpions of the earth have authority.
  Joel 1:4 — shared image: Joel describes a devouring plague of locusts sent as judgment. Revelation's locusts come from the smoke of the abyss and are given authority like scorpions, unlike Joel's crop-devouring insects.
    Joel 1:4 What the caterpillar left, the locust devoured; what the locust left, the bruchus locust devoured; what the bruchus locust left, the blight devoured.
  Revelation 9:10–9:11 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:10 explains the scorpion-like authority: the locusts “have tails like scorpions and stings,” and in their tails is their authority to harm people.
    Revelation 9:10 And they have tails like scorpions and stings, and in their tails is their authority to harm people for five months.
    Revelation 9:11 They have as king over them the angel of the abyss. His name in Hebrew is Abaddon, and in Greek he hath the name Apollyon.

Revelation 9:4
And they were told not to harm the grass of the earth or any green thing or any tree, but only the people who do not have the seal of God upon their foreheads.
  Revelation 7:2–7:4 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 7:3 commands that the earth, sea and trees not be harmed until God's slaves are sealed “upon their foreheads.” Here the locusts may harm only those who lack that seal.
    Revelation 7:2 And I saw another angel coming up from the rising of the sun, having a seal of the living God, and he cried with a loud voice to the four angels who were given to harm the earth and the sea,
    Revelation 7:3 saying, “Do not harm the earth or the sea or the trees until we seal the slaves of our God upon their foreheads.”
    Revelation 7:4 And I heard the number of those sealed: a hundred forty-four thousand, sealed from every tribe of the sons of Israel.
  Ezekiel 9:4 — verbal allusion: In Ezekiel 9:4 a sign is put “upon the foreheads” of the faithful before judgment. Revelation's locusts harm only those without “the seal of God upon their foreheads.”
    Ezekiel 9:4 and said to him, “Pass through the midst of Jerusalem and put the sign upon the foreheads of the men who sigh deeply and grieve over all the lawlessness being committed within her.”
  Ezekiel 9:6 — shared protective sign: Ezekiel's executioners are told, “do not approach anyone upon whom the sign is.” The locusts are likewise restrained from those bearing God's seal; Ezekiel's judgment kills, whereas the locusts torment.
    Ezekiel 9:6 Old man, young man, maiden, infants and women—kill them to obliteration. But do not approach anyone upon whom the sign is. Begin at My holy places.” They began with the elderly men who were inside the house.

Revelation 9:5
And it was given to them not to kill them, but that they should be tormented for five months; and their torment was like the torment of a scorpion when it striketh a human being.
  Revelation 9:10 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:10 restates the locusts' authority “to harm people for five months” and locates their sting in scorpion-like tails, matching the five months of scorpion-like torment here.
    Revelation 9:10 And they have tails like scorpions and stings, and in their tails is their authority to harm people for five months.
  Job 2:6 — shared limit on harm: The Lord permits the devil to harm Job but says “only preserve his life.” The locusts are likewise permitted to torment but “not to kill.”
    Job 2:6 The Lord said to the devil, “Behold, I hand him over to you; only preserve his life.”

Revelation 9:6
And in those days people will seek death and will certainly not find it, and will desire to die, and death fleeth from them.
  Job 3:20–3:22 — verbal parallel: Job speaks of those “who long for death and do not obtain it,” digging for it as for treasure. Revelation describes people who “will seek death and will certainly not find it.” Job speaks of sufferers in general, not a period of judgment.
    Job 3:20 For why has light been given to those in bitterness, and life to souls in pain,
    Job 3:21 who long for death and do not obtain it, digging for it as for treasures,
    Job 3:22 and become overjoyed if they obtain it?

Revelation 9:7
And the likenesses of the locusts were like horses prepared for war, and upon their heads were things like crowns resembling gold, and their faces were like human faces,
  Joel 2:4–2:5 — shared image: Joel's locust army has an “appearance … like the sight of horses” and is “arrayed for war.” Revelation's locusts are “like horses prepared for war”; Joel does not mention crowns or human faces.
    Joel 2:4 Their appearance is like the sight of horses; like riders they will pursue.
    Joel 2:5 With a sound like chariots they will leap upon mountain peaks, like the sound of a fiery flame devouring stubble, like a numerous and strong people arrayed for war.

Revelation 9:8
and they had hair like women’s hair, and their teeth were like lions’ teeth,
  Joel 1:6 — verbal allusion: Joel says of the invading locust nation, “Its teeth are a lion's teeth.” Revelation's locusts likewise have teeth “like lions' teeth”; Joel does not mention hair.
    Joel 1:6 For a nation has come up against My land, strong and uncountable. Its teeth are a lion’s teeth, and its grinding teeth a young lion’s.

Revelation 9:9
and they had breastplates like iron breastplates, and the sound of their wings was like the sound of chariots of many horses running to war.
  Joel 2:5–2:8 — verbal allusion: Joel's locusts move “with a sound like chariots” and are “arrayed for war.” Revelation compares the sound of the locusts' wings to “chariots of many horses running to war”; Joel does not mention breastplates.
    Joel 2:5 With a sound like chariots they will leap upon mountain peaks, like the sound of a fiery flame devouring stubble, like a numerous and strong people arrayed for war.
    Joel 2:6 Before it peoples will be crushed; every face is like the scorching of a pot.
    Joel 2:7 Like fighters they will run, and like soldiers they will climb the walls. Each will go on his own road, and they certainly will not turn aside from their paths,
    Joel 2:8 and no one will hold back from his brother. Burdened with their weapons they will go, and among their missiles they will fall, yet they will certainly not be destroyed.
  Revelation 9:17 — internal cross-reference: The cavalry of the sixth trumpet also wear breastplates, described as “fiery red and hyacinth blue and sulfur yellow,” paralleling the locusts' breastplates “like iron breastplates.”
    Revelation 9:17 And thus I saw the horses in the vision and those sitting upon them, having breastplates of fiery red and hyacinth blue and sulfur yellow; and the heads of the horses were like lions’ heads, and from their mouths come out fire and smoke and sulfur.

Revelation 9:10
And they have tails like scorpions and stings, and in their tails is their authority to harm people for five months.
  Revelation 9:5 — internal cross-reference: Both verses describe the locusts’ power to hurt people for “five months” and compare it to a scorpion. Revelation 9:5 adds that they are not allowed to kill, only to torment.
    Revelation 9:5 And it was given to them not to kill them, but that they should be tormented for five months; and their torment was like the torment of a scorpion when it striketh a human being.
  Revelation 9:3 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:3 introduces the locusts as given “authority” like that of “the scorpions of the earth”. Revelation 9:10 locates this authority in their scorpion-like tails.
    Revelation 9:3 And from the smoke locusts went out to the earth, and authority was given to them as the scorpions of the earth have authority.
  Revelation 9:19 — verbal parallel: Both verses say the creatures’ “authority” is “in their tails” and that with them they “harm”. Revelation 9:19 speaks of the horses of the sixth trumpet, whose tails are like snakes, not of the scorpion-like locusts.
    Revelation 9:19 For the authority of the horses is in their mouths and in their tails, for their tails are like snakes, having heads, and with them they harm.

Revelation 9:11
They have as king over them the angel of the abyss. His name in Hebrew is Abaddon, and in Greek he hath the name Apollyon.
  Revelation 9:1–9:2 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:1–2 opens “the shaft of the abyss”, from which the locusts come. Revelation 9:11 names their king as “the angel of the abyss”. The earlier passage does not say whether the fallen star is the same figure as this angel.
    Revelation 9:1 And the fifth angel sounded, and I saw a star fallen from heaven to the earth, and the key of the shaft of the abyss was given to him.
    Revelation 9:2 And he opened the shaft of the abyss, and smoke went up from the shaft like smoke of a great furnace, and the sun and the air were darkened from the smoke of the shaft.

Revelation 9:12
The one woe hath passed. Behold, two woes are still coming after these things.
  Revelation 11:14 — internal cross-reference: Both verses use the same formula to mark the series of woes: “The one woe hath passed” and “The second woe hath passed”, each followed by “Behold” and an announcement of what is still to come.
    Revelation 11:14 The second woe hath passed. Behold, the third woe is coming quickly.
  Revelation 8:13–9:2 — internal cross-reference: The eagle’s triple “Woe, woe, woe” in Revelation 8:13 announces the three woes tied to the last trumpets. Revelation 9:12 marks the end of the first of these, which followed the fifth angel’s trumpet in 9:1–2.
    Revelation 8:13 And I saw, and heard one eagle flying in midheaven saying with a loud voice, “Woe, woe, woe to those dwelling upon the earth, because of the remaining voices of the trumpet of the three angels who are about to sound!”
    Revelation 9:1 And the fifth angel sounded, and I saw a star fallen from heaven to the earth, and the key of the shaft of the abyss was given to him.
    Revelation 9:2 And he opened the shaft of the abyss, and smoke went up from the shaft like smoke of a great furnace, and the sun and the air were darkened from the smoke of the shaft.

Revelation 9:13
And the sixth angel sounded, and I heard one voice from the horns of the golden altar before God,
  Revelation 8:3–8:5 — internal cross-reference: Both passages name “the golden altar” before God. In Revelation 8:3–5 the saints’ prayers are offered on it and fire from it is thrown to the earth before the trumpets begin; in 9:13 a voice comes from its horns.
    Revelation 8:3 And another angel came and stood at the altar, having a golden censer, and much incense was given to him to offer with the prayers of all the saints upon the golden altar before the throne.
    Revelation 8:4 And the smoke of the incense went up with the prayers of the saints from the angel’s hand before God.
    Revelation 8:5 And the angel took the censer and filled it from the fire of the altar, and threw it to the earth; and there were thunders and voices and lightnings and an earthquake.
  Exodus 30:2–30:3 — shared image: Exodus 30:2–3 prescribes an object with “horns of one piece with it”, all overlaid “with pure gold”. Revelation 9:13 speaks of “the horns of the golden altar”. The verses shown do not themselves name the object, which is introduced in the verse before them.
    Exodus 30:2 It shall be square, a cubit long and a cubit wide, and two cubits high, with horns of one piece with it.
    Exodus 30:3 You shall overlay its grating, all its walls, and its horns with pure gold, and make a twisted gold rim around it.

Revelation 9:14
saying to the sixth angel, the one who hath the trumpet, “Loose the four angels bound at the great river Euphrates.”
  Revelation 9:15 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:15 carries out the command of 9:14: “the four angels” are “loosed” and are said to have been prepared for a set hour so that they might kill a third of the people.
    Revelation 9:15 And the four angels prepared for the hour and day and month and year were loosed, so that they might kill a third of the people.
  Revelation 16:12 — internal cross-reference: Both the sixth trumpet and the sixth bowl are directed at “the great river Euphrates”. In 16:12 the river is dried up to prepare the way of kings from the east; in 9:14 four angels are bound there.
    Revelation 16:12 And the sixth poured out his bowl upon the great river Euphrates, and its water was dried up, so that the way of the kings from the rising of the sun might be prepared.

Revelation 9:15
And the four angels prepared for the hour and day and month and year were loosed, so that they might kill a third of the people.
  Revelation 9:18 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:18 reports the outcome of 9:15: “a third of the people were killed”, using the same wording as the purpose given for the release of the four angels.
    Revelation 9:18 From these three plagues a third of the people were killed: from the fire and the smoke and the sulfur coming out of their mouths.

Revelation 9:16
And the number of the armies of the cavalry was two myriads of myriads; I heard their number.
  Revelation 5:11 — verbal parallel: Both verses give a number of “myriads of myriads”. In Revelation 5:11 they are angels around the throne; here they are mounted armies.
    Revelation 5:11 And I saw, and heard a voice of many angels around the throne and the living creatures and the elders; and their number was myriads of myriads and thousands of thousands,
  Daniel 7:10 — shared image: Daniel 7:10 counts “ten thousand times ten thousand” standing before the Ancient One, a figure close to Revelation’s “two myriads of myriads”. Daniel’s host attends a heavenly court, not a cavalry army.
    Daniel 7:10 and a river of fire came out before Him. A thousand thousands attended Him, and ten thousand times ten thousand stood beside Him. A court sat, and books were opened.
  Revelation 7:4 — verbal parallel: In both verses the seer reports a number he heard: “I heard the number” in 7:4 and “I heard their number” in 9:16. Revelation 7:4 gives the number of those sealed; 9:16 the number of the cavalry.
    Revelation 7:4 And I heard the number of those sealed: a hundred forty-four thousand, sealed from every tribe of the sons of Israel.

Revelation 9:17
And thus I saw the horses in the vision and those sitting upon them, having breastplates of fiery red and hyacinth blue and sulfur yellow; and the heads of the horses were like lions’ heads, and from their mouths come out fire and smoke and sulfur.
  Revelation 9:18 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:18 repeats “fire and smoke and sulfur” coming out of the horses’ mouths and calls them three plagues that killed a third of the people.
    Revelation 9:18 From these three plagues a third of the people were killed: from the fire and the smoke and the sulfur coming out of their mouths.
  Revelation 9:9 — internal cross-reference: Both the locusts of the fifth trumpet and the cavalry of the sixth are described as wearing “breastplates”. The locusts’ breastplates are “like iron breastplates”; the riders’ are fiery red, hyacinth blue and sulfur yellow.
    Revelation 9:9 and they had breastplates like iron breastplates, and the sound of their wings was like the sound of chariots of many horses running to war.

Revelation 9:18
From these three plagues a third of the people were killed: from the fire and the smoke and the sulfur coming out of their mouths.
  Revelation 9:17 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:17 describes the “fire and smoke and sulfur” coming from the horses’ mouths, which 9:18 identifies as the three plagues.
    Revelation 9:17 And thus I saw the horses in the vision and those sitting upon them, having breastplates of fiery red and hyacinth blue and sulfur yellow; and the heads of the horses were like lions’ heads, and from their mouths come out fire and smoke and sulfur.
  Revelation 9:15 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:15 states the purpose of the four angels’ release, “so that they might kill a third of the people”. Revelation 9:18 reports that this happened.
    Revelation 9:15 And the four angels prepared for the hour and day and month and year were loosed, so that they might kill a third of the people.

Revelation 9:19
For the authority of the horses is in their mouths and in their tails, for their tails are like snakes, having heads, and with them they harm.
  Revelation 9:10 — verbal parallel: Both verses place the creatures’ “authority” “in their tails” and say they “harm” with them. Revelation 9:10 concerns the scorpion-tailed locusts; 9:19 the horses whose tails are like snakes with heads.
    Revelation 9:10 And they have tails like scorpions and stings, and in their tails is their authority to harm people for five months.

Revelation 9:20
And the rest of the people, who were not killed by these plagues, did not even repent of the works of their hands, so as not to worship the demons and the idols of gold and silver and bronze and stone and wood, which can neither see nor hear nor walk.
  Revelation 9:21 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:21 continues the same sentence of judgment: those who survived the plagues “did not repent” of their murders, sorceries, sexual immorality or thefts, as well as of their idolatry.
    Revelation 9:21 And they did not repent of their murders or their sorcerous potions or their sexual immorality or their thefts.
  Jeremiah 1:16 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah 1:16 condemns those who “bowed down to the works of their hands”. Revelation 9:20 uses the same phrase, “the works of their hands”, for the idols that the survivors kept worshipping.
    Jeremiah 1:16 I will speak to them with judgment concerning all their evil, because they abandoned Me, sacrificed to foreign gods and bowed down to the works of their hands.
  Daniel 5:23 — shared theme: Daniel 5:23 rebukes a king who “praised all the idols made by human hands” but did not bless the living God. The Daniel text shown does not list the idols’ materials or say they cannot see or hear, as Revelation 9:20 does.
    Daniel 5:23 O king, you held a banquet for your friends and drank wine. The vessels of the living God’s house were brought to you, and you and your magnates drank from them and praised all the idols made by human hands. But you did not bless the living God. Your breath is in His hand, and He gave you your kingdom, yet you neither blessed Him nor offered Him praise.

Revelation 9:21
And they did not repent of their murders or their sorcerous potions or their sexual immorality or their thefts.
  Revelation 21:8 — shared vice list: Revelation 21:8 names “murderers”, the “sexually immoral” and “sorcerers” among those whose share is in the lake of fire. These are three of the four sins of which the survivors in 9:21 do not repent.
    Revelation 21:8 But as for the cowardly and unbelieving and abominable and murderers and sexually immoral and sorcerers and idolaters and all liars, their share is in the lake burning with fire and sulfur, which is the second death.”
  Revelation 22:15 — shared vice list: Revelation 22:15 places “the sorcerers and the sexually immoral and the murderers” outside the city. These match three of the four sins named in 9:21.
    Revelation 22:15 Outside are the dogs and the sorcerers and the sexually immoral and the murderers and the idolaters and everyone loving and practicing falsehood.
  Matthew 15:19 — shared vice list: Matthew 15:19 lists “murders”, “sexual immoralities” and “thefts” among the evils that come out of the heart. Three of the four sins in Revelation 9:21 appear, but sorcery does not.
    Matthew 15:19 For out of the heart come evil reasonings, murders, adulteries, sexual immoralities, thefts, false testimonies, and blasphemies.

Revelation 10:1
And I saw another mighty angel coming down from heaven, clothed with a cloud, and the rainbow upon his head, and his face like the sun, and his feet like pillars of fire.
  Revelation 5:2 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 10:1 introduces “another mighty angel”, which recalls the “mighty angel” of 5:2 who asks who is worthy to open the scroll.
    Revelation 5:2 And I saw a mighty angel proclaiming in a loud voice, “Who is worthy to open the scroll and loose its seals?”
  Ezekiel 1:28 — shared image: Ezekiel compares the brightness around the Lord’s glory to “a bow when it is in the cloud”. Revelation’s angel is “clothed with a cloud” and has “the rainbow upon his head”.
    Ezekiel 1:28 Like the appearance of a bow when it is in the cloud on a rainy day, so was the arrangement of the brightness all around. This was the appearance of the likeness of the Lord’s glory. I saw it, and I fall upon my face, and I heard the voice of One speaking.
  Revelation 1:15–1:16 — parallel vision: Both descriptions give the figure a face or appearance “like the sun” and describe his feet: “like burnished bronze” in 1:15 and “like pillars of fire” in 10:1. In chapter 1 the figure is the risen Christ; in chapter 10 he is called an angel.
    Revelation 1:15 and His feet like burnished bronze, as refined in a furnace, and His voice like the sound of many waters.
    Revelation 1:16 And He held seven stars in His right hand, and from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out, and His appearance was like the sun shining in its strength.

Revelation 10:2
And he had in his hand a little scroll opened. And he placed his right foot upon the sea and his left upon the earth,
  Revelation 10:8–10:10 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 10:8–10 returns to “the opened scroll” in the hand of the angel “standing upon the sea and upon the earth”. There the seer takes the little scroll and eats it.
    Revelation 10:8 And the voice that I heard from heaven spoke with me again, saying, “Go, take the opened scroll in the hand of the angel standing upon the sea and upon the earth.”
    Revelation 10:9 And I went to the angel, telling him to give me the little scroll. And he saith unto me, “Take and devour it. It shall make thy belly bitter, but in thy mouth it shall be sweet as honey.”
    Revelation 10:10 And I took the little scroll from the angel’s hand and devoured it, and in my mouth it was sweet as honey; and when I ate it, my belly was made bitter.
  Revelation 10:5 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 10:5 again describes the angel as “standing upon the sea and upon the earth”, the stance set up in 10:2.
    Revelation 10:5 And the angel whom I saw standing upon the sea and upon the earth raised his right hand to heaven,
  Ezekiel 2:9–2:10 — shared image: In Ezekiel a hand holds out “a scroll of a book” and unrolls it before the prophet; Revelation’s angel holds “a little scroll opened”. Ezekiel’s scroll is written on the back and the front.
    Ezekiel 2:9 I looked, and behold, a hand was stretched toward me, and in it was a scroll of a book;
    Ezekiel 2:10 and He unrolled it before me. It had writing on the back and the front, and upon it were written a lament, a song and woe.

Revelation 10:3
and cried with a loud voice just as a lion roareth. And when he cried, the seven thunders spoke their own voices.
  Amos 3:8 — shared image: Amos 3:8 sets a lion’s roar beside the Lord’s speaking, and Revelation 10:3 compares the angel’s loud cry to a lion that roars. In Revelation the cry is followed by the seven thunders speaking their own voices.
    Amos 3:8 A lion will roar—and who will not fear? The Lord God has spoken—and who will not prophesy?
  Isaiah 31:4 — shared image: Isaiah compares the Lord to a lion that “cries over prey”, filling the mountains with its voice. Revelation 10:3 likewise says the angel “cried with a loud voice just as a lion roareth”.
    Isaiah 31:4 For thus the Lord said to me: “As a lion or lion cub cries over prey it has caught and shouts over it until the mountains fill with its voice, and they are defeated and terrified by the greatness of its wrath, so the Lord Sabaoth will descend to march upon Mount Zion, upon her mountains.

Revelation 10:4
And when the seven thunders spoke, I was about to write; and I heard a voice from heaven saying, “Seal up what the seven thunders spoke, and do not write it.”
  Daniel 12:4 — verbal allusion: Daniel is told to “seal the book until the time of completion”, and the seer is told to “Seal up” what the seven thunders spoke. In Revelation the seer is also forbidden to write the words at all.
    Daniel 12:4 But you, Daniel, cover the commands and seal the book until the time of completion, until the many go mad and the earth is filled with injustice.”
  Revelation 22:10 — contrasting instruction: Revelation 22:10 reverses the command of 10:4: “Do not seal the words of the prophecy of this scroll, for the time is near.” The thunders’ words stay sealed, while the book of the prophecy itself is left open.
    Revelation 22:10 And he saith unto me, “Do not seal the words of the prophecy of this scroll, for the time is near.
  Daniel 12:9 — verbal allusion: Daniel 12:9 says “the commands are covered and sealed” until a set time. This is another Danielic instance of sealing revelation, the action commanded in Revelation 10:4.
    Daniel 12:9 He said to me, “Go away, Daniel, for the commands are covered and sealed until

Revelation 10:5
And the angel whom I saw standing upon the sea and upon the earth raised his right hand to heaven,
  Daniel 12:7 — verbal allusion: Daniel’s figure raises his hands “to heaven” and swears by God who lives for ever; Revelation’s angel, standing upon the sea and upon the earth, raises his right hand “to heaven”. Daniel’s figure raises both right and left hands.
    Daniel 12:7 I heard the one clothed in fine linen above the river’s water say, “Until the time of completion.” He raised his right hand and his left to heaven and swore by God who lives for ever that in a time, times, and half a time would be the completion of the “hands of release” of a holy people, and all these things would be completed.
  Deuteronomy 32:40 — verbal allusion: In Deuteronomy the Lord says, “I will lift My hand unto heaven, and swear”, together with “I live for ever”. Revelation 10:5–6 combines the same raised hand with an oath by the One who lives for ever.
    Deuteronomy 32:40 For I will lift My hand unto heaven, and swear by My right hand, and say, I live for ever.
  Revelation 10:2 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 10:2 sets the angel’s right foot on the sea and his left on the earth, the stance behind the phrase “standing upon the sea and upon the earth” in 10:5.
    Revelation 10:2 And he had in his hand a little scroll opened. And he placed his right foot upon the sea and his left upon the earth,

Revelation 10:6
and swore by the One living for the ages of the ages, who created heaven and the things in it, and the earth and the things in it, and the sea and the things in it, that there will be no more delay.
  Revelation 4:9 — same divine title: Both verses call God “the One living for the ages of the ages”. Revelation 4:9 uses it of the One sitting upon the throne; 10:6 uses it in the angel’s oath.
    Revelation 4:9 And whenever the living creatures give glory and honour and thanksgiving to the One sitting upon the throne, the One living for the ages of the ages,
  Revelation 4:11 — shared theme: Revelation 4:11 praises God because He “didst create all things”. The oath in 10:6 identifies Him at length as the One who created heaven, earth and sea and the things in them.
    Revelation 4:11 “Thou art worthy, our Lord and God, to receive the glory and the honour and the power, because Thou didst create all things, and because of Thy will they existed and were created.”

Revelation 10:7
But in the days of the voice of the seventh angel, when he is about to sound, the mystery of God will be completed, as He announced the good news to His own slaves, the prophets.
  Revelation 11:15–11:18 — fulfilment of announcement: Revelation 10:7 points to the days when the seventh angel is about to sound. In 11:15–18 “the seventh angel sounded”, God is said to have reigned, and the reward is given to “Thy slaves the prophets”.
    Revelation 11:15 And the seventh angel sounded, and loud voices arose in heaven, saying, “The kingdom of the world hath become the kingdom of our Lord and of His Christ, and He will reign for the ages of the ages.”
    Revelation 11:16 And the twenty-four elders sitting upon their thrones before God fell upon their faces and worshipped God,
    Revelation 11:17 saying, “We give Thee thanks, Lord God Almighty, the One who is and who was, because Thou hast taken Thy great power and reigned.
    Revelation 11:18 And the nations were enraged, and Thy wrath came, and the time for the dead to be judged, and to give the reward to Thy slaves the prophets and to the saints and to those who fear Thy name, the small and the great, and to destroy those destroying the earth.”
  Amos 3:7 — verbal allusion: Amos 3:7 says the Lord does nothing without revealing it “to His servants the prophets”. Revelation 10:7 says God announced His mystery “to His own slaves, the prophets”.
    Amos 3:7 For the Lord God will certainly do no deed unless He reveals His discipline to His servants the prophets.

Revelation 10:8
And the voice that I heard from heaven spoke with me again, saying, “Go, take the opened scroll in the hand of the angel standing upon the sea and upon the earth.”
  Revelation 10:2 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 10:2 first shows the angel with “a little scroll opened” in his hand, his feet upon the sea and the earth. In 10:8 the seer is told to take that scroll.
    Revelation 10:2 And he had in his hand a little scroll opened. And he placed his right foot upon the sea and his left upon the earth,
  Revelation 10:4–10:5 — internal cross-reference: The “voice from heaven” that forbade writing in 10:4 speaks “again” in 10:8. Revelation 10:5 gives the description of the angel “standing upon the sea and upon the earth” that 10:8 repeats.
    Revelation 10:4 And when the seven thunders spoke, I was about to write; and I heard a voice from heaven saying, “Seal up what the seven thunders spoke, and do not write it.”
    Revelation 10:5 And the angel whom I saw standing upon the sea and upon the earth raised his right hand to heaven,

Revelation 10:9
And I went to the angel, telling him to give me the little scroll. And he saith unto me, “Take and devour it. It shall make thy belly bitter, but in thy mouth it shall be sweet as honey.”
  Ezekiel 3:1–3:3 — verbal allusion: Ezekiel is told to “eat this scroll”, his belly is to be filled with it, and in his mouth it is “like sweet honey”. Revelation 10:9 takes up the scroll, the eating, the belly and sweetness “as honey” in the mouth, and adds that it will make the belly bitter.
    Ezekiel 3:1 He said to me, “Son of man, eat this scroll, then go and speak to the sons of Israel.”
    Ezekiel 3:2 I opened my mouth wide, and He fed me the scroll.
    Ezekiel 3:3 He said to me, “Son of man, your mouth will eat, and your belly will be filled with this scroll that has been given to you.” I ate it, and in my mouth it became like sweet honey.
  Ezekiel 2:8 — verbal allusion: Ezekiel 2:8 tells the prophet to “Open your mouth wide and eat what I give you”. In Revelation 10:9 the angel tells the seer, of the little scroll, “Take and devour it”.
    Ezekiel 2:8 And you, son of man, hear the One speaking to you. Do not become embittering like the embittering house. Open your mouth wide and eat what I give you.”

Revelation 10:10
And I took the little scroll from the angel’s hand and devoured it, and in my mouth it was sweet as honey; and when I ate it, my belly was made bitter.
  Ezekiel 3:3 — verbal allusion: Both prophets eat a scroll given to them, and in Ezekiel’s words it was “in my mouth” “like sweet honey”, as in Revelation’s “in my mouth it was sweet as honey”. Ezekiel 3:3 mentions the belly being filled but not made bitter.
    Ezekiel 3:3 He said to me, “Son of man, your mouth will eat, and your belly will be filled with this scroll that has been given to you.” I ate it, and in my mouth it became like sweet honey.
  Psalms 118:103 — shared image: The psalmist says God’s sayings are sweet “beyond honey” to “my mouth”. Revelation 10:10 gives the same sweetness in the mouth to the scroll the seer eats.
    Psalms 118:103 How sweet are Thy sayings to my throat, beyond honey and honeycomb to my mouth!

Revelation 10:11
And they say unto me, “Thou must prophesy again concerning peoples and nations and tongues and many kings.”
  Jeremiah 1:9–1:10 — shared commission: Jeremiah is given the Lord’s words in his mouth and appointed “over nations and kingdoms”. Revelation 10:11 commissions the seer to prophesy concerning “peoples and nations and tongues and many kings”.
    Jeremiah 1:9 The Lord stretched out His hand toward me and touched my mouth. The Lord said to me, Behold, I have put My words into your mouth.
    Jeremiah 1:10 Behold, today I have appointed you over nations and kingdoms, to uproot, demolish, destroy, build and plant.
  Revelation 11:9 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 11:9 uses a similar group of terms, “the peoples and tribes and tongues and nations”, for those who look on the dead bodies; 10:11 has “peoples and nations and tongues and many kings”.
    Revelation 11:9 And people from the peoples and tribes and tongues and nations look at their dead body for three and a half days, and do not allow their dead bodies to be placed in a tomb.
  Revelation 14:6 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 14:6 sends a message to “every nation and tribe and tongue and people”. This is a proclamation to the same universal audience as in 10:11.
    Revelation 14:6 And I saw another angel flying in midheaven, having eternal good news to proclaim to those seated upon the earth and to every nation and tribe and tongue and people,

Revelation 11:1
And a reed like a rod was given to me, with the words, “Rise and measure the temple of God and the altar and those worshipping in it.
  Ezekiel 42:15–42:20 — shared image: Ezekiel’s guide measures the house all around “with the measuring reed”, “to distinguish between the holy areas and the forewall”. Revelation 11:1 gives the seer a reed to measure the temple of God, and 11:2 excludes the outer court.
    Ezekiel 42:15 The measurement of the house inside was completed. He brought me out by the gate facing east and measured the plan of the house all around in its arrangement.
    Ezekiel 42:16 He stood behind the gate facing east and measured five hundred with the measuring reed.
    Ezekiel 42:17 He turned toward the north and measured the north-facing side: five hundred cubits with the measuring reed.
    Ezekiel 42:18 He turned toward the sea and measured the sea-facing side: five hundred with the measuring reed.
    Ezekiel 42:19 He turned toward the south and measured opposite the south: five hundred with the measuring reed.
    Ezekiel 42:20 The four sides were measured with the same reed. He laid it out, with their enclosure all around: five hundred toward the east and five hundred cubits in breadth, to distinguish between the holy areas and the forewall in the arrangement of the house.
  Revelation 21:15 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 21:15 has a “golden measuring reed” used to measure the city, its gates and its wall. In 11:1 the seer himself is given a reed to measure the temple, altar and worshippers.
    Revelation 21:15 And the one speaking with me had a golden measuring reed, to measure the city and its gates and its wall.
  Zechariah 2:5–2:6 — shared image: Zechariah sees a man with “a land-measuring cord” going “To measure Jerusalem”. Revelation 11:1 commands the measuring of God’s temple with a reed rather than a cord.
    Zechariah 2:5 I lifted my eyes and saw: look, a man with a land-measuring cord in his hand!
    Zechariah 2:6 I said to him, “Where are you going?” He said to me, “To measure Jerusalem, to see how wide it is and how long.”

Revelation 11:2
And leave out the court outside the temple, and do not measure it, because it was given to the nations, and they will trample the holy city for forty-two months.
  Luke 21:24 — verbal allusion: Luke 21:24 says “Jerusalem will be trampled by nations” until the times of nations are fulfilled. Revelation 11:2 says the nations “will trample the holy city” for forty-two months. Revelation does not name the city here.
    Luke 21:24 They will fall by the edge of the sword and be led captive into all the nations. And Jerusalem will be trampled by nations until the times of nations are fulfilled.
  Revelation 11:3 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 11:3 sets the witnesses’ prophesying at “a thousand two hundred sixty days”. This is a period stated in days next to the “forty-two months” of the trampling in 11:2.
    Revelation 11:3 And I will grant authority to My two witnesses, and they will prophesy for a thousand two hundred sixty days, clothed in sackcloth.”
  Daniel 7:25 — shared theme: In Daniel 7:25 the one who will “wear down the holy ones of the Most High” has everything handed into his hands “for a time, times, and half a time”. Revelation 11:2 gives a set period, forty-two months, for the nations to trample the holy city. Neither text states that the two periods are equal.
    Daniel 7:25 He will speak words against the Most High, wear down the holy ones of the Most High, and expect to alter seasons and law. Everything will be handed into his hands for a time, times, and half a time.

Revelation 11:3
And I will grant authority to My two witnesses, and they will prophesy for a thousand two hundred sixty days, clothed in sackcloth.”
  Revelation 12:6 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 12:6 uses the same period, “a thousand two hundred sixty days”, for the time the woman is nourished in the wilderness.
    Revelation 12:6 And the woman fled into the wilderness, where she hath a place prepared by God, so that there they may nourish her for a thousand two hundred sixty days.
  Deuteronomy 19:15 — background: Deuteronomy 19:15 requires testimony “At the mouth of two witnesses”. Revelation 11:3 sends exactly “two witnesses”, though Revelation does not cite the legal rule.
    Deuteronomy 19:15 One witness shall not suffice to testify against a man concerning any injustice, or any trespass, or any sin which he sins. At the mouth of two witnesses, and at the mouth of three witnesses, shall every matter be established.
  Revelation 11:2 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 11:2 gives the nations “forty-two months” to trample the holy city, a period stated in months next to the 1,260 days of the witnesses’ prophesying.
    Revelation 11:2 And leave out the court outside the temple, and do not measure it, because it was given to the nations, and they will trample the holy city for forty-two months.

Revelation 11:4
These are the two olive trees and the two lampstands standing before the Lord of the earth.
  Zechariah 4:11–4:14 — verbal allusion: Zechariah asks about “these two olive trees” and is told that they “stand beside the Lord of all the earth”. Revelation 11:4 calls the witnesses “the two olive trees” “standing before the Lord of the earth”.
    Zechariah 4:11 I answered him, “What are these two olive trees to the lampstand’s right and left?”
    Zechariah 4:12 I asked a second time, “What are the two olive branches in the hands of the two golden nozzles that pour upon and draw up the golden pouring channels?”
    Zechariah 4:13 He said to me, “Do you not know what these are?” I said, “No, lord.”
    Zechariah 4:14 He said, “These two sons of richness stand beside the Lord of all the earth.”
  Zechariah 4:2–4:3 — shared image: Zechariah’s vision sets “Two olive trees” beside a golden lampstand. Revelation pairs “two olive trees” with “two lampstands”, where Zechariah has a single lampstand.
    Zechariah 4:2 and said to me, “What do you see?” I said, “I see a lampstand entirely of gold, with its lamp-bowl above it, seven lamps upon it, and seven pouring channels for the lamps above it.
    Zechariah 4:3 Two olive trees stand over it, one to the right of the lamp-bowl and one to the left.”
  Revelation 1:20 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 1:20 explains the lampstands as “seven Churches”. This gives the book’s own key to lampstand imagery, although 11:4 has two lampstands, not seven.
    Revelation 1:20 The mystery of the seven stars thou sawest upon My right hand, and the seven golden lampstands: the seven stars are angels of the seven Churches, and the seven lampstands are seven Churches.”

Revelation 11:5
And if anyone wisheth to harm them, fire cometh out of their mouth and devoureth their enemies; and if anyone should wish to harm them, in this way he must be killed.
  Jeremiah 5:14 — verbal allusion: The Lord makes His words “fire in your mouth” to “devour” the people, and the witnesses have fire that “cometh out of their mouth and devoureth their enemies”.
    Jeremiah 5:14 Therefore thus says the Lord Almighty: Because you spoke this utterance, behold, I have made My words fire in your mouth, and this people wood, and it will devour them.
  2 Kings 1:10–1:12 — shared image: Elijah calls down fire that consumes the men sent against him, and the witnesses destroy those who would harm them with fire. Elijah’s fire comes “down from heaven”, while the witnesses’ fire comes out of their mouth.
    2 Kings 1:10 Elijah answered and said to the captain of fifty, ‘If I am a man of God, fire will come down from heaven and consume you and your fifty men.’ Fire came down from heaven and consumed him and his fifty men.
    2 Kings 1:11 The king again sent to him another captain of fifty with his fifty men. The captain went up and spoke to him, saying, ‘Man of God, thus says the king: Come down quickly!’
    2 Kings 1:12 Elijah answered and spoke to him, saying, ‘If I am a man of God, fire will come down from heaven and consume you and your fifty men.’ Fire came down from heaven and consumed him and his fifty men.

Revelation 11:6
These have authority to shut heaven, so that rain may not fall during the days of their prophecy; and they have authority over the waters to turn them into blood and to strike the earth with every plague as often as they wish.
  1 Kings 17:1 — shared image: Elijah declares there will be “neither dew nor rain” except at his word. The witnesses likewise “have authority to shut heaven, so that rain may not fall” during their prophecy.
    1 Kings 17:1 Elijah the prophet, the Tishbite from Thesbon in Gilead, said to Ahab, “As the Lord, the God of hosts, the God of Israel, lives, before whom I stand, there will be neither dew nor rain these years except through the utterance of my word.”
  Luke 4:25 — verbal parallel: Luke 4:25 recalls Elijah’s days “when heaven was shut”, which matches Revelation’s “authority to shut heaven”. Luke gives the drought’s length as three years and six months.
    Luke 4:25 But in truth I tell you, there were many widows in Israel in Elijah’s days, when heaven was shut for three years and six months and a great famine came over the whole land.
  1 Samuel 4:8 — verbal allusion: The Philistines recall the gods who “struck Egypt with every plague”. Revelation gives the witnesses power “to strike the earth with every plague”, alongside the Egyptian sign of turning waters into blood.
    1 Samuel 4:8 Woe to us! Who will rescue us from the hand of these powerful gods? These are the gods who struck Egypt with every plague and in the wilderness.

Revelation 11:7
And when they complete their testimony, the beast coming up from the abyss will make war with them and conquer them and kill them.
  Revelation 13:7 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 13:7 uses the same sequence, the beast is given “to make war with the saints and conquer them”. In 11:7 the beast does this to the two witnesses.
    Revelation 13:7 And it was given to it to make war with the saints and conquer them, and authority was given to it over every tribe and people and tongue and nation.
  Daniel 7:21–7:22 — verbal allusion: Daniel sees a horn “waging war against the holy ones and routing them”. Revelation 11:7 has the beast make war on the witnesses and conquer them. Daniel goes on to the Ancient of Days giving judgment for the holy ones.
    Daniel 7:21 I observed that horn waging war against the holy ones and routing them,
    Daniel 7:22 until the Ancient of Days came and gave judgment to the holy ones of the Most High. The time was given, and the holy ones took possession of the kingship.
  Revelation 17:6–17:8 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 17:8 describes the beast as “about to come up from the abyss”, the same origin given in 11:7.
    Revelation 17:6 And I saw the woman drunk from the blood of the saints and from the blood of the witnesses of Jesus. And seeing her, I marvelled with great amazement.
    Revelation 17:7 And the angel said unto me, “Why didst thou marvel? I will tell thee the mystery of the woman and of the beast carrying her, which hath the seven heads and the ten horns.
    Revelation 17:8 The beast thou sawest was and is not, and is about to come up from the abyss, and goeth into destruction. And those dwelling upon the earth, whose name hath not been written in the book of life from the foundation of the world, will marvel when they see the beast, because it was and is not and will be present.

Revelation 11:8
And their dead body lieth upon the broad street of the great city, which is spiritually called Sodom and Egypt, where also their Lord was crucified.
  Jeremiah 23:14 — shared image: Jeremiah says that Jerusalem’s prophets “all became like Sodom to Me”. Revelation calls the great city where the Lord was crucified “spiritually” Sodom. Revelation 11:8 does not name Jerusalem.
    Jeremiah 23:14 And among Jerusalem’s prophets I have seen horrifying things: committing adultery, walking in lies, and supporting evil hands so that no one turns from his evil way. They all became like Sodom to Me, and her inhabitants like Gomorrah.
  Luke 13:33–13:34 — shared theme: Jesus says “it is not possible for a prophet to perish outside Jerusalem”, a city that “kills the prophets”. Revelation sets the prophets’ dead bodies in the city “where also their Lord was crucified”.
    Luke 13:33 Nevertheless, I must travel today and tomorrow and the day following, because it is not possible for a prophet to perish outside Jerusalem.
    Luke 13:34 Jerusalem, Jerusalem, who kills the prophets and stones those sent to her! How often I wanted to gather your children as a bird gathers her own brood under her wings, and you were not willing!
  Revelation 11:9 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 11:9 continues the scene of 11:8: the peoples look at “their dead body” and do not let it be buried.
    Revelation 11:9 And people from the peoples and tribes and tongues and nations look at their dead body for three and a half days, and do not allow their dead bodies to be placed in a tomb.

Revelation 11:9
And people from the peoples and tribes and tongues and nations look at their dead body for three and a half days, and do not allow their dead bodies to be placed in a tomb.
  Revelation 11:11 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 11:11 picks up the same “three and a half days” and reports the witnesses brought back to life when they are over.
    Revelation 11:11 And after the three and a half days a spirit of life from God entered into them, and they stood upon their feet, and great fear fell upon those watching them.
  Psalms 78:2–78:3 — shared image: The psalm laments that the “dead bodies” of God’s servants lay exposed and “there was no one to bury them”. Revelation 11:9 says the onlookers “do not allow their dead bodies to be placed in a tomb”.
    Psalms 78:2 They made the dead bodies of Thy servants food for the birds of heaven, the flesh of Thy holy ones for the beasts of the earth.
    Psalms 78:3 They poured out their blood like water around Jerusalem, and there was no one to bury them.

Revelation 11:10
And those dwelling upon the earth rejoice over them and make merry, and will send gifts to one another, because these two prophets tormented those dwelling upon the earth.
  Revelation 11:5–11:6 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 11:5–6 describes the fire, drought and plagues by which the two prophets “tormented those dwelling upon the earth”, the reason for the celebration in 11:10.
    Revelation 11:5 And if anyone wisheth to harm them, fire cometh out of their mouth and devoureth their enemies; and if anyone should wish to harm them, in this way he must be killed.
    Revelation 11:6 These have authority to shut heaven, so that rain may not fall during the days of their prophecy; and they have authority over the waters to turn them into blood and to strike the earth with every plague as often as they wish.
  John 16:20 — shared theme: Jesus foretells that “the world will rejoice” while His disciples weep. Revelation 11:10 has those dwelling on the earth “rejoice” over the dead witnesses.
    John 16:20 Amen, amen, I say to you that you will weep and lament, but the world will rejoice. You will be sorrowful, but your sorrow will become joy.
  Micah 7:8 — shared theme: Micah tells the enemy, “Do not rejoice over me … because I have fallen! I will rise”. Revelation pictures enemies rejoicing over the fallen prophets, who rise again in 11:11.
    Micah 7:8 Do not rejoice over me, my enemy, because I have fallen! I will rise, for if I sit in darkness, the Lord will shine for me.

Revelation 11:11
And after the three and a half days a spirit of life from God entered into them, and they stood upon their feet, and great fear fell upon those watching them.
  Revelation 11:9 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 11:9 sets the period of “three and a half days” during which the dead bodies lie exposed. Revelation 11:11 marks its end.
    Revelation 11:9 And people from the peoples and tribes and tongues and nations look at their dead body for three and a half days, and do not allow their dead bodies to be placed in a tomb.
  Genesis 2:7 — verbal allusion: God breathes into the man “the breath of life” and he becomes a living soul. Revelation 11:11 has “a spirit of life from God” enter the dead witnesses so that they stand.
    Genesis 2:7 And God formed man from the dust of the earth, and breathed into his face the breath of life; and man became a living soul.

Revelation 11:12
And they heard a loud voice from heaven saying to them, “Come up here.” And they went up into heaven in the cloud, and their enemies watched them.
  Acts 1:9 — shared image: Jesus is lifted up “as they watched, and a cloud took Him”. The witnesses go up into heaven “in the cloud, and their enemies watched them”.
    Acts 1:9 After saying these things, He was lifted up as they watched, and a cloud took Him from their sight.
  Revelation 4:1 — verbal parallel: In both verses a heavenly voice calls, “Come up hither” or “Come up here”. In 4:1 the seer is summoned; in 11:12 the two witnesses are.
    Revelation 4:1 After these things I saw, and behold, a door opened in heaven, and the first voice I heard like a trumpet speaking with me said, “Come up hither, and I will show thee what must take place after these things.”
  2 Kings 2:11 — shared image: Elijah is “taken up … as into heaven”, and the witnesses likewise go up into heaven. Elijah is taken up in a tumult, with a chariot of fire, rather than in a cloud, and the shown text does not mention onlookers as Revelation does.
    2 Kings 2:11 As they went, walking and speaking, behold—a chariot of fire and horses of fire separated the two of them, and Elijah was taken up in a tumult, as into heaven.

Revelation 11:13
And in that hour a great earthquake occurred, and a tenth of the city fell, and seven thousand human names were killed in the earthquake; and the rest became fearful and gave glory to the God of heaven.
  Revelation 14:7 — verbal parallel: Revelation 14:7 calls people to “Fear God and give Him glory”. This is the response recorded of the survivors in 11:13, who “became fearful and gave glory to the God of heaven”.
    Revelation 14:7 saying in a loud voice, “Fear God and give Him glory, because the hour of His judgment hath come, and worship the One who made heaven and the earth and sea and springs of waters.”
  Revelation 16:9–16:11 — contrast: In Revelation 16:9–11 people “did not repent to give Him glory” and blasphemed “the God of heaven”. In 11:13 the survivors do give glory to the God of heaven.
    Revelation 16:9 And people were scorched with great heat, and blasphemed the name of God, who hath authority over these plagues, and did not repent to give Him glory.
    Revelation 16:10 And the fifth poured out his bowl upon the beast’s throne, and its kingdom became darkened, and they were chewing their tongues because of the pain,
    Revelation 16:11 and blasphemed the God of heaven because of their pains and their sores, and did not repent of their works.
  Revelation 16:18–16:19 — internal cross-reference: Both passages report “a great earthquake” that brings down part of a city. In 11:13 a tenth of the city falls; in 16:18–19 the great city splits into three parts.
    Revelation 16:18 And there were lightnings and voices and thunders, and a great earthquake occurred, such as had not occurred since human beings came to be upon the earth—so mighty an earthquake, so great.
    Revelation 16:19 And the great city became three parts, and the cities of the nations fell. And Babylon the great was remembered before God, to give her the cup of the wine of the fury of His wrath.

Revelation 11:14
The second woe hath passed. Behold, the third woe is coming quickly.
  Revelation 9:12 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:12 uses the same formula for the first woe, “The one woe hath passed. Behold …”. Revelation 11:14 continues the count to the second and third woes.
    Revelation 9:12 The one woe hath passed. Behold, two woes are still coming after these things.
  Revelation 8:13 — internal cross-reference: The eagle’s “Woe, woe, woe” in 8:13 announces the three woes connected with the remaining trumpets. Revelation 11:14 marks the second as passed and the third as coming.
    Revelation 8:13 And I saw, and heard one eagle flying in midheaven saying with a loud voice, “Woe, woe, woe to those dwelling upon the earth, because of the remaining voices of the trumpet of the three angels who are about to sound!”

Revelation 11:15
And the seventh angel sounded, and loud voices arose in heaven, saying, “The kingdom of the world hath become the kingdom of our Lord and of His Christ, and He will reign for the ages of the ages.”
  Revelation 10:7 — fulfilment of announcement: Revelation 10:7 says the mystery of God will be completed “in the days of the voice of the seventh angel”. In 11:15 “the seventh angel sounded” and heaven proclaims the kingdom.
    Revelation 10:7 But in the days of the voice of the seventh angel, when he is about to sound, the mystery of God will be completed, as He announced the good news to His own slaves, the prophets.
  Revelation 12:10 — verbal parallel: Both proclamations are loud voices in heaven announcing “the kingdom” of God together with His Christ: “the kingdom of our God and the authority of His Christ” in 12:10.
    Revelation 12:10 And I heard a loud voice in heaven saying, “Now have come the salvation and the power and the kingdom of our God and the authority of His Christ, because the accuser of our brothers hath been thrown down, the one accusing them before our God day and night.
  Daniel 7:14 — shared theme: Daniel’s figure receives a kingdom that “will never be destroyed” and an everlasting authority. Revelation proclaims that the Lord and His Christ “will reign for the ages of the ages”.
    Daniel 7:14 Authority was given him, and all the nations of the earth according to their families, and all glory, worshipped him. His authority is everlasting authority that will never be taken away, and his kingdom will never be destroyed.

Revelation 11:16
And the twenty-four elders sitting upon their thrones before God fell upon their faces and worshipped God,
  Revelation 4:4 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 4:4 introduces “twenty-four elders sitting” upon “twenty-four thrones” around the throne. Revelation 11:16 refers back to them in the same terms.
    Revelation 4:4 And around the throne were twenty-four thrones, and upon the thrones twenty-four elders sitting, clothed in white garments, and upon their heads golden crowns.
  Revelation 19:4 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 19:4 likewise says that “the twenty-four elders … fell and worshipped God”, here God “who sitteth upon the throne”.
    Revelation 19:4 And the twenty-four elders and the four living creatures fell and worshipped God, who sitteth upon the throne, saying, “Amen, Hallelujah!”
  Revelation 7:11 — verbal parallel: Revelation 7:11 uses the same words, “fell upon their faces … and worshipped God”, for the angels around the throne.
    Revelation 7:11 And all the angels were standing around the throne and the elders and the four living creatures, and fell upon their faces before the throne and worshipped God,

Revelation 11:17
saying, “We give Thee thanks, Lord God Almighty, the One who is and who was, because Thou hast taken Thy great power and reigned.
