  Revelation 19:6 — verbal parallel: Revelation 19:6 proclaims that “the Lord God Almighty hath reigned”. This is the same title and the same claim as the elders’ thanksgiving in 11:17.
    Revelation 19:6 And I heard something like a voice of a great multitude, and like a sound of many waters, and like a sound of mighty thunders, saying, “Hallelujah, because the Lord God Almighty hath reigned.
  Revelation 16:5 — same divine title: Revelation 16:5 addresses God as “the One who is and who was”, the same shortened form as 11:17, without “who is coming”.
    Revelation 16:5 And I heard the angel of the waters saying, “Thou art righteous, the One who is and who was, the Holy One, because Thou didst judge these things.
  Revelation 1:8 — same divine title: Revelation 1:8 combines “the Lord God”, “the One who is and who was” and “the Almighty”. In the fuller form it adds “who is coming”, which 11:17 leaves out.
    Revelation 1:8 “I am the Alpha and the Omega,” saith the Lord God, the One who is and who was and who is coming, the Almighty.

Revelation 11:18
And the nations were enraged, and Thy wrath came, and the time for the dead to be judged, and to give the reward to Thy slaves the prophets and to the saints and to those who fear Thy name, the small and the great, and to destroy those destroying the earth.”
  Revelation 20:12 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 20:12 shows the scene announced in 11:18: “the dead, the great and the small” are judged before the throne.
    Revelation 20:12 And I saw the dead, the great and the small, standing before the throne, and scrolls were opened; and another scroll was opened, which is of life. And the dead were judged from the things written in the scrolls, according to their works.
  Revelation 19:5 — verbal parallel: Revelation 19:5 addresses God’s “slaves and those who fear Him, the small and the great”. This is nearly the same grouping as those who receive the reward in 11:18.
    Revelation 19:5 And a voice came out from the throne, saying, “Praise our God, all His slaves and those who fear Him, the small and the great.”
  Revelation 22:12 — verbal parallel: In 22:12 Christ says, “My reward is with Me, to repay each according to what his work is.” This echoes 11:18’s announcement of the time “to give the reward”.
    Revelation 22:12 “Behold, I am coming quickly, and My reward is with Me, to repay each according to what his work is.

Revelation 11:19
And God’s temple in heaven was opened, and the ark of His covenant was seen in His temple; and there were lightnings and voices and thunders and an earthquake and great hail.
  Revelation 8:5 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 8:5 closes the seventh seal with “thunders and voices and lightnings and an earthquake”, and 11:19 closes the seventh trumpet with the same group, adding great hail.
    Revelation 8:5 And the angel took the censer and filled it from the fire of the altar, and threw it to the earth; and there were thunders and voices and lightnings and an earthquake.
  Revelation 16:18 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 16:18 has “lightnings and voices and thunders” and “a great earthquake” at the seventh bowl. Great hail follows in 16:21, giving the same set of signs as 11:19.
    Revelation 16:18 And there were lightnings and voices and thunders, and a great earthquake occurred, such as had not occurred since human beings came to be upon the earth—so mighty an earthquake, so great.
  Exodus 25:21–25:22 — background: Exodus 25:21–22 describes “the ark of the testimony”, from above which God will make Himself known and speak. Revelation 11:19 shows “the ark of His covenant” seen in God’s temple in heaven.
    Exodus 25:21 You shall place the propitiatory cover on top of the ark, and put into the ark the testimonies I will give you.
    Exodus 25:22 And I will be made known to you from there, and speak to you from above the propitiatory cover, from between the two cherubim upon the ark of the testimony, concerning all that I command you for the sons of Israel.

Revelation 12:1
And a great sign was seen in heaven: a woman clothed with the sun, and the moon beneath her feet, and upon her head a crown of twelve stars.
  Revelation 12:3 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 12:3 introduces “another sign … in heaven”, the dragon, as a counterpart to the “great sign” of the woman in 12:1.
    Revelation 12:3 And another sign was seen in heaven, and behold, a great fiery red dragon, having seven heads and ten horns, and upon his heads seven diadems.
  Revelation 15:1 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 15:1 uses similar language for “another sign in heaven, great and marvellous”, the seven angels with the last plagues.
    Revelation 15:1 And I saw another sign in heaven, great and marvellous: seven angels having seven plagues, the last ones, because in them the fury of God was completed.
  Song of Songs 6:10 — shared image: The Song describes a woman “beautiful like the moon, chosen like the sun”. Revelation’s woman is clothed with the sun with the moon under her feet. The Song uses the sun and moon as comparisons, not as clothing or footstool.
    Song of Songs 6:10 Who is this looking out like dawn, beautiful like the moon, chosen like the sun, a terror like troops drawn up?

Revelation 12:2
And she was pregnant, and crieth out in labour and torment to give birth.
  Isaiah 66:7–66:9 — shared image: Isaiah speaks of a woman in labor who “bore a male” and of Zion who “labored and bore her children”; Revelation’s woman likewise “crieth out in labour and torment to give birth.” Isaiah stresses a birth before the pains, while Revelation dwells on the torment.
    Isaiah 66:7 Before the woman in labor gave birth, before the pain of labor came, she escaped and bore a male.
    Isaiah 66:8 Who heard such a thing, and who has seen anything like it? Did a land labor in one day, or was a nation born all at once? For Zion labored and bore her children.
    Isaiah 66:9 ‘But I gave this expectation, and you did not remember Me,’ said the Lord. ‘Behold, did I not make the woman who gives birth and the barren woman?’ said God.
  Micah 5:2 — shared image: Micah refers to “the time of the woman giving birth”, after which the scattered brothers return. Revelation 12:2 shows a woman in the moment of giving birth. Micah does not describe her pain.
    Micah 5:2 Therefore he will give them up until the time of the woman giving birth, when she gives birth; then the remaining ones of their brothers will return to the sons of Israel.
  John 16:21 — shared image: Jesus speaks of a woman giving birth who has “sorrow” and “anguish” until the child is born. Revelation 12:2 describes her “labour and torment to give birth”.
    John 16:21 When a woman is giving birth, she has sorrow because her hour has come; but when she has borne the child, she no longer remembers the anguish because of the joy that a human being has been born into the world.

Revelation 12:3
And another sign was seen in heaven, and behold, a great fiery red dragon, having seven heads and ten horns, and upon his heads seven diadems.
  Revelation 12:9 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 12:9 identifies “the great dragon” as “the ancient serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan”.
    Revelation 12:9 And the great dragon was thrown down, the ancient serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan, the one deceiving the whole inhabited world; he was thrown to the earth, and his angels were thrown with him.
  Revelation 17:7 — internal cross-reference: The beast in 17:7 also has “the seven heads and the ten horns”, the same features given to the dragon in 12:3.
    Revelation 17:7 And the angel said unto me, “Why didst thou marvel? I will tell thee the mystery of the woman and of the beast carrying her, which hath the seven heads and the ten horns.
  Daniel 7:20 — shared image: Daniel’s fourth beast has “ten horns upon its head”, matching the dragon’s ten horns. Daniel’s beast has ten horns but no seven heads or diadems.
    Daniel 7:20 and of its ten horns upon its head and the single other that sprouted, through which three fell out. That horn had eyes and a mouth speaking great things, and its appearance surpassed the others.

Revelation 12:4
And his tail draggeth a third of the stars of heaven and threw them to the earth. And the dragon standeth before the woman about to give birth, so that when she giveth birth he may devour her child.
  Revelation 12:2–12:3 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 12:2–3 introduces the woman in labour and the great fiery red dragon who stand facing each other in 12:4.
    Revelation 12:2 And she was pregnant, and crieth out in labour and torment to give birth.
    Revelation 12:3 And another sign was seen in heaven, and behold, a great fiery red dragon, having seven heads and ten horns, and upon his heads seven diadems.
  Exodus 1:16 — shared theme: Pharaoh orders that Hebrew male children be killed as the women are “about to give birth”. The dragon waits before the woman “about to give birth” to devour her child.
    Exodus 1:16 And he said, “When you attend the Hebrew women in childbirth, and they are about to give birth, if the child is male, kill him; but if the child is female, preserve her.”
  Daniel 8:9–8:12 — shared image: Daniel’s horn is “raised as high as heaven’s stars” and then dashed to the earth, much as the dragon’s tail throws “a third of the stars of heaven” to the earth. In the Daniel text shown it is the horn, not the stars, that is dashed to the earth.
    Daniel 8:9 From one of them a single strong horn sprouted. It grew strong and struck toward the south, toward the east, and toward the north.
    Daniel 8:10 It was raised as high as heaven’s stars, but was dashed to the earth from the stars and trampled by them,
    Daniel 8:11 until the chief commander rescues the captivity. Because of him the ancient mountains were dashed down; their place and sacrifice were removed. He set it down upon the earth, and it prospered and came to pass, and the sanctuary will be made desolate.
    Daniel 8:12 Sins came upon the sacrifice; righteousness was thrown down to the ground, and he acted and prospered.

Revelation 12:5
And she bore a son, a male, who is to shepherd all the nations with an iron rod; and her child was caught up to God and to His throne.
  Psalms 2:9–2:10 — quotation: Psalm 2:9 says “You will shepherd them with an iron rod”. Revelation 12:5 applies this to the male child “who is to shepherd all the nations with an iron rod”.
    Psalms 2:9 You will shepherd them with an iron rod; you will crush them like a potter’s vessel.’”
    Psalms 2:10 And now, kings, understand; be instructed, all who judge the earth.
  Revelation 19:15 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 19:15 says of the rider on the white horse, “He will shepherd them with an iron rod”, using the same phrase from the Psalm as 12:5.
    Revelation 19:15 And from His mouth cometh out a sharp sword, so that with it He may strike the nations; and He will shepherd them with an iron rod, and He treadeth the winepress of the wine of the fury of the wrath of God Almighty.
  Mark 16:19 — shared image: Mark records that the Lord Jesus “was taken up into heaven and sat at the right of God”. Revelation’s child “was caught up to God and to His throne”. Revelation 12:5 does not name the child.
    Mark 16:19 So the Lord Jesus, after speaking to them, was taken up into heaven and sat at the right of God.

Revelation 12:6
And the woman fled into the wilderness, where she hath a place prepared by God, so that there they may nourish her for a thousand two hundred sixty days.
  Revelation 12:14 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 12:14 retells the woman’s flight “into the wilderness to her place, where she is nourished”. There the period is given as “a time and times and half a time” instead of 1,260 days.
    Revelation 12:14 And the two wings of the great eagle were given to the woman, so that she might fly into the wilderness to her place, where she is nourished for a time and times and half a time, away from the face of the serpent.
  Revelation 11:2–11:3 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 11:3 uses the same “thousand two hundred sixty days” for the prophesying of the two witnesses, and 11:2 has forty-two months.
    Revelation 11:2 And leave out the court outside the temple, and do not measure it, because it was given to the nations, and they will trample the holy city for forty-two months.
    Revelation 11:3 And I will grant authority to My two witnesses, and they will prophesy for a thousand two hundred sixty days, clothed in sackcloth.”
  1 Kings 19:4–19:8 — shared image: Elijah goes “into the wilderness” and is fed there by the Lord’s angel. The woman flees into the wilderness to a place where she is nourished. Elijah’s flight is not tied to a set number of days, except the later forty days’ journey.
    1 Kings 19:4 He himself went a day’s journey into the wilderness, came, and sat under a single broom bush. He asked that his life might end and said, “Let it be enough now! I pray Thee, take my life from me, Lord, for I am no better than my fathers.”
    1 Kings 19:5 He lay down and slept there under a plant. Behold, someone touched him and said to him, “Rise and eat.”
    1 Kings 19:6 Elijah looked, and there at his head were a hearth cake of spelt and a flask of water. He rose, ate, and drank, then lay down again.
    1 Kings 19:7 The Lord’s angel returned a second time, touched him, and said to him, “Rise, eat, for the journey is too much for you.”
    1 Kings 19:8 He rose, ate, and drank, and went in the strength of that food forty days and forty nights to Mount Horeb.

Revelation 12:7
And war arose in heaven: Michael and his angels were to wage war with the dragon. And the dragon and his angels fought,
  Jude 1:9 — same named figure: Jude calls Michael “the archangel” and describes him “contending with the devil”. Revelation shows Michael and his angels waging war with the dragon, who is identified in 12:9 as the Devil. Jude’s dispute is over the body of Moses.
    Jude 1:9 But Michael the archangel, when he was contending with the devil and disputing about the body of Moses, did not dare to bring a slanderous judgment against him, but said, “May the Lord rebuke you.”
  Daniel 10:13 — same named figure: Daniel 10:13 shows “Michael, one of the foremost rulers” coming to help against a hostile power. Revelation 12:7 likewise shows Michael in conflict, here against the dragon in heaven.
    Daniel 10:13 The general of the king of the Persians stood against me for twenty-one days. Then Michael, one of the foremost rulers, came to help me, and I left him there with the general of the king of the Persians.”
  Daniel 12:1 — same named figure: Daniel 12:1 calls Michael “the great angel who stands over the sons of your people” in a time of great affliction. This is the same angel who leads the heavenly war in Revelation 12:7.
    Daniel 12:1 “At that hour Michael will pass by, the great angel who stands over the sons of your people. That will be a day of affliction such as has not occurred since they came into being until that day. On that day all the people found inscribed in the book will be exalted.

Revelation 12:8
and he did not prevail, nor was a place found for them any more in heaven.
  Psalms 36:10 — verbal allusion: The psalm says of the sinner, “you will seek his place and certainly not find it”. Revelation says of the dragon and his angels that no “place” was “found for them any more in heaven”.
    Psalms 36:10 A little longer, and the sinner will certainly not exist; you will seek his place and certainly not find it.
  Revelation 12:11 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 12:11 says the brothers “conquered him” by the blood of the Lamb. This is a further statement of the dragon’s defeat reported in 12:8.
    Revelation 12:11 And they conquered him because of the blood of the Lamb and because of the word of their testimony, and they did not love their life until death.

Revelation 12:9
And the great dragon was thrown down, the ancient serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan, the one deceiving the whole inhabited world; he was thrown to the earth, and his angels were thrown with him.
  Luke 10:18 — shared image: Jesus says, “I was watching Satan fall from heaven”. Revelation 12:9 shows Satan “thrown down” to the earth after the war in heaven.
    Luke 10:18 He said to them, “I was watching Satan fall from heaven like lightning.
  Genesis 3:1 — background: Genesis 3:1 introduces “the serpent” who deceives the woman. Revelation identifies the dragon as “the ancient serpent” and “the one deceiving the whole inhabited world”. Genesis does not itself name the serpent as the Devil.
    Genesis 3:1 Now the serpent was more cunning than all the wild beasts upon the earth that the Lord God had made. And the serpent said to the woman, “Why has God said, ‘You shall not eat from any tree in the garden’?”
  Revelation 20:10 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 20:10 completes the dragon’s fall: “the devil who deceived them” is thrown into the lake of fire. The verb “thrown” and the role of deceiver are the same as in 12:9.
    Revelation 20:10 And the devil who deceived them was thrown into the lake of fire and sulfur, where also are the beast and the false prophet, and they will be tormented day and night for the ages of the ages.

Revelation 12:10
And I heard a loud voice in heaven saying, “Now have come the salvation and the power and the kingdom of our God and the authority of His Christ, because the accuser of our brothers hath been thrown down, the one accusing them before our God day and night.
  Revelation 11:15 — parallel heavenly proclamation: In both verses voices in heaven announce “the kingdom” of “our God”/“our Lord” together with “His Christ.” Revelation 11:15 speaks of the kingdom of the world becoming His, while 12:10 links the arrival of the kingdom to the accuser being thrown down.
    Revelation 11:15 And the seventh angel sounded, and loud voices arose in heaven, saying, “The kingdom of the world hath become the kingdom of our Lord and of His Christ, and He will reign for the ages of the ages.”
  Zechariah 3:1–3:2 — shared image of the accuser: Zechariah sees “the slanderer” standing beside Joshua the high priest “to oppose him” before the angel of the Lord, as Revelation describes one “accusing them before our God.” Zechariah ends with the Lord’s rebuke of the slanderer rather than his being thrown down.
    Zechariah 3:1 He showed me Joshua the high priest standing before the angel of the Lord, with the slanderer standing at his right hand to oppose him.
    Zechariah 3:2 The Lord said to the slanderer, “May the Lord rebuke you, slanderer! May the Lord, who chose Jerusalem, rebuke you! Look, is this not like a brand pulled from fire?”
  Job 1:9 — shared image of the accuser: Job shows “the devil” speaking “before the Lord” to cast doubt on a righteous man’s devotion, matching the picture of an accuser of the faithful before God. Job does not describe the accuser being cast down.
    Job 1:9 The devil answered before the Lord, “Does Job worship God for nothing?

Revelation 12:11
And they conquered him because of the blood of the Lamb and because of the word of their testimony, and they did not love their life until death.
  Revelation 20:4 — parallel martyr description: Both describe faithful people who die for their witness: “the word of their testimony” and “did not love their life until death” in 12:11, those “beheaded because of the testimony of Jesus and because of the word of God” in 20:4. Revelation 20:4 adds that they lived and reigned with Christ.
    Revelation 20:4 And I saw thrones, and they sat upon them, and judgment was given to them; and the souls of those beheaded because of the testimony of Jesus and because of the word of God, and those who did not worship the beast or its image and did not receive the mark upon their forehead and upon their hand. And they lived and reigned with Christ for a thousand years.
  Acts 20:24 — verbal echo: Paul says “I consider my life of no value to myself” for the sake of bearing “solemn witness,” close to the conquerors who “did not love their life until death” because of their testimony. Paul speaks of his own ministry, not of a conflict with the accuser.
    Acts 20:24 But I consider my life of no value to myself, so that I may complete my course and the ministry I received from the Lord Jesus: to bear solemn witness to the good news of God’s grace.

Revelation 12:12
For this reason rejoice, heavens and those dwelling in them. Woe to the earth and the sea, because the devil came down to you, having great fury, knowing that he hath a little time.”
  Revelation 8:13 — parallel woe announcement: Both pronounce “Woe” on the earth’s inhabitants in view of coming disaster: an eagle’s threefold woe because of the remaining trumpets, and in 12:12 because “the devil came down to you.”
    Revelation 8:13 And I saw, and heard one eagle flying in midheaven saying with a loud voice, “Woe, woe, woe to those dwelling upon the earth, because of the remaining voices of the trumpet of the three angels who are about to sound!”
  Isaiah 44:23 — verbal echo: Isaiah opens with the same summons, “Rejoice, heavens,” grounded in God’s saving act for Israel. Isaiah calls the earth to rejoice as well, whereas Revelation pronounces woe on the earth and the sea.
    Isaiah 44:23 Rejoice, heavens, because God had mercy on Israel! Sound trumpets, earth’s foundations! Cry out joy, mountains, hills and all their trees! For God redeemed Jacob, and Israel will be glorified.
  Isaiah 49:13 — verbal echo: Isaiah begins “Rejoice, heavens,” giving the reason that God had mercy on His people. Unlike Revelation, Isaiah calls the earth to exult rather than announcing woe upon it.
    Isaiah 49:13 Rejoice, heavens, and let earth exult! Let mountains burst out in joy and hills in righteousness, for God had mercy on His people and comforted His people’s lowly ones.

Revelation 12:13
And when the dragon saw that he had been thrown to the earth, he pursued the woman who bore the male child.
  Revelation 12:3–12:5 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 12:3–5 introduces the dragon standing before the woman to devour her child and records that “she bore a son, a male.” Verse 13 resumes that scene: the dragon now pursues “the woman who bore the male child.”
    Revelation 12:3 And another sign was seen in heaven, and behold, a great fiery red dragon, having seven heads and ten horns, and upon his heads seven diadems.
    Revelation 12:4 And his tail draggeth a third of the stars of heaven and threw them to the earth. And the dragon standeth before the woman about to give birth, so that when she giveth birth he may devour her child.
    Revelation 12:5 And she bore a son, a male, who is to shepherd all the nations with an iron rod; and her child was caught up to God and to His throne.
  Genesis 3:15 — shared image: Genesis announces enmity between the one addressed and “the woman,” and between their seeds, as Revelation shows hostility directed at “the woman” and her child. The Genesis verse as shown does not name the addressee; Revelation calls the dragon a serpent elsewhere in the chapter.
    Genesis 3:15 And I will put enmity between you and the woman, and between your seed and her seed. He shall watch for your head, and you shall watch for his heel.

Revelation 12:14
And the two wings of the great eagle were given to the woman, so that she might fly into the wilderness to her place, where she is nourished for a time and times and half a time, away from the face of the serpent.
  Revelation 12:6 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 12:6 already reports the woman fleeing “into the wilderness” to a “place” where she is nourished. Verse 14 repeats the scene, giving the period as “a time and times and half a time” rather than “a thousand two hundred sixty days.”
    Revelation 12:6 And the woman fled into the wilderness, where she hath a place prepared by God, so that there they may nourish her for a thousand two hundred sixty days.
  Exodus 19:4 — shared image: God says He bore Israel “as upon eagles’ wings” after dealing with the Egyptians, as the woman is given “the two wings of the great eagle” to escape. Exodus uses the wings as a figure of God’s carrying His people, not as a gift of wings to fly into the wilderness.
    Exodus 19:4 ‘You yourselves have seen all that I have done to the Egyptians, and I bore you as upon eagles’ wings, and brought you to Myself.
  Daniel 7:25 — verbal allusion: Daniel gives the same period, “a time, times, and half a time”, for which everything is handed into the hands of the one who wears down the holy ones of the Most High. Revelation uses it for the time the woman is nourished away from the serpent.
    Daniel 7:25 He will speak words against the Most High, wear down the holy ones of the Most High, and expect to alter seasons and law. Everything will be handed into his hands for a time, times, and half a time.

Revelation 12:15
And the serpent threw water like a river from his mouth after the woman, to make her swept away by the river.
  Isaiah 8:7 — shared image: Isaiah pictures “the river’s strong and abundant water” brought up against a people to overrun them, as the serpent sends water “like a river” to sweep the woman away. In Isaiah the Lord brings the river and identifies it as the king of the Assyrians.
    Isaiah 8:7 therefore, behold, the Lord brings up against you the river’s strong and abundant water—the king of the Assyrians and his glory. He will ascend every ravine of yours and walk over every wall of yours,
  Psalms 17:5 — shared image: The psalmist describes “torrents of lawlessness” that threw him into turmoil, the same image of a hostile flood threatening the righteous. The psalm does not describe the water coming from a serpent’s mouth.
    Psalms 17:5 The birth pangs of death encompassed me, and torrents of lawlessness threw me into turmoil.

Revelation 12:16
And the earth helped the woman, and the earth opened its mouth and swallowed the river that the dragon threw from his mouth.
  Numbers 26:10 — verbal allusion: Both say that “the earth opened its mouth and swallowed.” In Numbers the earth swallows Korah’s company in judgment; in Revelation it swallows the river the dragon sends against the woman, so the earth acts to rescue.
    Numbers 26:10 And the earth opened its mouth and swallowed them and Korah when his congregation died, when the fire consumed the two hundred fifty; they became a sign.
  Deuteronomy 11:6 — verbal allusion: Deuteronomy recalls “when the earth opened her mouth and swallowed” Dathan and Abiram; Revelation uses the same image of the earth opening its mouth to swallow, here to protect the woman rather than to punish rebels.
    Deuteronomy 11:6 and what He did unto Dathan and Abiram, the sons of Eliab son of Reuben, when the earth opened her mouth and swallowed them, and their houses, and their tents, and all their substance that was with them, in the midst of all Israel.

Revelation 12:17
And the dragon was enraged at the woman, and went away to make war with the rest of her offspring, those keeping the commandments of God and holding the testimony of Jesus.
  Revelation 14:12 — verbal parallel: Both describe the saints as “those keeping the commandments of God,” paired with a reference to Jesus. Revelation 14:12 has “the faith of Jesus” where 12:17 has “the testimony of Jesus.”
    Revelation 14:12 Here is the endurance of the saints, those keeping the commandments of God and the faith of Jesus.
  Revelation 19:10 — verbal parallel: Revelation 19:10 speaks of the brothers “who hold the testimony of Jesus,” the same description given to the woman’s offspring “holding the testimony of Jesus.”
    Revelation 19:10 And I fell before his feet to worship him. And he saith unto me, “See that thou do it not! I am thy fellow slave and of thy brothers who hold the testimony of Jesus. Worship God, for the testimony of Jesus is the spirit of prophecy.”
  Genesis 3:15 — shared image: Genesis announces enmity with “the woman” and “her seed,” as the dragon, enraged at the woman, makes war with “the rest of her offspring.” The Genesis verse as shown does not name the one addressed.
    Genesis 3:15 And I will put enmity between you and the woman, and between your seed and her seed. He shall watch for your head, and you shall watch for his heel.

Revelation 12:18
And he stood upon the sand of the sea.
  Revelation 13:1 — internal cross-reference: The dragon takes his stand “upon the sand of the sea,” and the next verse sees “a beast coming up from the sea.” 13:2 confirms the link: the dragon gives this beast his power and throne.
    Revelation 13:1 And I saw a beast coming up from the sea, having ten horns and seven heads, and upon its horns ten diadems, and upon its heads names of blasphemy.

Revelation 13:1
And I saw a beast coming up from the sea, having ten horns and seven heads, and upon its horns ten diadems, and upon its heads names of blasphemy.
  Daniel 7:3 — verbal allusion: Both visions see beasts rising out of the sea: Daniel’s “four beasts came up from the sea,” and John’s “beast coming up from the sea.” Daniel sees four distinct beasts; Revelation describes one.
    Daniel 7:3 Four beasts came up from the sea, each differing from the other.
  Daniel 7:7 — shared image: Daniel’s fourth beast “had ten horns,” as does the beast John sees. Daniel’s beast has iron teeth and no seven heads; the ten horns are the specific shared feature.
    Daniel 7:7 After this I watched in the night vision a fourth beast, fearsome, its terror surpassing in strength. It had great iron teeth, devoured and pounded, trampled all around with its feet, and acted differently from all the beasts before it. It had ten horns,
  Revelation 12:3 — internal cross-reference: The dragon of 12:3 has “seven heads and ten horns” with diadems, and the beast from the sea has the same “ten horns and seven heads,” its diadems on the horns rather than the heads. 13:2 then says the dragon gives the beast his power.
    Revelation 12:3 And another sign was seen in heaven, and behold, a great fiery red dragon, having seven heads and ten horns, and upon his heads seven diadems.

Revelation 13:2
And the beast I saw was like a leopard, and its feet like a bear’s, and its mouth like a lion’s mouth. And the dragon gave it his power and his throne and great authority.
  Revelation 13:4 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 13:4 restates the transfer: worship is given to the dragon “because he gave authority to the beast,” echoing “the dragon gave it his power and his throne and great authority.”
    Revelation 13:4 and they worshipped the dragon because he gave authority to the beast, and they worshipped the beast, saying, “Who is like the beast, and who is able to wage war with it?”
  Revelation 12:9 — identification of figure: Revelation 12:9 identifies “the great dragon” as “the ancient serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan,” naming who gives the beast its power and authority in 13:2.
    Revelation 12:9 And the great dragon was thrown down, the ancient serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan, the one deceiving the whole inhabited world; he was thrown to the earth, and his angels were thrown with him.
  Revelation 16:10 — later development: The throne the dragon gives the beast reappears as “the beast’s throne,” on which the fifth bowl is poured, darkening its kingdom.
    Revelation 16:10 And the fifth poured out his bowl upon the beast’s throne, and its kingdom became darkened, and they were chewing their tongues because of the pain,

Revelation 13:3
And one of its heads was as slaughtered to death, and its wound of death was healed. And the whole earth marvelled after the beast,
  Revelation 13:12 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 13:12 refers back to “the first beast … whose wound of death was healed,” repeating the phrase from 13:3 and connecting it with the earth worshipping the beast.
    Revelation 13:12 And it exerciseth all the authority of the first beast before it, and maketh the earth and those dwelling in it worship the first beast, whose wound of death was healed.
  Revelation 13:14 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 13:14 describes the same beast as the one “that hath the sword wound and lived,” and specifies that the deadly wound was from a sword.
    Revelation 13:14 And it deceiveth those dwelling upon the earth because of the signs it was given to perform before the beast, telling those dwelling upon the earth to make an image for the beast that hath the sword wound and lived.
  Revelation 17:8 — parallel description: Both say the earth’s inhabitants “marvel” at the beast. Revelation 17:8 adds that these are people whose names are not written in the book of life and that the beast “was and is not and will be present.”
    Revelation 17:8 The beast thou sawest was and is not, and is about to come up from the abyss, and goeth into destruction. And those dwelling upon the earth, whose name hath not been written in the book of life from the foundation of the world, will marvel when they see the beast, because it was and is not and will be present.

Revelation 13:4
and they worshipped the dragon because he gave authority to the beast, and they worshipped the beast, saying, “Who is like the beast, and who is able to wage war with it?”
  Revelation 13:2 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 13:2 narrates what 13:4 summarises: “the dragon gave it his power and his throne and great authority.”
    Revelation 13:2 And the beast I saw was like a leopard, and its feet like a bear’s, and its mouth like a lion’s mouth. And the dragon gave it his power and his throne and great authority.
  Exodus 15:11 — verbal echo: The acclamation “Who is like the beast?” uses the form of Moses’ song, “Who is like Thee among the gods, O Lord?” Revelation applies to the beast the praise that Exodus gives to the Lord.
    Exodus 15:11 Who is like Thee among the gods, O Lord? Who is like Thee, glorified among the holy ones, marvellous in glories, working wonders?
  Revelation 17:14 — answer to question: The worshippers ask, “who is able to wage war with it?”; Revelation 17:14 says that the beast’s allies “will wage war with the Lamb, and the Lamb will conquer them.”
    Revelation 17:14 These will wage war with the Lamb, and the Lamb will conquer them, because He is Lord of lords and King of kings, and those with Him are called and chosen and faithful.”

Revelation 13:5
And a mouth speaking great things and blasphemies was given to it, and authority to act for forty-two months was given to it.
  Daniel 7:8 — verbal allusion: Daniel’s little horn has “a mouth speaking great things,” the same phrase used for the beast’s mouth. Daniel’s horn sprouts on the fourth beast; in Revelation the mouth is given to the beast itself.
    Daniel 7:8 and many plans were in its horns. Another single horn sprouted among them, small among its horns, and three of the earlier horns were withered through it. This horn had eyes like human eyes and a mouth speaking great things, and it made war against the holy ones.
  Daniel 7:25 — shared image: Daniel’s figure “will speak words against the Most High” and is given power “for a time, times, and half a time,” paralleling the beast’s blasphemies and its limited “forty-two months.” The two periods are expressed differently in the two texts.
    Daniel 7:25 He will speak words against the Most High, wear down the holy ones of the Most High, and expect to alter seasons and law. Everything will be handed into his hands for a time, times, and half a time.
  Daniel 11:36 — shared image: Daniel’s king “will speak extraordinary things against the God of gods” and prosper for a set time, as the beast is given a mouth for “blasphemies” and authority for a fixed period.
    Daniel 11:36 The king will act according to his will, become enraged, and be exalted above every god. He will speak extraordinary things against the God of gods and prosper until the wrath is completed, for completion is coming upon him.

Revelation 13:6
And it opened its mouth in blasphemies against God, to blaspheme His name and His tent, those dwelling in heaven.
  Revelation 12:12 — verbal echo: Revelation 12:12 calls on the “heavens and those dwelling in them” to rejoice, while in 13:6 the beast blasphemes “those dwelling in heaven.” Both concern those in heaven, beyond the reach of the dragon’s fury on earth.
    Revelation 12:12 For this reason rejoice, heavens and those dwelling in them. Woe to the earth and the sea, because the devil came down to you, having great fury, knowing that he hath a little time.”
  Revelation 21:3 — shared image: The beast blasphemes God’s “tent,” and Revelation 21:3 announces “the tabernacle of God” dwelling with men. The Saints texts use “tent” and “tabernacle” for these, and 21:3 places God’s dwelling among people rather than in heaven.
    Revelation 21:3 And I heard a great voice from the throne, saying, “Behold, the tabernacle of God is with men, and He shall dwell with them, and they shall be His peoples, and God Himself shall be with them as their God,

Revelation 13:7
And it was given to it to make war with the saints and conquer them, and authority was given to it over every tribe and people and tongue and nation.
  Daniel 7:21 — verbal allusion: Daniel sees the horn “waging war against the holy ones and routing them,” closely matching “to make war with the saints and conquer them.” Daniel attributes this to a horn on the fourth beast.
    Daniel 7:21 I observed that horn waging war against the holy ones and routing them,
  Revelation 11:7 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 11:7 uses the same sequence for the beast from the abyss: it “will make war with them and conquer them,” there directed at the two witnesses.
    Revelation 11:7 And when they complete their testimony, the beast coming up from the abyss will make war with them and conquer them and kill them.
  Revelation 5:9 — contrasting parallel: Both use the fourfold formula of tribe, people, tongue and nation: 5:9 has “every tribe and tongue and people and nation,” and 13:7 has “every tribe and people and tongue and nation.” In 5:9 the Lamb purchases people from them for God; in 13:7 the beast receives authority over them.
    Revelation 5:9 And they sing a new song, saying, “Thou art worthy to take the scroll and open its seals, because Thou wast slaughtered and didst purchase for God in Thy blood people from every tribe and tongue and people and nation,

Revelation 13:8
And all those dwelling upon the earth will worship it, each whose name hath not been written in the book of life of the Lamb slaughtered from the foundation of the world.
  Revelation 17:8 — parallel description: Revelation 17:8 repeats that “those dwelling upon the earth, whose name hath not been written in the book of life from the foundation of the world” respond to the beast, there by marvelling rather than worshipping.
    Revelation 17:8 The beast thou sawest was and is not, and is about to come up from the abyss, and goeth into destruction. And those dwelling upon the earth, whose name hath not been written in the book of life from the foundation of the world, will marvel when they see the beast, because it was and is not and will be present.
  Revelation 21:27 — same title: Both speak of the book of life as belonging to the Lamb, “the Lamb’s book of life” in 21:27. There those written in it enter the city; in 13:8 those not written in it worship the beast.
    Revelation 21:27 And nothing common, and no one practicing abomination and falsehood, will ever enter it, but only those written in the Lamb’s book of life.
  1 Peter 1:19–1:20 — shared image: Peter speaks of the blood of Christ “as of a lamb” who was foreknown “before the foundation of the world,” connecting the slaughtered Lamb with the foundation of the world as 13:8 does.
    1 Peter 1:19 but with precious blood, as of a lamb without blemish and without spot—the blood of Christ,
    1 Peter 1:20 who was foreknown before the foundation of the world but was manifested at the end of the times for your sake.

Revelation 13:9
If anyone hath an ear, let him hear.
  Revelation 2:7 — repeated formula: The call “Let the one who hath an ear hear” in the letters to the churches is echoed in “If anyone hath an ear, let him hear.” Here the formula lacks the words “what the Spirit saith to the Churches.”
    Revelation 2:7 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. To the one who conquereth I will give to eat from the tree of life, which is in the Paradise of God.’
  Revelation 2:29 — repeated formula: Revelation 2:29 gives the hearing formula on its own, “Let the one who hath an ear hear,” which 13:9 adapts as “If anyone hath an ear, let him hear.”
    Revelation 2:29 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches.’

Revelation 13:10
If anyone is for captivity, into captivity he goeth; if anyone is to be killed by a sword, by a sword he must be killed. Here is the endurance and the faith of the saints.
  Jeremiah 15:2 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah’s oracle has the same pattern: “as many for sword, to sword … as many for captivity, to captivity.” Jeremiah lists death and famine too and announces judgment, while Revelation applies the pattern to the saints’ endurance.
    Jeremiah 15:2 And if they say to you, ‘Where shall we go out?’ you will say to them: Thus says the Lord: As many as are for death, to death; as many for sword, to sword; as many for famine, to famine; as many for captivity, to captivity.
  Jeremiah 50:11 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah repeats the formula against Egypt: “those for deportation, to deportation; and those for the sword, to the sword,” the pattern Revelation uses for captivity and the sword.
    Jeremiah 50:11 He will enter and strike Egypt’s land: those for death, to death; those for deportation, to deportation; and those for the sword, to the sword.
  Revelation 14:12 — repeated formula: Revelation 14:12 repeats the closing words, “Here is the endurance of the saints,” adding a description of the saints as keeping God’s commandments and the faith of Jesus.
    Revelation 14:12 Here is the endurance of the saints, those keeping the commandments of God and the faith of Jesus.

Revelation 13:11
And I saw another beast coming up from the earth, and it had two horns like a lamb, and spoke like a dragon.
  Revelation 12:17–13:1 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 13:1 presents the first beast “coming up from the sea”; 13:11 introduces “another beast coming up from the earth,” and its speech “like a dragon” links it to the dragon of 12:17–18.
    Revelation 12:17 And the dragon was enraged at the woman, and went away to make war with the rest of her offspring, those keeping the commandments of God and holding the testimony of Jesus.
    Revelation 12:18 And he stood upon the sand of the sea.
    Revelation 13:1 And I saw a beast coming up from the sea, having ten horns and seven heads, and upon its horns ten diadems, and upon its heads names of blasphemy.
  Matthew 7:15 — shared image: Jesus warns of false prophets who come “in sheep’s clothing,” a harmless appearance concealing a threat, much as the beast has horns “like a lamb” yet speaks “like a dragon.” Matthew does not mention horns or a beast.
    Matthew 7:15 Beware of false prophets, who come to you in sheep’s clothing but inwardly are ravenous wolves.

Revelation 13:12
And it exerciseth all the authority of the first beast before it, and maketh the earth and those dwelling in it worship the first beast, whose wound of death was healed.
  Revelation 13:3–13:4 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 13:3–4 describes the first beast whose “wound of death was healed” and the earth’s worship of it, which the second beast now enforces.
    Revelation 13:3 And one of its heads was as slaughtered to death, and its wound of death was healed. And the whole earth marvelled after the beast,
    Revelation 13:4 and they worshipped the dragon because he gave authority to the beast, and they worshipped the beast, saying, “Who is like the beast, and who is able to wage war with it?”
  Revelation 19:20 — later development: Revelation 19:20 refers to the false prophet who acted “before it,” on behalf of the beast, and to “those worshipping its image.” The same activity on the beast’s behalf is described in 13:12, and 19:20 records the end of both.
    Revelation 19:20 And the beast was seized, and with it the false prophet who performed the signs before it, by which he deceived those receiving the beast’s mark and those worshipping its image. The two were thrown alive into the lake of fire burning with sulfur.
  Revelation 14:9 — warning against same worship: The third angel warns, “If anyone worshippeth the beast and its image,” addressing the very worship that 13:12 says the second beast imposes on the earth.
    Revelation 14:9 And another angel, a third, followed them, saying in a loud voice, “If anyone worshippeth the beast and its image, and receiveth a mark upon his forehead or upon his hand,

Revelation 13:13
And it performeth great signs, so that it even maketh fire come down from heaven to the earth before people.
  2 Kings 1:10–1:14 — verbal echo: In the Elijah story “fire came down from heaven” and consumed the king’s men, the sign the second beast imitates when it makes “fire come down from heaven to the earth.” Elijah acts as a man of God; the beast performs the sign to deceive.
    2 Kings 1:10 Elijah answered and said to the captain of fifty, ‘If I am a man of God, fire will come down from heaven and consume you and your fifty men.’ Fire came down from heaven and consumed him and his fifty men.
    2 Kings 1:11 The king again sent to him another captain of fifty with his fifty men. The captain went up and spoke to him, saying, ‘Man of God, thus says the king: Come down quickly!’
    2 Kings 1:12 Elijah answered and spoke to him, saying, ‘If I am a man of God, fire will come down from heaven and consume you and your fifty men.’ Fire came down from heaven and consumed him and his fifty men.
    2 Kings 1:13 The king again sent a commander, a third captain of fifty, with his fifty men. The third captain of fifty came to him, bent his knees before Elijah, and pleaded with him, saying, ‘Man of God, please let my life and the lives of these fifty slaves of yours be honored in your eyes.’
    2 Kings 1:14 ‘Behold, fire came down from heaven and consumed the first two captains of fifty and their fifty men. But now please let the lives of your slaves be honored in your eyes.’
  Revelation 19:20 — later development: Revelation 19:20 names the one who “performed the signs before it” as the false prophet, who deceived people by them, the activity 13:13 introduces.
    Revelation 19:20 And the beast was seized, and with it the false prophet who performed the signs before it, by which he deceived those receiving the beast’s mark and those worshipping its image. The two were thrown alive into the lake of fire burning with sulfur.
  Matthew 24:24 — shared warning: Jesus foretells false prophets who “give great signs and wonders, so as to lead astray,” matching the “great signs” performed by the second beast.
    Matthew 24:24 For false christs and false prophets will arise and give great signs and wonders, so as to lead astray, if possible, even the chosen.

Revelation 13:14
And it deceiveth those dwelling upon the earth because of the signs it was given to perform before the beast, telling those dwelling upon the earth to make an image for the beast that hath the sword wound and lived.
  Revelation 19:20 — later development: Revelation 19:20 refers back to this deception: the false prophet “performed the signs before it, by which he deceived” those who received the mark and worshipped the image.
    Revelation 19:20 And the beast was seized, and with it the false prophet who performed the signs before it, by which he deceived those receiving the beast’s mark and those worshipping its image. The two were thrown alive into the lake of fire burning with sulfur.
  Revelation 13:15 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 13:15 continues the account of the image made for the beast: it is given breath, speaks, and causes non-worshippers to be killed.
    Revelation 13:15 And it was given to it to give breath to the beast’s image, so that the beast’s image might both speak and cause as many as would not worship the beast’s image to be killed.
  Revelation 12:9 — shared description: The dragon is “the one deceiving the whole inhabited world,” and the second beast likewise “deceiveth those dwelling upon the earth,” carrying on the dragon’s deception.
    Revelation 12:9 And the great dragon was thrown down, the ancient serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan, the one deceiving the whole inhabited world; he was thrown to the earth, and his angels were thrown with him.

Revelation 13:15
And it was given to it to give breath to the beast’s image, so that the beast’s image might both speak and cause as many as would not worship the beast’s image to be killed.
  Daniel 3:3–3:6 — shared image: Nebuchadnezzar orders the nations to “bow to the golden image” and decrees death in the furnace for anyone who does not, as the beast’s image causes those who “would not worship the beast’s image” to be killed.
    Daniel 3:3 Those previously listed stood opposite the image.
    Daniel 3:4 The herald proclaimed to the crowds, “You are commanded, nations and countries, peoples and languages:
    Daniel 3:5 when you hear the sound of trumpet, panpipe, kithara, sambuca, psaltery, symphonia, and every kind of musical instrument, fall down and bow to the golden image that King Nebuchadnezzar has set up.
    Daniel 3:6 They will throw into the blazing furnace anyone who does not fall down and bow.”
  Revelation 20:4 — later development: Revelation 20:4 includes among those enthroned “those who did not worship the beast or its image,” the faithful against whom the image’s death decree was directed.
    Revelation 20:4 And I saw thrones, and they sat upon them, and judgment was given to them; and the souls of those beheaded because of the testimony of Jesus and because of the word of God, and those who did not worship the beast or its image and did not receive the mark upon their forehead and upon their hand. And they lived and reigned with Christ for a thousand years.
  Revelation 14:9 — warning against same worship: The third angel warns against anyone who “worshippeth the beast and its image,” the worship the image enforces on pain of death.
    Revelation 14:9 And another angel, a third, followed them, saying in a loud voice, “If anyone worshippeth the beast and its image, and receiveth a mark upon his forehead or upon his hand,

Revelation 13:16
And it causeth all—the small and the great, and the rich and the poor, and the free and the slaves—to be given a mark upon their right hand or upon their forehead,
  Ezekiel 9:4 — shared image: In Ezekiel a “sign” is put “upon the foreheads” of those grieving over Jerusalem’s lawlessness, marking them out. Revelation’s mark identifies the beast’s followers, not the faithful.
    Ezekiel 9:4 and said to him, “Pass through the midst of Jerusalem and put the sign upon the foreheads of the men who sigh deeply and grieve over all the lawlessness being committed within her.”
  Revelation 7:3 — contrasting parallel: God’s slaves are sealed “upon their foreheads” in 7:3, the counterpart to the beast’s mark “upon their right hand or upon their forehead.”
    Revelation 7:3 saying, “Do not harm the earth or the sea or the trees until we seal the slaves of our God upon their foreheads.”
  Revelation 14:9–14:11 — warning against same mark: The third angel warns that anyone who receives “a mark upon his forehead or upon his hand” will drink the wine of God’s fury, the same mark that 13:16 says is imposed.
    Revelation 14:9 And another angel, a third, followed them, saying in a loud voice, “If anyone worshippeth the beast and its image, and receiveth a mark upon his forehead or upon his hand,
    Revelation 14:10 he too will drink from the wine of God’s fury, prepared unmixed in the cup of His wrath, and will be tormented in fire and sulfur before holy angels and before the Lamb.
    Revelation 14:11 And the smoke of their torment goeth up for ages of ages, and they have no rest day and night, those worshipping the beast and its image, and anyone who receiveth the mark of its name.”

Revelation 13:17
so that no one may be able to buy or sell except the one having the mark, the name of the beast or the number of its name.
  Revelation 13:18 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 13:18 continues by inviting the reader to calculate “the number of the beast,” explaining the “number of its name” mentioned in 13:17.
    Revelation 13:18 Here is wisdom. Let the one who hath understanding calculate the number of the beast, for it is a human number, and its number is six hundred sixty-six.
  Revelation 15:2 — verbal parallel: Revelation 15:2 lists “the beast and its image and the number of its name” as what the victors conquered, using the same phrase “the number of its name.”
    Revelation 15:2 And I saw something like a glassy sea mixed with fire, and those conquering the beast and its image and the number of its name standing upon the glassy sea, having lyres of God.
  Revelation 14:11 — verbal parallel: Revelation 14:11 speaks of “anyone who receiveth the mark of its name,” combining mark and name as 13:17 does, and describes the torment that awaits them.
    Revelation 14:11 And the smoke of their torment goeth up for ages of ages, and they have no rest day and night, those worshipping the beast and its image, and anyone who receiveth the mark of its name.”

Revelation 13:18
Here is wisdom. Let the one who hath understanding calculate the number of the beast, for it is a human number, and its number is six hundred sixty-six.
  Revelation 17:9 — repeated formula: Revelation 17:9 uses a similar call to discernment, “Here is the mind that hath wisdom,” before explaining the beast’s heads, as 13:18 says “Here is wisdom” before the number of the beast.
    Revelation 17:9 Here is the mind that hath wisdom. The seven heads are seven mountains, upon which the woman sitteth, and they are seven kings.
  Revelation 21:17 — verbal parallel: Revelation 21:17 calls the wall’s measure “a human measure,” a phrase like the “human number” of 13:18. The context is the city’s wall, not the beast.
    Revelation 21:17 And he measured its wall at a hundred forty-four cubits, a human measure, which is an angel’s.
  Revelation 15:2 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 15:2 mentions those who conquered “the number of its name,” the number that 13:18 asks the reader to calculate.
    Revelation 15:2 And I saw something like a glassy sea mixed with fire, and those conquering the beast and its image and the number of its name standing upon the glassy sea, having lyres of God.

Revelation 14:1
And I saw, and behold, the Lamb standing upon Mount Zion, and with Him a hundred forty-four thousand having His name and His Father’s name written upon their foreheads.
  Revelation 3:12 — fulfilment of promise: Christ promises to write on the conqueror “the name of My God” and “My new name”; in 14:1 the hundred forty-four thousand have “His name and His Father’s name written upon their foreheads.”
    Revelation 3:12 The one who conquereth, I will make him a pillar in the temple of My God, and he shall certainly go outside no more. And I will write upon him the name of My God and the name of the city of My God, the new Jerusalem, which cometh down out of heaven from My God, and My new name.
  Psalms 2:6 — shared setting: The psalm’s speaker is appointed king “on Zion, His holy mountain,” the place where the Lamb is seen standing in 14:1.
    Psalms 2:6 “But I was appointed king by Him on Zion, His holy mountain,
  Joel 3:5 — shared image: Joel says that “on Mount Zion and in Jerusalem there will be one who escapes,” a saved remnant on Zion like the company standing with the Lamb. Joel does not give their number or mention names on foreheads.
    Joel 3:5 Everyone who calls upon the Lord’s name will be saved, for on Mount Zion and in Jerusalem there will be one who escapes, as the Lord said, and bearers of good news whom the Lord has called.”

Revelation 14:2
And I heard a voice from heaven like the sound of many waters and like the sound of great thunder; and the voice I heard was like lyre players playing on their lyres.
  Revelation 1:15 — verbal parallel: The voice of the risen Christ is “like the sound of many waters,” the same comparison used for the voice from heaven in 14:2.
    Revelation 1:15 and His feet like burnished bronze, as refined in a furnace, and His voice like the sound of many waters.
  Revelation 15:2 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 15:2 shows the victors standing on the glassy sea “having lyres of God,” the instrument heard in 14:2.
    Revelation 15:2 And I saw something like a glassy sea mixed with fire, and those conquering the beast and its image and the number of its name standing upon the glassy sea, having lyres of God.
  Revelation 6:1 — verbal parallel: One of the living creatures speaks “as with a voice of thunder,” a comparison also used in 14:2, “like the sound of great thunder.”
    Revelation 6:1 And I saw when the Lamb opened one of the seven seals, and I heard one of the four living creatures saying as with a voice of thunder, “Come.”

Revelation 14:3
And they sing something like a new song before the throne and before the four living creatures and the elders; and no one was able to learn the song except the hundred forty-four thousand purchased from the earth.
  Revelation 5:9 — verbal parallel: In 5:9 the heavenly worshippers “sing a new song” to the Lamb who “didst purchase for God” people, matching the “new song” and those “purchased from the earth” in 14:3.
    Revelation 5:9 And they sing a new song, saying, “Thou art worthy to take the scroll and open its seals, because Thou wast slaughtered and didst purchase for God in Thy blood people from every tribe and tongue and people and nation,
  Revelation 14:1 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 14:1 introduces the “hundred forty-four thousand” with the Lamb on Mount Zion, the only ones able to learn the song in 14:3.
    Revelation 14:1 And I saw, and behold, the Lamb standing upon Mount Zion, and with Him a hundred forty-four thousand having His name and His Father’s name written upon their foreheads.
  Psalms 97:1 — verbal echo: The psalm calls, “Sing to the Lord a new song,” because of the salvation His right hand won, the kind of song sung in 14:3. The psalm does not restrict who can learn it.
    Psalms 97:1 A psalm of David. Sing to the Lord a new song, for the Lord has done wonders. His right hand and His holy arm won salvation for Him.

Revelation 14:4
These are the ones who were not defiled with women, for they are virgins. These are the ones following the Lamb wherever He goeth. These were purchased from among people as firstfruits to God and to the Lamb,
  Matthew 8:19 — verbal echo: The scribe promises Jesus, “I will follow Thee wherever Thou goest,” closely matching “following the Lamb wherever He goeth.” Matthew records only an offer to follow, not the outcome.
    Matthew 8:19 One scribe came and said to Him, “Teacher, I will follow Thee wherever Thou goest.”
  James 1:18 — shared image: James says believers were given birth so they might be “a kind of firstfruits of His creatures,” the same image applied to the redeemed as “firstfruits to God and to the Lamb.”
    James 1:18 By His will He gave us birth through the word of truth, so that we might be a kind of firstfruits of His creatures.
  2 Corinthians 11:2 — shared image: Paul wants to present the church “as a pure virgin to Christ,” comparable to calling the followers of the Lamb “virgins.” Paul uses the image for a whole church betrothed to Christ.
    2 Corinthians 11:2 For I am jealous for you with God’s jealousy, for I betrothed you to one husband, to present you as a pure virgin to Christ.

Revelation 14:5
and no lie was found in their mouth; they are unblemished.
  Zephaniah 3:13 — verbal allusion: Zephaniah says of the remnant of Israel that “no deceitful tongue will be found in their mouth,” closely paralleling “no lie was found in their mouth.”
    Zephaniah 3:13 the remnant of Israel; they will do no injustice or speak vain things, and no deceitful tongue will be found in their mouth. For they will graze and lie down, and no one will frighten them.
  Isaiah 53:9 — verbal echo: Isaiah says of the servant that “nor was deceit found in his mouth,” language applied in 14:5 to the Lamb’s followers.
    Isaiah 53:9 And I will give the wicked in exchange for his burial, and the rich in exchange for his death; for he committed no iniquity, nor was deceit found in his mouth.
  Ephesians 5:27 — shared description: Paul says Christ will present the church “holy and without blemish,” the same quality ascribed to the redeemed, who “are unblemished.”
    Ephesians 5:27 so that He Himself might present the Church to Himself glorious, having no spot or wrinkle or any such thing, but so that she should be holy and without blemish.

Revelation 14:6
And I saw another angel flying in midheaven, having eternal good news to proclaim to those seated upon the earth and to every nation and tribe and tongue and people,
  Revelation 8:13 — verbal parallel: Revelation 8:13 likewise has a messenger “flying in midheaven” addressing those on the earth; there an eagle announces woes rather than good news.
    Revelation 8:13 And I saw, and heard one eagle flying in midheaven saying with a loud voice, “Woe, woe, woe to those dwelling upon the earth, because of the remaining voices of the trumpet of the three angels who are about to sound!”
  Revelation 13:7 — repeated formula: Revelation 13:7 lists “every tribe and people and tongue and nation” as under the beast’s authority; in 14:6 the same peoples receive the eternal good news.
    Revelation 13:7 And it was given to it to make war with the saints and conquer them, and authority was given to it over every tribe and people and tongue and nation.
  Mark 16:15 — shared commission: Jesus commands, “proclaim the gospel to all creation,” as the angel has “good news to proclaim” to every nation. Mark’s commission is given to the disciples, not to an angel.
    Mark 16:15 He said to them, “Go into the whole world and proclaim the gospel to all creation.

Revelation 14:7
saying in a loud voice, “Fear God and give Him glory, because the hour of His judgment hath come, and worship the One who made heaven and the earth and sea and springs of waters.”
  Exodus 20:11 — verbal allusion: The commandment states that “the Lord made heaven and earth and the sea,” the creation formula on which the angel bases his call to worship. Revelation adds “springs of waters.”
    Exodus 20:11 For in six days the Lord made heaven and earth and the sea and everything in them, and rested on the seventh day. Therefore the Lord blessed the seventh day and made it holy.
  Nehemiah 9:6 — verbal echo: Esdras confesses that God made heaven, “the earth and everything in it, the seas,” and that heaven’s armies “worship Thee,” combining creation and worship as 14:7 does.
    Nehemiah 9:6 Esdras said, “Thou alone art the Lord. Thou madest heaven, the heaven of heaven and all their array, the earth and everything in it, the seas and everything in them. Thou givest life to all things, and the armies of heaven worship Thee.
  Revelation 15:4 — parallel worship call: Revelation 15:4 asks, “Who shall not fear, Lord, and glorify Thy name,” and says the nations “shall come and worship,” matching the angel’s “Fear God and give Him glory … and worship.”
    Revelation 15:4 Who shall not fear, Lord, and glorify Thy name, because Thou alone art holy? For all the nations shall come and worship before Thee, because Thy righteous acts have been manifested.”

Revelation 14:8
And another, a second angel, followed, saying, “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great, who hath made all the nations drink from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality.”
  Revelation 18:2–18:3 — verbal parallel: Revelation 18:2–3 repeats nearly word for word: “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great” and “all the nations have drunk from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality.”
    Revelation 18:2 And he cried with a mighty voice, saying, “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great, and she became a dwelling of demons and a prison of every unclean spirit and a prison of every unclean bird and a prison of every unclean and hated beast,
    Revelation 18:3 because all the nations have drunk from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality, and the kings of the earth committed sexual immorality with her, and the merchants of the earth became rich from the power of her luxury.”
  Isaiah 21:9 — verbal allusion: Isaiah’s watchman reports, “Babylon has fallen,” with her statues shattered, the announcement repeated by the second angel.
    Isaiah 21:9 and behold, he himself comes, a rider of a two-horse team.” He answered and said, “Babylon has fallen, and all her statues and handmade objects were shattered to the ground.”
  Jeremiah 28:7–28:8 — shared image: Jeremiah describes Babylon as a cup from which “Nations drank her wine,” and says “Suddenly Babylon fell,” combining the fall and the nations’ drinking as in 14:8. Jeremiah calls the cup the Lord’s rather than linking the wine to sexual immorality.
    Jeremiah 28:7 Babylon was a golden cup in the Lord’s hand, making the whole earth drunk. Nations drank her wine; therefore they were shaken.
    Jeremiah 28:8 Suddenly Babylon fell and was shattered. Lament her! Take resin for her corruption, if somehow she may be healed.

Revelation 14:9
And another angel, a third, followed them, saying in a loud voice, “If anyone worshippeth the beast and its image, and receiveth a mark upon his forehead or upon his hand,
  Revelation 14:11 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 14:11 completes the third angel’s warning about “those worshipping the beast and its image, and anyone who receiveth the mark,” describing their unending torment.
    Revelation 14:11 And the smoke of their torment goeth up for ages of ages, and they have no rest day and night, those worshipping the beast and its image, and anyone who receiveth the mark of its name.”
  Revelation 14:6–14:8 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 14:6–8 introduces the first and second angels; 14:9 continues the sequence with “another angel, a third.”
    Revelation 14:6 And I saw another angel flying in midheaven, having eternal good news to proclaim to those seated upon the earth and to every nation and tribe and tongue and people,
    Revelation 14:7 saying in a loud voice, “Fear God and give Him glory, because the hour of His judgment hath come, and worship the One who made heaven and the earth and sea and springs of waters.”
    Revelation 14:8 And another, a second angel, followed, saying, “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great, who hath made all the nations drink from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality.”

Revelation 14:10
he too will drink from the wine of God’s fury, prepared unmixed in the cup of His wrath, and will be tormented in fire and sulfur before holy angels and before the Lamb.
  Psalms 74:9 — verbal allusion: The psalm says “in the Lord’s hand is a cup of unmixed wine” of which “all the earth’s sinners will drink,” matching the wine “prepared unmixed in the cup of His wrath.” The psalm also speaks of mixture and dregs.
    Psalms 74:9 For in the Lord’s hand is a cup of unmixed wine, full of mixture; and He tilted from this into that, but its dregs were not emptied out; all the earth’s sinners will drink.
  Psalms 10:6 — shared image: The psalm says “fire, sulfur” are “the portion of their cup” for sinners, combining cup, fire and sulfur as 14:10 does.
    Psalms 10:6 He will rain traps upon sinners; fire, sulfur, and a storm wind are the portion of their cup.
  Revelation 16:19 — verbal parallel: Revelation 16:19 speaks of “the cup of the wine of the fury of His wrath” given to Babylon, the same wording as in 14:10, applied to the city rather than to individual worshippers.
    Revelation 16:19 And the great city became three parts, and the cities of the nations fell. And Babylon the great was remembered before God, to give her the cup of the wine of the fury of His wrath.

Revelation 14:11
And the smoke of their torment goeth up for ages of ages, and they have no rest day and night, those worshipping the beast and its image, and anyone who receiveth the mark of its name.”
  Isaiah 34:10 — verbal allusion: Isaiah says of Edom’s land that its burning will continue “night and day” and “her smoke will rise upward” for an everlasting time, the image applied to the beast’s worshippers.
    Isaiah 34:10 night and day. It will not be quenched for an everlasting time, and her smoke will rise upward. For generations and a long time she will be deserted.
  Revelation 19:3 — verbal parallel: Revelation 19:3 says of Babylon, “her smoke goeth up for the ages of the ages,” the same expression used in 14:11.
    Revelation 19:3 And a second time they said, “Hallelujah! And her smoke goeth up for the ages of the ages.”
  Revelation 20:10 — parallel judgment: Revelation 20:10 says the devil, the beast and the false prophet “will be tormented day and night for the ages of the ages,” matching the unceasing torment “day and night” in 14:11.
    Revelation 20:10 And the devil who deceived them was thrown into the lake of fire and sulfur, where also are the beast and the false prophet, and they will be tormented day and night for the ages of the ages.

Revelation 14:12
Here is the endurance of the saints, those keeping the commandments of God and the faith of Jesus.
  Revelation 13:10 — repeated formula: Revelation 13:10 ends with the same formula, “Here is the endurance and the faith of the saints.”
    Revelation 13:10 If anyone is for captivity, into captivity he goeth; if anyone is to be killed by a sword, by a sword he must be killed. Here is the endurance and the faith of the saints.
  Revelation 12:17 — verbal parallel: Revelation 12:17 describes the woman’s offspring as “those keeping the commandments of God and holding the testimony of Jesus,” the same description of the saints with “testimony” in place of “faith.”
    Revelation 12:17 And the dragon was enraged at the woman, and went away to make war with the rest of her offspring, those keeping the commandments of God and holding the testimony of Jesus.

Revelation 14:13
And I heard a voice from heaven saying, “Write: Blessed are the dead who die in the Lord from now on.” “Yea,” saith the Spirit, “so that they may rest from their labours, for their works follow with them.”
  Revelation 19:9 — repeated formula: Revelation 19:9 has the same command and beatitude form, “Write: Blessed are …,” there for those called to the Lamb’s marriage supper.
    Revelation 19:9 And he saith unto me, “Write: Blessed are those called to the supper of the Lamb’s marriage.” And he saith unto me, “These are true words of God.”
  Hebrews 4:9–4:11 — verbal echo: Hebrews says the one who entered God’s rest “rested from his own works,” paralleling the dead who “rest from their labours.” Hebrews speaks of a Sabbath rest that remains for God’s people, not explicitly of the dead.
    Hebrews 4:9 So then, a Sabbath rest remains for the people of God.
    Hebrews 4:10 For the one who entered His rest also rested from his own works, just as God did from His own.
    Hebrews 4:11 Therefore let us be diligent to enter that rest, so that no one may fall in the same pattern of disobedience.
  Wisdom of Solomon 4:7 — shared image: Wisdom says that a righteous person who dies early “will be at rest,” the same rest promised to the dead who die in the Lord.
    Wisdom of Solomon 4:7 But if a righteous person dies early, he will be at rest.

Revelation 14:14
And I saw, and behold, a white cloud, and upon the cloud One sitting like a son of man, having upon His head a golden crown and in His hand a sharp sickle.
  Daniel 7:13 — verbal allusion: Daniel sees “upon the clouds of heaven, one like a son of man,” the figure John sees sitting “upon the cloud … like a son of man.” Daniel does not mention a crown or a sickle.
    Daniel 7:13 I watched in the night vision, and there, upon the clouds of heaven, one like a son of man was coming. He was present as an Ancient of Days, and those standing by were present with him.
  Revelation 1:13 — same title of Christ: Revelation 1:13 describes “One like a son of man” among the lampstands, using the same title for the figure who appears here with a golden crown and sickle.
    Revelation 1:13 and in the midst of the lampstands One like a son of man, clothed in a robe reaching to the feet and girded around the chest with a golden belt.
  Joel 4:12–4:13 — shared image: Joel calls, “Send out sickles,” where the Lord will sit to judge the nations, the harvest-judgment imagery behind the sickle in the hand of the One on the cloud.
    Joel 4:12 Let all the nations be roused and come up to the Valley of Jehoshaphat, because there I will sit to judge all the surrounding nations.
    Joel 4:13 Send out sickles, because the vintage is at hand! Enter and tread, because the winepress is full; the vats overflow, because their evils have multiplied.

Revelation 14:15
And another angel came out of the temple, crying with a loud voice to the One sitting upon the cloud, “Send Thy sickle and reap, because the hour to reap hath come, because the harvest of the earth hath dried.”
  Joel 4:13 — verbal allusion: Joel’s command “Send out sickles, because the vintage is at hand” is the pattern for “Send Thy sickle and reap, because the hour to reap hath come.” Joel speaks of the vintage and winepress, which Revelation takes up in 14:18–20.
    Joel 4:13 Send out sickles, because the vintage is at hand! Enter and tread, because the winepress is full; the vats overflow, because their evils have multiplied.
  Mark 4:29 — verbal echo: In the parable the farmer “sends in the sickle, because the harvest has arrived,” using the same sequence of sickle and ripe harvest. The parable concerns the growth of the kingdom rather than judgment.
    Mark 4:29 When the crop is ready, he immediately sends in the sickle, because the harvest has arrived.”
  Matthew 13:39 — shared image: Jesus explains that “the harvest is the consummation of the age” and “the reapers are angels,” the end-time harvest image in which an angel here calls for the reaping.
    Matthew 13:39 the enemy who sowed them is the devil; the harvest is the consummation of the age; and the reapers are angels.

Revelation 14:16
And the One sitting upon the cloud cast His sickle upon the earth, and the earth was reaped.
  Revelation 14:14 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 14:14 introduces the “One sitting upon the cloud” holding “a sharp sickle,” the figure who now casts the sickle on the earth.
    Revelation 14:14 And I saw, and behold, a white cloud, and upon the cloud One sitting like a son of man, having upon His head a golden crown and in His hand a sharp sickle.
  Revelation 14:19 — parallel action: Revelation 14:19 repeats the action with an angel who “cast his sickle into the earth,” this time gathering the vine for the winepress of God’s fury.
    Revelation 14:19 And the angel cast his sickle into the earth and gathered the vine of the earth, and threw it into the great winepress of God’s fury.

Revelation 14:17
And another angel came out of the temple in heaven, himself also having a sharp sickle.
  Revelation 14:14–14:15 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 14:14–15 shows the One on the cloud with “a sharp sickle” and an angel coming “out of the temple”; 14:17 introduces another angel from the temple, “himself also having a sharp sickle.”
    Revelation 14:14 And I saw, and behold, a white cloud, and upon the cloud One sitting like a son of man, having upon His head a golden crown and in His hand a sharp sickle.
    Revelation 14:15 And another angel came out of the temple, crying with a loud voice to the One sitting upon the cloud, “Send Thy sickle and reap, because the hour to reap hath come, because the harvest of the earth hath dried.”
  Revelation 14:18 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 14:18 addresses this angel as “the one having the sharp sickle” and commands him to gather the clusters of the vine.
    Revelation 14:18 And another angel came from the altar, the one having authority over the fire, and called with a loud voice to the one having the sharp sickle, saying, “Send thy sharp sickle and gather the clusters of the vine of the earth, because its grapes have ripened.”

Revelation 14:18
And another angel came from the altar, the one having authority over the fire, and called with a loud voice to the one having the sharp sickle, saying, “Send thy sharp sickle and gather the clusters of the vine of the earth, because its grapes have ripened.”
  Joel 4:13 — verbal allusion: Joel’s “Send out sickles, because the vintage is at hand!” lies behind “Send thy sharp sickle and gather the clusters … because its grapes have ripened.” Joel goes on to the trodden winepress, as Revelation does in 14:19–20.
    Joel 4:13 Send out sickles, because the vintage is at hand! Enter and tread, because the winepress is full; the vats overflow, because their evils have multiplied.
  Revelation 14:15–14:16 — parallel vision: Revelation 14:15–16 has the same pattern: an angel cries “with a loud voice” to the one with the sickle, “Send Thy sickle and reap,” and the earth is reaped. The first is a reaping of “the harvest of the earth”; the second is a gathering of “the clusters of the vine of the earth.”
    Revelation 14:15 And another angel came out of the temple, crying with a loud voice to the One sitting upon the cloud, “Send Thy sickle and reap, because the hour to reap hath come, because the harvest of the earth hath dried.”
    Revelation 14:16 And the One sitting upon the cloud cast His sickle upon the earth, and the earth was reaped.

Revelation 14:19
And the angel cast his sickle into the earth and gathered the vine of the earth, and threw it into the great winepress of God’s fury.
  Deuteronomy 32:32–32:33 — shared image: Deuteronomy speaks of a wicked people’s “vine” as “of the vine of Sodom,” with grapes of gall and a bitter “cluster,” the image of a corrupt vine taken up in “the vine of the earth.” Deuteronomy describes the vine’s fruit, not its harvest or a winepress.
    Deuteronomy 32:32 For their vine is of the vine of Sodom, and their tendril is of Gomorrah; their grape is a grape of gall, their cluster is bitterness.
    Deuteronomy 32:33 Their wine is the venom of serpents, and the incurable venom of asps.

Revelation 14:20
And the winepress was trodden outside the city, and blood came out of the winepress as far as the horses’ bridles, for a thousand six hundred stadia.
  Isaiah 63:1–63:3 — shared image: In Isaiah the Lord’s garments are red as from “a trodden winepress”: He trampled the nations “in wrath” and “brought their blood down to earth.” This is the image of a winepress of judgment yielding blood, as in 14:20.
    Isaiah 63:1 Who is this arriving from Edom, with redness of garments from Bosor, so beautiful in His robe, with force and strength? “I speak righteousness and saving judgment.”
    Isaiah 63:2 Why are Thy garments red and Thy clothes as though from a trodden winepress?
