    Isaiah 63:3 “I am full of what has been trodden. No man from the nations is with Me. I trampled them in wrath, crushed them like earth, and brought their blood down to earth.
  Lamentations 1:15 — shared image: Lamentations says “The Lord trod a winepress” against Daughter Judah, using the treading of the winepress as a picture of God’s judgment. There it falls on Judah, not on the nations.
    Lamentations 1:15 The Lord removed all my strong men from my midst. He called a time against me to crush my chosen men. The Lord trod a winepress for the virgin Daughter Judah; for these things I weep.

Revelation 15:1
And I saw another sign in heaven, great and marvellous: seven angels having seven plagues, the last ones, because in them the fury of God was completed.
  Revelation 15:6–15:7 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 15:6–7 continues the vision: “the seven angels having the seven plagues” come out of the temple and receive “seven golden bowls full of the fury of God.”
    Revelation 15:6 and the seven angels having the seven plagues came out of the temple, clothed in pure, bright linen and girded around their chests with golden belts.
    Revelation 15:7 And one of the four living creatures gave the seven angels seven golden bowls full of the fury of God, who liveth for the ages of the ages.
  Revelation 21:9 — verbal parallel: Revelation 21:9 refers back to “the seven angels having the seven bowls full of the seven last plagues,” repeating the description from 15:1.
    Revelation 21:9 And one of the seven angels having the seven bowls full of the seven last plagues came and spoke with me, saying, “Come, I will show thee the bride, the wife of the Lamb.”
  Leviticus 26:21 — shared image: God warns that He will “add seven blows” upon those who will not obey, the sevenfold stroke of judgment that Revelation presents as seven plagues.
    Leviticus 26:21 And if after these things you walk crookedly and will not obey Me, I will add seven blows upon you according to your sins.

Revelation 15:2
And I saw something like a glassy sea mixed with fire, and those conquering the beast and its image and the number of its name standing upon the glassy sea, having lyres of God.
  Revelation 4:6 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 4:6 first shows “something like a glassy sea” before the throne; 15:2 sees it again, now “mixed with fire” with the victors standing on it.
    Revelation 4:6 And before the throne was something like a glassy sea, like crystal. And in the midst of the throne and around the throne were four living creatures, full of eyes in front and behind.
  Revelation 5:8 — shared image: In 5:8 the living creatures and elders each hold “a lyre” in worship before the Lamb, as the victors in 15:2 hold “lyres of God.”
    Revelation 5:8 And when He took the scroll, the four living creatures and the twenty-four elders fell before the Lamb, each having a lyre and golden bowls full of incense, which are the prayers of the saints.

Revelation 15:3
And they sing the song of Moses, the slave of God, and the song of the Lamb, saying, “Great and marvellous are Thy works, Lord God Almighty; righteous and true are Thy ways, O King of the nations.
  Psalms 110:2 — verbal echo: The psalm says, “Great are the works of the Lord,” echoed in the song “Great and marvellous are Thy works.”
    Psalms 110:2 Great are the works of the Lord, searched out for all His purposes.
  Psalms 144:17 — verbal echo: The psalm says “The Lord is righteous in all His ways,” matching “righteous and true are Thy ways.”
    Psalms 144:17 The Lord is righteous in all His ways and holy in all His works.
  Psalms 138:14 — verbal echo: The psalmist says “wondrous are Thy works,” paralleling “marvellous are Thy works.” The psalm praises God for how the speaker was made.
    Psalms 138:14 I will thank Thee, for I was made fearfully wonderful; wondrous are Thy works, and my soul knows very well.

Revelation 15:4
Who shall not fear, Lord, and glorify Thy name, because Thou alone art holy? For all the nations shall come and worship before Thee, because Thy righteous acts have been manifested.”
  Psalms 85:9 — verbal allusion: The psalm says, “All the nations Thou madest will come and bow down before Thee, Lord, and glorify Thy name,” which the song closely reproduces: “glorify Thy name … all the nations shall come and worship before Thee.”
    Psalms 85:9 All the nations Thou madest will come and bow down before Thee, Lord, and glorify Thy name,
  Revelation 14:7 — internal cross-reference: The first angel calls, “Fear God and give Him glory … and worship,” the response that 15:4 says the nations will finally give.
    Revelation 14:7 saying in a loud voice, “Fear God and give Him glory, because the hour of His judgment hath come, and worship the One who made heaven and the earth and sea and springs of waters.”
  Malachi 1:11 — shared image: Malachi says the Lord’s name “has been glorified among the nations” and is great among them, matching the expectation that all nations will glorify His name.
    Malachi 1:11 “For from the sun’s rising to its setting, My name has been glorified among the nations, and in every place incense is brought to My name, together with a pure sacrifice, because My name is great among the nations,” says the Lord Almighty.

Revelation 15:5
And after these things I saw, and the temple of the tent of the testimony in heaven was opened,
  Revelation 11:19 — parallel vision: Both verses describe the temple in heaven being “opened.” In 11:19 the ark of His covenant is then seen; here the opening leads to the coming out of the seven angels with the seven plagues.
    Revelation 11:19 And God’s temple in heaven was opened, and the ark of His covenant was seen in His temple; and there were lightnings and voices and thunders and an earthquake and great hail.
  Numbers 1:50 — shared title: Revelation calls the heavenly sanctuary “the temple of the tent of the testimony,” using the same title Numbers gives the wilderness “Tent of Testimony” served by the Levites. Numbers speaks of the earthly tent and its furnishings, not a temple in heaven.
    Numbers 1:50 And you shall appoint the Levites over the Tent of Testimony, and over all its furnishings, and over all that is therein. They shall carry the Tent and all its furnishings, and they shall minister therein, and shall camp round about the Tent.

Revelation 15:6
and the seven angels having the seven plagues came out of the temple, clothed in pure, bright linen and girded around their chests with golden belts.
  Revelation 1:13 — shared image: The angels are “girded around their chests with golden belts,” as the One like a son of man is “girded around the chest with a golden belt.” The angels wear pure, bright linen, whereas the son of man wears a robe reaching to the feet.
    Revelation 1:13 and in the midst of the lampstands One like a son of man, clothed in a robe reaching to the feet and girded around the chest with a golden belt.
  Revelation 15:1 — internal cross-reference: Both verses speak of the “seven angels having seven plagues.” 15:1 introduces them as a sign in heaven; 15:6 shows them coming out of the temple.
    Revelation 15:1 And I saw another sign in heaven, great and marvellous: seven angels having seven plagues, the last ones, because in them the fury of God was completed.

Revelation 15:7
And one of the four living creatures gave the seven angels seven golden bowls full of the fury of God, who liveth for the ages of the ages.
  Revelation 21:9 — internal cross-reference: Both verses speak of the seven angels with seven bowls full of judgement: here “seven golden bowls full of the fury of God,” there “the seven bowls full of the seven last plagues.” In 21:9 one of these angels says to the seer, “Come, I will show thee the bride, the wife of the Lamb.”
    Revelation 21:9 And one of the seven angels having the seven bowls full of the seven last plagues came and spoke with me, saying, “Come, I will show thee the bride, the wife of the Lamb.”
  Revelation 5:8 — verbal parallel: Both scenes pair the four living creatures with “golden bowls full of” something. In 5:8 the bowls are full of incense, “the prayers of the saints,” whereas here they are full of “the fury of God.”
    Revelation 5:8 And when He took the scroll, the four living creatures and the twenty-four elders fell before the Lamb, each having a lyre and golden bowls full of incense, which are the prayers of the saints.
  Revelation 4:6–4:9 — same divine title: The throne vision introduces the four living creatures and describes God as “the One living for the ages of the ages”; 15:7 uses the same description of God and has one of the four living creatures hand over the bowls.
    Revelation 4:6 And before the throne was something like a glassy sea, like crystal. And in the midst of the throne and around the throne were four living creatures, full of eyes in front and behind.
    Revelation 4:7 And the first living creature was like a lion, and the second living creature like a calf, and the third living creature had a face like a human being’s, and the fourth living creature was like an eagle flying.
    Revelation 4:8 And the four living creatures, each one of them having six wings, are full of eyes around and within; and they have no rest day and night, saying, “Holy, holy, holy, Lord God Almighty, the One who was and who is and who is coming.”
    Revelation 4:9 And whenever the living creatures give glory and honour and thanksgiving to the One sitting upon the throne, the One living for the ages of the ages,

Revelation 15:8
And the temple was filled with smoke from the glory of God and from His power, and no one was able to enter the temple until the seven plagues of the seven angels were completed.
  Exodus 40:34–40:35 — shared image: In both, the sanctuary is filled with the glory of God and no one can go in: “Moses could not enter the Tent of Testimony” because the tent “was filled with the glory of the Lord.” Exodus speaks of a cloud; Revelation speaks of smoke and adds that entry is barred until the seven plagues are completed.
    Exodus 40:34 The cloud covered the Tent of Testimony, and the tent was filled with the glory of the Lord.
    Exodus 40:35 Moses could not enter the Tent of Testimony because the cloud overshadowed it and the tent was filled with the glory of the Lord.
  Isaiah 6:4 — shared image: In both visions the sanctuary is filled with smoke: “the house was filled with smoke” and “the temple was filled with smoke.” In Isaiah this happens as the lintel is lifted “at the sound of their cry,” while Revelation says the smoke comes “from the glory of God and from His power.”
    Isaiah 6:4 And the lintel was lifted at the sound of their cry, and the house was filled with smoke.
  1 Kings 8:10–8:11 — shared image: At the temple’s dedication the Lord’s glory filled the house and the priests “could not stand to minister,” just as in Revelation no one could enter the temple. Kings speaks of a cloud rather than smoke.
    1 Kings 8:10 When the priests came out of the Holy Place, the cloud filled the house.
    1 Kings 8:11 The priests could not stand to minister because of the cloud, for the Lord’s glory filled the house.

Revelation 16:1
And I heard a loud voice from the temple saying to the seven angels, “Go and pour out the seven bowls of God’s fury into the earth.”
  Revelation 15:5–15:8 — internal cross-reference: The voice sends out the seven angels who in 15:5–8 came out of the temple and received “seven golden bowls full of the fury of God.” 16:1 orders that these bowls now be poured out on the earth.
    Revelation 15:5 And after these things I saw, and the temple of the tent of the testimony in heaven was opened,
    Revelation 15:6 and the seven angels having the seven plagues came out of the temple, clothed in pure, bright linen and girded around their chests with golden belts.
    Revelation 15:7 And one of the four living creatures gave the seven angels seven golden bowls full of the fury of God, who liveth for the ages of the ages.
    Revelation 15:8 And the temple was filled with smoke from the glory of God and from His power, and no one was able to enter the temple until the seven plagues of the seven angels were completed.
  Revelation 16:17 — internal cross-reference: The bowl sequence opens and closes with “a loud voice” from the temple. In 16:1 it orders the bowls poured out; in 16:17, after the seventh bowl, it declares “It hath happened.”
    Revelation 16:17 And the seventh poured out his bowl upon the air, and a loud voice came out of the temple from the throne, saying, “It hath happened.”
  Jeremiah 10:25 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah prays, “Pour Thy fury upon nations,” and Revelation commands the angels to “pour out the seven bowls of God’s fury into the earth.” Jeremiah asks this against nations that devoured Jacob, while Revelation’s command extends to the earth.
    Jeremiah 10:25 Pour Thy fury upon nations who do not know Thee and upon generations who did not call on Thy name, because they devoured Jacob and consumed him and made his pasture desolate.

Revelation 16:2
And the first went and poured out his bowl into the earth, and a bad and painful sore came upon the people having the mark of the beast and worshipping its image.
  Exodus 9:8–9:11 — shared image: Like the Egyptian plague in which boils broke out “on people,” the first bowl brings a painful sore upon people. In Exodus the boils come from scattered furnace soot and fall on Egypt’s people and animals, while in Revelation the sore falls on those bearing the beast’s mark.
    Exodus 9:8 The Lord said to Moses and Aaron, “Take handfuls of soot from a furnace, and let Moses scatter it toward heaven before Pharaoh and his attendants.
    Exodus 9:9 Let it become fine dust over all the land of Egypt. Boils, with blisters breaking out, shall come upon people and four-footed animals, on people and four-footed animals throughout Egypt.”
    Exodus 9:10 He took soot from the furnace before Pharaoh. Moses scattered it toward heaven, and boils with blisters broke out on people and four-footed animals.
    Exodus 9:11 The sorcerers could not stand before Moses because of the boils, for the boils were on the sorcerers and throughout the land of Egypt.
  Revelation 16:11 — internal cross-reference: The sores of the first bowl are mentioned again after the fifth, when people blaspheme God “because of their pains and their sores” and do not repent.
    Revelation 16:11 and blasphemed the God of heaven because of their pains and their sores, and did not repent of their works.
  Revelation 13:15–13:18 — internal cross-reference: The sore falls on “the people having the mark of the beast and worshipping its image,” the people described in 13:15–17, where the beast’s image is worshipped and all are made to receive the mark.
    Revelation 13:15 And it was given to it to give breath to the beast’s image, so that the beast’s image might both speak and cause as many as would not worship the beast’s image to be killed.
    Revelation 13:16 And it causeth all—the small and the great, and the rich and the poor, and the free and the slaves—to be given a mark upon their right hand or upon their forehead,
    Revelation 13:17 so that no one may be able to buy or sell except the one having the mark, the name of the beast or the number of its name.
    Revelation 13:18 Here is wisdom. Let the one who hath understanding calculate the number of the beast, for it is a human number, and its number is six hundred sixty-six.

Revelation 16:3
And the second poured out his bowl into the sea, and it became blood like a dead person’s, and every living soul died, the things in the sea.
  Revelation 8:8–8:9 — parallel judgment: The second trumpet and the second bowl both strike the sea: it “became blood” and creatures in the sea died. The trumpet affects a third of the sea and its creatures, while the bowl kills “every living soul” in the sea.
    Revelation 8:8 And the second angel sounded, and something like a great mountain burning with fire was thrown into the sea; and a third of the sea became blood,
    Revelation 8:9 and a third of the creatures in the sea, those having life, died, and a third of the ships were destroyed.
  Exodus 7:17–7:21 — shared image: Water turned to blood and the death of the creatures in it recall the Egyptian plague, where “all the water in the river turned to blood” and “the fish in the river died.” Exodus concerns Egypt’s river and waters, while Revelation concerns the sea.
    Exodus 7:17 Thus says the Lord: “By this you shall know that I am the Lord: behold, I strike with the staff in My hand upon the water in the river, and it shall be turned to blood.
    Exodus 7:18 The fish in the river will die, the river will stink, and the Egyptians will be unable to drink its water.”’”
    Exodus 7:19 And the Lord said to Moses, “Say to Aaron your brother, ‘Take your staff, and stretch out your hand over the waters of Egypt, over their rivers, and over their canals, and over their marshes, and over all their standing water, and it shall become blood.’” And there was blood throughout all the land of Egypt, both in wood and in stone.
    Exodus 7:20 Moses and Aaron did as the Lord commanded. He raised his staff and struck the river’s water before Pharaoh and his attendants, and all the water in the river turned to blood.
    Exodus 7:21 The fish in the river died, and the river stank. The Egyptians could not drink its water, and there was blood throughout the land of Egypt.
  Psalms 104:29 — shared image: The psalm summarises the Egyptian plague: “He turned their waters into blood and killed their fish,” the same pairing of blood and death of water creatures found in the second bowl.
    Psalms 104:29 He turned their waters into blood and killed their fish.

Revelation 16:4
And the third poured out his bowl into the rivers and the springs of the waters, and they became blood.
  Exodus 7:17–7:20 — shared image: As in Egypt, where the waters over “their rivers” were to “become blood,” the third bowl turns the rivers and springs to blood. Exodus is limited to the waters of Egypt.
    Exodus 7:17 Thus says the Lord: “By this you shall know that I am the Lord: behold, I strike with the staff in My hand upon the water in the river, and it shall be turned to blood.
    Exodus 7:18 The fish in the river will die, the river will stink, and the Egyptians will be unable to drink its water.”’”
    Exodus 7:19 And the Lord said to Moses, “Say to Aaron your brother, ‘Take your staff, and stretch out your hand over the waters of Egypt, over their rivers, and over their canals, and over their marshes, and over all their standing water, and it shall become blood.’” And there was blood throughout all the land of Egypt, both in wood and in stone.
    Exodus 7:20 Moses and Aaron did as the Lord commanded. He raised his staff and struck the river’s water before Pharaoh and his attendants, and all the water in the river turned to blood.
  Revelation 8:10–8:11 — parallel judgment: The third trumpet and the third bowl both fall on “the rivers and … the springs of the waters.” The trumpet affects a third of them and makes them bitter as wormwood, whereas the bowl turns them to blood.
    Revelation 8:10 And the third angel sounded, and a great star fell from heaven, burning like a lamp, and fell upon a third of the rivers and upon the springs of the waters.
    Revelation 8:11 And the name of the star is called Wormwood; and a third of the waters became wormwood, and many people died from the waters because they were made bitter.
  Revelation 16:5 — internal cross-reference: The next verse continues the scene: “the angel of the waters” declares God righteous for judging “these things,” meaning the waters turned to blood.
    Revelation 16:5 And I heard the angel of the waters saying, “Thou art righteous, the One who is and who was, the Holy One, because Thou didst judge these things.

Revelation 16:5
And I heard the angel of the waters saying, “Thou art righteous, the One who is and who was, the Holy One, because Thou didst judge these things.
  Revelation 11:17 — same divine title: Both address God as “the One who is and who was.” In 11:17 the speakers give thanks because He has taken His great power and reigned; here the angel of the waters declares Him righteous because He judged “these things.”
    Revelation 11:17 saying, “We give Thee thanks, Lord God Almighty, the One who is and who was, because Thou hast taken Thy great power and reigned.
  Revelation 16:7 — internal cross-reference: The angel’s confession that God is righteous in His judging is echoed at once by the altar: “true and righteous are Thy judgments.”
    Revelation 16:7 And I heard the altar saying, “Yea, Lord God Almighty, true and righteous are Thy judgments.”
  Psalms 144:17 — verbal echo: The psalm pairs the same attributes, calling the Lord “righteous in all His ways and holy in all His works,” as the angel calls God “righteous” and “the Holy One.” The psalm states this generally, while Revelation applies it to a specific judgment.
    Psalms 144:17 The Lord is righteous in all His ways and holy in all His works.

Revelation 16:6
Because they poured out the blood of saints and prophets, Thou hast also given them blood to drink; they are worthy of it.”
  Revelation 18:24 — internal cross-reference: Both verses speak of the blood of “saints and prophets” (18:24: “prophets and saints”). 18:24 locates that blood in Babylon, while 16:6 speaks of those who poured it out being given blood to drink.
    Revelation 18:24 And in her was found blood of prophets and saints and of all those slaughtered upon the earth.”
  Isaiah 49:26 — shared image: In both, God makes oppressors drink blood as punishment: “drink their own blood like new wine” and “given them blood to drink.” Isaiah’s oppressors afflicted Jacob, while Revelation’s shed the blood of saints and prophets.
    Isaiah 49:26 Those who afflicted you will eat their own flesh, drink their own blood like new wine, and become drunk. All flesh will perceive that I am the Lord who rescued you and upholds Jacob’s strength.”
  Revelation 6:10–6:11 — answered plea: The souls under the altar ask how long before God will “judge and avenge our blood upon those dwelling on the earth.” 16:6 describes God giving blood to drink to those who poured out the blood of saints.
    Revelation 6:10 And they cried with a loud voice, saying, “How long, O Master, holy and true, before Thou dost judge and avenge our blood upon those dwelling on the earth?”
    Revelation 6:11 And a white robe was given to each of them, and they were told to rest for a little time yet, until their fellow slaves and their brothers, who were about to be killed as they themselves had been, were also complete in number.

Revelation 16:7
And I heard the altar saying, “Yea, Lord God Almighty, true and righteous are Thy judgments.”
  Revelation 19:2 — verbal parallel: Both use the same praise of God’s judgments: “true and righteous are Thy judgments” and “His judgments are true and righteous.” 19:2 applies it to the judgment of the great prostitute.
    Revelation 19:2 because His judgments are true and righteous, because He judged the great prostitute who corrupted the earth by her sexual immorality, and avenged the blood of His slaves from her hand.”
  Revelation 15:3–15:4 — verbal parallel: The song of Moses and of the Lamb also addresses the “Lord God Almighty” and confesses “righteous and true are Thy ways.” The song praises God’s ways and works generally, while 16:7 speaks of His judgments.
    Revelation 15:3 And they sing the song of Moses, the slave of God, and the song of the Lamb, saying, “Great and marvellous are Thy works, Lord God Almighty; righteous and true are Thy ways, O King of the nations.
    Revelation 15:4 Who shall not fear, Lord, and glorify Thy name, because Thou alone art holy? For all the nations shall come and worship before Thee, because Thy righteous acts have been manifested.”
  Revelation 6:9 — internal cross-reference: The altar speaks here, and in 6:9 the altar is where the souls of those slaughtered for the word of God were seen. 16:7 does not itself identify the speaker beyond “the altar.”
    Revelation 6:9 And when He opened the fifth seal, I saw beneath the altar the souls of those slaughtered because of the word of God and because of the testimony they held.

Revelation 16:8
And the fourth poured out his bowl upon the sun, and it was given to it to scorch people with fire.
  Revelation 8:12 — parallel judgment: The fourth trumpet and the fourth bowl both affect the sun. The trumpet darkens a third of the sun, while the bowl makes the sun “scorch people with fire.”
    Revelation 8:12 And the fourth angel sounded, and a third of the sun and a third of the moon and a third of the stars were struck, so that a third of them might be darkened and the day might not shine for a third of it, and the night likewise.
  Revelation 7:16 — contrasting image: Those standing before the throne are promised that the sun will not fall upon them, “nor any burning heat,” whereas under the fourth bowl the sun is given power to scorch people with fire.
    Revelation 7:16 They will hunger no more or thirst any more, nor will the sun fall upon them, nor any burning heat,

Revelation 16:9
And people were scorched with great heat, and blasphemed the name of God, who hath authority over these plagues, and did not repent to give Him glory.
  Revelation 16:10–16:11 — internal cross-reference: After the fifth bowl the same reaction is repeated: people “blasphemed the God of heaven” and “did not repent.” Here the cause is great heat; there it is pains and sores.
    Revelation 16:10 And the fifth poured out his bowl upon the beast’s throne, and its kingdom became darkened, and they were chewing their tongues because of the pain,
    Revelation 16:11 and blasphemed the God of heaven because of their pains and their sores, and did not repent of their works.
  Revelation 9:20 — parallel response: After the trumpet plagues the survivors likewise “did not even repent,” as people under the fourth bowl “did not repent.” In 9:20 the unrepented sins are named as idolatry; 16:9 adds blasphemy of God’s name.
    Revelation 9:20 And the rest of the people, who were not killed by these plagues, did not even repent of the works of their hands, so as not to worship the demons and the idols of gold and silver and bronze and stone and wood, which can neither see nor hear nor walk.
  Revelation 14:7 — contrasting response: The angel calls all to “Fear God and give Him glory,” and the scorched people refuse to “give Him glory.” 14:7 is a summons; 16:9 records its refusal.
    Revelation 14:7 saying in a loud voice, “Fear God and give Him glory, because the hour of His judgment hath come, and worship the One who made heaven and the earth and sea and springs of waters.”

Revelation 16:10
And the fifth poured out his bowl upon the beast’s throne, and its kingdom became darkened, and they were chewing their tongues because of the pain,
  Exodus 10:21–10:23 — shared image: As the ninth Egyptian plague brought darkness on Pharaoh’s land, the fifth bowl darkens the beast’s kingdom. Exodus specifies three days of darkness and light for Israel, details Revelation does not give.
    Exodus 10:21 And the Lord said to Moses, “Stretch out your hand toward heaven, and let there be darkness upon the land of Egypt, darkness that may be felt.”
    Exodus 10:22 Moses stretched out his hand toward heaven, and there was darkness, gloom, and a tempest throughout the land of Egypt for three days.
    Exodus 10:23 No one saw his brother for three days, and no one rose from his bed for three days. But all the sons of Israel had light everywhere they lived.
  Revelation 13:2–13:4 — internal cross-reference: The bowl is poured on “the beast’s throne,” the throne the dragon gave to the beast in 13:2 along with his power and great authority.
    Revelation 13:2 And the beast I saw was like a leopard, and its feet like a bear’s, and its mouth like a lion’s mouth. And the dragon gave it his power and his throne and great authority.
    Revelation 13:3 And one of its heads was as slaughtered to death, and its wound of death was healed. And the whole earth marvelled after the beast,
    Revelation 13:4 and they worshipped the dragon because he gave authority to the beast, and they worshipped the beast, saying, “Who is like the beast, and who is able to wage war with it?”

Revelation 16:11
and blasphemed the God of heaven because of their pains and their sores, and did not repent of their works.
  Revelation 16:9 — internal cross-reference: The fourth and fifth bowls end alike: people blaspheme God and do not repent. 16:9 adds that they refuse “to give Him glory.”
    Revelation 16:9 And people were scorched with great heat, and blasphemed the name of God, who hath authority over these plagues, and did not repent to give Him glory.
  Revelation 16:2 — internal cross-reference: The “sores” for which people blaspheme recall the “bad and painful sore” of the first bowl, which came upon those having the mark of the beast.
    Revelation 16:2 And the first went and poured out his bowl into the earth, and a bad and painful sore came upon the people having the mark of the beast and worshipping its image.
  Revelation 11:13 — contrasting response: Both verses use the title “the God of heaven.” After the earthquake in 11:13 the survivors “gave glory to the God of heaven,” whereas here people blaspheme Him and do not repent.
    Revelation 11:13 And in that hour a great earthquake occurred, and a tenth of the city fell, and seven thousand human names were killed in the earthquake; and the rest became fearful and gave glory to the God of heaven.

Revelation 16:12
And the sixth poured out his bowl upon the great river Euphrates, and its water was dried up, so that the way of the kings from the rising of the sun might be prepared.
  Revelation 9:14 — internal cross-reference: The sixth trumpet and the sixth bowl both centre on “the great river Euphrates.” In 9:14 four angels bound there are loosed; here its water is dried up.
    Revelation 9:14 saying to the sixth angel, the one who hath the trumpet, “Loose the four angels bound at the great river Euphrates.”
  Isaiah 11:15 — shared image: Isaiah describes the Lord bringing His hand upon “the river” so “that one may pass through it in sandals,” as the Euphrates is dried so that a way may be prepared. Isaiah’s passage serves a returning people, Revelation’s the kings from the east.
    Isaiah 11:15 The Lord will lay Egypt’s sea waste and bring His hand upon the river with a violent wind. He will strike seven ravines so that one may pass through it in sandals.
  Isaiah 41:25 — verbal echo: Isaiah speaks of one roused “from the sun’s rising,” as Revelation speaks of “the kings from the rising of the sun.” Isaiah does not mention the drying of a river.
    Isaiah 41:25 But I roused the one from the north and the one from the sun’s rising; they will be called by My name. Let rulers come, and like a potter’s clay, as a potter tramples clay, so you will be trampled.

Revelation 16:13
And I saw from the dragon’s mouth and from the beast’s mouth and from the false prophet’s mouth three unclean spirits like frogs.
  Revelation 16:14 — internal cross-reference: The next verse explains the three frog-like spirits: they are “spirits of demons performing signs” who go out to gather the kings for war.
    Revelation 16:14 For they are spirits of demons performing signs, which go out to the kings of the whole inhabited world to gather them for the war of the great day of God Almighty.
  Revelation 19:20 — internal cross-reference: The beast and the false prophet named here appear together again at their capture, where the false prophet is described as performing signs and deceiving, and both are thrown into the lake of fire.
    Revelation 19:20 And the beast was seized, and with it the false prophet who performed the signs before it, by which he deceived those receiving the beast’s mark and those worshipping its image. The two were thrown alive into the lake of fire burning with sulfur.
  Psalms 104:30 — shared image: Frogs recall the Egyptian plague, when the land “teemed with frogs in the chambers of their kings.” In Revelation the frogs are an image for unclean spirits, not a literal plague.
    Psalms 104:30 Their land teemed with frogs in the chambers of their kings.

Revelation 16:14
For they are spirits of demons performing signs, which go out to the kings of the whole inhabited world to gather them for the war of the great day of God Almighty.
  Revelation 16:16 — internal cross-reference: The gathering for war announced here is carried out two verses later: “they gathered them to the place called in Hebrew Harmagedon.”
    Revelation 16:16 And they gathered them to the place called in Hebrew Harmagedon.
  Revelation 20:8 — parallel event: In both, a deceiving power goes out “to gather them for war” on a worldwide scale. In 20:8 it is Satan gathering Gog and Magog after the thousand years, whereas here demonic spirits gather the kings.
    Revelation 20:8 and will go out to deceive the nations at the four corners of the earth, Gog and Magog, to gather them for war, whose number is like the sand of the sea.
  Revelation 6:17 — shared phrase: Both name the coming judgment as “the great day”: “the great day of Their wrath” and “the great day of God Almighty.”
    Revelation 6:17 because the great day of Their wrath hath come, and who is able to stand?”

Revelation 16:15
“Behold, I am coming like a thief. Blessed is the one who watcheth and keepeth his garments, so that he may not walk naked and they see his shame.”
  Revelation 3:3–3:4 — same saying of Christ: The warning to Sardis uses the same words: “I will come like a thief,” with a call to watch, and then speaks of those who “did not defile their garments.”
    Revelation 3:3 Therefore remember how thou hast received and heard, and keep it and repent. Therefore if thou watch not, I will come like a thief, and thou shalt certainly not know at what hour I will come upon thee.
    Revelation 3:4 But thou hast a few names in Sardis who did not defile their garments, and they shall walk with Me in white, because they are worthy.
  Revelation 3:18 — shared image: The counsel to Laodicea to buy white garments so “the shame of thy nakedness may not be manifested” matches the blessing on keeping one’s garments so as not to walk naked and have one’s shame seen.
    Revelation 3:18 I counsel thee to buy from Me gold refined by fire, that thou mayest become rich, and white garments, that thou mayest clothe thyself and the shame of thy nakedness may not be manifested, and eye salve to anoint thine eyes, that thou mayest see.
  Matthew 24:42–24:43 — parallel teaching: Jesus’ call to “keep watch” because the householder does not know when “the thief was coming” lies behind the blessing on the one who “watcheth.” Matthew uses a parable of a house broken into, without the image of garments.
    Matthew 24:42 So keep watch, because you do not know on what day your Lord comes.
    Matthew 24:43 But know this: if the householder had known in what watch the thief was coming, he would have kept watch and would not have allowed his house to be broken into.

Revelation 16:16
And they gathered them to the place called in Hebrew Harmagedon.
  Judges 5:19 — place association: Judges describes kings who “drew up in battle” and fought “by Megiddo’s water,” a battle of kings at the place whose name resembles Harmagedon. Revelation gives only the Hebrew name and does not explain it.
    Judges 5:19 Kings came and drew up in battle; then Canaan’s kings fought at Taanach by Megiddo’s water. They took no greedy gain of silver.
  2 Kings 23:29–23:30 — place association: King Josiah is killed by Pharaoh Nechao “at Mageddo,” another clash of kings at the place whose name resembles Harmagedon. The connection rests on the similarity of the place-names.
    2 Kings 23:29 In his days Pharaoh Nechao king of Egypt went up against the king of the Assyrians to the River Euphrates. Josiah went to meet him, and Nechao killed him at Mageddo when he saw him.
    2 Kings 23:30 His servants transported him dead from Mageddo, brought him to Jerusalem, and buried him in his grave in David’s city. The people of the land took Joachas son of Josiah, anointed him, and made him king in his father’s place.
  Revelation 19:17–19:21 — internal cross-reference: The gathering is completed when the beast, “the kings of the earth and their armies” are “gathered to wage war” against the rider on the horse. 19:19 does not name the place.
    Revelation 19:17 And I saw one angel standing in the sun, and he cried with a loud voice, saying to all the birds flying in midheaven, “Come, gather for the great supper of God,
    Revelation 19:18 that ye may eat flesh of kings and flesh of commanders of thousands and flesh of mighty ones and flesh of horses and of those sitting upon them, and flesh of all, both free and slaves and small and great.”
    Revelation 19:19 And I saw the beast and the kings of the earth and their armies gathered to wage war with the One sitting upon the horse and with His army.
    Revelation 19:20 And the beast was seized, and with it the false prophet who performed the signs before it, by which he deceived those receiving the beast’s mark and those worshipping its image. The two were thrown alive into the lake of fire burning with sulfur.
    Revelation 19:21 And the rest were killed with the sword of the One sitting upon the horse, the sword coming out of His mouth, and all the birds were filled with their flesh.

Revelation 16:17
And the seventh poured out his bowl upon the air, and a loud voice came out of the temple from the throne, saying, “It hath happened.”
  Revelation 16:1 — internal cross-reference: The bowl sequence that began with “a loud voice from the temple” ends with “a loud voice” coming “out of the temple from the throne.”
    Revelation 16:1 And I heard a loud voice from the temple saying to the seven angels, “Go and pour out the seven bowls of God’s fury into the earth.”
  Revelation 21:6 — parallel declaration: Both are short divine declarations of completion: “It hath happened” and “They have come to be.” 16:17 closes the bowl judgments, while 21:6 refers to the making of all things new.
    Revelation 21:6 And He said unto me, “They have come to be. I am the Alpha and the Omega, the beginning and the end. To the one who thirsteth I will give freely from the spring of the water of life.
  Revelation 11:15 — parallel sequence: At the seventh trumpet, as at the seventh bowl, the final element of the series is followed by loud voices in heaven. The trumpet voices proclaim the kingdom, while the bowl voice says “It hath happened.”
    Revelation 11:15 And the seventh angel sounded, and loud voices arose in heaven, saying, “The kingdom of the world hath become the kingdom of our Lord and of His Christ, and He will reign for the ages of the ages.”

Revelation 16:18
And there were lightnings and voices and thunders, and a great earthquake occurred, such as had not occurred since human beings came to be upon the earth—so mighty an earthquake, so great.
  Revelation 11:19 — parallel sequence: Both describe “lightnings and voices and thunders” with an earthquake at the close of a series of seven, and both include great hail (16:21). 16:18 stresses that this earthquake is unmatched.
    Revelation 11:19 And God’s temple in heaven was opened, and the ark of His covenant was seen in His temple; and there were lightnings and voices and thunders and an earthquake and great hail.
  Daniel 12:1 — verbal allusion: Daniel’s day of affliction “such as has not occurred since they came into being” has the same unmatched-in-history formula as the earthquake “such as had not occurred since human beings came to be upon the earth.” Daniel speaks of affliction, not an earthquake.
    Daniel 12:1 “At that hour Michael will pass by, the great angel who stands over the sons of your people. That will be a day of affliction such as has not occurred since they came into being until that day. On that day all the people found inscribed in the book will be exalted.
  Revelation 8:5 — parallel sequence: The same cluster of “thunders and voices and lightnings and an earthquake” follows the angel’s censer being thrown to earth before the trumpets.
    Revelation 8:5 And the angel took the censer and filled it from the fire of the altar, and threw it to the earth; and there were thunders and voices and lightnings and an earthquake.

Revelation 16:19
And the great city became three parts, and the cities of the nations fell. And Babylon the great was remembered before God, to give her the cup of the wine of the fury of His wrath.
  Revelation 18:5 — internal cross-reference: Babylon being “remembered before God” is restated in 18:5: “God remembered her wrongdoings.”
    Revelation 18:5 because her sins have piled up as far as heaven, and God remembered her wrongdoings.
  Revelation 18:10 — internal cross-reference: 18:10 identifies “the great city” as “Babylon, the mighty city,” matching the pairing here of “the great city” with “Babylon the great,” and announces that her judgment came.
    Revelation 18:10 standing far away because of the fear of her torment, saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, Babylon, the mighty city, because in one hour thy judgment came!”
  Revelation 18:2 — internal cross-reference: The judgment on “Babylon the great” announced here is proclaimed as accomplished in 18:2: “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great.”
    Revelation 18:2 And he cried with a mighty voice, saying, “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great, and she became a dwelling of demons and a prison of every unclean spirit and a prison of every unclean bird and a prison of every unclean and hated beast,

Revelation 16:20
And every island fled, and mountains were not found.
  Revelation 6:14 — parallel judgment: At the sixth seal “every mountain and island were moved from their places,” as at the seventh bowl “every island fled, and mountains were not found.”
    Revelation 6:14 And heaven withdrew like a scroll being rolled up, and every mountain and island were moved from their places.
  Revelation 20:11 — verbal parallel: Before the great white throne earth and heaven “fled, and no place was found for them,” using the same verbs “fled” and “found” as the islands and mountains here.
    Revelation 20:11 And I saw a great white throne and the One sitting upon it, from whose face the earth and heaven fled, and no place was found for them.

Revelation 16:21
And great hail, about a talent in weight, cometh down from heaven upon people; and people blasphemed God because of the plague of hail, because its plague is exceedingly great.
  Revelation 11:19 — parallel sequence: The seventh trumpet sequence ends with “an earthquake and great hail,” as the seventh bowl brings a great earthquake (16:18) followed by “great hail.”
    Revelation 11:19 And God’s temple in heaven was opened, and the ark of His covenant was seen in His temple; and there were lightnings and voices and thunders and an earthquake and great hail.
  Joshua 10:11 — shared image: In both, hail comes down “from heaven” in judgment. In Joshua the Lord hurls hailstones from heaven on the fleeing armies, and more die from the hailstones than by the sword; in Revelation great hail, about a talent in weight, comes down from heaven upon people, who then blaspheme God because of the plague of hail.
    Joshua 10:11 As they fled before the sons of Israel down the descent of Horonin, the Lord hurled hailstones from heaven upon them as far as Azekah. More died from the hailstones than the sons of Israel killed with the sword in battle.
  Ezekiel 38:21–38:22 — shared image: God’s judgment on Gog includes “hailstones,” as the final bowl brings great hail. Ezekiel also lists fire and sulfur, which Revelation does not mention here.
    Ezekiel 38:21 I will summon every fear against him, says the Lord. A man’s sword will be against his brother.
    Ezekiel 38:22 I will judge him with death, blood, flooding rain and hailstones. I will rain fire and sulfur upon him, all those with him and the many nations with him.

Revelation 17:1
And one of the seven angels having the seven bowls came and spoke with me, saying, “Come, I will show thee the judgment of the great prostitute sitting upon many waters,
  Revelation 21:9 — parallel vision: Both visions are introduced in nearly the same words: one of the seven angels having the seven bowls “came and spoke with me, saying, ‘Come, I will show thee.’” Here the angel shows the great prostitute; in 21:9 he shows the bride, the wife of the Lamb.
    Revelation 21:9 And one of the seven angels having the seven bowls full of the seven last plagues came and spoke with me, saying, “Come, I will show thee the bride, the wife of the Lamb.”
  Revelation 19:2 — internal cross-reference: The “judgment of the great prostitute” promised here is celebrated as accomplished in 19:2: God “judged the great prostitute.”
    Revelation 19:2 because His judgments are true and righteous, because He judged the great prostitute who corrupted the earth by her sexual immorality, and avenged the blood of His slaves from her hand.”
  Jeremiah 28:13 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah addresses those “who dwell beside many waters” and announces their end, as the prostitute is “sitting upon many waters.” The verse shown does not itself name the one addressed.
    Jeremiah 28:13 who dwell beside many waters and her abundance of treasures. Your end has truly come to your inward parts.

Revelation 17:2
with whom the kings of the earth committed sexual immorality, and those dwelling on the earth became drunk from the wine of her sexual immorality.”
  Revelation 18:3 — internal cross-reference: 18:3 repeats the charge almost word for word: “the kings of the earth committed sexual immorality with her,” and the nations drank “the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality.”
    Revelation 18:3 because all the nations have drunk from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality, and the kings of the earth committed sexual immorality with her, and the merchants of the earth became rich from the power of her luxury.”
  Revelation 14:8 — internal cross-reference: The second angel’s announcement already speaks of Babylon making “all the nations drink from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality.”
    Revelation 14:8 And another, a second angel, followed, saying, “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great, who hath made all the nations drink from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality.”
  Jeremiah 28:7 — shared image: Jeremiah portrays Babylon as a cup “making the whole earth drunk,” and “Nations drank her wine,” as the earth’s inhabitants became drunk from her wine. Jeremiah does not speak of sexual immorality.
    Jeremiah 28:7 Babylon was a golden cup in the Lord’s hand, making the whole earth drunk. Nations drank her wine; therefore they were shaken.

Revelation 17:3
And he carried me away in the Spirit into a wilderness. And I saw a woman sitting upon a scarlet beast, full of names of blasphemy, having seven heads and ten horns.
  Revelation 21:10 — parallel vision: The two contrasting visions open alike: “he carried me away in the Spirit.” Here John is taken into a wilderness to see the prostitute; in 21:10 to a great and high mountain to see the holy city.
    Revelation 21:10 And he carried me away in the Spirit upon a great and high mountain, and showed me the holy city Jerusalem coming down out of heaven from God,
  Revelation 12:3 — shared image: The scarlet beast has “seven heads and ten horns,” as does the great fiery red dragon. The dragon also has seven diadems, and the beast is full of names of blasphemy.
    Revelation 12:3 And another sign was seen in heaven, and behold, a great fiery red dragon, having seven heads and ten horns, and upon his heads seven diadems.
  Daniel 7:20 — shared image: Daniel’s beast has “ten horns upon its head” and a horn “speaking great things,” as the beast here has ten horns and is full of names of blasphemy. Daniel’s beast has ten horns but no seven heads.
    Daniel 7:20 and of its ten horns upon its head and the single other that sprouted, through which three fell out. That horn had eyes and a mouth speaking great things, and its appearance surpassed the others.

Revelation 17:4
And the woman was clothed in purple and scarlet, and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearls, having in her hand a golden cup full of abominations and the unclean things of her sexual immorality.
  Revelation 18:16 — internal cross-reference: The great city is lamented as “clothed in fine linen and purple and scarlet and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearl,” nearly the same description given of the woman.
    Revelation 18:16 saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, clothed in fine linen and purple and scarlet and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearl,
  Jeremiah 28:7 — shared image: Jeremiah calls Babylon “a golden cup” from which the nations drank, as the woman holds “a golden cup.” In Jeremiah the cup is in the Lord’s hand; here it is in hers and full of abominations.
    Jeremiah 28:7 Babylon was a golden cup in the Lord’s hand, making the whole earth drunk. Nations drank her wine; therefore they were shaken.
  Revelation 18:6–18:7 — internal cross-reference: The woman’s cup reappears in the call for judgment: “in the cup in which she mixed, mix a double portion for her.”
    Revelation 18:6 Repay her as she also repaid, and double the double according to her works; in the cup in which she mixed, mix a double portion for her.
    Revelation 18:7 As much as she glorified herself and lived in luxury, so much torment and mourning give her, because in her heart she saith, ‘I sit a queen, and I am not a widow, and I will certainly not see mourning.’

Revelation 17:5
And upon her forehead a name was written, a mystery: “Babylon the great, the mother of prostitutes and of the abominations of the earth.”
  Revelation 17:7 — internal cross-reference: The name on her forehead is called “a mystery,” and the angel then promises to explain “the mystery of the woman.”
    Revelation 17:7 And the angel said unto me, “Why didst thou marvel? I will tell thee the mystery of the woman and of the beast carrying her, which hath the seven heads and the ten horns.
  Revelation 14:8 — same title: The name “Babylon the great” first appears in the second angel’s cry, “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great,” where she makes the nations drink the wine of her sexual immorality.
    Revelation 14:8 And another, a second angel, followed, saying, “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great, who hath made all the nations drink from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality.”
  Revelation 18:2 — same title: The fall of “Babylon the great” is announced in the same words in 18:2, where she is described as a dwelling of demons and unclean things.
    Revelation 18:2 And he cried with a mighty voice, saying, “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great, and she became a dwelling of demons and a prison of every unclean spirit and a prison of every unclean bird and a prison of every unclean and hated beast,

Revelation 17:6
And I saw the woman drunk from the blood of the saints and from the blood of the witnesses of Jesus. And seeing her, I marvelled with great amazement.
  Revelation 18:20–18:24 — internal cross-reference: The charge is repeated at Babylon’s fall: “in her was found blood of prophets and saints.” 18:24 adds “all those slaughtered upon the earth.”
    Revelation 18:20 Rejoice over her, heaven and saints and apostles and prophets, because God hath judged your case against her.
    Revelation 18:21 And one mighty angel lifted a stone like a great millstone and threw it into the sea, saying, “In this way Babylon the great city will be thrown with a rush, and will certainly not be found any more.
    Revelation 18:22 And the sound of lyre players and musicians and flute players and trumpeters will certainly not be heard in thee any more, and any craftsman of any craft will certainly not be found in thee any more, and the sound of a mill will certainly not be heard in thee any more,
    Revelation 18:23 and the light of a lamp will certainly not shine in thee any more, and the voice of bridegroom and bride will certainly not be heard in thee any more, because thy merchants were the great ones of the earth, because by thy sorcery all the nations were deceived.
    Revelation 18:24 And in her was found blood of prophets and saints and of all those slaughtered upon the earth.”
  Revelation 16:6 — verbal parallel: Both speak of the “blood of saints” and of drinking blood. In 17:6 the woman is drunk with the saints’ blood; in 16:6 God gives those who shed it blood to drink as punishment.
    Revelation 16:6 Because they poured out the blood of saints and prophets, Thou hast also given them blood to drink; they are worthy of it.”
  Revelation 2:13 — shared term: Antipas is called “My witness, My faithful one, who was killed,” an example of the “witnesses of Jesus” whose blood the woman drinks. 2:13 names Satan’s throne, not the woman, as the setting.
    Revelation 2:13 ‘I know where thou dwellest, where Satan’s throne is, and thou holdest fast My name and didst not deny My faith, even in the days of Antipas, My witness, My faithful one, who was killed among you, where Satan dwelleth.

Revelation 17:7
And the angel said unto me, “Why didst thou marvel? I will tell thee the mystery of the woman and of the beast carrying her, which hath the seven heads and the ten horns.
  Revelation 17:1–17:6 — internal cross-reference: The “mystery of the woman and of the beast” refers back to the vision of 17:1–6, where the woman sits upon a scarlet beast with seven heads and ten horns and John marvels at her.
    Revelation 17:1 And one of the seven angels having the seven bowls came and spoke with me, saying, “Come, I will show thee the judgment of the great prostitute sitting upon many waters,
    Revelation 17:2 with whom the kings of the earth committed sexual immorality, and those dwelling on the earth became drunk from the wine of her sexual immorality.”
    Revelation 17:3 And he carried me away in the Spirit into a wilderness. And I saw a woman sitting upon a scarlet beast, full of names of blasphemy, having seven heads and ten horns.
    Revelation 17:4 And the woman was clothed in purple and scarlet, and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearls, having in her hand a golden cup full of abominations and the unclean things of her sexual immorality.
    Revelation 17:5 And upon her forehead a name was written, a mystery: “Babylon the great, the mother of prostitutes and of the abominations of the earth.”
    Revelation 17:6 And I saw the woman drunk from the blood of the saints and from the blood of the witnesses of Jesus. And seeing her, I marvelled with great amazement.
  Revelation 17:8 — internal cross-reference: The explanation promised here begins in the next verse with the beast that “was and is not,” at which those dwelling on the earth “will marvel.”
    Revelation 17:8 The beast thou sawest was and is not, and is about to come up from the abyss, and goeth into destruction. And those dwelling upon the earth, whose name hath not been written in the book of life from the foundation of the world, will marvel when they see the beast, because it was and is not and will be present.

Revelation 17:8
The beast thou sawest was and is not, and is about to come up from the abyss, and goeth into destruction. And those dwelling upon the earth, whose name hath not been written in the book of life from the foundation of the world, will marvel when they see the beast, because it was and is not and will be present.
  Revelation 17:11 — internal cross-reference: 17:11 repeats the description of “the beast that was and is not” and that “goeth into destruction,” adding that it is an eighth and from the seven.
    Revelation 17:11 And the beast that was and is not is itself also an eighth, and is from the seven, and goeth into destruction.
  Revelation 11:7 — same figure: Both describe “the beast coming up from the abyss.” In 11:7 it makes war on the two witnesses and kills them.
    Revelation 11:7 And when they complete their testimony, the beast coming up from the abyss will make war with them and conquer them and kill them.
  Revelation 20:15 — shared image: Those whose names are not written “in the book of life” marvel at the beast here, and in 20:15 anyone “not found written in the book of life” is thrown into the lake of fire.
    Revelation 20:15 And if anyone was not found written in the book of life, he was thrown into the lake of fire.

Revelation 17:9
Here is the mind that hath wisdom. The seven heads are seven mountains, upon which the woman sitteth, and they are seven kings.
  Revelation 13:18 — verbal parallel: Both open an interpretive riddle about the beast with a call to wisdom: “Here is wisdom” and “Here is the mind that hath wisdom.” 13:18 concerns the beast’s number, 17:9 its seven heads.
    Revelation 13:18 Here is wisdom. Let the one who hath understanding calculate the number of the beast, for it is a human number, and its number is six hundred sixty-six.
  Revelation 17:3 — internal cross-reference: The seven heads explained here are those of the scarlet beast on which the woman was seen sitting in 17:3.
    Revelation 17:3 And he carried me away in the Spirit into a wilderness. And I saw a woman sitting upon a scarlet beast, full of names of blasphemy, having seven heads and ten horns.
  Revelation 17:7 — internal cross-reference: The angel had promised to explain the mystery of the beast “which hath the seven heads and the ten horns”; 17:9 begins that explanation with the heads.
    Revelation 17:7 And the angel said unto me, “Why didst thou marvel? I will tell thee the mystery of the woman and of the beast carrying her, which hath the seven heads and the ten horns.

Revelation 17:10
The five fell, the one is, the other hath not yet come, and whenever he cometh, he must remain a little while.
  Revelation 17:9 — internal cross-reference: 17:9 identifies the beast’s seven heads as “seven kings”; 17:10 continues that interpretation, counting five who “fell,” one who “is,” and one not yet come.
    Revelation 17:9 Here is the mind that hath wisdom. The seven heads are seven mountains, upon which the woman sitteth, and they are seven kings.
  Revelation 17:11 — internal cross-reference: After the seven kings of 17:10, the beast itself is called “an eighth” that “is from the seven,” completing the same count.
    Revelation 17:11 And the beast that was and is not is itself also an eighth, and is from the seven, and goeth into destruction.

Revelation 17:11
And the beast that was and is not is itself also an eighth, and is from the seven, and goeth into destruction.
  Revelation 17:8 — internal cross-reference: 17:11 restates 17:8: the beast “was and is not” and “goeth into destruction.” 17:11 adds that it is an eighth and is from the seven.
    Revelation 17:8 The beast thou sawest was and is not, and is about to come up from the abyss, and goeth into destruction. And those dwelling upon the earth, whose name hath not been written in the book of life from the foundation of the world, will marvel when they see the beast, because it was and is not and will be present.

Revelation 17:12
And the ten horns thou sawest are ten kings, who have not yet received a kingdom, but receive authority as kings for one hour with the beast.
  Daniel 7:24 — interpretive parallel: Daniel interprets the beast’s horns in the same way: “ten horns, ten kings will arise.” Daniel adds another king who humbles three, while Revelation says the ten receive authority “for one hour with the beast.”
    Daniel 7:24 As for the kingdom’s ten horns, ten kings will arise, and another king will arise after them. He will surpass the earlier ones in evils and humble three kings.
  Revelation 12:18–13:1 — same figure: The beast from the sea has “ten horns,” with ten diadems on them, as here the beast’s “ten horns” are ten kings. 13:1 does not interpret the horns.
    Revelation 12:18 And he stood upon the sand of the sea.
    Revelation 13:1 And I saw a beast coming up from the sea, having ten horns and seven heads, and upon its horns ten diadems, and upon its heads names of blasphemy.
  Revelation 17:16 — internal cross-reference: The same “ten horns thou sawest” later turn with the beast against the prostitute and make her desolate.
    Revelation 17:16 And the ten horns thou sawest and the beast, these will hate the prostitute and make her desolate and naked, and eat her flesh and burn her up with fire.

Revelation 17:13
These have one purpose, and give their power and authority to the beast.
  Revelation 17:17 — internal cross-reference: 17:17 repeats that the kings “do one purpose and give their kingdom to the beast,” and adds that God put it into their hearts to do His purpose.
    Revelation 17:17 For God put it into their hearts to do His purpose, and to do one purpose and give their kingdom to the beast, until the words of God are completed.

Revelation 17:14
These will wage war with the Lamb, and the Lamb will conquer them, because He is Lord of lords and King of kings, and those with Him are called and chosen and faithful.”
  Deuteronomy 10:17 — same divine title: Deuteronomy calls the Lord God “God of gods and Lord of lords,” a title Revelation gives to the Lamb as “Lord of lords and King of kings.”
    Deuteronomy 10:17 For the Lord your God, He is God of gods and Lord of lords, the great and mighty and fearful God, who shows no partiality, neither takes a bribe;
  1 Timothy 6:15 — parallel title: Paul calls God “the King of those who reign and Lord of those who rule,” the same pairing of kingship and lordship over rulers as “Lord of lords and King of kings.” The wording differs.
    1 Timothy 6:15 which the blessed and only Sovereign will display at the proper times, the King of those who reign and Lord of those who rule,
  Matthew 22:14 — shared terms: Those with the Lamb are “called and chosen and faithful,” using the two terms of Jesus’ saying “many are called, but few chosen.” Matthew distinguishes the called from the chosen; Revelation applies both to the same group.
    Matthew 22:14 For many are called, but few chosen.”

Revelation 17:15
And he saith unto me, “The waters thou sawest, where the prostitute sitteth, are peoples and multitudes and nations and tongues.
  Revelation 17:1 — internal cross-reference: The waters explained here are the “many waters” on which the great prostitute was said to sit in 17:1.
    Revelation 17:1 And one of the seven angels having the seven bowls came and spoke with me, saying, “Come, I will show thee the judgment of the great prostitute sitting upon many waters,
  Revelation 13:7–13:8 — verbal parallel: The beast is given authority over “every tribe and people and tongue and nation,” a list closely matching “peoples and multitudes and nations and tongues” here.
    Revelation 13:7 And it was given to it to make war with the saints and conquer them, and authority was given to it over every tribe and people and tongue and nation.
    Revelation 13:8 And all those dwelling upon the earth will worship it, each whose name hath not been written in the book of life of the Lamb slaughtered from the foundation of the world.
  Jeremiah 28:13 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah addresses those “who dwell beside many waters,” the image Revelation interprets as peoples and nations. The verse shown does not itself name the one addressed.
    Jeremiah 28:13 who dwell beside many waters and her abundance of treasures. Your end has truly come to your inward parts.

Revelation 17:16
And the ten horns thou sawest and the beast, these will hate the prostitute and make her desolate and naked, and eat her flesh and burn her up with fire.
  Revelation 18:8 — internal cross-reference: The burning of the prostitute is announced again in 18:8: “she will be burned up with fire.” 18:8 attributes the judgment to the Lord God, while 17:16 names the ten horns and the beast as its agents.
    Revelation 18:8 For this reason in one day her plagues will come, death and mourning and famine, and she will be burned up with fire, because the Lord God who judged her is mighty.”
  Revelation 18:19 — internal cross-reference: The desolation of the prostitute is lamented in 18:19: “in one hour she was made desolate!”
    Revelation 18:19 And they threw dust upon their heads and cried, weeping and mourning, saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, in which all who had ships in the sea became rich from her costly wealth, because in one hour she was made desolate!”
  Ezekiel 23:45–23:49 — shared image: Ezekiel describes adulteresses punished by a gathered multitude who kill and “burn their houses,” as the beast and the horns turn on the prostitute and burn her. Ezekiel speaks of stoning and swords, not of eating her flesh.
    Ezekiel 23:45 Righteous men will punish them with the punishment of an adulteress and the punishment of bloodshed, for they are adulteresses, and blood is on their hands.
    Ezekiel 23:46 Thus says the Lord, the Lord: Bring a multitude against them and set turmoil and plunder among them,
    Ezekiel 23:47 and stone them with the multitude’s stones and pierce them with their swords. They will kill their sons and daughters and burn their houses.
    Ezekiel 23:48 I will turn impiety away from the land. All women will be disciplined and certainly will not act according to their impieties.
    Ezekiel 23:49 Your impiety will be brought upon you, and you will receive the sins of your reflections. You will know that I am the Lord.”

Revelation 17:17
For God put it into their hearts to do His purpose, and to do one purpose and give their kingdom to the beast, until the words of God are completed.
  Revelation 17:13 — internal cross-reference: 17:13 had said the kings “have one purpose, and give their power and authority to the beast”; 17:17 explains that God put this purpose into their hearts.
    Revelation 17:13 These have one purpose, and give their power and authority to the beast.
  Ezra 7:27 — verbal parallel: Ezra blesses the Lord “who put it thus in the king’s heart,” as Revelation says “God put it into their hearts to do His purpose.” In Ezra the purpose is to glorify the Lord’s house, here to give their kingdom to the beast.
    Ezra 7:27 Blessed be the Lord, the God of our fathers, who put it thus in the king’s heart, to glorify the Lord’s house in Jerusalem,
  Revelation 10:7 — shared theme of completion: Both speak of what God has announced being “completed”: “the mystery of God will be completed” and “until the words of God are completed.”
    Revelation 10:7 But in the days of the voice of the seventh angel, when he is about to sound, the mystery of God will be completed, as He announced the good news to His own slaves, the prophets.

Revelation 17:18
And the woman thou sawest is the great city having kingship over the kings of the earth.”
  Revelation 16:19 — internal cross-reference: The woman is identified as “the great city,” and in 16:19 “the great city” is spoken of together with “Babylon the great.”
    Revelation 16:19 And the great city became three parts, and the cities of the nations fell. And Babylon the great was remembered before God, to give her the cup of the wine of the fury of His wrath.

Revelation 18:1
After these things I saw another angel coming down from heaven, having great authority, and the earth was illuminated from his glory.
  Ezekiel 43:2 — verbal allusion: In Ezekiel “the earth flashed like radiance from the glory,” and here “the earth was illuminated from his glory.” In Ezekiel the glory is the God of Israel’s; here it belongs to an angel.
    Ezekiel 43:2 Look—the glory of the God of Israel was coming by the way of the gate facing east. The sound of the camp was like the sound of many redoubling their sound, and the earth flashed like radiance from the glory all around.
  Revelation 10:1 — parallel vision: Both introduce another angel “coming down from heaven”: in 10:1 a mighty angel with his face like the sun, here one with great authority whose glory lights the earth.
    Revelation 10:1 And I saw another mighty angel coming down from heaven, clothed with a cloud, and the rainbow upon his head, and his face like the sun, and his feet like pillars of fire.

Revelation 18:2
And he cried with a mighty voice, saying, “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great, and she became a dwelling of demons and a prison of every unclean spirit and a prison of every unclean bird and a prison of every unclean and hated beast,
  Revelation 14:8 — internal cross-reference: The same cry, “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great,” was first announced by the second angel in 14:8.
    Revelation 14:8 And another, a second angel, followed, saying, “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great, who hath made all the nations drink from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality.”
  Jeremiah 28:8 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah announces, “Suddenly Babylon fell and was shattered,” as the angel cries that Babylon is “fallen.”
    Jeremiah 28:8 Suddenly Babylon fell and was shattered. Lament her! Take resin for her corruption, if somehow she may be healed.
  Jeremiah 28:37 — shared image: Jeremiah foretells that Babylon “will become a ruin and will not be inhabited,” as here she becomes a haunt of demons and unclean creatures rather than people.
    Jeremiah 28:37 Babylon will become a ruin and will not be inhabited.

Revelation 18:3
because all the nations have drunk from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality, and the kings of the earth committed sexual immorality with her, and the merchants of the earth became rich from the power of her luxury.”
  Revelation 17:2 — internal cross-reference: 17:2 makes the same charge: “the kings of the earth committed sexual immorality” with her and the earth’s inhabitants became drunk “from the wine of her sexual immorality.”
    Revelation 17:2 with whom the kings of the earth committed sexual immorality, and those dwelling on the earth became drunk from the wine of her sexual immorality.”
  Revelation 14:8 — internal cross-reference: 14:8 uses the same phrase about the nations drinking “from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality.”
    Revelation 14:8 And another, a second angel, followed, saying, “Fallen, fallen is Babylon the great, who hath made all the nations drink from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality.”
  Jeremiah 28:7 — shared image: Jeremiah says of Babylon, “Nations drank her wine,” as here “all the nations have drunk from the wine” of Babylon. Jeremiah does not mention sexual immorality or merchants.
    Jeremiah 28:7 Babylon was a golden cup in the Lord’s hand, making the whole earth drunk. Nations drank her wine; therefore they were shaken.

Revelation 18:4
And I heard another voice from heaven saying, “Come out of her, My people, that ye may not share in her sins and may not receive from her plagues,
  Jeremiah 28:6 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah urges, “Flee from Babylon’s midst … Do not be cast away in her injustice,” matching the call to come out of her so as not to share in her sins and plagues.
    Jeremiah 28:6 Flee from Babylon’s midst; each of you save your life. Do not be cast away in her injustice, for her time of vengeance is from the Lord; He is repaying her recompense.
  Jeremiah 27:8 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah commands, “Separate yourselves from Babylon’s midst … come out,” as the heavenly voice says, “Come out of her, My people.”
    Jeremiah 27:8 Separate yourselves from Babylon’s midst and the Chaldeans’ land; come out and become like dragons before sheep.
  Isaiah 52:11 — verbal allusion: Isaiah’s “Go out from her midst; separate yourselves” and “do not touch anything unclean” parallel the call to leave so as not to share her sins. Isaiah does not name Babylon in this verse.
    Isaiah 52:11 Depart, depart; go out from there, and do not touch anything unclean. Go out from her midst; separate yourselves, you who carry the Lord’s vessels.

Revelation 18:5
because her sins have piled up as far as heaven, and God remembered her wrongdoings.
  Jeremiah 28:9 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah says of Babylon that “her judgment reached heaven,” as here “her sins have piled up as far as heaven.” Jeremiah speaks of her judgment, Revelation of her sins.
    Jeremiah 28:9 We treated Babylon, but she was not healed. Let us leave her; let each of us go to our own land, for her judgment reached heaven and rose to the stars.
  Revelation 16:19 — internal cross-reference: “God remembered her wrongdoings” restates 16:19, where Babylon the great “was remembered before God” to receive the cup of His wrath.
    Revelation 16:19 And the great city became three parts, and the cities of the nations fell. And Babylon the great was remembered before God, to give her the cup of the wine of the fury of His wrath.
  Ezra 9:6 — shared image: Ezra confesses that “our offenses have grown to heaven,” the same picture of sins rising to heaven. Ezra is confessing Israel’s own sins, not announcing judgment on another city.
    Ezra 9:6 and said, “Lord, I am ashamed and abashed to lift my face toward Thee, for our lawless deeds have multiplied above our heads and our offenses have grown to heaven.

Revelation 18:6
Repay her as she also repaid, and double the double according to her works; in the cup in which she mixed, mix a double portion for her.
  Jeremiah 27:29 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah commands, “Repay her according to her works; do to her all she did,” as Revelation says, “Repay her as she also repaid … according to her works.” Revelation adds a doubled repayment.
    Jeremiah 27:29 Give orders against Babylon to many, to everyone drawing a bow. Encamp against her all around; let none of her people escape. Repay her according to her works; do to her all she did, for she resisted the Lord, Israel’s holy God.
  Psalms 136:8 — verbal allusion: The psalm blesses the one “who will repay you the repayment you gave us,” addressing daughter Babylon, as here Babylon is to be repaid as she repaid.
    Psalms 136:8 Daughter Babylon, miserable one, happy is the one who will repay you the repayment you gave us.
  Revelation 17:4 — internal cross-reference: “The cup in which she mixed” is the golden cup full of abominations the woman held in 17:4.
    Revelation 17:4 And the woman was clothed in purple and scarlet, and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearls, having in her hand a golden cup full of abominations and the unclean things of her sexual immorality.

Revelation 18:7
As much as she glorified herself and lived in luxury, so much torment and mourning give her, because in her heart she saith, ‘I sit a queen, and I am not a widow, and I will certainly not see mourning.’
  Isaiah 47:7–47:9 — verbal allusion: Babylon’s boast in Isaiah, “I will not sit as a widow or know bereavement,” spoken “in her heart,” is closely echoed: “in her heart she saith, ‘I sit a queen, and I am not a widow.’” Isaiah adds that widowhood and childlessness will come “in one day.”
    Isaiah 47:7 You said, ‘I will rule for ever.’ You did not consider these things in your heart or remember the last things.
    Isaiah 47:8 Now hear these things, pampered one who sits confident, saying in her heart, ‘I am, and there is no other. I will not sit as a widow or know bereavement.’
    Isaiah 47:9 Now these two will come upon you suddenly, in one day: widowhood and childlessness will come suddenly upon you, amid your sorcery and the overwhelming power of your enchanters,
  Zephaniah 2:15 — verbal parallel: Zephaniah’s city, “saying in her heart, ‘I am, and there is no one after me,’” makes the same kind of self-exalting inner boast as Babylon. Zephaniah does not mention widowhood or queenship.
    Zephaniah 2:15 This is the contemptuous city dwelling in confidence, saying in her heart, “I am, and there is no one after me.” How she became a wasteland, pasture for wild animals! Everyone passing through her will hiss and wave his hands.

Revelation 18:8
For this reason in one day her plagues will come, death and mourning and famine, and she will be burned up with fire, because the Lord God who judged her is mighty.”
  Revelation 17:16 — internal cross-reference: Both verses describe the woman being burned with fire: “she will be burned up with fire” and “burn her up with fire.” Revelation 17:16 names the beast and the ten horns as the agents, while 18:8 grounds the burning in the Lord God who judged her.
    Revelation 17:16 And the ten horns thou sawest and the beast, these will hate the prostitute and make her desolate and naked, and eat her flesh and burn her up with fire.
  Jeremiah 27:34 — verbal allusion: Both oracles against Babylon ground her judgment in the strength of the Lord: “the Lord God who judged her is mighty” and “The One redeeming them is strong; the Lord Almighty is His name. He will judge a case against His adversaries.” Jeremiah speaks of Babylon’s inhabitants; Revelation applies the language to the city pictured as a woman.
    Jeremiah 27:34 The One redeeming them is strong; the Lord Almighty is His name. He will judge a case against His adversaries, to remove the land, and He will provoke Babylon’s inhabitants.

Revelation 18:9
And the kings of the earth who committed sexual immorality and lived in luxury with her will weep and mourn over her when they see the smoke of her burning,
  Revelation 18:18 — internal cross-reference: Both verses describe onlookers who “see the smoke of her burning” and lament the city. In 18:9 the mourners are the kings of the earth; in 18:18 they are the seafarers.
    Revelation 18:18 and cried as they saw the smoke of her burning, saying, “What is like the great city?”
  Revelation 18:3 — internal cross-reference: Both verses say “the kings of the earth committed sexual immorality with her” and associate her with luxury. Revelation 18:3 gives the cause of her judgment; 18:9 shows the same kings mourning her fall.
    Revelation 18:3 because all the nations have drunk from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality, and the kings of the earth committed sexual immorality with her, and the merchants of the earth became rich from the power of her luxury.”

Revelation 18:10
standing far away because of the fear of her torment, saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, Babylon, the mighty city, because in one hour thy judgment came!”
  Revelation 18:15–18:17 — parallel lament: The merchants repeat the kings’ posture and cry: they “stand far away because of fear of her torment” and say “Woe, woe, the great city … because in one hour.” The kings speak of her judgment; the merchants speak of her wealth made desolate.
    Revelation 18:15 The merchants of these things, who became rich from her, will stand far away because of fear of her torment, weeping and mourning,
    Revelation 18:16 saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, clothed in fine linen and purple and scarlet and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearl,
    Revelation 18:17 because in one hour so great a wealth was made desolate!” And every helmsman and everyone sailing from place to place, and sailors and as many as work the sea, stood far away,
