  Revelation 18:19 — parallel lament: Both laments open with “Woe, woe, the great city” and close “because in one hour.” Revelation 18:19 is spoken by seafarers who grew rich from her, and ends with her being “made desolate” rather than with her judgment.
    Revelation 18:19 And they threw dust upon their heads and cried, weeping and mourning, saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, in which all who had ships in the sea became rich from her costly wealth, because in one hour she was made desolate!”
  Jeremiah 28:8–28:9 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah speaks of Babylon falling “suddenly” and calls for lament, adding that “her judgment reached heaven.” Revelation’s “in one hour thy judgment came” over “Babylon, the mighty city” echoes the sudden fall and the judgment of the same named city.
    Jeremiah 28:8 Suddenly Babylon fell and was shattered. Lament her! Take resin for her corruption, if somehow she may be healed.
    Jeremiah 28:9 We treated Babylon, but she was not healed. Let us leave her; let each of us go to our own land, for her judgment reached heaven and rose to the stars.

Revelation 18:11
And the merchants of the earth weep and mourn over her, because no one buyeth their cargo any more:
  Revelation 18:15 — internal cross-reference: Both verses present the merchants “weeping and mourning” over her. Revelation 18:15 adds that they “became rich from her” and stand far away for fear of her torment.
    Revelation 18:15 The merchants of these things, who became rich from her, will stand far away because of fear of her torment, weeping and mourning,
  Revelation 18:3 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 18:3 introduces “the merchants of the earth” who “became rich from the power of her luxury”; 18:11 shows the same merchants mourning because no one buys their cargo any more.
    Revelation 18:3 because all the nations have drunk from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality, and the kings of the earth committed sexual immorality with her, and the merchants of the earth became rich from the power of her luxury.”

Revelation 18:12
cargo of gold and silver and precious stone and pearls and fine linen and purple and silk and scarlet, and every fragrant wood and every ivory vessel and every vessel of most precious wood and bronze and iron and marble,
  Revelation 17:4 — shared image: The cargo list repeats the adornment of the woman in 17:4: “gold and silver and precious stone and pearls … purple … scarlet” corresponds to her being clothed “in purple and scarlet, and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearls.” The city’s trade goods are thus the woman’s own finery.
    Revelation 17:4 And the woman was clothed in purple and scarlet, and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearls, having in her hand a golden cup full of abominations and the unclean things of her sexual immorality.

Revelation 18:13
and cinnamon and amomum and incense and ointment and frankincense and wine and oil and fine flour and wheat and cattle and sheep, and horses and carriages and slaves—human lives.
  Ezekiel 27:13 — verbal allusion: Both lists of a great trading city’s commerce include trade “in human lives,” and Ezekiel’s Tyre also receives “bronze vessels,” as Babylon’s cargo includes bronze in 18:12. Ezekiel names Greece and its regions as trading partners; Revelation names no partners.
    Ezekiel 27:13 Greece, all of it, and the adjoining regions traded with you in human lives, and gave bronze vessels for your commerce.

Revelation 18:14
And the fruit thy soul desired went away from thee, and all the rich and splendid things perished from thee, and they will certainly not find them any more.
  Revelation 18:21 — shared refrain: Both pronounce a permanent loss with the same formula: the desired riches “will certainly not” be found “any more,” and Babylon itself “will certainly not be found any more.”
    Revelation 18:21 And one mighty angel lifted a stone like a great millstone and threw it into the sea, saying, “In this way Babylon the great city will be thrown with a rush, and will certainly not be found any more.
  Revelation 18:11 — internal cross-reference: The fruit and splendid things that perished from Babylon correspond to the merchants’ lament in 18:11 that “no one buyeth their cargo any more.”
    Revelation 18:11 And the merchants of the earth weep and mourn over her, because no one buyeth their cargo any more:

Revelation 18:15
The merchants of these things, who became rich from her, will stand far away because of fear of her torment, weeping and mourning,
  Revelation 18:10–18:11 — parallel lament: The merchants adopt the kings’ posture, “standing far away because of the fear of her torment,” and 18:11 already shows “the merchants of the earth weep and mourn over her.” Revelation 18:15 repeats both elements as the merchants’ lament begins.
    Revelation 18:10 standing far away because of the fear of her torment, saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, Babylon, the mighty city, because in one hour thy judgment came!”
    Revelation 18:11 And the merchants of the earth weep and mourn over her, because no one buyeth their cargo any more:
  Revelation 18:3 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 18:3 states that “the merchants of the earth became rich from the power of her luxury”; 18:15 refers back to the same merchants “who became rich from her.”
    Revelation 18:3 because all the nations have drunk from the wine of the fury of her sexual immorality, and the kings of the earth committed sexual immorality with her, and the merchants of the earth became rich from the power of her luxury.”

Revelation 18:16
saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, clothed in fine linen and purple and scarlet and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearl,
  Revelation 17:4 — shared image: The city is described in the same terms as the woman in 17:4: “purple and scarlet” and “adorned with gold and precious stone and pearl.” Revelation 18:16 adds “fine linen,” which 17:4 does not mention.
    Revelation 17:4 And the woman was clothed in purple and scarlet, and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearls, having in her hand a golden cup full of abominations and the unclean things of her sexual immorality.
  Revelation 18:10–18:11 — parallel lament: The merchants’ cry “Woe, woe, the great city” repeats the kings’ lament in 18:10. The kings name “Babylon, the mighty city,” while the merchants describe her costly clothing and adornment.
    Revelation 18:10 standing far away because of the fear of her torment, saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, Babylon, the mighty city, because in one hour thy judgment came!”
    Revelation 18:11 And the merchants of the earth weep and mourn over her, because no one buyeth their cargo any more:

Revelation 18:17
because in one hour so great a wealth was made desolate!” And every helmsman and everyone sailing from place to place, and sailors and as many as work the sea, stood far away,
  Revelation 18:19 — parallel lament: Both verses lament that “in one hour” the city’s wealth was “made desolate,” and both concern those who have ships at sea. Revelation 18:19 adds that the seafarers threw dust on their heads and became rich from her costly wealth.
    Revelation 18:19 And they threw dust upon their heads and cried, weeping and mourning, saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, in which all who had ships in the sea became rich from her costly wealth, because in one hour she was made desolate!”
  Revelation 18:10–18:11 — parallel lament: The seafarers “stood far away” as the kings stand “far away” in 18:10, and both laments turn on judgment or ruin coming “in one hour.”
    Revelation 18:10 standing far away because of the fear of her torment, saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, Babylon, the mighty city, because in one hour thy judgment came!”
    Revelation 18:11 And the merchants of the earth weep and mourn over her, because no one buyeth their cargo any more:
  Isaiah 23:14 — shared image: Isaiah calls on the “ships of Carthage” to wail over the fall of Tyre’s stronghold; Revelation shows helmsmen and sailors standing far away from the fallen city. Isaiah addresses Tyre, not Babylon.
    Isaiah 23:14 Wail, ships of Carthage, because your fortress has perished!

Revelation 18:18
and cried as they saw the smoke of her burning, saying, “What is like the great city?”
  Revelation 18:9–18:10 — internal cross-reference: The seafarers see “the smoke of her burning” as the kings do in 18:9, and both groups lament “the great city.” The kings stand far away for fear of her torment; the seafarers cry, “What is like the great city?”
    Revelation 18:9 And the kings of the earth who committed sexual immorality and lived in luxury with her will weep and mourn over her when they see the smoke of her burning,
    Revelation 18:10 standing far away because of the fear of her torment, saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, Babylon, the mighty city, because in one hour thy judgment came!”
  Ezekiel 27:30–27:32 — parallel lament: Ezekiel’s mourners over Tyre “raise a cry over you with their voice and cry bitterly” and take up a lament, as the seafarers cry out over the burning city. The Ezekiel text shown does not include a “What is like” question.
    Ezekiel 27:30 They will raise a cry over you with their voice and cry bitterly; they will put earth on their head and spread ashes beneath them.
    Ezekiel 27:32 Their sons will take up a lament over you and a lamentation for you:
  Revelation 13:4 — verbal parallel: Both verses use an acclamation of incomparability: “What is like the great city?” and “Who is like the beast?” The parallel ties the lament for the city to the earlier worship of the beast.
    Revelation 13:4 and they worshipped the dragon because he gave authority to the beast, and they worshipped the beast, saying, “Who is like the beast, and who is able to wage war with it?”

Revelation 18:19
And they threw dust upon their heads and cried, weeping and mourning, saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, in which all who had ships in the sea became rich from her costly wealth, because in one hour she was made desolate!”
  Ezekiel 27:30 — verbal allusion: Ezekiel’s mourners over Tyre “cry bitterly” and “put earth on their head,” as the seafarers “threw dust upon their heads and cried.” Both laments are over a maritime trading city.
    Ezekiel 27:30 They will raise a cry over you with their voice and cry bitterly; they will put earth on their head and spread ashes beneath them.
  Revelation 18:15–18:17 — parallel lament: The merchants’ lament has the same frame as the seafarers’: “Woe, woe, the great city” and “in one hour so great a wealth was made desolate.” Both groups are said to have become rich from her.
    Revelation 18:15 The merchants of these things, who became rich from her, will stand far away because of fear of her torment, weeping and mourning,
    Revelation 18:16 saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, clothed in fine linen and purple and scarlet and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearl,
    Revelation 18:17 because in one hour so great a wealth was made desolate!” And every helmsman and everyone sailing from place to place, and sailors and as many as work the sea, stood far away,
  Revelation 18:10 — parallel lament: Both laments open “Woe, woe, the great city” and close “because in one hour.” In 18:10 the kings speak of her judgment; in 18:19 the seafarers speak of her being made desolate.
    Revelation 18:10 standing far away because of the fear of her torment, saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, Babylon, the mighty city, because in one hour thy judgment came!”

Revelation 18:20
Rejoice over her, heaven and saints and apostles and prophets, because God hath judged your case against her.
  Revelation 6:10 — answered prayer: The souls under the altar ask how long before the Master will “judge and avenge our blood”; 18:20 announces that “God hath judged your case against her.” Revelation 6:10 names those dwelling on the earth, while 18:20 names her.
    Revelation 6:10 And they cried with a loud voice, saying, “How long, O Master, holy and true, before Thou dost judge and avenge our blood upon those dwelling on the earth?”
  Revelation 19:1–19:3 — internal cross-reference: The heavenly multitude answers the call to rejoice, praising God because “He judged the great prostitute” and “avenged the blood of His slaves from her hand.” Revelation 18:20 calls heaven to rejoice; 19:1–3 records that rejoicing.
    Revelation 19:1 After these things I heard something like a loud voice of a great multitude in heaven, saying, “Hallelujah! The salvation and the glory and the power belong to our God,
    Revelation 19:2 because His judgments are true and righteous, because He judged the great prostitute who corrupted the earth by her sexual immorality, and avenged the blood of His slaves from her hand.”
    Revelation 19:3 And a second time they said, “Hallelujah! And her smoke goeth up for the ages of the ages.”
  Revelation 12:12 — verbal parallel: Both verses summon heaven to rejoice: “Rejoice over her, heaven” and “rejoice, heavens and those dwelling in them.” In 12:12 the occasion is the devil’s fall to earth; in 18:20 it is the judgment of the city.
    Revelation 12:12 For this reason rejoice, heavens and those dwelling in them. Woe to the earth and the sea, because the devil came down to you, having great fury, knowing that he hath a little time.”

Revelation 18:21
And one mighty angel lifted a stone like a great millstone and threw it into the sea, saying, “In this way Babylon the great city will be thrown with a rush, and will certainly not be found any more.
  Jeremiah 28:63–28:64 — parallel symbolic act: In both passages a stone is thrown into water as a sign of Babylon’s end: the one addressed in Jeremiah is told to bind a stone to the scroll and throw it into the Euphrates, saying “Thus Babylon will sink and certainly not rise.” Revelation’s mighty angel throws a stone like a great millstone into the sea, saying “In this way Babylon the great city will be thrown.”
    Jeremiah 28:63 When you finish reading this scroll, bind a stone to it and throw it into the Euphrates,
    Jeremiah 28:64 and say, Thus Babylon will sink and certainly not rise because of the evils I am bringing upon her.
  Revelation 18:22 — internal cross-reference: The refrain “will certainly not be found any more” in 18:21 continues in 18:22, where craftsmen “will certainly not be found in thee any more” and the sound of a mill is no longer heard.
    Revelation 18:22 And the sound of lyre players and musicians and flute players and trumpeters will certainly not be heard in thee any more, and any craftsman of any craft will certainly not be found in thee any more, and the sound of a mill will certainly not be heard in thee any more,

Revelation 18:22
And the sound of lyre players and musicians and flute players and trumpeters will certainly not be heard in thee any more, and any craftsman of any craft will certainly not be found in thee any more, and the sound of a mill will certainly not be heard in thee any more,
  Ezekiel 26:13 — verbal allusion: Ezekiel says of Tyre, “He will put an end to the multitude of your musicians, and the sound of your psalteries will certainly no longer be heard,” matching Revelation’s musicians whose sound “will certainly not be heard in thee any more.”
    Ezekiel 26:13 He will put an end to the multitude of your musicians, and the sound of your psalteries will certainly no longer be heard.
  Jeremiah 25:10 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah’s judgment removes “the sound of joy and delight,” as Revelation’s city will no longer hear the sound of its musicians. Jeremiah’s list goes on to “the sound of bridegroom and bride” and “the light of a lamp,” which Revelation takes up in the next verse (18:23). In this reading Jeremiah has “the fragrance of ointment” and no mill.
    Jeremiah 25:10 And I will remove from them the sound of joy and delight, the sound of bridegroom and bride, the fragrance of ointment and the light of a lamp.
  Isaiah 24:8–24:9 — shared image: Isaiah’s judgment says “the lyre’s sound has ceased” along with tambourines, as Revelation silences the lyre players and musicians of the city.
    Isaiah 24:8 The joy of tambourines has ceased; the impudence and wealth of the ungodly have ceased; the lyre’s sound has ceased.
    Isaiah 24:9 They were ashamed and did not drink wine; sikera became bitter to those drinking it.

Revelation 18:23
and the light of a lamp will certainly not shine in thee any more, and the voice of bridegroom and bride will certainly not be heard in thee any more, because thy merchants were the great ones of the earth, because by thy sorcery all the nations were deceived.
  Isaiah 23:8 — verbal allusion: Isaiah says of Tyre, “Her merchants are glorious, rulers of the earth,” and Revelation says “thy merchants were the great ones of the earth.” Isaiah’s oracle concerns Tyre; Revelation applies the description to Babylon.
    Isaiah 23:8 Who planned these things against Tyre? Is she weaker, or has she no strength? Her merchants are glorious, rulers of the earth.
  Nahum 3:4 — shared image: Nahum’s city is a prostitute and “mistress of potions,” “selling nations through her prostitution and tribes through her potions,” as Babylon deceived “all the nations” by her sorcery. Nahum addresses Nineveh rather than Babylon.
    Nahum 3:4 because of the multitude of prostitution. A prostitute, beautiful and pleasing, mistress of potions, selling nations through her prostitution and tribes through her potions,
  Jeremiah 7:34 — verbal allusion: Both oracles of judgment silence the voice of bridegroom and bride: Jeremiah will stop “the voice of bridegroom and the voice of bride,” and in Revelation “the voice of bridegroom and bride will certainly not be heard in thee any more.” Jeremiah applies this to Judah’s cities and Jerusalem; Revelation applies it to Babylon.
    Jeremiah 7:34 And I will stop the sound of those rejoicing and the sound of those glad, the voice of bridegroom and the voice of bride, from Judah’s cities and Jerusalem’s thoroughfares, because the whole land will become a desolation.

Revelation 18:24
And in her was found blood of prophets and saints and of all those slaughtered upon the earth.”
  Revelation 16:6 — internal cross-reference: Both verses charge the persecutors with shedding the blood of “saints and prophets.” Revelation 16:6 gives the bowl judgment as repayment; 18:24 locates that blood “in her.”
    Revelation 16:6 Because they poured out the blood of saints and prophets, Thou hast also given them blood to drink; they are worthy of it.”
  Jeremiah 28:49 — verbal allusion: Jeremiah says “in Babylon wounded men from all the earth will fall,” and Revelation finds in Babylon the blood “of all those slaughtered upon the earth.” Jeremiah does not mention prophets or saints.
    Jeremiah 28:49 And in Babylon wounded men from all the earth will fall.
  Revelation 17:6 — internal cross-reference: The woman is “drunk from the blood of the saints and from the blood of the witnesses of Jesus,” matching the blood of saints found in the city. Revelation 17:18 identifies the woman as the great city.
    Revelation 17:6 And I saw the woman drunk from the blood of the saints and from the blood of the witnesses of Jesus. And seeing her, I marvelled with great amazement.

Revelation 19:1
After these things I heard something like a loud voice of a great multitude in heaven, saying, “Hallelujah! The salvation and the glory and the power belong to our God,
  Revelation 19:6 — internal cross-reference: Both verses report “a voice of a great multitude” crying “Hallelujah.” Revelation 19:6 adds that the voice was like many waters and mighty thunders and praises God’s reign.
    Revelation 19:6 And I heard something like a voice of a great multitude, and like a sound of many waters, and like a sound of mighty thunders, saying, “Hallelujah, because the Lord God Almighty hath reigned.
  Revelation 12:10 — verbal parallel: A loud voice in heaven proclaims “the salvation and the power” of “our God” in both verses. Revelation 12:10 adds the kingdom and the authority of His Christ; 19:1 adds the glory.
    Revelation 12:10 And I heard a loud voice in heaven saying, “Now have come the salvation and the power and the kingdom of our God and the authority of His Christ, because the accuser of our brothers hath been thrown down, the one accusing them before our God day and night.
  Psalms 3:9 — verbal allusion: The psalm’s confession “Salvation belongs to the Lord” is echoed in “The salvation … belong to our God.” The psalm speaks of one sufferer’s deliverance; Revelation sets the ascription in heavenly praise.
    Psalms 3:9 Salvation belongs to the Lord, and Thy blessing is upon Thy people.

Revelation 19:2
because His judgments are true and righteous, because He judged the great prostitute who corrupted the earth by her sexual immorality, and avenged the blood of His slaves from her hand.”
  2 Kings 9:7 — verbal allusion: Both passages speak of avenging “the blood of My slaves” at a woman’s hand: Jezebel’s in 2 Kings, the great prostitute’s in Revelation (“avenged the blood of His slaves from her hand”).
    2 Kings 9:7 and you shall destroy the house of Ahab your master from before Me, and you shall avenge the blood of My slaves the prophets and the blood of all the Lord’s slaves at Jezebel’s hand
  Psalms 18:10 — verbal allusion: The psalm says “the Lord’s judgments are true, justified together,” as Revelation says “His judgments are true and righteous.”
    Psalms 18:10 The fear of the Lord is pure, enduring for the age of the age; the Lord’s judgments are true, justified together,
  Revelation 6:10 — answered prayer: The martyrs ask God to “judge and avenge our blood”; 19:2 praises Him because He “judged the great prostitute” and “avenged the blood of His slaves.”
    Revelation 6:10 And they cried with a loud voice, saying, “How long, O Master, holy and true, before Thou dost judge and avenge our blood upon those dwelling on the earth?”

Revelation 19:3
And a second time they said, “Hallelujah! And her smoke goeth up for the ages of the ages.”
  Isaiah 34:10 — verbal allusion: Isaiah says of the judged land, “her smoke will rise upward” and it “will not be quenched for an everlasting time”; Revelation says “her smoke goeth up for the ages of the ages.” Isaiah’s oracle is set in Edom’s land, not Babylon.
    Isaiah 34:10 night and day. It will not be quenched for an everlasting time, and her smoke will rise upward. For generations and a long time she will be deserted.
  Revelation 14:11 — verbal parallel: Both verses describe smoke that “goeth up for ages of ages.” In 14:11 it is the smoke of the torment of the beast’s worshippers; in 19:3 it is the city’s smoke.
    Revelation 14:11 And the smoke of their torment goeth up for ages of ages, and they have no rest day and night, those worshipping the beast and its image, and anyone who receiveth the mark of its name.”
  Revelation 18:18 — internal cross-reference: The seafarers saw “the smoke of her burning”; the heavenly multitude declares that the same smoke goes up for the ages of the ages.
    Revelation 18:18 and cried as they saw the smoke of her burning, saying, “What is like the great city?”

Revelation 19:4
And the twenty-four elders and the four living creatures fell and worshipped God, who sitteth upon the throne, saying, “Amen, Hallelujah!”
  Revelation 5:14 — parallel worship scene: The four living creatures say “Amen” and the elders fall and worship in both scenes. In 5:14 the worship follows praise of the Lamb; in 19:4 it follows the judgment of the prostitute and adds “Hallelujah.”
    Revelation 5:14 And the four living creatures were saying, “Amen.” And the elders fell and worshipped.
  Revelation 11:15–11:16 — parallel worship scene: The “twenty-four elders” fall and worship God in response to a heavenly announcement in both passages. In 11:16 the announcement is the kingdom of our Lord and His Christ.
    Revelation 11:15 And the seventh angel sounded, and loud voices arose in heaven, saying, “The kingdom of the world hath become the kingdom of our Lord and of His Christ, and He will reign for the ages of the ages.”
    Revelation 11:16 And the twenty-four elders sitting upon their thrones before God fell upon their faces and worshipped God,
  Revelation 5:8–5:11 — parallel worship scene: The four living creatures and the twenty-four elders fall down together in both verses. In 5:8 they fall before the Lamb with lyres and bowls of incense; in 19:4 they worship God on the throne.
    Revelation 5:8 And when He took the scroll, the four living creatures and the twenty-four elders fell before the Lamb, each having a lyre and golden bowls full of incense, which are the prayers of the saints.
    Revelation 5:9 And they sing a new song, saying, “Thou art worthy to take the scroll and open its seals, because Thou wast slaughtered and didst purchase for God in Thy blood people from every tribe and tongue and people and nation,
    Revelation 5:10 and Thou didst make them a kingdom and priests unto our God, and they shall reign upon the earth.”
    Revelation 5:11 And I saw, and heard a voice of many angels around the throne and the living creatures and the elders; and their number was myriads of myriads and thousands of thousands,

Revelation 19:5
And a voice came out from the throne, saying, “Praise our God, all His slaves and those who fear Him, the small and the great.”
  Revelation 11:18–11:19 — verbal parallel: Both verses group God’s “slaves” with “those who fear” Him and add “the small and the great.” In 11:18 they receive their reward; in 19:5 they are called to praise.
    Revelation 11:18 And the nations were enraged, and Thy wrath came, and the time for the dead to be judged, and to give the reward to Thy slaves the prophets and to the saints and to those who fear Thy name, the small and the great, and to destroy those destroying the earth.”
    Revelation 11:19 And God’s temple in heaven was opened, and the ark of His covenant was seen in His temple; and there were lightnings and voices and thunders and an earthquake and great hail.
  Psalms 134:1 — verbal allusion: The psalm’s summons “Praise the Lord’s name; praise the Lord, slaves” is echoed in “Praise our God, all His slaves.” The psalm opens with “Hallelujah,” the word that frames the surrounding praise in Revelation 19.
    Psalms 134:1 Hallelujah! Praise the Lord’s name; praise the Lord, slaves,
  Psalms 21:24 — verbal allusion: The psalm calls “You who fear the Lord, praise Him!”, as Revelation calls “those who fear Him” to praise our God.
    Psalms 21:24 You who fear the Lord, praise Him! All Jacob’s seed, glorify Him! Let all Israel’s seed fear Him,

Revelation 19:6
And I heard something like a voice of a great multitude, and like a sound of many waters, and like a sound of mighty thunders, saying, “Hallelujah, because the Lord God Almighty hath reigned.
  Revelation 14:2 — verbal parallel: Both voices are compared to “the sound of many waters” and to thunder (“great thunder,” “mighty thunders”). In 14:2 the voice is also like lyre players; in 19:6 it is the voice of a great multitude.
    Revelation 14:2 And I heard a voice from heaven like the sound of many waters and like the sound of great thunder; and the voice I heard was like lyre players playing on their lyres.
  Psalms 92:1 — verbal allusion: The psalm proclaims “The Lord reigned,” and the multitude cries “the Lord God Almighty hath reigned.”
    Psalms 92:1 For the day before the Sabbath, when the earth has been inhabited; a praise of a song, of David. The Lord reigned, clothed Himself in comeliness; the Lord clothed Himself in power and girded Himself. For He established the inhabited world, which will not be shaken.
  Psalms 98:1 — verbal allusion: The psalm proclaims “The Lord has become King!”, as the multitude cries “Hallelujah, because the Lord God Almighty hath reigned.”
    Psalms 98:1 A psalm of David. The Lord has become King! Let peoples be angry. He sits upon the cherubim; let the earth be shaken.

Revelation 19:7
Let us rejoice and exult, and we will give Him the glory, because the marriage of the Lamb hath come, and His wife prepared herself.
  Revelation 21:9 — internal cross-reference: The angel shows John “the bride, the wife of the Lamb,” the wife announced in 19:7 who “prepared herself.”
    Revelation 21:9 And one of the seven angels having the seven bowls full of the seven last plagues came and spoke with me, saying, “Come, I will show thee the bride, the wife of the Lamb.”
  Revelation 21:2 — internal cross-reference: The new Jerusalem comes down “prepared as a bride adorned for her husband,” matching the announcement that “His wife prepared herself.”
    Revelation 21:2 And I saw the holy city, new Jerusalem, coming down out of heaven from God, prepared as a bride adorned for her husband.
  Isaiah 62:5 — shared image: Isaiah pictures the Lord rejoicing over His people as a bridegroom rejoices over a bride; Revelation calls for rejoicing because the marriage of the Lamb has come. Isaiah speaks of the Lord’s joy, Revelation of the worshippers’ joy.
    Isaiah 62:5 As a young man lives with a virgin, so your sons will dwell with you. As a bridegroom rejoices over a bride, so the Lord will rejoice over you.

Revelation 19:8
And it was given to her to clothe herself in fine linen, bright and pure, for the fine linen is the righteous acts of the saints.”
  Isaiah 61:10 — shared image: Isaiah’s speaker is clothed by God “in a garment of salvation” and adorned “like a bride,” as the Lamb’s wife is given fine linen to clothe herself. Isaiah’s garment signifies salvation; Revelation’s linen signifies “the righteous acts of the saints.”
    Isaiah 61:10 and they will rejoice greatly in the Lord.” Let my soul exult in the Lord, for He clothed me in a garment of salvation and a tunic of joy. Like a bridegroom He placed a turban on me, and like a bride He adorned me with ornaments.
  Psalms 131:9 — verbal allusion: The psalm says God’s priests “will clothe themselves in righteousness,” and Revelation explains the bride’s clothing as “the righteous acts of the saints.”
    Psalms 131:9 Thy priests will clothe themselves in righteousness, and Thy holy ones will exult.
  Ezekiel 16:10 — shared image: In Ezekiel God clothes Jerusalem, pictured as His bride, and girds her “with fine linen.” Revelation’s bride is likewise given fine linen; Ezekiel’s passage goes on to her unfaithfulness, which Revelation does not.
    Ezekiel 16:10 and clothed you in patterned garments, gave you blue-purple footwear, girded you with fine linen, and wrapped you in hair-woven cloth,

Revelation 19:9
And he saith unto me, “Write: Blessed are those called to the supper of the Lamb’s marriage.” And he saith unto me, “These are true words of God.”
  Matthew 22:2–22:4 — shared image: Jesus’ parable speaks of “those invited to the wedding feast” prepared by a king for his son, as Revelation blesses “those called to the supper of the Lamb’s marriage.” In the parable many of those invited refuse to come.
    Matthew 22:2 “The kingdom of the heavens was likened to a king who made a wedding feast for his son.
    Matthew 22:3 He sent his slaves to call those invited to the wedding feast, but they were unwilling to come.
    Matthew 22:4 Again he sent other slaves, saying, ‘Tell those invited: Behold, I have prepared my meal. My bulls and fattened animals have been slaughtered, and everything is ready. Come to the wedding feast.’
  Revelation 14:13 — verbal parallel: Both verses contain a beatitude introduced by the command “Write: Blessed are …” In 14:13 the blessed are those who die in the Lord.
    Revelation 14:13 And I heard a voice from heaven saying, “Write: Blessed are the dead who die in the Lord from now on.” “Yea,” saith the Spirit, “so that they may rest from their labours, for their works follow with them.”
  Revelation 21:5 — verbal parallel: Both verses pair the command “Write” with an affirmation of the words: “these words are faithful and true” and “These are true words of God.”
    Revelation 21:5 And the One sitting upon the throne said, “Behold, I make all things new.” And He saith, “Write, because these words are faithful and true.”

Revelation 19:10
And I fell before his feet to worship him. And he saith unto me, “See that thou do it not! I am thy fellow slave and of thy brothers who hold the testimony of Jesus. Worship God, for the testimony of Jesus is the spirit of prophecy.”
  Revelation 12:17 — verbal parallel: Both verses describe believers “holding the testimony of Jesus.” In 12:17 they are the woman’s offspring whom the dragon attacks; in 19:10 they are John’s brothers, of whom the angel is a fellow slave.
    Revelation 12:17 And the dragon was enraged at the woman, and went away to make war with the rest of her offspring, those keeping the commandments of God and holding the testimony of Jesus.
  Revelation 1:9 — internal cross-reference: John introduces himself as “your brother” on Patmos because of “the testimony of Jesus,” the same testimony the angel says John and his brothers hold.
    Revelation 1:9 I, John, your brother and fellow partner in the affliction and kingdom and endurance in Jesus, came to be on the island called Patmos because of the word of God and the testimony of Jesus.
  Matthew 4:10 — shared principle: Jesus answers Satan with “You shall worship the Lord your God,” and the angel refuses John’s homage with “Worship God.” Both reject worship directed to anyone but God.
    Matthew 4:10 Then Jesus said to him, “Go away, Satan! For it is written: ‘You shall worship the Lord your God, and Him alone shall you serve.’”

Revelation 19:11
And I saw heaven opened, and behold, a white horse, and the One sitting upon it is called Faithful and True, and in righteousness He judgeth and wageth war.
  Revelation 3:14 — same title of Christ: The rider is called “Faithful and True,” and in the Laodicean letter Christ is “the faithful and true witness.”
    Revelation 3:14 And to the angel of the Church in Laodicea write: These things saith the Amen, the faithful and true witness, the beginning of God’s creation:
  Isaiah 11:3–11:5 — messianic background: Isaiah’s ruler judges and strikes the earth, and “his loins will be girded with righteousness, and his sides bound with truth.” The rider likewise judges and wages war “in righteousness” and is called “True.”
    Isaiah 11:3 The spirit of the fear of God will fill him. He will not judge according to appearance, nor reprove according to report;
    Isaiah 11:4 but he will judge the cause of the lowly, and reprove the lowly of the earth; and he will strike the earth with the word of his mouth, and with breath through his lips he will slay the ungodly.
    Isaiah 11:5 His loins will be girded with righteousness, and his sides bound with truth.
  Psalms 95:13 — verbal allusion: The psalm says the Lord is coming to judge the world “with righteousness” and the peoples “with His truth.” Revelation’s rider is “True” and judges “in righteousness.”
    Psalms 95:13 before the Lord, for He is coming, for He is coming to judge the earth. He will judge the inhabited world with righteousness and peoples with His truth.

Revelation 19:12
And His eyes are a flame of fire, and upon His head are many diadems, having a name written that no one knoweth except Himself.
  Revelation 1:14 — same description of Christ: In the inaugural vision the Son of Man’s eyes are “like a flame of fire,” as the rider’s “eyes are a flame of fire.”
    Revelation 1:14 And His head and hair were white like white wool, like snow, and His eyes like a flame of fire,
  Revelation 2:17–2:18 — verbal parallel: Revelation 2:17–18 has both details: “a new name written, which no one knoweth except the one who receiveth it,” and the Son of God “who hath His eyes like a flame of fire.” In 2:17 the hidden name is given to the one who conquers; in 19:12 it belongs to the rider himself.
    Revelation 2:17 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. To the one who conquereth I will give some of the hidden manna, and I will give him a white stone, and upon the stone a new name written, which no one knoweth except the one who receiveth it.’
    Revelation 2:18 And to the angel of the Church in Thyatira write: These things saith the Son of God, who hath His eyes like a flame of fire, and His feet like burnished bronze:

Revelation 19:13
And He is clothed in a garment dipped in blood, and His name is called the Word of God.
  Isaiah 63:1–63:6 — verbal allusion: Isaiah’s warrior has red garments “as though from a trodden winepress” and brings his enemies’ “blood down to earth,” as the rider wears “a garment dipped in blood.” Revelation 19:15 goes on to describe him treading the winepress.
    Isaiah 63:1 Who is this arriving from Edom, with redness of garments from Bosor, so beautiful in His robe, with force and strength? “I speak righteousness and saving judgment.”
    Isaiah 63:2 Why are Thy garments red and Thy clothes as though from a trodden winepress?
    Isaiah 63:3 “I am full of what has been trodden. No man from the nations is with Me. I trampled them in wrath, crushed them like earth, and brought their blood down to earth.
    Isaiah 63:4 For a day of recompense came upon them, and a year of redemption is here.
    Isaiah 63:5 I looked, but there was no helper; I considered, but no one came to aid. My arm rescued them, and My wrath stood by.
    Isaiah 63:6 I trampled them in My anger and brought their blood down to earth.”
  John 1:1 — same title of Christ: John’s Gospel calls Christ “the Word,” who was with God and was God; here the rider’s name is “the Word of God.”
    John 1:1 In the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with God, and the Word was God.
  Wisdom of Solomon 18:15 — shared image: Wisdom describes God’s “all-powerful word” leaping from heaven as “a stern warrior” carrying His command “as a sharp sword.” In Revelation the Word of God rides out of heaven to war with a sharp sword from His mouth.
    Wisdom of Solomon 18:15 Thine all-powerful word leaped from heaven, from royal thrones, a stern warrior, into the midst of the doomed land, carrying Thine unfeigned command as a sharp sword;

Revelation 19:14
And the armies in heaven were following Him upon white horses, clothed in fine linen, white and pure.
  Revelation 19:8 — internal cross-reference: The armies are “clothed in fine linen, white and pure,” as the bride is clothed “in fine linen, bright and pure,” which is explained as the righteous acts of the saints.
    Revelation 19:8 And it was given to her to clothe herself in fine linen, bright and pure, for the fine linen is the righteous acts of the saints.”
  Revelation 17:14 — internal cross-reference: In 17:14 the Lamb wages war and conquers, and “those with Him are called and chosen and faithful.” Revelation 19:14 shows those who accompany Him in that war.
    Revelation 17:14 These will wage war with the Lamb, and the Lamb will conquer them, because He is Lord of lords and King of kings, and those with Him are called and chosen and faithful.”
  Zechariah 14:5 — shared image: Zechariah says “The Lord my God will come, and all the holy ones with Him,” as the heavenly armies follow the rider. Zechariah does not describe horses or garments.
    Zechariah 14:5 The ravine of My mountains will be blocked; the mountain ravine will be joined as far as Iasol and blocked as it was blocked in the days of the earthquake, in the days of Oziah king of Judah. The Lord my God will come, and all the holy ones with Him.

Revelation 19:15
And from His mouth cometh out a sharp sword, so that with it He may strike the nations; and He will shepherd them with an iron rod, and He treadeth the winepress of the wine of the fury of the wrath of God Almighty.
  Psalms 2:9 — quotation: Revelation takes up the psalm’s words “You will shepherd them with an iron rod” for the rider who strikes the nations. The psalm adds “you will crush them like a potter’s vessel.”
    Psalms 2:9 You will shepherd them with an iron rod; you will crush them like a potter’s vessel.’”
  Revelation 1:16 — same description of Christ: In the inaugural vision “from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out,” as from the rider’s mouth “cometh out a sharp sword.”
    Revelation 1:16 And He held seven stars in His right hand, and from His mouth a sharp two-edged sword was coming out, and His appearance was like the sun shining in its strength.
  Isaiah 11:4 — messianic background: Isaiah’s ruler “will strike the earth with the word of his mouth” and slay the ungodly with his breath; the rider strikes the nations with a sword coming from His mouth.
    Isaiah 11:4 but he will judge the cause of the lowly, and reprove the lowly of the earth; and he will strike the earth with the word of his mouth, and with breath through his lips he will slay the ungodly.

Revelation 19:16
And upon the garment and upon His thigh He hath a name written: “King of kings and Lord of lords.”
  Revelation 17:14 — same title of Christ: The Lamb is “Lord of lords and King of kings” in 17:14; the rider bears the written name “King of kings and Lord of lords,” with the two titles in reverse order.
    Revelation 17:14 These will wage war with the Lamb, and the Lamb will conquer them, because He is Lord of lords and King of kings, and those with Him are called and chosen and faithful.”
  1 Timothy 6:15 — parallel title: Paul calls God “the King of those who reign and Lord of those who rule,” which corresponds to the paired title “King of kings and Lord of lords.” The wording differs.
    1 Timothy 6:15 which the blessed and only Sovereign will display at the proper times, the King of those who reign and Lord of those who rule,
  Daniel 2:47 — verbal allusion: Nebuchadnezzar confesses Daniel’s God as “God of gods and Lord of kings,” a similar superlative title of sovereignty over kings.
    Daniel 2:47 The king spoke aloud to Daniel, “Truly your God is God of gods and Lord of kings, the One who alone brings hidden mysteries to light, because you have been able to disclose this mystery.”

Revelation 19:17
And I saw one angel standing in the sun, and he cried with a loud voice, saying to all the birds flying in midheaven, “Come, gather for the great supper of God,
  Ezekiel 39:17–39:20 — verbal allusion: Ezekiel is told to say to “every flying bird … Gather and come!” to God’s great sacrifice, as the angel calls the birds to “gather for the great supper of God.” Ezekiel also summons the beasts of the plain.
    Ezekiel 39:17 And you, son of man, say, Thus says the Lord: Say to every flying bird and all the plain’s beasts, Gather and come! Gather from all those around to My sacrifice that I have sacrificed for you, a great sacrifice upon Israel’s mountains. You will eat flesh and drink blood.
    Ezekiel 39:18 You will eat giants’ flesh and drink the blood of the land’s rulers: rams, calves and male goats, and the calves all fattened.
    Ezekiel 39:19 You will eat fat to fullness and drink blood to drunkenness from My sacrifice that I sacrificed for you.
    Ezekiel 39:20 At My table you will be filled with horse and rider, giant and every warrior, says the Lord.
  Revelation 19:21 — fulfilment within the vision: The birds summoned to the supper in 19:17 are shown in 19:21 when “all the birds were filled with their flesh.”
    Revelation 19:21 And the rest were killed with the sword of the One sitting upon the horse, the sword coming out of His mouth, and all the birds were filled with their flesh.

Revelation 19:18
that ye may eat flesh of kings and flesh of commanders of thousands and flesh of mighty ones and flesh of horses and of those sitting upon them, and flesh of all, both free and slaves and small and great.”
  Ezekiel 39:18–39:20 — verbal allusion: Ezekiel’s summons promises the birds “giants’ flesh” and the blood of rulers, and says they will be filled “with horse and rider, giant and every warrior.” Revelation’s list likewise includes “flesh of mighty ones and flesh of horses and of those sitting upon them.”
    Ezekiel 39:18 You will eat giants’ flesh and drink the blood of the land’s rulers: rams, calves and male goats, and the calves all fattened.
    Ezekiel 39:19 You will eat fat to fullness and drink blood to drunkenness from My sacrifice that I sacrificed for you.
    Ezekiel 39:20 At My table you will be filled with horse and rider, giant and every warrior, says the Lord.
  Revelation 6:15 — verbal parallel: Both verses list the kings, “commanders of thousands,” the mighty or strong, and both slave and free. In 6:15 they hide from wrath; in 19:18 their flesh is given to the birds.
    Revelation 6:15 And the kings of the earth and the great ones and the commanders of thousands and the rich and the strong and every slave and free person hid themselves in the caves and among the rocks of the mountains.

Revelation 19:19
And I saw the beast and the kings of the earth and their armies gathered to wage war with the One sitting upon the horse and with His army.
  Revelation 17:12–17:14 — internal cross-reference: The ten kings give their authority to the beast and “will wage war with the Lamb.” Revelation 19:19 shows the beast and the kings gathered to wage war with the rider.
    Revelation 17:12 And the ten horns thou sawest are ten kings, who have not yet received a kingdom, but receive authority as kings for one hour with the beast.
    Revelation 17:13 These have one purpose, and give their power and authority to the beast.
    Revelation 17:14 These will wage war with the Lamb, and the Lamb will conquer them, because He is Lord of lords and King of kings, and those with Him are called and chosen and faithful.”
  Revelation 16:14 — internal cross-reference: Demonic spirits go to “the kings of the whole inhabited world to gather them for the war,” and 19:19 shows the kings and their armies “gathered to wage war.”
    Revelation 16:14 For they are spirits of demons performing signs, which go out to the kings of the whole inhabited world to gather them for the war of the great day of God Almighty.
  Joel 4:9–4:14 — shared image: Joel summons the nations to “Consecrate war!”, assemble and gather to be judged, and speaks of treading a full winepress. Revelation shows the gathering of kings for war against the rider who treads the winepress (19:15).
    Joel 4:9 Proclaim these things among the nations: Consecrate war! Rouse the fighters! Draw near and come up, all soldiers!
    Joel 4:10 Beat your plows into swords and your sickles into lances; let the powerless say, “I am strong!”
    Joel 4:11 Assemble and enter, all surrounding nations, and gather there! Let the meek become a fighter!
    Joel 4:12 Let all the nations be roused and come up to the Valley of Jehoshaphat, because there I will sit to judge all the surrounding nations.
    Joel 4:13 Send out sickles, because the vintage is at hand! Enter and tread, because the winepress is full; the vats overflow, because their evils have multiplied.
    Joel 4:14 Sounds rang out in the Valley of Judgment, because the Lord’s day is near in the Valley of Judgment.

Revelation 19:20
And the beast was seized, and with it the false prophet who performed the signs before it, by which he deceived those receiving the beast’s mark and those worshipping its image. The two were thrown alive into the lake of fire burning with sulfur.
  Revelation 20:10 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 20:10 says the devil was thrown into “the lake of fire and sulfur, where also are the beast and the false prophet,” referring back to their being thrown there in 19:20.
    Revelation 20:10 And the devil who deceived them was thrown into the lake of fire and sulfur, where also are the beast and the false prophet, and they will be tormented day and night for the ages of the ages.
  Revelation 16:13–16:14 — internal cross-reference: The false prophet appears with the dragon and the beast, sending spirits “performing signs” to gather the kings, as 19:20 describes him as the one who performed the signs.
    Revelation 16:13 And I saw from the dragon’s mouth and from the beast’s mouth and from the false prophet’s mouth three unclean spirits like frogs.
    Revelation 16:14 For they are spirits of demons performing signs, which go out to the kings of the whole inhabited world to gather them for the war of the great day of God Almighty.
  Revelation 21:8 — verbal parallel: Revelation 19:20 speaks of “the lake of fire burning with sulfur” and 21:8 of “the lake burning with fire and sulfur.” Revelation 21:8 assigns it to the unbelieving and other sinners and identifies it as “the second death.”
    Revelation 21:8 But as for the cowardly and unbelieving and abominable and murderers and sexually immoral and sorcerers and idolaters and all liars, their share is in the lake burning with fire and sulfur, which is the second death.”

Revelation 19:21
And the rest were killed with the sword of the One sitting upon the horse, the sword coming out of His mouth, and all the birds were filled with their flesh.
  Revelation 19:11–19:15 — internal cross-reference: The sword is that of “the One sitting upon the horse,” from whose mouth “cometh out a sharp sword” to strike the nations in 19:15.
    Revelation 19:11 And I saw heaven opened, and behold, a white horse, and the One sitting upon it is called Faithful and True, and in righteousness He judgeth and wageth war.
    Revelation 19:12 And His eyes are a flame of fire, and upon His head are many diadems, having a name written that no one knoweth except Himself.
    Revelation 19:13 And He is clothed in a garment dipped in blood, and His name is called the Word of God.
    Revelation 19:14 And the armies in heaven were following Him upon white horses, clothed in fine linen, white and pure.
    Revelation 19:15 And from His mouth cometh out a sharp sword, so that with it He may strike the nations; and He will shepherd them with an iron rod, and He treadeth the winepress of the wine of the fury of the wrath of God Almighty.
  Revelation 19:17–19:19 — fulfilment within the vision: The birds summoned in 19:17–18 to eat the flesh of kings and riders are “filled with their flesh” in 19:21.
    Revelation 19:17 And I saw one angel standing in the sun, and he cried with a loud voice, saying to all the birds flying in midheaven, “Come, gather for the great supper of God,
    Revelation 19:18 that ye may eat flesh of kings and flesh of commanders of thousands and flesh of mighty ones and flesh of horses and of those sitting upon them, and flesh of all, both free and slaves and small and great.”
    Revelation 19:19 And I saw the beast and the kings of the earth and their armies gathered to wage war with the One sitting upon the horse and with His army.

Revelation 20:1
And I saw an angel coming down from heaven, having the key of the abyss and a great chain upon his hand.
  Revelation 9:1–9:2 — verbal parallel: In 20:1 an angel coming down from heaven has “the key of the abyss”; in 9:1 “the key of the shaft of the abyss” is given to a star fallen from heaven, who opens the shaft.
    Revelation 9:1 And the fifth angel sounded, and I saw a star fallen from heaven to the earth, and the key of the shaft of the abyss was given to him.
    Revelation 9:2 And he opened the shaft of the abyss, and smoke went up from the shaft like smoke of a great furnace, and the sun and the air were darkened from the smoke of the shaft.
  Jude 1:6 — shared image: Jude says God keeps the angels who abandoned their proper dwelling “in everlasting chains under gloom for the judgment of the great day,” and in 20:1 an angel comes down from heaven with the key of the abyss and “a great chain” upon his hand.
    Jude 1:6 And He has kept the angels who did not keep their own domain, but abandoned their proper dwelling, in everlasting chains under gloom for the judgment of the great day.

Revelation 20:2
And he seized the dragon, the ancient serpent, who is the Devil and Satan, and bound him for a thousand years,
  Revelation 12:9 — same title: Both verses identify “the dragon, the ancient serpent,” as the Devil and Satan. In 12:9 he is thrown down to the earth; in 20:2 he is seized and bound.
    Revelation 12:9 And the great dragon was thrown down, the ancient serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan, the one deceiving the whole inhabited world; he was thrown to the earth, and his angels were thrown with him.
  Isaiah 27:1 — shared image: Isaiah foresees God bringing His sword against “the dragon, the fleeing serpent,” and killing it, as the angel seizes the dragon, the ancient serpent. Isaiah describes slaying; Revelation here describes binding.
    Isaiah 27:1 On that day God will bring the holy, great and strong sword against the dragon, the fleeing serpent, against the dragon, the crooked serpent, and will kill the dragon.

Revelation 20:3
and threw him into the abyss, and shut it and sealed it over him, so that he might no longer deceive the nations until the thousand years were completed. After these things he must be loosed for a little time.
  Revelation 20:7–20:10 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 20:7–8 fulfils the statement that he “must be loosed”: when the thousand years are completed Satan is loosed from his prison and goes out to deceive the nations.
    Revelation 20:7 And when the thousand years are completed, Satan will be loosed from his prison,
    Revelation 20:8 and will go out to deceive the nations at the four corners of the earth, Gog and Magog, to gather them for war, whose number is like the sand of the sea.
    Revelation 20:9 And they came up upon the breadth of the earth and surrounded the camp of the saints and the beloved city. And fire came down from heaven and devoured them.
    Revelation 20:10 And the devil who deceived them was thrown into the lake of fire and sulfur, where also are the beast and the false prophet, and they will be tormented day and night for the ages of the ages.
  Revelation 20:1 — internal cross-reference: The angel who has “the key of the abyss” in 20:1 is the one who throws the dragon into the abyss and shuts it.
    Revelation 20:1 And I saw an angel coming down from heaven, having the key of the abyss and a great chain upon his hand.
  Revelation 12:9 — internal cross-reference: In 12:9 the serpent is “the one deceiving the whole inhabited world”; 20:3 binds him so that he may “no longer deceive the nations.”
    Revelation 12:9 And the great dragon was thrown down, the ancient serpent, who is called the Devil and Satan, the one deceiving the whole inhabited world; he was thrown to the earth, and his angels were thrown with him.

Revelation 20:4
And I saw thrones, and they sat upon them, and judgment was given to them; and the souls of those beheaded because of the testimony of Jesus and because of the word of God, and those who did not worship the beast or its image and did not receive the mark upon their forehead and upon their hand. And they lived and reigned with Christ for a thousand years.
  Daniel 7:22 — verbal allusion: Daniel says the Ancient of Days “gave judgment to the holy ones of the Most High” and they took possession of the kingship, as John sees thrones where “judgment was given to them” and they reign with Christ.
    Daniel 7:22 until the Ancient of Days came and gave judgment to the holy ones of the Most High. The time was given, and the holy ones took possession of the kingship.
  Revelation 6:9 — internal cross-reference: Both verses speak of the souls of those killed “because of the word of God” and because of their testimony. In 6:9 they are seen beneath the altar; in 20:4 they live and reign with Christ.
    Revelation 6:9 And when He opened the fifth seal, I saw beneath the altar the souls of those slaughtered because of the word of God and because of the testimony they held.
  Matthew 19:28 — shared image: Jesus promises his followers that they will “sit upon twelve thrones, judging” in the regeneration, as John sees those seated on thrones with judgment given to them. Matthew limits the scope to the twelve tribes of Israel.
    Matthew 19:28 Jesus said to them, “Truly I tell you that you who have followed Me, in the regeneration, when the Son of Man sits upon the throne of His glory, shall also sit upon twelve thrones, judging the twelve tribes of Israel.

Revelation 20:5
The rest of the dead did not live until the thousand years were completed. This is the first resurrection.
  Revelation 20:4 — internal cross-reference: 20:4 says the faithful “lived and reigned with Christ for a thousand years”; 20:5 contrasts “the rest of the dead,” who did not live until the thousand years were completed.
    Revelation 20:4 And I saw thrones, and they sat upon them, and judgment was given to them; and the souls of those beheaded because of the testimony of Jesus and because of the word of God, and those who did not worship the beast or its image and did not receive the mark upon their forehead and upon their hand. And they lived and reigned with Christ for a thousand years.
  Revelation 20:6 — internal cross-reference: 20:5 names this “the first resurrection,” and 20:6 pronounces blessed the one who “hath a share in the first resurrection,” over whom the second death has no authority.
    Revelation 20:6 Blessed and holy is the one who hath a share in the first resurrection; over these the second death hath no authority, but they will be priests of God and of Christ, and will reign with Him for a thousand years.

Revelation 20:6
Blessed and holy is the one who hath a share in the first resurrection; over these the second death hath no authority, but they will be priests of God and of Christ, and will reign with Him for a thousand years.
  Revelation 2:11 — verbal parallel: The one who conquers “shall certainly not be harmed by the second death,” as over those in the first resurrection “the second death hath no authority.”
    Revelation 2:11 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. The one who conquereth shall certainly not be harmed by the second death.’
  Revelation 5:10 — verbal parallel: The redeemed are made “a kingdom and priests unto our God,” and “they shall reign”; 20:6 says they will be “priests of God and of Christ” and reign with Him for a thousand years.
    Revelation 5:10 and Thou didst make them a kingdom and priests unto our God, and they shall reign upon the earth.”
  Revelation 1:6 — verbal parallel: Christ has made believers “a kingdom, priests to His God and Father,” anticipating their being “priests of God and of Christ” in 20:6.
    Revelation 1:6 and made us a kingdom, priests to His God and Father—to Him be the glory and dominion for the ages of the ages. Amen.

Revelation 20:7
And when the thousand years are completed, Satan will be loosed from his prison,
  Revelation 20:2 — internal cross-reference: Satan is bound “for a thousand years” in 20:2, and 20:7 describes his release “when the thousand years are completed.”
    Revelation 20:2 And he seized the dragon, the ancient serpent, who is the Devil and Satan, and bound him for a thousand years,

Revelation 20:8
and will go out to deceive the nations at the four corners of the earth, Gog and Magog, to gather them for war, whose number is like the sand of the sea.
  Revelation 20:3 — internal cross-reference: Satan was sealed in the abyss “so that he might no longer deceive the nations until the thousand years were completed”; once loosed, he goes out “to deceive the nations.”
    Revelation 20:3 and threw him into the abyss, and shut it and sealed it over him, so that he might no longer deceive the nations until the thousand years were completed. After these things he must be loosed for a little time.
  Revelation 16:14 — parallel gathering: Both verses describe going out to the kings or nations of the world “to gather them for the war.” In 16:14 demonic spirits do the gathering; in 20:8 Satan does.
    Revelation 16:14 For they are spirits of demons performing signs, which go out to the kings of the whole inhabited world to gather them for the war of the great day of God Almighty.
  1 Samuel 13:5 — verbal parallel: The foreigners “gathered for war” against Israel with people “as numerous as the sand by the sea,” as Satan gathers the nations for war, “whose number is like the sand of the sea.” Samuel narrates a muster of the foreigners against Israel.
    1 Samuel 13:5 The foreigners gathered for war against Israel. They went up against Israel with thirty thousand chariots, six thousand horsemen, and people as numerous as the sand by the sea. They went up and camped at Michmash, opposite Baithon toward the south.

Revelation 20:9
And they came up upon the breadth of the earth and surrounded the camp of the saints and the beloved city. And fire came down from heaven and devoured them.
  Ezekiel 39:6 — fulfilment of oracle: Ezekiel’s Lord says “I will send fire upon Gog,” and in Revelation fire comes down from heaven on the hosts gathered under the name Gog and Magog (20:8).
    Ezekiel 39:6 I will send fire upon Gog, and the islands will be inhabited in peace. They will know that I am the Lord.
  Ezekiel 38:16 — shared image: Ezekiel’s Gog will “come up against My people Israel like a cloud covering the land,” as the nations “came up upon the breadth of the earth” and surrounded the camp of the saints.
    Ezekiel 38:16 and come up against My people Israel like a cloud covering the land. It will be at the end of days; I will bring you against My land so that all the nations may know Me when I am sanctified in you before them.
  Ezekiel 38:22 — shared image: God will “rain fire and sulfur” on Gog and the many nations with him; in Revelation fire from heaven devours the gathered nations, and the devil is then thrown into a lake of fire and sulfur (20:10).
    Ezekiel 38:22 I will judge him with death, blood, flooding rain and hailstones. I will rain fire and sulfur upon him, all those with him and the many nations with him.

Revelation 20:10
And the devil who deceived them was thrown into the lake of fire and sulfur, where also are the beast and the false prophet, and they will be tormented day and night for the ages of the ages.
  Revelation 19:20 — internal cross-reference: The beast and the false prophet were thrown alive “into the lake of fire burning with sulfur” in 19:20; 20:10 says the devil joins them there.
    Revelation 19:20 And the beast was seized, and with it the false prophet who performed the signs before it, by which he deceived those receiving the beast’s mark and those worshipping its image. The two were thrown alive into the lake of fire burning with sulfur.
  Revelation 14:10–14:11 — verbal parallel: The beast’s worshippers are “tormented in fire and sulfur,” with no rest “day and night,” their smoke rising “for ages of ages.” Revelation 20:10 uses the same terms of the devil, the beast and the false prophet.
    Revelation 14:10 he too will drink from the wine of God’s fury, prepared unmixed in the cup of His wrath, and will be tormented in fire and sulfur before holy angels and before the Lamb.
    Revelation 14:11 And the smoke of their torment goeth up for ages of ages, and they have no rest day and night, those worshipping the beast and its image, and anyone who receiveth the mark of its name.”
  Matthew 25:41 — shared image: Jesus speaks of “the eternal fire prepared for the devil and his angels,” and Revelation shows the devil thrown into the lake of fire for the ages of the ages.
    Matthew 25:41 Then He will also say to those at His left, ‘Depart from Me, you cursed, into the eternal fire prepared for the devil and his angels.

Revelation 20:11
And I saw a great white throne and the One sitting upon it, from whose face the earth and heaven fled, and no place was found for them.
  Revelation 12:8 — verbal parallel: Both verses say “nor was a place found for them” / “no place was found for them.” In 12:8 it is the dragon and his angels in heaven; in 20:11 it is the earth and heaven fleeing before the One on the throne.
    Revelation 12:8 and he did not prevail, nor was a place found for them any more in heaven.
  Revelation 21:1 — internal cross-reference: After earth and heaven flee from the throne, John sees a new heaven and a new earth, “for the first heaven and the first earth passed away.”
    Revelation 21:1 And I saw a new heaven and a new earth; for the first heaven and the first earth passed away, and the sea is no more.
  Revelation 16:20 — shared image: Under the seventh bowl “every island fled, and mountains were not found,” as earth and heaven flee and are not found before the throne.
    Revelation 16:20 And every island fled, and mountains were not found.

Revelation 20:12
And I saw the dead, the great and the small, standing before the throne, and scrolls were opened; and another scroll was opened, which is of life. And the dead were judged from the things written in the scrolls, according to their works.
  Daniel 7:10 — verbal allusion: Daniel’s court “sat, and books were opened,” as John sees “scrolls were opened” before the throne for the judgment of the dead.
    Daniel 7:10 and a river of fire came out before Him. A thousand thousands attended Him, and ten thousand times ten thousand stood beside Him. A court sat, and books were opened.
  Revelation 11:18 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 11:18 announces “the time for the dead to be judged” and names “the small and the great”; 20:12 shows the dead, “the great and the small,” judged before the throne.
    Revelation 11:18 And the nations were enraged, and Thy wrath came, and the time for the dead to be judged, and to give the reward to Thy slaves the prophets and to the saints and to those who fear Thy name, the small and the great, and to destroy those destroying the earth.”
  Jeremiah 17:10 — shared principle: The Lord gives “each according to his ways and the fruits of his practices,” as the dead are judged “according to their works.”
    Jeremiah 17:10 I, the Lord, examine hearts and test kidneys, to give each according to his ways and the fruits of his practices.

Revelation 20:13
And the sea gave the dead in it, and Death and Hades gave the dead in them, and they were judged, each according to their works.
  Revelation 20:12 — internal cross-reference: Both verses say the dead were judged “according to their works”; 20:13 adds that the sea, Death and Hades gave up their dead.
    Revelation 20:12 And I saw the dead, the great and the small, standing before the throne, and scrolls were opened; and another scroll was opened, which is of life. And the dead were judged from the things written in the scrolls, according to their works.
  John 5:28–5:29 — shared image: Jesus says all in the tombs will come out, some to “a resurrection of judgment,” according to what they practiced, as the dead are given up and judged by their works.
    John 5:28 Do not marvel at this, because an hour is coming in which all who are in the tombs will hear His voice
    John 5:29 and come out: those who did good things to a resurrection of life, but those who practiced base things to a resurrection of judgment.
  Revelation 1:18 — internal cross-reference: The risen Christ holds “the keys of death and Hades,” and in 20:13 Death and Hades give up the dead in them.
    Revelation 1:18 and the living One. I became dead, and behold, I am living for the ages of the ages, and I have the keys of death and Hades.

Revelation 20:14
And Death and Hades were thrown into the lake of fire. This is the second death, the lake of fire.
  Revelation 21:8 — internal cross-reference: Both verses identify the lake of fire as “the second death”. Revelation 21:8 assigns a list of evildoers to that lake, whereas here Death and Hades themselves are thrown into it.
    Revelation 21:8 But as for the cowardly and unbelieving and abominable and murderers and sexually immoral and sorcerers and idolaters and all liars, their share is in the lake burning with fire and sulfur, which is the second death.”
  Revelation 20:10 — shared image: Both describe something being “thrown into the lake of fire”. In Revelation 20:10 it is the devil, joining the beast and the false prophet; here it is Death and Hades.
    Revelation 20:10 And the devil who deceived them was thrown into the lake of fire and sulfur, where also are the beast and the false prophet, and they will be tormented day and night for the ages of the ages.
  1 Corinthians 15:26 — thematic parallel: Paul calls death “the last enemy to be brought to nothing”, and Revelation shows Death itself thrown into the lake of fire. Paul does not use the lake-of-fire image.
    1 Corinthians 15:26 The last enemy to be brought to nothing is death,

Revelation 20:15
And if anyone was not found written in the book of life, he was thrown into the lake of fire.
  Revelation 20:12 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 20:12 introduces the scroll “which is of life” and the judgment of the dead. This verse gives the outcome for anyone not found written in “the book of life”.
    Revelation 20:12 And I saw the dead, the great and the small, standing before the throne, and scrolls were opened; and another scroll was opened, which is of life. And the dead were judged from the things written in the scrolls, according to their works.
  Revelation 19:20 — shared image: Both verses describe those judged being thrown into the lake of fire. Revelation 19:20 concerns the beast and the false prophet, “thrown alive”, while this verse concerns anyone not written in the book of life.
    Revelation 19:20 And the beast was seized, and with it the false prophet who performed the signs before it, by which he deceived those receiving the beast’s mark and those worshipping its image. The two were thrown alive into the lake of fire burning with sulfur.
  Matthew 25:41 — parallel judgment: Both describe people condemned at the final judgment being sent into fire. Matthew speaks of “the eternal fire prepared for the devil and his angels”, not of a lake or a book of life.
    Matthew 25:41 Then He will also say to those at His left, ‘Depart from Me, you cursed, into the eternal fire prepared for the devil and his angels.

Revelation 21:1
And I saw a new heaven and a new earth; for the first heaven and the first earth passed away, and the sea is no more.
  Isaiah 66:22 — verbal allusion: Isaiah speaks of “the new heaven and new earth that I make”, which matches John’s “a new heaven and a new earth”. Isaiah uses them as a pledge that Israel’s offspring and name will endure.
    Isaiah 66:22 ‘For just as the new heaven and new earth that I make remain before Me,’ says the Lord, ‘so your offspring and your name will stand.
  Isaiah 65:17–65:19 — fulfilment of promise: Isaiah promises that “heaven will be new and earth new” and that former things will not be remembered. John sees this new heaven and earth after the first ones have passed away.
    Isaiah 65:17 For heaven will be new and earth new, and they will never remember former things, nor will those things enter their heart,
    Isaiah 65:18 but they will find joy and exultation in it. For behold, I am making Jerusalem an exultation and My people joy.
    Isaiah 65:19 I will exult over Jerusalem and rejoice over My people, and no sound of weeping or crying will ever again be heard in her.
  2 Peter 3:13 — verbal parallel: Peter speaks of awaiting, according to God’s promise, “new heavens and a new earth”, and John sees “a new heaven and a new earth”. Peter adds that righteousness dwells there; John adds that the sea is no more.
    2 Peter 3:13 But according to His promise we await new heavens and a new earth, in which righteousness dwells.

Revelation 21:2
And I saw the holy city, new Jerusalem, coming down out of heaven from God, prepared as a bride adorned for her husband.
  Revelation 3:12 — internal cross-reference: Both verses name “the new Jerusalem” as coming down out of heaven from God. Revelation 3:12 promises its name to the one who conquers, and here John sees the city itself.
    Revelation 3:12 The one who conquereth, I will make him a pillar in the temple of My God, and he shall certainly go outside no more. And I will write upon him the name of My God and the name of the city of My God, the new Jerusalem, which cometh down out of heaven from My God, and My new name.
  Revelation 21:10 — parallel vision: Both describe “the holy city” Jerusalem “coming down out of heaven from God”. Revelation 21:10 adds that John is carried to a great and high mountain to see it.
    Revelation 21:10 And he carried me away in the Spirit upon a great and high mountain, and showed me the holy city Jerusalem coming down out of heaven from God,
  Isaiah 61:10 — shared image: Isaiah’s speaker says “like a bride He adorned me with ornaments”, and the city comes “prepared as a bride adorned for her husband”. Isaiah applies the image to the rejoicing speaker, not to a city.
    Isaiah 61:10 and they will rejoice greatly in the Lord.” Let my soul exult in the Lord, for He clothed me in a garment of salvation and a tunic of joy. Like a bridegroom He placed a turban on me, and like a bride He adorned me with ornaments.

Revelation 21:3
And I heard a great voice from the throne, saying, “Behold, the tabernacle of God is with men, and He shall dwell with them, and they shall be His peoples, and God Himself shall be with them as their God,
  Ezekiel 37:27 — verbal allusion: Ezekiel’s “My dwelling will be among them. I will be their God, and they will be My people” matches the voice’s announcement that God will dwell with men and that they will be His peoples. Revelation uses the plural “peoples”.
    Ezekiel 37:27 My dwelling will be among them. I will be their God, and they will be My people.
  Zechariah 8:8 — verbal allusion: Zechariah joins God’s dwelling in Jerusalem with the formula “They will be My people, and I will be their God”. Revelation joins the same two elements, with God’s tabernacle with men.
    Zechariah 8:8 and I will bring them in and dwell within Jerusalem. They will be My people, and I will be their God in truth and righteousness.
  2 Corinthians 6:16 — shared covenant formula: Paul quotes God as saying “I will dwell in them and walk among them, and I will be their God, and they will be My people.” Paul applies it to believers as the temple of the living God now, while Revelation announces it at the coming of the new Jerusalem.
    2 Corinthians 6:16 And what agreement is there between God’s temple and idols? For we are the temple of the living God, just as God said: “I will dwell in them and walk among them, and I will be their God, and they will be My people.

Revelation 21:4
and He shall wipe away every tear from their eyes, and death shall be no more; neither mourning nor crying nor pain shall be any more, for the former things passed away.”
  Isaiah 25:8 — verbal allusion: Isaiah says death is swallowed and that “God removed every tear from every face”. Revelation joins the same two promises: God will wipe away every tear, and death shall be no more.
    Isaiah 25:8 Death swallowed, having prevailed; and again God removed every tear from every face. He removed the people’s reproach from all the earth, for the Lord’s mouth has spoken.
  Revelation 7:17 — internal cross-reference: Both say that God will wipe every tear from their eyes. In Revelation 7:17 this is said of those whom the Lamb shepherds and guides to springs of waters of life, and here of those with whom God dwells.
    Revelation 7:17 because the Lamb in the midst of the throne will shepherd them and guide them to springs of waters of life; and God will wipe every tear from their eyes.”
  Isaiah 35:10 — shared image: Isaiah says of those returning to Zion that “Pain, grief and groaning have fled.” Revelation likewise says pain, mourning and crying will be no more. Isaiah’s setting is the return to Zion.
    Isaiah 35:10 Those gathered through the Lord will return and come to Zion with gladness, everlasting gladness above their heads; for praise and exultation are upon their heads, and gladness will overtake them. Pain, grief and groaning have fled.

Revelation 21:5
And the One sitting upon the throne said, “Behold, I make all things new.” And He saith, “Write, because these words are faithful and true.”
  Isaiah 43:19 — verbal allusion: God’s words “Behold, I am doing new things” in Isaiah correspond closely to “Behold, I make all things new.” Isaiah illustrates this with a way in the wilderness and rivers in dry land.
    Isaiah 43:19 Behold, I am doing new things which will now spring up, and you will know them. I will make a way in the wilderness and rivers in the waterless land.
  Revelation 22:6 — internal cross-reference: Both verses declare that “these words are faithful and true”. Revelation 22:6 repeats the assurance when the vision closes.
    Revelation 22:6 And he said unto me, “These words are faithful and true, and the Lord, the God of the spirits of the prophets, sent His angel to show His slaves the things that must take place soon.”
  2 Corinthians 5:17 — thematic parallel: Paul writes that in Christ “the old things passed away; behold, new things have come to be.” Revelation speaks of God making all things new. Paul applies the language to the person who is in Christ.
    2 Corinthians 5:17 Therefore, if anyone is in Christ, there is a new creation. The old things passed away; behold, new things have come to be.

Revelation 21:6
And He said unto me, “They have come to be. I am the Alpha and the Omega, the beginning and the end. To the one who thirsteth I will give freely from the spring of the water of life.
  Revelation 22:13 — same divine title: Both have the self-designation “I am the Alpha and the Omega … the beginning and the end.” Revelation 22:13 also adds “the first and the last”.
    Revelation 22:13 I am the Alpha and the Omega, the first and the last, the beginning and the end.”
  Revelation 22:17 — internal cross-reference: Both offer water of life freely to the one who thirsts. In Revelation 22:17 the offer is part of the invitation to come and take, while here God says He will give it.
    Revelation 22:17 And the Spirit and the bride say, “Come.” And let the one who heareth say, “Come.” And let the one who thirsteth come; let the one who wisheth take water of life freely.
  Revelation 1:8 — same divine title: The title “I am the Alpha and the Omega” is spoken by the Lord God in Revelation 1:8. Here the One on the throne uses it again at the completion of all things.
    Revelation 1:8 “I am the Alpha and the Omega,” saith the Lord God, the One who is and who was and who is coming, the Almighty.

Revelation 21:7
The one who conquereth will inherit these things, and I will be his God and he will be My son.
  2 Corinthians 6:18 — verbal parallel: Both state a filial relationship with God: “you will be sons and daughters to Me” and “he will be My son”. Paul frames it as God being a Father, while Revelation says “I will be his God”.
    2 Corinthians 6:18 And I will be a Father to you, and you will be sons and daughters to Me, says the Lord Almighty.”
  Revelation 21:3 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 21:3 announces that God will be with His peoples “as their God”. This verse applies the promise to each person who conquers: “I will be his God”.
    Revelation 21:3 And I heard a great voice from the throne, saying, “Behold, the tabernacle of God is with men, and He shall dwell with them, and they shall be His peoples, and God Himself shall be with them as their God,
