  Revelation 2:11 — internal cross-reference: Both verses contain a promise to “the one who conquereth”, and Revelation 2:11 says that person will not be harmed by the second death. The next verse, 21:8, names the second death as the share of those excluded.
    Revelation 2:11 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. The one who conquereth shall certainly not be harmed by the second death.’

Revelation 21:8
But as for the cowardly and unbelieving and abominable and murderers and sexually immoral and sorcerers and idolaters and all liars, their share is in the lake burning with fire and sulfur, which is the second death.”
  Revelation 22:15 — parallel list: Both list sorcerers, the sexually immoral, murderers, idolaters and those given to falsehood as excluded. Revelation 22:15 places them “outside” the city, while 21:8 assigns them to the lake of fire.
    Revelation 22:15 Outside are the dogs and the sorcerers and the sexually immoral and the murderers and the idolaters and everyone loving and practicing falsehood.
  Revelation 2:11 — internal cross-reference: Both mention “the second death”. Revelation 2:11 promises that the one who conquers will not be harmed by it, while this verse gives it as the share of those listed.
    Revelation 2:11 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. The one who conquereth shall certainly not be harmed by the second death.’
  Revelation 19:20 — shared image: Both describe the lake burning with fire and sulfur. In Revelation 19:20 the beast and the false prophet are thrown into it, and here it is the share of the listed evildoers.
    Revelation 19:20 And the beast was seized, and with it the false prophet who performed the signs before it, by which he deceived those receiving the beast’s mark and those worshipping its image. The two were thrown alive into the lake of fire burning with sulfur.

Revelation 21:9
And one of the seven angels having the seven bowls full of the seven last plagues came and spoke with me, saying, “Come, I will show thee the bride, the wife of the Lamb.”
  Revelation 17:1 — parallel introduction: Both open with “one of the seven angels having the seven bowls” who spoke with John: “Come, I will show thee”. One shows the judgment of the great prostitute, and the other shows the bride, the wife of the Lamb.
    Revelation 17:1 And one of the seven angels having the seven bowls came and spoke with me, saying, “Come, I will show thee the judgment of the great prostitute sitting upon many waters,
  Revelation 19:7 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 19:7 announces the marriage of the Lamb and says “His wife prepared herself”. Here the angel offers to show “the bride, the wife of the Lamb”.
    Revelation 19:7 Let us rejoice and exult, and we will give Him the glory, because the marriage of the Lamb hath come, and His wife prepared herself.
  Revelation 21:2 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 21:2 already describes the new Jerusalem as “prepared as a bride adorned for her husband”. Here the angel offers to show the bride, and in the next verse the bride appears as the holy city.
    Revelation 21:2 And I saw the holy city, new Jerusalem, coming down out of heaven from God, prepared as a bride adorned for her husband.

Revelation 21:10
And he carried me away in the Spirit upon a great and high mountain, and showed me the holy city Jerusalem coming down out of heaven from God,
  Revelation 21:2 — internal cross-reference: Both describe the holy city Jerusalem “coming down out of heaven from God”. Revelation 21:2 calls it new Jerusalem and compares it to a bride.
    Revelation 21:2 And I saw the holy city, new Jerusalem, coming down out of heaven from God, prepared as a bride adorned for her husband.
  Revelation 17:3 — parallel vision: Both use the phrase “he carried me away in the Spirit”. In Revelation 17:3 John is taken into a wilderness to see the woman on the scarlet beast, while here he is taken to a great and high mountain to see the holy city.
    Revelation 17:3 And he carried me away in the Spirit into a wilderness. And I saw a woman sitting upon a scarlet beast, full of names of blasphemy, having seven heads and ten horns.
  Ezekiel 8:3 — shared visionary experience: In Ezekiel a spirit lifts the prophet and brings him “to Jerusalem in a vision of God”. Here John is carried away in the Spirit and shown the holy city Jerusalem. Ezekiel is brought to the entrance of the inner gate facing north rather than shown a city descending.
    Ezekiel 8:3 He stretched out the likeness of a hand and lifted me by the crown of my head. A spirit lifted me between earth and heaven and brought me to Jerusalem in a vision of God, to the entrance of the inner gate facing north, where the pillar of the one acquiring stood.

Revelation 21:11
having the glory of God. Her radiance was like a most precious stone, like a jasper stone shining as crystal,
  Ezekiel 43:2 — shared image: Ezekiel sees “the glory of the God of Israel” coming, with the earth flashing “like radiance from the glory”. The city likewise has “the glory of God” and a radiance. In Ezekiel the glory comes by the way of the gate facing east.
    Ezekiel 43:2 Look—the glory of the God of Israel was coming by the way of the gate facing east. The sound of the camp was like the sound of many redoubling their sound, and the earth flashed like radiance from the glory all around.
  Revelation 4:3 — shared image: Both use the image of “a jasper stone”. In Revelation 4:3 the One seated on the throne looks like jasper, and here the city’s radiance does.
    Revelation 4:3 And the One sitting was in appearance like a jasper stone and a sardius, and a rainbow around the throne was in appearance like an emerald.
  Revelation 21:22–21:23 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 21:23 explains that “the glory of God illuminated” the city, which develops the statement here that it has “the glory of God”.
    Revelation 21:22 And I saw no temple therein, for the Lord God Almighty is its temple, and the Lamb.
    Revelation 21:23 And the city hath no need of the sun or the moon to shine upon it, for the glory of God illuminated it, and its lamp is the Lamb.

Revelation 21:12
having a great and high wall, having twelve gates, and at the gates twelve angels, and names inscribed, which are those of the twelve tribes of the sons of Israel.
  Ezekiel 48:31–48:34 — verbal allusion: Ezekiel writes that “The city’s gates will bear the names of Israel’s tribes,” three on each side. Revelation’s city has twelve gates inscribed with the names of the twelve tribes. Ezekiel does not mention angels at the gates.
    Ezekiel 48:31 The city’s gates will bear the names of Israel’s tribes. Three gates will face north: one gate of Reuben, one gate of Judah, and one gate of Levi.
    Ezekiel 48:32 Toward the east, four thousand five hundred, with three gates: one gate of Joseph, one gate of Benjamin, and one gate of Dan.
    Ezekiel 48:33 Toward the south, four thousand five hundred by measure, with three gates: one gate of Simeon, one gate of Issachar, and one gate of Zebulun.
    Ezekiel 48:34 Toward the sea, four thousand five hundred by measure, with three gates: one gate of Gad, one gate of Asher, and one gate of Naphtali.
  Revelation 21:17–21:21 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 21:17–21 describes the same wall and twelve gates in more detail: the wall’s measure and jasper construction, and gates of pearl.
    Revelation 21:17 And he measured its wall at a hundred forty-four cubits, a human measure, which is an angel’s.
    Revelation 21:18 And the construction of its wall was jasper, and the city pure gold like pure glass.
    Revelation 21:19 The foundations of the wall of the city were adorned with every precious stone: the first foundation jasper, the second sapphire, the third chalcedony, the fourth emerald,
    Revelation 21:20 the fifth sardonyx, the sixth sardius, the seventh chrysolite, the eighth beryl, the ninth topaz, the tenth chrysoprase, the eleventh hyacinth, the twelfth amethyst.
    Revelation 21:21 And the twelve gates were twelve pearls; each one of the gates was from one pearl. And the broad street of the city was pure gold, like transparent glass.
  Revelation 7:4–7:8 — internal cross-reference: Both name the tribes “of the sons of Israel”: 7:4–8 counts those sealed “from every tribe of the sons of Israel,” tribe by tribe, while 21:12 inscribes the names of the twelve tribes on the city’s gates.
    Revelation 7:4 And I heard the number of those sealed: a hundred forty-four thousand, sealed from every tribe of the sons of Israel.
    Revelation 7:5 From the tribe of Judah, twelve thousand sealed; from the tribe of Reuben, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Gad, twelve thousand;
    Revelation 7:6 from the tribe of Asher, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Naphtali, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Manasseh, twelve thousand;
    Revelation 7:7 from the tribe of Simeon, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Levi, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Issachar, twelve thousand;
    Revelation 7:8 from the tribe of Zebulun, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Joseph, twelve thousand; from the tribe of Benjamin, twelve thousand sealed.

Revelation 21:13
From the east, three gates; and from the north, three gates; and from the south, three gates; and from the west, three gates.
  Ezekiel 48:31–48:34 — verbal allusion: Ezekiel gives the city three gates facing north, east and south and three toward the sea, each named for a tribe. Revelation gives three gates on each of the four sides. The order differs, and Ezekiel names the western side “toward the sea”.
    Ezekiel 48:31 The city’s gates will bear the names of Israel’s tribes. Three gates will face north: one gate of Reuben, one gate of Judah, and one gate of Levi.
    Ezekiel 48:32 Toward the east, four thousand five hundred, with three gates: one gate of Joseph, one gate of Benjamin, and one gate of Dan.
    Ezekiel 48:33 Toward the south, four thousand five hundred by measure, with three gates: one gate of Simeon, one gate of Issachar, and one gate of Zebulun.
    Ezekiel 48:34 Toward the sea, four thousand five hundred by measure, with three gates: one gate of Gad, one gate of Asher, and one gate of Naphtali.

Revelation 21:14
And the wall of the city had twelve foundations, and upon them twelve names of the twelve apostles of the Lamb.
  Ephesians 2:20 — shared image: Paul speaks of believers “built upon the foundation of the apostles and prophets”, and the city’s foundations carry the names of the twelve apostles of the Lamb. Paul also names the prophets and Christ as cornerstone.
    Ephesians 2:20 having been built upon the foundation of the apostles and prophets, Christ Jesus Himself being the cornerstone,
  Hebrews 11:10 — thematic parallel: Hebrews says “he was awaiting the city that has foundations, whose craftsman and maker is God.” Revelation describes the twelve foundations of the city's wall.
    Hebrews 11:10 For he was awaiting the city that has foundations, whose craftsman and maker is God.
  Revelation 21:19–21:21 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 21:19–21 continues the description of the same twelve foundations and lists the precious stones that adorn each one.
    Revelation 21:19 The foundations of the wall of the city were adorned with every precious stone: the first foundation jasper, the second sapphire, the third chalcedony, the fourth emerald,
    Revelation 21:20 the fifth sardonyx, the sixth sardius, the seventh chrysolite, the eighth beryl, the ninth topaz, the tenth chrysoprase, the eleventh hyacinth, the twelfth amethyst.
    Revelation 21:21 And the twelve gates were twelve pearls; each one of the gates was from one pearl. And the broad street of the city was pure gold, like transparent glass.

Revelation 21:15
And the one speaking with me had a golden measuring reed, to measure the city and its gates and its wall.
  Revelation 11:1–11:2 — internal cross-reference: Both feature a measuring reed. In Revelation 11:1 John is given a reed to measure the temple of God and the altar, while here the angel’s golden reed measures the city, its gates and its wall.
    Revelation 11:1 And a reed like a rod was given to me, with the words, “Rise and measure the temple of God and the altar and those worshipping in it.
    Revelation 11:2 And leave out the court outside the temple, and do not measure it, because it was given to the nations, and they will trample the holy city for forty-two months.
  Zechariah 2:5 — shared image: Zechariah sees “a man with a land-measuring cord in his hand”, and here the one speaking with John holds a measuring reed. The verse shown does not state what Zechariah’s figure will measure.
    Zechariah 2:5 I lifted my eyes and saw: look, a man with a land-measuring cord in his hand!
  Ezekiel 41:1–41:5 — shared image: In Ezekiel a guide measures the temple's porch, doorways and wall, and here the one speaking with John has a reed to measure the city's gates and wall. Ezekiel's measurements concern the temple, not a city.
    Ezekiel 41:1 He brought me into the temple, for which he measured the porch: six cubits in breadth on this side and six cubits in the porch’s width on that side.
    Ezekiel 41:2 The gateway was ten cubits wide, with sidepieces of five cubits on this side and five cubits on that side. He measured its length, forty cubits, and its breadth, twenty cubits.
    Ezekiel 41:3 He entered the inner court and measured the doorway’s jamb, two cubits; the doorway, six cubits; and the doorway’s sidepieces, seven cubits on this side and seven cubits on that side.
    Ezekiel 41:4 He measured the length of the doors, forty cubits, and the breadth, twenty cubits, in front of the temple. He said, “This is the Most Holy Place.”
    Ezekiel 41:5 He measured the house’s wall, six cubits, and the side chamber’s breadth, four cubits, all around.

Revelation 21:16
And the city lieth foursquare, and its length is as great as its width. And he measured the city with the reed at twelve thousand stadia; its length and width and height are equal.
  Ezekiel 48:35 — parallel vision: Both give a measurement of the whole city: Ezekiel's circuit is “eighteen thousand”, and Revelation's city is twelve thousand stadia. The figures and units differ, and the verse in Ezekiel does not say the city is foursquare.
    Ezekiel 48:35 The circuit will be eighteen thousand. And the city’s name, from whatever day it comes into being, will be its name.”

Revelation 21:17
And he measured its wall at a hundred forty-four cubits, a human measure, which is an angel’s.
  Deuteronomy 3:11 — verbal parallel: Deuteronomy measures Og’s bed “by the cubit of a man”, and Revelation measures the wall in cubits by “a human measure”. Both use the phrase to specify the standard of measure, and Revelation adds that it is also an angel’s.
    Deuteronomy 3:11 For only Og king of Bashan remained of the Rephaim. Behold, his bed was a bed of iron; behold, it is in the citadel of the sons of Ammon, nine cubits in length and four cubits in breadth, by the cubit of a man.
  Revelation 13:18 — verbal parallel: Both pause to explain a figure. The beast’s number is “a human number”, and the wall’s measure is “a human measure, which is an angel’s”.
    Revelation 13:18 Here is wisdom. Let the one who hath understanding calculate the number of the beast, for it is a human number, and its number is six hundred sixty-six.

Revelation 21:18
And the construction of its wall was jasper, and the city pure gold like pure glass.
  Revelation 21:21 — internal cross-reference: Both describe pure gold that is like glass. Here it is the city, “pure gold like pure glass”, and in Revelation 21:21 it is the broad street, “pure gold, like transparent glass”.
    Revelation 21:21 And the twelve gates were twelve pearls; each one of the gates was from one pearl. And the broad street of the city was pure gold, like transparent glass.
  Revelation 21:11 — internal cross-reference: The jasper wall echoes the city’s radiance in Revelation 21:11, “like a jasper stone shining as crystal”.
    Revelation 21:11 having the glory of God. Her radiance was like a most precious stone, like a jasper stone shining as crystal,

Revelation 21:19
The foundations of the wall of the city were adorned with every precious stone: the first foundation jasper, the second sapphire, the third chalcedony, the fourth emerald,
  Isaiah 54:11–54:12 — verbal allusion: Isaiah promises the afflicted woman “sapphire as your foundations” and “battlements jasper”, and Revelation names jasper and sapphire as the first two foundations of the city's wall. Isaiah lists fewer stones and assigns jasper to the battlements.
    Isaiah 54:11 Humbled and unsettled woman, you were not comforted! Behold, I am preparing carbuncle as your stone and sapphire as your foundations,
    Isaiah 54:12 and I will make your battlements jasper, your gates crystal stones, and your enclosing wall choice stones,
  Exodus 28:17–28:21 — shared image: Exodus prescribes a fabric set with four rows of stones, including sapphire, jasper and emerald, bearing the names of the twelve tribes. Revelation's foundations are adorned with precious stones, the first four being jasper, sapphire, chalcedony and emerald. The two stone lists overlap only in part.
    Exodus 28:17 You shall weave into it a fabric set with four rows of stones. The first row shall be a sardius, topaz, and emerald;
    Exodus 28:18 the second row a carbuncle, sapphire, and jasper;
    Exodus 28:19 the third row a ligure, agate, and amethyst;
    Exodus 28:20 the fourth row a chrysolite, beryl, and onyx. They shall be enclosed in gold and bound in gold according to their rows.
    Exodus 28:21 The stones shall bear the names of the sons of Israel, twelve according to their names, each engraved like a seal with the name of one of the twelve tribes.

Revelation 21:20
the fifth sardonyx, the sixth sardius, the seventh chrysolite, the eighth beryl, the ninth topaz, the tenth chrysoprase, the eleventh hyacinth, the twelfth amethyst.
  Exodus 28:17–28:20 — shared image: Five of the stones named for these foundations also appear in the four rows of stones woven into the fabric in Exodus: “sardius,” “topaz,” “chrysolite,” “beryl” and “amethyst.” The two lists differ in order and in their other stones.
    Exodus 28:17 You shall weave into it a fabric set with four rows of stones. The first row shall be a sardius, topaz, and emerald;
    Exodus 28:18 the second row a carbuncle, sapphire, and jasper;
    Exodus 28:19 the third row a ligure, agate, and amethyst;
    Exodus 28:20 the fourth row a chrysolite, beryl, and onyx. They shall be enclosed in gold and bound in gold according to their rows.

Revelation 21:21
And the twelve gates were twelve pearls; each one of the gates was from one pearl. And the broad street of the city was pure gold, like transparent glass.
  Revelation 21:18 — internal cross-reference: Both compare the city’s gold to glass. Revelation 21:18 says the city is “pure gold like pure glass”, and here the broad street is “pure gold, like transparent glass”.
    Revelation 21:18 And the construction of its wall was jasper, and the city pure gold like pure glass.
  Revelation 22:2 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 22:2 continues from “its broad street” and places the river and the tree of life in the middle of it.
    Revelation 22:2 in the middle of its broad street. And on this side and that side of the river was a tree of life bearing twelve fruits, yielding its fruit each month; and the leaves of the tree are for the healing of the nations.
  Revelation 18:16 — contrasting image: Babylon, “the great city”, is mourned as adorned with gold and precious stone and pearl. The holy city has gates of pearl and a street of pure gold, so the same materials describe both cities.
    Revelation 18:16 saying, “Woe, woe, the great city, clothed in fine linen and purple and scarlet and adorned with gold and precious stone and pearl,

Revelation 21:22
And I saw no temple therein, for the Lord God Almighty is its temple, and the Lamb.
  Revelation 1:8 — same divine title: Both use the title “Lord God” together with “Almighty”. Revelation 1:8 attaches it to the self-designation “the Alpha and the Omega”, while here the Lord God Almighty is the city’s temple.
    Revelation 1:8 “I am the Alpha and the Omega,” saith the Lord God, the One who is and who was and who is coming, the Almighty.
  John 2:19–2:21 — thematic parallel: John explains that Jesus spoke of “the sanctuary of His body”, and Revelation names the Lamb, with the Lord God Almighty, as the city’s temple. John’s context is the raising of that sanctuary in three days.
    John 2:19 Jesus answered them, “Destroy this sanctuary, and in three days I will raise it.”
    John 2:20 So the Jews said, “This sanctuary was built in forty-six years, and wilt Thou raise it in three days?”
    John 2:21 But He was speaking about the sanctuary of His body.

Revelation 21:23
And the city hath no need of the sun or the moon to shine upon it, for the glory of God illuminated it, and its lamp is the Lamb.
  Isaiah 60:19–60:20 — verbal allusion: Isaiah says the sun and moon will not be the city’s light but “the Lord will be your everlasting light and God your glory.” Revelation says the city needs no sun or moon, for God’s glory illuminates it. Revelation adds that its lamp is the Lamb.
    Isaiah 60:19 The sun will not be your light by day, nor will the rising moon light your night. Instead, the Lord will be your everlasting light and God your glory.
    Isaiah 60:20 For your sun will not set, and your moon will not fail. The Lord will be your everlasting light, and your days of mourning will be fulfilled.
  Revelation 22:5 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 22:5 repeats that there is no need of lamplight and sunlight, “because the Lord God will shine upon them.”
    Revelation 22:5 And night will be no more, and they have no need of lamplight and sunlight, because the Lord God will shine upon them, and they will reign for the ages of the ages.
  Revelation 21:11 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 21:11 first says the city has “the glory of God” with a shining radiance, and this verse names that glory as its light.
    Revelation 21:11 having the glory of God. Her radiance was like a most precious stone, like a jasper stone shining as crystal,

Revelation 21:24
And the nations will walk through its light, and the kings of the earth bring their glory into it.
  Revelation 21:26 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 21:26 restates the same idea, that they will bring “the glory and the honour of the nations” into the city.
    Revelation 21:26 and they will bring the glory and the honour of the nations into it.
  Isaiah 60:16 — thematic parallel: Isaiah tells the one addressed, “You will suck the nations' milk and eat kings' wealth”, and Revelation says nations walk by the city's light and kings bring their glory into it. Isaiah speaks of wealth received rather than glory brought.
    Isaiah 60:16 You will suck the nations’ milk and eat kings’ wealth. You will know that I am the Lord who saves and delivers you, Israel’s God.
  Isaiah 2:2 — thematic parallel: Isaiah foresees that “all the nations will come” to the Lord’s mountain and God’s house in the last days. Revelation pictures the nations walking by the light of the city.
    Isaiah 2:2 In the last days the Lord’s mountain and God’s house will be clearly visible on the summits of the mountains and be raised above the hills; all the nations will come to it,

Revelation 21:25
And its gates will certainly not be shut by day, for there will be no night there,
  Isaiah 60:11 — verbal allusion: Isaiah says “Your gates will be opened continually; day and night they will not be shut”. Revelation says the gates will not be shut by day and that there will be no night.
    Isaiah 60:11 Your gates will be opened continually; day and night they will not be shut, to bring you the nations’ resources and kings being led.
  Revelation 22:5 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 22:5 repeats “night will be no more” and gives the reason that the Lord God will shine upon them.
    Revelation 22:5 And night will be no more, and they have no need of lamplight and sunlight, because the Lord God will shine upon them, and they will reign for the ages of the ages.
  Zechariah 14:7 — shared image: Zechariah describes a day that is “neither day nor night” with light at evening. Revelation says there will be no night in the city. Zechariah describes a unique day of the Lord rather than a city.
    Zechariah 14:7 It will last one day, and that day is known to the Lord, neither day nor night; toward evening there will be light.

Revelation 21:26
and they will bring the glory and the honour of the nations into it.
  Revelation 21:24 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 21:24 says the kings of the earth bring their glory into the city, and this verse extends it to “the glory and the honour of the nations”.
    Revelation 21:24 And the nations will walk through its light, and the kings of the earth bring their glory into it.

Revelation 21:27
And nothing common, and no one practicing abomination and falsehood, will ever enter it, but only those written in the Lamb’s book of life.
  Isaiah 52:1 — verbal allusion: Isaiah calls Jerusalem “holy city” and says “No uncircumcised and unclean person will pass through you again.” Revelation likewise says nothing common will ever enter the holy city.
    Isaiah 52:1 Awake, awake, Zion! Clothe yourself with your strength, Zion, and put on your glory, Jerusalem, holy city. No uncircumcised and unclean person will pass through you again.
  Revelation 13:8 — internal cross-reference: Both refer to the book of life belonging to the Lamb. In Revelation 13:8 those whose names are not written in it worship “it”, and here only those written in it enter the city.
    Revelation 13:8 And all those dwelling upon the earth will worship it, each whose name hath not been written in the book of life of the Lamb slaughtered from the foundation of the world.
  Revelation 20:15 — internal cross-reference: Both turn on being written in the book of life. In Revelation 20:15 anyone not written is thrown into the lake of fire, and here only those written enter the city.
    Revelation 20:15 And if anyone was not found written in the book of life, he was thrown into the lake of fire.

Revelation 22:1
And he showed me a river of water of life, bright as crystal, coming out of the throne of God and of the Lamb,
  Revelation 7:17 — internal cross-reference: Both join the throne, the Lamb and waters of life. In Revelation 7:17 the Lamb guides the redeemed to “springs of waters of life”, and here a river of water of life flows from the throne of God and of the Lamb.
    Revelation 7:17 because the Lamb in the midst of the throne will shepherd them and guide them to springs of waters of life; and God will wipe every tear from their eyes.”
  Zechariah 14:8 — shared image: Zechariah sees “living water” going out from Jerusalem, and John is shown a river of water of life coming out of the throne of God and of the Lamb. Zechariah's water flows to the first and last seas.
    Zechariah 14:8 On that day living water will go out from Jerusalem, half into the first sea and half into the last sea; it will be so in summer and spring.
  Psalms 45:5 — shared image: The psalm speaks of a river whose streams “gladden God's city”, and Revelation shows a river of water of life coming out of the throne of God and of the Lamb.
    Psalms 45:5 The river’s rushing streams gladden God’s city; the Most High sanctified His dwelling.

Revelation 22:2
in the middle of its broad street. And on this side and that side of the river was a tree of life bearing twelve fruits, yielding its fruit each month; and the leaves of the tree are for the healing of the nations.
  Genesis 2:9 — shared image: Genesis places “the tree of life in the midst of the garden”, and Revelation places a tree of life in the middle of the city’s street by the river. Revelation adds twelve fruits and leaves for the healing of the nations.
    Genesis 2:9 And God caused every tree that was beautiful to behold and good for food to spring up from the earth, with the tree of life in the midst of the garden, and the tree of the knowledge of good and evil.
  Revelation 2:7 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 2:7 promises the one who conquers to eat from the tree of life in the Paradise of God, and here John sees that tree bearing fruit in the city.
    Revelation 2:7 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. To the one who conquereth I will give to eat from the tree of life, which is in the Paradise of God.’
  Revelation 22:14 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 22:14 speaks of those who have “right to the tree of life” and enter the city, which refers back to the tree described here.
    Revelation 22:14 Blessed are they who wash their robes, that they may have right to the tree of life and may enter through the gates into the city.

Revelation 22:3
And no accursed thing will be any more. And the throne of God and of the Lamb will be in it, and His slaves will serve Him,
  Zechariah 14:11 — verbal allusion: Zechariah says of Jerusalem “there will no longer be anything accursed”, which closely matches “no accursed thing will be any more.”
    Zechariah 14:11 They will dwell in her; there will no longer be anything accursed, and Jerusalem will dwell confidently.
  Revelation 7:15–7:17 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 7:15 says the redeemed are before the throne of God and “serve Him day and night”. Here His slaves serve Him where the throne of God and of the Lamb is.
    Revelation 7:15 For this reason they are before the throne of God, and serve Him day and night in His temple, and the One sitting upon the throne will spread His tent over them.
    Revelation 7:16 They will hunger no more or thirst any more, nor will the sun fall upon them, nor any burning heat,
    Revelation 7:17 because the Lamb in the midst of the throne will shepherd them and guide them to springs of waters of life; and God will wipe every tear from their eyes.”

Revelation 22:4
and they will see His face, and His name will be upon their foreheads.
  Revelation 14:1 — internal cross-reference: Both describe the name of God or the Lamb on the foreheads of the faithful. In Revelation 14:1 the hundred forty-four thousand have His name and His Father’s name written there.
    Revelation 14:1 And I saw, and behold, the Lamb standing upon Mount Zion, and with Him a hundred forty-four thousand having His name and His Father’s name written upon their foreheads.
  Revelation 3:12 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 3:12 promises that the name of God will be written on the one who conquers. Here His name is on the foreheads of His servants.
    Revelation 3:12 The one who conquereth, I will make him a pillar in the temple of My God, and he shall certainly go outside no more. And I will write upon him the name of My God and the name of the city of My God, the new Jerusalem, which cometh down out of heaven from My God, and My new name.
  Matthew 5:8 — thematic parallel: Jesus promises that the pure in heart “shall see God”, and here His servants “will see His face.” Matthew does not mention a name on the forehead.
    Matthew 5:8 Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God.

Revelation 22:5
And night will be no more, and they have no need of lamplight and sunlight, because the Lord God will shine upon them, and they will reign for the ages of the ages.
  Daniel 7:18 — verbal allusion: Daniel says the holy ones of the Most High will hold the kingdom “for the age of ages”, which closely matches “they will reign for the ages of the ages.”
    Daniel 7:18 The holy ones of the Most High will receive the kingdom and hold the kingdom for ever, for the age of ages.’
  Daniel 7:27 — verbal allusion: Daniel says the kingdom is given to a holy people of the Most High “to reign with everlasting kingship”, and here God’s servants reign forever.
    Daniel 7:27 He gave the kingdom, authority, their greatness, and rule over all the kingdoms under heaven to a holy people of the Most High, to reign with everlasting kingship; all authorities will be subject to him and obey him.

Revelation 22:6
And he said unto me, “These words are faithful and true, and the Lord, the God of the spirits of the prophets, sent His angel to show His slaves the things that must take place soon.”
  Revelation 1:1 — internal cross-reference: The book’s opening and closing both say that God sent His angel to show His slaves “the things that must take place soon.” Revelation 1:1 adds that the revelation was given to Jesus Christ and made known to John.
    Revelation 1:1 A revelation of Jesus Christ, which God gave Him to show His slaves the things that must take place soon. He made it known by sending through His angel to His slave John,
  Revelation 21:5 — internal cross-reference: Both declare that “these words are faithful and true.” In Revelation 21:5 the One on the throne says it, and here the angel repeats it.
    Revelation 21:5 And the One sitting upon the throne said, “Behold, I make all things new.” And He saith, “Write, because these words are faithful and true.”

Revelation 22:7
“And behold, I am coming quickly. Blessed is the one who keepeth the words of the prophecy of this scroll.”
  Revelation 1:3 — internal cross-reference: Both pronounce a blessing on those who keep “the words of the prophecy” written in this book, at its opening and its close.
    Revelation 1:3 Blessed is he that readeth, and they that hear the words of the prophecy and keep the things written therein, for the time is near.
  Revelation 22:12 — internal cross-reference: Both open with “Behold, I am coming quickly.” Revelation 22:12 adds the promise of reward according to each person’s work.
    Revelation 22:12 “Behold, I am coming quickly, and My reward is with Me, to repay each according to what his work is.
  Revelation 3:11 — internal cross-reference: Both contain the saying “I am coming quickly.” In Revelation 3:11 it comes with the call to hold fast so that no one takes the crown.
    Revelation 3:11 I am coming quickly. Hold fast what thou hast, that no one may take thy crown.

Revelation 22:8
And I, John, am the one hearing and seeing these things. And when I heard and saw, I fell to worship before the feet of the angel showing me these things.
  Revelation 19:10 — parallel scene: Both describe John falling before the feet of the one showing him the visions in order to worship. Revelation 19:10 records the same act earlier in the book, where the angel forbids it.
    Revelation 19:10 And I fell before his feet to worship him. And he saith unto me, “See that thou do it not! I am thy fellow slave and of thy brothers who hold the testimony of Jesus. Worship God, for the testimony of Jesus is the spirit of prophecy.”
  Revelation 1:1 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 1:1 says the revelation was made known “through His angel to His slave John”. Here John names himself as the one who heard and saw what the angel showed him.
    Revelation 1:1 A revelation of Jesus Christ, which God gave Him to show His slaves the things that must take place soon. He made it known by sending through His angel to His slave John,

Revelation 22:9
And he saith unto me, “See that thou do it not! I am thy fellow slave and of thy brothers the prophets and of those keeping the words of this scroll. Worship God.”
  Revelation 19:10 — parallel scene: Both have the angel’s rebuke “See that thou do it not! I am thy fellow slave … Worship God.” Revelation 19:10 refers to those holding the testimony of Jesus, and here to the prophets and those keeping the words of this scroll.
    Revelation 19:10 And I fell before his feet to worship him. And he saith unto me, “See that thou do it not! I am thy fellow slave and of thy brothers who hold the testimony of Jesus. Worship God, for the testimony of Jesus is the spirit of prophecy.”
  Colossians 2:18–2:19 — thematic parallel: Paul warns against those who delight in “worship of angels”, and here the angel refuses John’s worship and directs it to God.
    Colossians 2:18 Let no one disqualify you, delighting in humility and worship of angels, dwelling on what he has seen, puffed up without cause by the mind of his flesh,
    Colossians 2:19 and not holding fast to the head, from whom the whole body, supplied and knit together through joints and ligaments, grows with the growth of God.

Revelation 22:10
And he saith unto me, “Do not seal the words of the prophecy of this scroll, for the time is near.
  Daniel 12:4 — contrasting instruction: Daniel is told to “seal the book until the time of completion”, while John is told “Do not seal the words of the prophecy of this scroll, for the time is near.”
    Daniel 12:4 But you, Daniel, cover the commands and seal the book until the time of completion, until the many go mad and the earth is filled with injustice.”
  Daniel 8:26 — contrasting instruction: Daniel’s vision is “closed up, for it is still for many days”, while John’s prophecy is left unsealed because “the time is near.”
    Daniel 8:26 The evening-and-morning vision was found to be true. Now the vision is closed up, for it is still for many days.”
  Revelation 1:3 — internal cross-reference: Both concern “the words of the prophecy” and give the reason “the time is near”, at the book’s opening and its close.
    Revelation 1:3 Blessed is he that readeth, and they that hear the words of the prophecy and keep the things written therein, for the time is near.

Revelation 22:11
Let the one doing wrong do wrong still, and let the filthy one be filthy still, and let the righteous one do righteousness still, and let the holy one be sanctified still.”
  Daniel 12:10 — verbal allusion: Daniel says that “many are tested and made holy, and sinners sin”, which Revelation echoes in letting the wrongdoer still do wrong and the holy one still be sanctified.
    Daniel 12:10 many are tested and made holy, and sinners sin. None of the sinners will understand, but those who understand will pay attention.
  Ezekiel 3:27 — similar formula: Ezekiel's “Let the one who hears hear, and let the one who disobeys disobey” has the same paired “let the one who … ” form as Revelation's sayings. Ezekiel is to say it to an “embittering house.”
    Ezekiel 3:27 But when I speak to you, I will open your mouth, and you will say to them, ‘Thus says the Lord: Let the one who hears hear, and let the one who disobeys disobey,’ for it is an embittering house.”

Revelation 22:12
“Behold, I am coming quickly, and My reward is with Me, to repay each according to what his work is.
  Isaiah 40:10 — verbal allusion: Isaiah’s “behold, the Lord comes … behold, His reward is with Him” closely matches “Behold, I am coming quickly, and My reward is with Me.”
    Isaiah 40:10 Behold, the Lord comes with might, and His arm with dominion; behold, His reward is with Him, and His work before Him.
  Matthew 16:27 — verbal parallel: Jesus says the Son of Man will come and “repay each person according to his conduct”, and Revelation says He comes to repay each according to his work.
    Matthew 16:27 For the Son of Man is to come in the glory of His Father with His angels, and then He will repay each person according to his conduct.
  Isaiah 62:11 — verbal allusion: Isaiah announces the savior arriving for Zion, “bringing His own reward”. Revelation’s coming One likewise says His reward is with Him.
    Isaiah 62:11 For behold, the Lord has made it heard to earth’s farthest end: “Tell daughter Zion, ‘Behold, the savior is arriving for you, bringing His own reward and His work before His face.’”

Revelation 22:13
I am the Alpha and the Omega, the first and the last, the beginning and the end.”
  Isaiah 44:6 — verbal allusion: In Isaiah God says “I am first, and I am after these things; apart from Me there is no God.” Revelation’s “the first and the last” uses the same self-description. In the Septuagint wording Isaiah says “after these things” rather than “last”.
    Isaiah 44:6 Thus says God, Israel’s King, who rescued him, God Sabaoth: “I am first, and I am after these things; apart from Me there is no God.
  Revelation 1:17 — same title of Christ: Both use the self-designation “the first and the last.” In Revelation 1:17 the risen One says it to John as he lies at His feet.
    Revelation 1:17 And when I saw Him, I fell at His feet as dead. And He placed His right hand upon me, saying, “Fear not. I am the first and the last,
  Revelation 21:6 — same divine title: Both have “I am the Alpha and the Omega … the beginning and the end.” Here the title is also joined with “the first and the last”.
    Revelation 21:6 And He said unto me, “They have come to be. I am the Alpha and the Omega, the beginning and the end. To the one who thirsteth I will give freely from the spring of the water of life.

Revelation 22:14
Blessed are they who wash their robes, that they may have right to the tree of life and may enter through the gates into the city.
  Revelation 7:14 — internal cross-reference: Both speak of those who wash their robes. Revelation 7:14 adds that they made them white in the blood of the Lamb.
    Revelation 7:14 And I said to him, “My lord, thou knowest.” And he said unto me, “These are they who are coming out of the great tribulation, and they washed their robes and made them white in the blood of the Lamb.
  Revelation 2:7 — internal cross-reference: Both promise access to the tree of life. In Revelation 2:7 the promise is to the one who conquers, and here to those who wash their robes.
    Revelation 2:7 Let the one who hath an ear hear what the Spirit saith to the Churches. To the one who conquereth I will give to eat from the tree of life, which is in the Paradise of God.’
  Revelation 21:27 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 21:27 states who may “enter” the city, those written in the Lamb’s book of life. Here those who wash their robes enter through its gates.
    Revelation 21:27 And nothing common, and no one practicing abomination and falsehood, will ever enter it, but only those written in the Lamb’s book of life.

Revelation 22:15
Outside are the dogs and the sorcerers and the sexually immoral and the murderers and the idolaters and everyone loving and practicing falsehood.
  Revelation 21:8 — parallel list: Both list sorcerers, the sexually immoral, murderers, idolaters and those given to falsehood. Revelation 21:8 assigns them to the lake of fire, and here they are “outside”.
    Revelation 21:8 But as for the cowardly and unbelieving and abominable and murderers and sexually immoral and sorcerers and idolaters and all liars, their share is in the lake burning with fire and sulfur, which is the second death.”
  Revelation 21:27 — internal cross-reference: Both exclude from the city anyone practicing falsehood. Revelation 21:27 says such people will never enter, and here they are outside.
    Revelation 21:27 And nothing common, and no one practicing abomination and falsehood, will ever enter it, but only those written in the Lamb’s book of life.
  Revelation 9:20–9:21 — internal cross-reference: Revelation 9:20–21 names the unrepented sins of idol worship, murders, sorcerous potions and sexual immorality, the same kinds of sin listed among those outside the city.
    Revelation 9:20 And the rest of the people, who were not killed by these plagues, did not even repent of the works of their hands, so as not to worship the demons and the idols of gold and silver and bronze and stone and wood, which can neither see nor hear nor walk.
    Revelation 9:21 And they did not repent of their murders or their sorcerous potions or their sexual immorality or their thefts.

Revelation 22:16
“I, Jesus, sent My angel to testify these things to you for the Churches. I am the Root and the offspring of David, the bright morning star.”
  Revelation 5:5 — same title of Christ: Both use the title “the Root of David”. In Revelation 5:5 it is joined with “the Lion from the tribe of Judah”.
    Revelation 5:5 And one of the elders saith unto me, “Weep not. Behold, the Lion from the tribe of Judah, the Root of David, conquered, to open the scroll and its seven seals.”
  Isaiah 11:1 — shared image: Isaiah speaks of a shoot and blossom rising from “the root of Jesse”, and Jesus calls Himself “the Root and the offspring of David”. Both use root imagery for a promised descendant, but Isaiah names Jesse rather than David.
    Isaiah 11:1 And a shoot will come from the root of Jesse, and a blossom will rise from the root.
  Numbers 24:17 — shared image: Balaam’s oracle says “A star shall rise from Jacob, and a man shall arise from Israel”, and Jesus calls Himself “the bright morning star”. Numbers does not use the phrase “morning star”.
    Numbers 24:17 I will show him, but not now; I pronounce him blessed, but he does not draw near. A star shall rise from Jacob, and a man shall arise from Israel. He shall crush Moab's leaders and plunder all the sons of Seth.

Revelation 22:17
And the Spirit and the bride say, “Come.” And let the one who heareth say, “Come.” And let the one who thirsteth come; let the one who wisheth take water of life freely.
  Revelation 21:6 — internal cross-reference: Both offer water of life freely to the one who thirsts. In Revelation 21:6 God promises to give from its spring, and here the thirsty are invited to come and take.
    Revelation 21:6 And He said unto me, “They have come to be. I am the Alpha and the Omega, the beginning and the end. To the one who thirsteth I will give freely from the spring of the water of life.
  John 7:37 — verbal parallel: Jesus cries “If anyone thirsts, let him come to Me and drink”, and Revelation says “let the one who thirsteth come”. Revelation adds the invitation of the Spirit and the bride.
    John 7:37 On the last day, the great day of the feast, Jesus stood and cried out, saying, “If anyone thirsts, let him come to Me and drink.

Revelation 22:18
I testify to everyone who heareth the words of the prophecy of this scroll: if anyone addeth to them, God will add to him the plagues written in this scroll.
  Deuteronomy 4:2 — verbal allusion: Deuteronomy commands “You shall not add unto the word which I command you, neither shall you take away from it”. Revelation 22:18–19 applies the same warning against adding to or taking from the words of this scroll.
    Deuteronomy 4:2 You shall not add unto the word which I command you, neither shall you take away from it; you shall keep the commandments of the Lord your God, which I command you this day.
  Deuteronomy 13:1 — verbal allusion: Deuteronomy says of every commanded word “you shall not add unto it, neither shall you take away from it”, and Revelation warns that anyone who adds to its words will receive its plagues.
    Deuteronomy 13:1 Every word which I command you this day, you shall take heed to do; you shall not add unto it, neither shall you take away from it.
  Proverbs 30:6 — verbal parallel: Proverbs warns “Do not add to His words, so that He may not reprove you”. Revelation warns that God will add plagues to anyone who adds to its words.
    Proverbs 30:6 Do not add to His words, so that He may not reprove you and you become false.

Revelation 22:19
And if anyone taketh away from the words of the scroll of this prophecy, God will take away his share from the tree of life and from the holy city, the things written in this scroll.
  Deuteronomy 4:2 — verbal allusion: Deuteronomy commands “neither shall you take away from it”, and Revelation warns against taking away from the words of this prophecy. Revelation adds the penalty of losing one’s share in the tree of life and the holy city.
    Deuteronomy 4:2 You shall not add unto the word which I command you, neither shall you take away from it; you shall keep the commandments of the Lord your God, which I command you this day.
  Revelation 22:2 — internal cross-reference: The tree of life that a person may lose a share in is the one described in Revelation 22:2.
    Revelation 22:2 in the middle of its broad street. And on this side and that side of the river was a tree of life bearing twelve fruits, yielding its fruit each month; and the leaves of the tree are for the healing of the nations.
  Revelation 21:2 — internal cross-reference: The “holy city” from which a person’s share may be taken is the one described in Revelation 21:2, coming down out of heaven from God.
    Revelation 21:2 And I saw the holy city, new Jerusalem, coming down out of heaven from God, prepared as a bride adorned for her husband.

Revelation 22:20
The One testifying these things saith, “Yea, I am coming quickly.” Amen. Come, Lord Jesus.
  1 Corinthians 16:22 — verbal parallel: Paul’s closing cry “Our Lord, come!” matches the book’s closing response “Come, Lord Jesus.”
    1 Corinthians 16:22 If anyone does not love the Lord, let him be accursed. Our Lord, come!
  Revelation 22:12 — internal cross-reference: Both have the promise “I am coming quickly”. Revelation 22:12 adds that His reward is with Him.
    Revelation 22:12 “Behold, I am coming quickly, and My reward is with Me, to repay each according to what his work is.
  Revelation 22:7 — internal cross-reference: Both have the promise “I am coming quickly”. Revelation 22:7 adds a blessing on those who keep the words of the prophecy.
    Revelation 22:7 “And behold, I am coming quickly. Blessed is the one who keepeth the words of the prophecy of this scroll.”

Revelation 22:21
The grace of the Lord Jesus be with all.
  2 Thessalonians 3:18 — parallel benediction: Both letters close by wishing the grace of the Lord Jesus to be with all: “be with all of you” in 2 Thessalonians and “be with all” here. 2 Thessalonians adds “our” and “Christ”.
    2 Thessalonians 3:18 The grace of our Lord Jesus Christ be with all of you.
  Romans 16:20 — parallel benediction: Romans has the closing grace wish “The grace of our Lord Jesus be with you”, which is nearly identical to Revelation’s. Revelation extends it to “all”.
    Romans 16:20 And the God of peace will crush Satan under your feet soon. The grace of our Lord Jesus be with you.
